# Opaque third audit — new findings master list

Post-remediation tree at **12146ec4** (delta `4b13d82..12146ec4`, 124 commits). Format follows the
July and August master lists; numbering continues at **OPQ-400** (August's highest was OPQ-388) and
runs to **OPQ-538**, so the three lists never collide. Findings are grouped by package/subsystem and
severity-ordered within each group. Near-duplicates reported by more than one sweep are merged under
a single ID, with the merged titles noted.

Legend: `file:line` · category · severity · ε:yes/no (does the defect move a reported or deployed
epsilon) · **VERDICT** (CONFIRMED / PLAUSIBLE — every finding here survived independent adversarial
verification; duplicates of open tracked issues are in §8 and refuted candidates in §9).

---

Eleven defects were reported independently by two or three sweeps looking through different lenses and
are written up more than once, because each write-up is the view a different owner will arrive
through. They are **one fix each**, not two or three: OPQ-401 / OPQ-433 / OPQ-447 (the DDP resume
sampler collapse, reached from accounting, engine-distributed and resume), OPQ-403 / OPQ-420 (the
lost `poisson()` inner guard), OPQ-408 / OPQ-505 (the `calibrate()` fallback union bound),
OPQ-417 / OPQ-513 (the stale `bound` allowlist entry), OPQ-430 / OPQ-512 (`tree_leaves` fail-open),
OPQ-406 / OPQ-424 (`random_allocation` in the accounting guide), OPQ-425 / OPQ-524 (the bare-BandMF
participation warning), OPQ-478 / OPQ-483 (Python 3.12 advertised and never executed),
OPQ-480 / OPQ-521 (the extras install commands), OPQ-426 / OPQ-496 (the research diary at the
repository root) and OPQ-495 / OPQ-520 (the privacy-auditing tutorial's amplification). §11 groups
them so no one pays twice.

### The findings that matter most

1. **OPQ-445 (critical) — PEFT/LoRA resume silently restores no adapter weights.** `strict=False` is
   applied to a checkpoint whose key space does not overlap the live module's at all, so the resumed
   run continues from fresh random init while the accountant, noise counter, sampler cursor and
   optimizer state are all restored faithfully — every signal a user checks says the resume worked,
   and in a DP setting the lost steps cannot be re-run because their budget is already spent.
2. **OPQ-401 / OPQ-433 / OPQ-447 — DDP resume installs rank 0's sampler snapshot on every rank**,
   collapsing the per-rank Poisson streams the rank fold exists to keep independent while the
   accountant keeps pricing i.i.d. Poisson amplification: the only ε-affecting defect in the delta
   that destroys an amplification *hypothesis* rather than mispricing a parameter.
3. **OPQ-400 — adaptive clipping releases a `{0,1}` clipped-count query while AdaClip accounting
   prices it at the `{−1/2,+1/2}` sensitivity**, understating the quantile stream's µ by exactly 2×;
   measured with the repo's own accountant, documented per-group configurations report 1.354 against
   a correctly-priced 5.356 (3.96×), and the pairing is what `DPTrainer(clipping_mode="adaptive")`
   wires by default.
4. **OPQ-517 — the flagship LLM fine-tuning tutorial publishes ε = 1.09 for a run whose accounted
   cost is 8.99**, because it prices an adaptively-clipped loop with the plain Gaussian chain: a
   wrong privacy number served today on a live public page, not a latent code defect.
5. **OPQ-465 + OPQ-466 — the generic fused LoRA QKV wrapper trains a different architecture than the
   one loaded**, applying RoPE on Cohere2's NoPE full-attention layers (25 % of layers at the default
   config) and dropping `sliding_window` / `softcap` for Mistral, Ministral and Gemma2 — silent
   numerical divergence on the GPU training path at default patch settings.
6. **OPQ-460 — the now-default fused-CE forward wrapper's non-fused fallback recomputes logits
   without the family's post-`lm_head` transform**, so Cohere/Cohere2/Granite/Gemma2 train against
   an unscaled, unsoftcapped objective on a documented first-class precision mode (fp32 weights on
   CUDA), with no warning and no test reaching the branch.
7. **OPQ-448 — full-finetune resume and `load_best_model_at_end` crash for every tied-embedding
   model**, because the checkpoint omits `lm_head.weight` and the loader is strict: loud rather than
   silent, but it means the documented resume path is broken for both model shapes at once — PEFT
   silently (OPQ-445) and full fine-tunes noisily.

**Honest note on the criticals.** Three findings arrived reported as critical; two were reduced to
**high** on verification, and both reductions were about reachability, not about whether the defect
is real. OPQ-460 (fused-CE fallback dropping the family logit transform) needs fp32 model weights on
CUDA to reach the fallback — a documented mode with an example flag, but not what the most literal
default flow produces, since `from_pretrained(dtype="auto")` on bf16 Cohere/Granite/Gemma2
checkpoints routes to the correct fused branch; impact is utility, not ε. OPQ-465 (RoPE on Cohere2's
NoPE layers) needs one specific family plus LoRA on all of q/k/v plus CUDA, and likewise has no
privacy effect. Only OPQ-445 kept the critical label: it fires on the documented default
fine-tuning shape, on every host, with no configuration required and no observable symptom.

### The theme of this round

The July and August audits found defects in code that had been wrong since it was written. This
round is different, and the difference should shape who fixes what. Six of the fifteen high
findings sit in `opaque-patches`, and **every one of them is a correctness regression introduced by
a commit whose stated purpose was throughput** — `c0f95ced` (#988, "activate fused loss-only
cross-entropy") made a pre-existing wrong fallback branch reachable at trainer defaults (OPQ-460)
and made `torch_compile` unusable for every registered dense family (OPQ-462); the four QKV-fusion
commits (#947/#949/#951/#950) built faithful wrappers for two families and left six more on a
generic wrapper that is faithful to none of them (OPQ-465, OPQ-466); `d80d3f99` (#954) and
`4e61b09d` (#925) extended the compact-SDPA mask elision and reopened OPQ-337 in a sibling path
(OPQ-472). The privacy core, by contrast, held: clipping-bound enforcement survived every mutation
injected into it, per-example isolation through the microbatch and vmap paths is exact, and the new
Poisson PLD math (#850) was conservative at every one of 3,360 points checked against an independent
60-digit closed form and every one of 142 checked against Google's `dp_accounting` (§10).

The second half of the theme is that the Marble milestone (#3, public-release readiness) was closed
15/15 and **verified for the first time this round** — and it does not hold. The repository is
public, the docs site is live, and the documented extras install commands resolve to an unrelated
PyPI project named `opaque` (OPQ-480 / OPQ-521) while six of the ten published distribution names sit
unregistered and claimable (OPQ-481). Closing a readiness milestone without executing its own
instructions is how a supply-chain exposure and a wrong published epsilon (OPQ-517) end up shipping
together.
## 2. Accounting and privacy mathematics

Eighteen findings (from nineteen verified reports, after one merge). Two are high and ε-affecting —
a sensitivity/accountant mismatch in the adaptive-clipping quantile release, and the loss of the
Poisson independence hypothesis on DDP resume — and both are led here. The rest cover the new
generic Poisson PLD amplification work (#850), the calibration and budget plumbing added by the
second-audit remediations, and the coherence of the horizon-accounting, loss-scaler and
bounded-Gaussian removals.

### 2.1 Sensitivity, sampling and amplification invariants

**OPQ-400 — Adaptive clipping releases a {0,1} clipped-count query while AdaClip accounting prices it at the {−1/2,+1/2} sensitivity, under-charging the quantile stream 2× in µ.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/clipping/_adaptive.py:99-122`, `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_clipped_fun.py:362-366`, `packages/opaque-accounting/src/transformations/adaclip.rs:30-34`, `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpsgd/mechanisms/_adaclip.py:78-80,123-126` · sensitivity-accounting-mismatch · **high** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the runtime quantile release is built from a plain 0/1 clipping indicator. `_clipped_fun.py:362` computes `num_clipped = float((norms > effective_cn).sum().item())` and `:365` divides it by the realized batch size to get `clipping_rate`; `_adaptive.py:99-108` recomputes the same count and `:111-122` returns `clipping_rate + randn() * fraction_noise_std`. Under Opaque's documented add-or-remove adjacency an indicator sum moves by up to 1 when a record is added, so the released rate has per-record sensitivity ≈ 1/|B| and µ_b = 1/σ_b. The accountant charges half that: `adaclip.rs:33` returns `(1.0/(z*z) + k/(4.0*sigma_b*sigma_b)).sqrt()` with `sigma_b = expected_batch_size * fraction_noise_std` (`_adaclip.py:78-80`), i.e. µ_b = √k/(2·σ_b), and the non-Gaussian branch composes `_native.gaussian_pld(2.0 * sigma_b, ...)` `num_groups` times (`_adaclip.py:123-126`) on the same assumption. That factor of 4 is Andrew et al.'s `z = (z_Δ^-2 + (2 z_b)^-2)^-1/2`, which is only valid with the ±1/2 shift TF Privacy applies in `quantile_estimator_query.py` (`below = cast(record <= estimate) - 0.5`, with the comment that this makes the count query's sensitivity 0.5 instead of 1.0). Nothing anywhere in Opaque subtracts 0.5 from the indicator, on the single-group path (`_adaptive.py:111-122`), the per-group loops (`:546-575`, `:580-607`) or the distributed path (`_distributed.py:83-92`, which divides the global count by the realized global batch and adds the same noise). A second, smaller error compounds it: the runtime divides by the *realized* batch size while the accountant divides by the constant `expected_batch_size`, so a below-average Poisson draw raises the true sensitivity further.
- **Failure scenario:** any run that pairs `adaptive_clipped_grad` with `dpsgd_acc.adaclip(...)` — including `DPTrainer(clipping_mode="adaptive")`, which wires that pairing itself at `_dp_trainer.py:4471-4480` with the shared default `fraction_noise_std=0.05`, since `_CLIPPING_KWARGS["adaptive"]` exposes only `target_quantile` and `clipping_norm_max` (`_training_arguments.py:213-217`). The quantile term's µ is understated by exactly 2× (4× in variance) and the reported ε is not an upper bound. Measured with the repo's own accountant and prebuilt Rust extension at δ=1e-5, plain-Poisson DP-SGD: the single-group default (nm=1.1, W=256, N=50 000, T=1000) reports 0.7316 against a correctly-priced 0.7354 (0.5 % low), but per-group configurations — documented and supported at `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:481-495` — are far worse because `num_groups` multiplies the mispriced term: K=8 at W=32, nm=0.8, T=1000 reports 1.3536 against 5.3562 (3.96×), and K=16 at nm=0.6 reports 4.6988 against 16.1743 (3.44×).
- **Evidence:** `_clipped_fun.py:362-366` and `:383-393` (0/1 indicator, realized-batch denominator); `_adaptive.py:99-108`, `:111-122`, `:546-575`, `:580-607`; `_distributed.py:83-92`; `adaclip.rs:30-34`; `_adaclip.py:78-80` and `:123-126`; `_dp_trainer.py:4471-4480` and `_training_arguments.py:213-217` (trainer wiring at the shared default); `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:230-249,481-495` (the same pairing taught as a recipe, with no mention of any 1/2 shift); TF Privacy's `quantile_estimator_query.py` as the primary source for the convention the formula assumes; `test_adaptive.py:349-356` checks only the clipped-fraction convention, never the sensitivity.
- **Fix:** preferably shift the released statistic to match the analysis — release `(Σ_i (b_i − 0.5)) / expected_batch_size + N(0, fraction_noise_std²)` and add 0.5 back after noising, using the *public* `expected_batch_size` as the divisor, which fixes both halves at once (this is what TF Privacy does). The alternative is to keep the 0/1 indicator and correct the accountant to `sqrt(1/z² + k/σ_b²)`, dropping the `/4`, and to charge the realized-batch worst case. Either way, add a regression test that pins `AdaClip.effective_noise_multiplier` against the sensitivity the runtime actually realizes, plus a cross-validation test against dp_accounting's adaptive-clipping composition.
- **Notes:** high rather than critical because `clipping_mode` defaults to `"fixed"` (`_training_arguments.py:560`), so adaptive clipping is opt-in, and the single-group error is only ~0.5 %; the 3–4× cases need many per-group quantile releases at small batches. Issue #345 ("Bind AdaClip released statistic to the parameters the accountant charges") appears in the 2026-08-27 open snapshot but not in either 2026-09-13 list and was recorded closed as not-planned, so this is not currently tracked.

**OPQ-401 — DDP resume restores rank 0's sampler key on every rank, collapsing the per-shard streams and destroying the independent-inclusion hypothesis Poisson amplification rests on.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:3944-3949, 5151, 5196, 1862-1877`, `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/sampling/_poisson.py:210-222, 255-265` · sampling-amplification-mismatch · **high** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges three independently reported instances: "DDP resume collapses every rank's Poisson sampler stream to rank 0's", "Resuming a DDP run restores rank 0's sampler key on every rank", "DDP resume installs rank 0's sampler snapshot on every rank".)*
- **Claim:** on a fresh start the trainer folds the rank into the sampler key (`_dp_trainer.py:3944-3949`), which is what makes each shard's Bernoulli(q) mask independent — but that branch is reached only when `ctx.current_sampler is None` (`:3938`). The sampler snapshot instead carries an *absolute* stream key (`_state_dict_poisson` stores `key_seed`/`key_impl`, `_poisson.py:210-222`) and is written by the saving rank only: `self._save_dp_runtime(staging_dir, ctx)` at `:5196` sits inside `if _distributed.should_save(a, self._ddp):` (`:5151`), and `should_save` returns `ddp.is_world_zero` by default (`_distributed.py:211-222`) — unlike the per-rank RNG snapshot at `:5205-5210`, which every rank deliberately writes. Restore then runs unconditionally on every rank (`:1862-1877`) and `_from_state_dict_poisson` rebuilds from the snapshot's seed with no rank re-fold (`_poisson.py:255-265`). All five sampler serializers do the same (`_poisson.py:261`, `_k_out_of_t.py:218`, dpftrl `_poisson.py:304`, `_b_min_sep.py:259`, `_balls_in_bins.py:260`). Because the dataset is trimmed to a multiple of `world_size` (`:3886-3908`), every shard has the same length, so from the resume point all ranks select *identical local index sets*: the global batch becomes the union of the W co-indexed records at each sampled local position. Marginals are still q, but each record's inclusion indicator is now perfectly correlated with its W−1 shard-mates, and that independence is exactly what lets the Poisson mixture decompose as (1−q)P + qQ against the same P. The in-code comment at `_dp_trainer.py:3874-3886` acknowledges the correlation and then asserts that "the per-record marginal stays Bernoulli(q) either way, so the privacy accounting is unaffected" — which is the wrong conclusion, and actively misinforms the reader.
- **Failure scenario:** a multi-GPU DP-SGD or DP-FTRL run that is checkpointed and resumed. Reproduced: two rank-folded `PoissonSampler(range(1000), 0.05, key=fold_in(key(7), r))` diverge on a fresh start (first batches `[19,29,42,…]` vs `[39,55,60,…]`), but after `from_state_dict(template_r, state_dict(rank0_sampler))` both hold `RngKey(seed=12201757873983228112, impl="opaque_threefry_like")` and emit the byte-identical batch `[20,43,76,86,160,197,215,224]`. The accounted bound is then violated: for the achievable neighbouring pair (all records zero except the target's W−1 co-indexed shard-mates at +C), the single-step hockey-stick at nm=1.1, q=0.01, W=8 is δ(ε=1.0) = 9.6e-4 by quadrature against the repo accountant's 1.3e-10 for the same step (2.0e-3 vs 2.7e-8 at ε=0.5) — a rigorous seven-order-of-magnitude violation. Over T=1000 steps the reported ε is 1.5154 while the idealized indicator-revealed mechanism needs 20.43 at δ=1e-5.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:3938,3944-3949` (rank fold-in only on a fresh build); `:5151,5196` with `_distributed.py:211-222` (rank-0-only DP runtime bundle) against `:5205-5210` (per-rank RNG bundle); `:1862-1877` (unconditional restore on every rank); `_poisson.py:210-222,255-265` and the four sibling serializers; the two-sampler `from_state_dict` reproduction; the quadrature evaluation of the co-indexed neighbouring pair against the accountant's own single-step δ; `_dp_trainer.py:3874-3886` (the comment asserting the accounting is unaffected); the July audit's clean-areas note certified this path for the fresh-start case only, so the resume path had never been checked.
- **Fix:** make the sampler stream key rank-derived at *restore* time rather than stored as an absolute — either persist only the rank-independent base key plus the cursor and re-apply `fold_in(base, rank)` in `from_state_dict`, or write a per-rank sampler snapshot (`dp_state.rank{r}.pt`) alongside the per-rank RNG snapshot and have each rank restore its own. Until one of those lands, fail closed: refuse to restore a sampler snapshot when `world_size > 1` unless the snapshot records the rank it was written for and that rank matches. Correct the comment at `_dp_trainer.py:3874-3886`. Lock it in with a 2-rank gloo test that saves, resumes, and asserts the ranks' post-resume index sets are disjoint in distribution (differing stream keys).
- **Notes:** the horizon figure (13.5×) is an idealization that assumes error-free per-step indicator decoding — at W=8 the separation is 6.4σ, so decoding error is ~7e-4; the single-step violation is exact. For cyclic-Poisson and balls-in-bins the shared key also fixes a common partition or bin assignment, so the co-participation structure becomes permanent rather than per-step.

**OPQ-402 — `paired_noise_stddevs` documents a second-moment sensitivity of `first * first`, which is `normalize_by` times too small for the averaged release the library produces.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/noise_allocation.py:205-211` · docs-safety · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the `second` parameter doc says the second-stream per-record sensitivity Δ² is "typically obtained as ``first * first`` element-wise", and the `first` doc says that for DP-SGD averaged clipping `first = C / n`. That recipe yields C²/n². The quantity actually released on the second stream is `(Σ_i g_i ⊗ g_i) / normalize_by`, whose per-record L2 bound is `‖g ⊗ g‖₂ / n ≤ ‖g‖² / n = C² / n` — a factor of `normalize_by` larger. Every shipped path uses the correct convention: `_clipped_fun.py:386-394` sets `squared_bound = (current * current) / normalize_by`, `_adaptive.py:396-397` does the same, and `resolve_paired_clipped` (`noise_allocation.py:255-281`) and `dpftrl/noise/_second_moment.py:146-150` both read the bound off `ClippedPytree.max_norm`. `_clipped_fun.py:466-473` even states the rule correctly for its own `second_moment` parameter. So nothing in the library follows its own docstring.
- **Failure scenario:** a monorepo contributor wiring a new paired first/second-moment mechanism follows the documented recipe with averaged clipping and passes C²/normalize_by² where the release has sensitivity C²/normalize_by, under-noising the second stream by a factor of `normalize_by` while the accountant still reports `gaussian(nm)`. No shipped call site does this, and with the default `normalize_by = 1.0` the recipe is not even wrong — `first * first = C²`.
- **Evidence:** `noise_allocation.py:205-211` (the two parameter docs, verbatim); `_clipped_fun.py:386-394` and `_adaptive.py:396-397` (the correct convention); `_clipped_fun.py:466-473` (the correct wording, in the same package); `noise_allocation.py:255-281` and `dpftrl/noise/_second_moment.py:146-150` (both consumers read the bound rather than recomputing it).
- **Fix:** change the `second` parameter doc to "the per-record squared bound divided by the same `normalize_by` as the first stream — `C**2 / normalize_by`, not `first * first`", mirroring the wording already used for `clipped_fun`'s `second_moment`. Add a unit test asserting that `resolve_paired_clipped` on a `second_moment=True` clipping output satisfies `second == first * normalize_by * first`, so the relation stays pinned.
- **Notes:** the module header states that `noise_allocation` is **not** a supported public API and it is imported only from `opaque.api.*` internals, so the reduced claim is documentation-only: there is no current miscalibration anywhere in the shipped tree. The original report was filed as ε-affecting; the verifier established that no shipped path follows the recipe, so it does not move any reported ε today.

### 2.2 Poisson PLD amplification (#850)

**OPQ-403 — `dpsgd_acc.poisson()` now accepts any `DpProcess` as its base, so a whole-horizon or already-amplified process is silently re-subsampled and priced as a mechanism no Opaque sampler realizes.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpsgd/amplification/_poisson.py:22, 40-47, 161-172, 185-186` · amplification-semantics / accounting-fail-open · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges two reports: "poisson() now accepts whole-horizon and composed processes as its base, silently accounting a mechanism no Opaque sampler realizes", "Generalizing `dpsgd_acc.poisson()` to any DpProcess removed the inner-type guard, so an already-amplified whole-horizon process is silently re-subsampled and reports a smaller epsilon".)*
- **Claim:** 3b22a6af ("feat(accounting): generalize Poisson PLD amplification (#850)") replaced `_Inner = Gaussian | AdaClip | NonPrivate` with `_Inner = DpProcess` (`_poisson.py:22`) and replaced the factory's `match` on the inner type with `if not isinstance(self.inner, DpProcess)` (`:40-47`) — the only inner-type check left on the plain path. The plain branch then calls `_native.poisson_pld(self.inner.pld(...), self.sample_rate)` for every inner type (`:161-172`). Nothing at construction or PLD time distinguishes a per-step base from a `DpHorizonProcess` subclass (`KOutOfT`, `CyclicPoisson`, `BallsInBins`, `BMinSep`) or from a `Repeated`/`Composed` spine, even though the library owns exactly that marker — `core/_horizon.py` documents `DpHorizonProcess` as "a complete fixed-horizon process that cannot be accounted by prefixes" and `docs/reference/accounting.md:283` types `k_out_of_t` as returning one. `Poisson.__post_init__` simply never consults it. The truncated path still enforces `Gaussian | AdaClip | NonPrivate` (`:91-99`), so the two branches now disagree about what a valid base is, and the factory docstring still reads "Returns a single-step process — compose externally with ``* num_steps`` for full-training privacy" (`:185-186`) while `docs/reference/accounting.md:232` and `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:114` advertise "any Opaque `DpProcess`" with no caveat. The resulting number is a valid bound only for "draw one dataset-level Bernoulli(q) coin per record, then run the entire horizon on the survivors" — a mechanism no shipped Opaque sampler implements, since `PoissonSampler` redraws every step and `KOutOfTSampler` has no dataset-level coin.
- **Failure scenario:** a user writes `dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.k_out_of_t(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(1.0), k=2, t=100, allocation="block"), 0.01)`. It is accepted at HEAD and reports `epsilon_at(1e-5) = 0.0097389` for the whole 100-step run, against 1.2339386 for the same horizon accounted correctly (`k_out_of_t` alone, which is what `KOutOfTSampler` realizes) — 127× higher — and 0.7180658 for the closest realizable per-step reading `poisson(gaussian(1.0),0.01)*100`. Before #850 the same call raised `TypeError`. The double-amplification variant is equally silent: `ftrl_acc.poisson(mf_gaussian(1.0, band_mf_strategy(bands=8)), sample_rate=0.08, n_steps=1000)` reports 6.2286, and wrapping that process again in the DP-SGD factory reports 2.6974 (2.3× smaller) with no error or warning; `da.poisson(da.poisson(gaussian(1.1),0.01),0.01)*1000` gives 0.0098 instead of 1.5154. Neither side of the step/horizon boundary is guarded — `dpsgd_acc.k_out_of_t(...) * 2` also succeeds, returning 1.6398.
- **Evidence:** `_poisson.py:22` (`_Inner = DpProcess`), `:40-47` (the sole check), `:161-172` (unconditional native call), `:185-186` (the unchanged per-step docstring), `:91-99` (the surviving guard on the truncated branch); `git show 3b22a6af^:…/_poisson.py` ends with `case Gaussian() | AdaClip() | NonPrivate(): pass; case _: raise TypeError(...)`, and 3b22a6af replaced it while adding tests that exercise only a generic *per-step* base (`test_amplification.py:163-167`, `test_accepts_generic_dp_process`); `core/_horizon.py:12-18` (the unconsulted marker); `docs/reference/accounting.md:232` and `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:114` (the uncaveated widening); all epsilon figures reproduced with the checked-in extension.
- **Fix:** keep the generalization but gate it in `Poisson.__post_init__` (so direct construction and deserialization are covered, not just the factory): reject `DpHorizonProcess` inners outright — they already describe a complete amplified run — and reject inners that already contain an amplification node (`Poisson`, `ParallelPoisson`, `KOutOfT`, `CyclicPoisson`, `BMinSep`, `BallsInBins`) unless the caller opts in with a flag that names the one-dataset-level-coin model. Apply the same guard to `ParallelPoisson` and to `dpftrl_acc.poisson`. Document that model separately from the DP-SGD per-step reading, say which sampler realizes it (none today), and cover it with a test asserting `dpsgd_acc.poisson(ftrl_acc.poisson(...), q)` raises.
- **Notes:** reachable only by a user writing the nested composition — no Opaque factory, trainer path, calibration helper or documented example does so, and DP-FTRL runs go through `opaque.dpftrl.accounting.poisson`, whose `_Inner` is still `MfGaussian`. The returned numbers are the mathematically correct accounting for the expression the caller wrote, so the loss is intent-checking rather than unsound arithmetic; the acceptance is now test-pinned and documented, which is why the second report's severity was cut to low. What remains real in both is the fail-open direction: an expression that used to raise now silently returns a smaller ε. Not independently verified: what mechanism, if any, the returned bound is correct for beyond the single dataset-level-coin reading — that was reasoned about but not proved against the Feldman–Shenfeld subsampling theorem cited in `poisson.rs:17-20`.

**OPQ-404 — Routing the subsampled Gaussian through the generic pointwise Poisson transform costs tightness against the external oracle, and the cross-validation tolerances were relaxed 60×–220× to absorb it.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/accounting/test_cross_validation.py:47-58, 265-267, 343-346, 389`, `packages/opaque-accounting/src/amplification/poisson.rs:24, 45-74, 233` · accuracy-and-test-integrity · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** #850 deleted the analytic `poisson_gaussian_pld` and routed the Poisson-subsampled Gaussian through `poisson_pld` → `poisson_from_gaussian` → `disc_dist` → `project_transformed_atoms` (`poisson.rs:24`, `:45-74`, `:233`): the Gaussian loss is discretized first, wrapped in `new_symmetric` so the Gaussian tag is gone, and then Connect-the-Dots-projected atom by atom, instead of evaluating the exact subsampled hockey-stick curve at each knot. `project_transformed_atoms` takes a `refinement` parameter that both call sites (`:337`, `:352`) pass as 1. The analytic `poisson_gaussian_epsilon_bounds` (`:399`) and `poisson_gaussian_get_delta` (`:486`) survive but are now reached only from `truncated_poisson.rs` and the crate's own unit tests, so the truncated sibling keeps the analytic curve (`truncated_poisson.rs:57-61` falls back to `poisson_pld` only when `p_trunc == 0.0`) and the two Poisson paths no longer share an accuracy characteristic. The same commit loosened the external-oracle assertions from a single `ATOL = 1e-6` to `POISSON_EPSILON_ATOL = 6e-5`, `POISSON_DELTA_ATOL = 3e-8`, `POISSON_ADVANTAGE_ATOL = 1.3e-5`, `POISSON_BETA_ATOL = 2.2e-4` (`test_cross_validation.py:47-54`) and moved the surviving 1e-6 assertion onto `_POISSON_*_GOLDENS` generated from Opaque's own output (`:265-267`, `:343-346`). The module note at `:56-58` still claims "opaque and dp_accounting agree to ~1e-9 relative error".
- **Failure scenario:** measured at HEAD on the test suite's own 34-point grid against Google dp_accounting 0.6.0 (`pld_lib.from_gaussian_mechanism(sigma, sampling_prob=q).self_compose(steps).get_epsilon_for_delta(1e-5)`): worst `|opaque − dp_accounting|` in `epsilon_at(1e-5)` is 5.33e-05 at σ=0.5, q=1e-3, 1000 steps (3.2389339 vs 3.2388806), and all 34 signed differences are positive (minimum +3.4e-08), so the bound is always conservative — ε is over-stated, never under-stated. The live risk is test integrity rather than privacy: a future change that loses another 6e-5 of ε against dp_accounting now passes cross-validation as long as the goldens are regenerated. The committed vector `poisson(adaclip(gaussian(1.1)), q=0.01) * 200` moved 0.7256467822715522 → 0.7256797187172928 (+3.3e-5) in the same commit.
- **Evidence:** `test_cross_validation.py:47-54` (the four new tolerances against `ATOL = 1e-6`), `:56-58` (the stale ~1e-9 note), `:265-267` and `:343-346` (the 1e-6 check retargeted onto self-generated goldens), `:389` (β against riskcal at 2.2e-4); the diff of 3b22a6af shows the oracle comparisons were previously `pytest.approx(ref, abs=ATOL)` with no goldens at all; `poisson.rs:24,45-74,65-70,233,337,352,399,486`; `truncated_poisson.rs:9,57-61,135-153,176-205`; the 34-point re-measurement against dp_accounting 0.6.0 with the checked-in HEAD extension.
- **Fix:** either restore the analytic fast path for a Gaussian-tagged base by feeding `poisson_gaussian_get_delta` / `poisson_gaussian_epsilon_bounds` into `discretize_asymmetric_mechanism` and keeping the generic pointwise transform only for untagged bases, or — if the generic path must be universal — raise the transformed-grid `refinement` until the oracle gap is back under 1e-6 and restore `ATOL` on the dp_accounting and riskcal comparisons. Fix the stale "~1e-9" note at `test_cross_validation.py:56-58` either way.
- **Notes:** direction is uniformly conservative, so no privacy claim is weakened and the original ε flag does not survive. The finding's "four orders of magnitude" framing could not be confirmed: the checked-in extension is a post-#850 build with no `poisson_gaussian_pld` export, so the pre-#850 path's claimed 3.98e-9 agreement is unverified and only "at least ~53× looser than the previously enforced 1e-6" is established.

**OPQ-405 — `ParallelPoisson`'s inner-mechanism guard lives only in the factory, so direct construction and deserialization bypass it and fail later with a self-contradictory message.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpsgd/amplification/_parallel_poisson.py:24-38, 93-98, 131-140` · validation-placement · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** #850 widened `Poisson`'s inner type and compensated by adding a `match inner: case Gaussian() | AdaClip() | NonPrivate()` guard to the `parallel_poisson()` factory (`:131-140`). `ParallelPoisson.__post_init__` (`:24-38`) validates only `num_workers` and `inner.truncated_batch_size`, so the guard is bypassed by direct construction and by `opaque.serialization.from_state_dict`, which reconstructs through the dataclass rather than the factory. That contradicts the convention the sibling file documents in a comment — `_poisson.py:68-72` says truncation pairing is validated in `__post_init__` "so direct construction and deserialization can't … fail at PLD time". The deferred error is also wrong: the `case _` arm at `:93-98` reports "ParallelPoisson requires a Poisson inner mechanism, got Poisson", naming the wrapper it did receive instead of the unsupported innermost mechanism.
- **Failure scenario:** `parallel_poisson(eps_delta(0.3), 0.1, 2)` correctly raises `InputTypeError: parallel_poisson() requires a Gaussian, AdaClip, or NonPrivate inner mechanism, got EpsDelta.`, but `ParallelPoisson(inner=poisson(eps_delta(0.3), 0.1), num_workers=4)` constructs fine, `state_dict`/`from_state_dict` round-trips it fine, and both `.pld()` and `.epsilon_at(1e-5)` then raise `InputTypeError: ParallelPoisson requires a Poisson inner mechanism, got Poisson.` A saved accountant state carrying an unsupported inner therefore reloads silently and dies on the first epsilon query, with a message that points at the wrong level of the tree.
- **Evidence:** `_parallel_poisson.py:24-38` (post-init checks only two fields), `:93-98` (the self-contradictory message), `:131-140` (the factory-only guard added by 3b22a6af); `_poisson.py:68-72` (the stated convention) and `:40-47` (`Poisson.__post_init__` accepts any `DpProcess`, so nothing upstream blocks the bypass); all four steps reproduced at HEAD, including the `from_state_dict` round-trip.
- **Fix:** move the `Gaussian | AdaClip | NonPrivate` check into `ParallelPoisson.__post_init__`, matching on `self.inner.inner`, and change the `case _` message in `pld()` to name `type(self.inner.inner).__name__`. Add a test constructing `ParallelPoisson` directly and via `from_state_dict` with an unsupported innermost mechanism and asserting both raise at construction.
- **Notes:** accounting fails closed in every case — no unsound ε is ever produced — so the cost is a late, misleading failure rather than a privacy defect.

**OPQ-406 — #850 reintroduced the removed `random_allocation` API into the accounting guide and narrowed the documented plain-Poisson `sample_rate` range below what the code accepts.** `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:586`, `docs/reference/accounting.md:236` · docs · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** fc44ec8d (#761) deleted the public `random_allocation` accountant and sampler (216 and 225 lines, plus their tests) in favour of `k_out_of_t`. Four days later 3b22a6af edited the same paragraph and put the name back: the diff is "`k_out_of_t`, use deterministic transforms" → "`random_allocation` and `k_out_of_t`, use deterministic transforms". At HEAD no public symbol of that name exists — the only survivors are the private Rust `random_allocation_gaussian_pld`, reached internally from `k_out_of_t` and `balls_in_bins` through `core/_random_allocation_cache.py`, and an unrelated third-party cross-validation package. Separately, the same commit set `docs/reference/accounting.md:236` to document plain-Poisson `sample_rate` as being in `(0, 1)`, while `_poisson.py:49` accepts `0 < sample_rate <= 1` and `Poisson.pld` has a documented `sample_rate == 1.0` passthrough at `:126` that the factory docstring advertises.
- **Failure scenario:** a reader who follows the guide and calls `dpsgd_acc.random_allocation(...)` gets an `AttributeError`; a reader who trusts the `(0, 1)` range avoids the supported no-amplification configuration, which works — `poisson(gaussian(1.1), 1.0)` constructs and `(step*10).epsilon_at(1e-5)` returns 15.78.
- **Evidence:** `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:586`; `git show 3b22a6af -- docs/user-guide/accounting.md` (the exact hunk) and `git show fc44ec8d --stat` (the deletions); a grep for `random_allocation` over all Python sources finds no public symbol; `docs/reference/accounting.md:236` against `_poisson.py:49` and `:126`, with the q=1 path executed.
- **Fix:** drop `random_allocation` from `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:586`, leaving Balls-in-Bins with `identity_strategy()` and `k_out_of_t` as the deterministic transforms, and restore `(0, 1]` at `docs/reference/accounting.md:236` for plain Poisson while keeping `(0, 1)` for the truncated form, which really does reject 1.0.
- **Notes:** the verifier found the inverse error eleven lines further down in the same file — `docs/reference/accounting.md:274` documents `parallel_poisson`'s `sample_rate` as `(0, 1]` while 1.0 actually raises `ConfigurationError`. Worth fixing in the same pass, though it was not part of the original report.

**OPQ-407 — `opaque_accounting.pyi` declares two native functions the compiled extension does not export.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/opaque_accounting.pyi:392, 413` · type-stubs · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the hand-maintained PyO3 stub declares `balls_in_bins_gaussian_pld` (`:392`) and `balls_in_bins_gaussian_pld_epochs` (`:413`). Neither symbol is registered anywhere in `packages/opaque-accounting/src/python/` — there is no `#[pyfunction]` and no `m.add_function(...)` for either name — and neither is present in the loaded extension. AST-parsing the stub gives 38 top-level `def`/`class` names against the extension's 36: stub-minus-runtime is exactly those two, runtime-minus-stub is empty. Nothing in the test suite checks stub/runtime parity, so the stub can drift, and #850 rewrote the neighbouring `poisson_pld` entry in this same file without noticing.
- **Failure scenario:** a contributor writing `_native.balls_in_bins_gaussian_pld(...)` inside `opaque` type-checks clean and fails at runtime with `AttributeError`. No public façade reaches these names, so there is no user-facing or accounting-correctness impact.
- **Evidence:** `opaque_accounting.pyi:392` and `:413`; the AST-vs-`dir()` diff against the loaded extension; a grep for `balls_in_bins` over `packages/opaque-accounting/src/python/` returns nothing (the Rust balls-in-bins code is exposed only through `bnb_mc_pld`-style entries).
- **Fix:** delete the two stale stub entries and add a parity test in `packages/opaque-accounting/tests` asserting that `{d for d in dir(_native) if not d.startswith("_")}` equals the set of top-level `def`/`class` names parsed out of the `.pyi`. That is machine-readable structure rather than prose, so it is permitted under the repo's test-design rule.
- **Notes:** pre-existing drift — the functions were dropped around 167b1254, before this delta — but untracked, and it sits in the file the Poisson change edited.

### 2.3 Calibration, budgets and accountant plumbing

**OPQ-408 — `calibrate()`'s runtime-safety fallback re-runs the whole adaptive search without the union bound while still reporting the undivided `mc_failure_probability`.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/calibration.py:301-309, 321-323, 331-339, 350-351` · accounting-confidence · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `calibrate()` deliberately runs its adaptive probes under `probe_config = replace(overall_config, mc_failure_probability = overall / (max_iterations + 2))` (`:301-309`) so that, by a union bound over the two endpoint probes and up to `max_iterations` interior probes, every probe's Monte-Carlo confidence band holds simultaneously with probability ≥ 1 − β. The OPQ-323 remediation (aa15e6ac) added a fallback: if the calibrated parameter is not privacy-safe when re-evaluated under the runtime config, the entire bisection is re-run — but inside `with _use_discretization(overall_config)` (`:321-323`), i.e. with *every* probe at the full β rather than β/(max_iterations+2). The returned `param` and `achieved` then come from that unprotected second search (`:331-339`), yet `:350-351` still stamps `result.mc_failure_probability = overall_config.mc_failure_probability` whenever the final PLD is MC-backed, so `CalibrateResult.mc_confidence` reports 1 − β. The honest simultaneous bound for the fallback search is 1 − (number of probes)·β.
- **Failure scenario:** calibrating any Monte-Carlo-backed process. Instrumented on a real mechanism (b-min-sep BandMF, bands=4, n_steps=40, `epsilon_budget(3.0, delta=1e-3)`, `mc_resolution=5e-3`, `mc_failure_probability=1e-2`, `max_iterations=30`, spying on `Budget.evaluate`): the fallback fired in 6 of 10 seed/p₀ combinations, each time running ~25–30 probes at β/32 followed by ~25–29 probes at the full β — e.g. `Counter({0.0003125: 28, 0.01: 28})` — and the returned result reported `mc_confidence = 0.990000` where the simultaneous bound over the 28 deciding probes is at best 0.72. At the library defaults (β=1e-6, `max_iterations=100`) a team publishing "ε = 3.0 at δ = 1e-5 with Monte-Carlo confidence 1 − 1e-6" from `result.mc_confidence` is overstating that confidence by up to ~100× on the fail-open side.
- **Evidence:** `calibration.py:301-309` (the probe config), `:321-323` (the context opened before the fallback), `:331-339` (the full re-bisection inside it, and `achieved` taken from it), `:350-351` (the unconditional stamp), `:70-92` (`CalibrateResult.mc_failure_probability` documented as "Overall failure probability covering every adaptive Monte Carlo probe"); `docs/reference/accounting.md:731-734` states verbatim the property the fallback discards ("result.mc_confidence therefore covers the adaptive search as a whole rather than only its selected final parameter"); `DiscretizationConfig.resolved_num_mc_samples` is 2 940 252 at β=1e-6 versus 3 417 797 at β=1e-6/102, so the probe and runtime configs differ only in sample count while the band half-width stays `mc_resolution` — which is why probe-vs-runtime differences are unsigned estimation noise and the fallback fires roughly half the time; `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/accounting/test_calibration.py:604-634` confirms the path is live and covered.
- **Fix:** run the fallback bisection under the probe config too and only the final evaluation under `overall_config` — e.g. bisect against a target tightened by the observed probe-to-runtime gap rather than re-searching at the loose failure probability. If a second adaptive search at `overall_config` is genuinely wanted, set `result.mc_failure_probability = overall_config.mc_failure_probability * (result.iterations + 2)` for that path so `mc_confidence` is honest. Either way, record which path produced the result, make `docs/reference/accounting.md:731-734` conditional on it, and add a test asserting `mc_confidence` after a forced fallback.
- **Notes:** ε itself is unchanged and the direction of the calibrated parameter is still re-checked for privacy safety; what is understated, by 25–100×, is the failure probability attached to the reported ε — which is why the original ε flag does not survive. The secondary observation that the fallback roughly doubles the probe count overlaps open issue #911 (Monte Carlo calibration performance), but the confidence-accounting defect is distinct and not tracked there.

**OPQ-409 — `CalibrateResult.achieved` can violate the documented `isclose(achieved, target, rel_tol=tolerance)` contract while still reporting `converged=True`.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/calibration.py:324-325, 340, 474-534` · api-contract · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** before aa15e6ac, `result.achieved` came from the bisection itself, and `_calibrate_impl` returns only when `math.isclose(current, budget.value, rel_tol=tolerance, abs_tol=0.0)` holds (`:474-534`), where `converged=True` is hard-coded. The fix now overwrites `achieved` at `:340` with a separate evaluation of the same process under the runtime discretization (`achieved = budget.evaluate(final_process)`, `:324-325`), which for Monte-Carlo PLDs is a different draw and therefore a different number. Only privacy-safety is re-checked (`:326-330`); closeness is not. Both `calibrate()`'s docstring (`:136-142`, "In both cases, achieved and target are close under the requested relative tolerance") and `docs/reference/accounting.md:722-725` ("`math.isclose(achieved, target, rel_tol=tolerance, abs_tol=0.0)` is true") still assert the old invariant.
- **Failure scenario:** for any Monte-Carlo-backed process, `calibrate()` returns `converged=True` with an `achieved` that is a fresh runtime re-measurement rather than the converged probe. Measured on b-min-sep BandMF (bands=4, n_steps=40, target ε=3.0 at δ=1e-3, `tolerance=1e-6`): in the four of ten seed/p₀ combinations where the OPQ-408 fallback did *not* fire, `achieved` came back as 2.9768990653998104, 2.982586082209384, 2.9858047534416134 and 2.999839744431425 — relative gaps of 5e-5 to 8e-3 against a 1e-6 tolerance, all failing `isclose` while `converged=True`. A downstream assertion of the documented contract fails, and a run card claiming "calibrated to within 1e-6 of the target" is wrong by up to ~0.8 %.
- **Evidence:** `calibration.py:324-325` (the runtime re-evaluation), `:326-330` (safety-only re-check), `:340` (the overwrite), `:474-534` (`isclose` applied only inside `_calibrate_impl`, `converged` hard-coded); `calibration.py:136-142` and `docs/reference/accounting.md:722-725` (the promise); reproduced with both a stub budget and the real b-min-sep mechanism; the existing `test_reported_achieved_is_runtime_upper_bound` asserts only `achieved <= target` and equality with the runtime re-evaluation, so nothing in the suite catches the broken `isclose` claim.
- **Fix:** recompute the reported convergence after the runtime re-evaluation — set `converged = math.isclose(achieved, target, rel_tol=tolerance, abs_tol=0.0)` instead of hard-coding `True`, or keep bisecting under the runtime config until closeness holds, or add an explicit `achieved_probe` field and document that `achieved` is a privacy-safe runtime re-measurement that is not tolerance-bounded. Update `calibration.py:136-142` and `docs/reference/accounting.md:722-725` to match whichever is chosen, and add a test that forces the drift and asserts the reported `converged`.
- **Notes:** the direction is always privacy-safe — `achieved <= target` is re-checked at `:326-330`, so the mechanism is over-noised, never under-noised. The defect is the contract and the reported convergence quality, not ε.

**OPQ-410 — `DpProcess.__or__` walks the whole accumulated process tree on every composition, making heterogeneous accountant accumulation quadratic.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_base.py:379, 405-415, 499-513` · performance-regression · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 4bfd0c16 ("fix(accounting): preserve schedule identity during composition", #883) replaced the O(1) structural-equality merge test in `DpProcess.__or__` with `_has_same_repeated_pld`, which materializes `Repeated(left_leaf, merged_count)._pld_cache_key()` (`:405-415`). When `self` is a `Composed` or `CachedProcess`, `_leaf_and_count()` falls through to the default at `:379` and returns `(self, 1)` — neither type overrides it; only `Repeated` does (`_repeated.py:51`) — so `left_leaf` is the entire accumulated tree, and `Composed._pld_cache_key` → `iter_cache_key` (`_composed.py:43-46`, `_iter_cache_key.py:11-40`) walks every node of it, descending into `CachedProcess.inner`, once per composition. The previous `==` path short-circuited on a class mismatch in O(1) (`_iter_eq.py:38-40`).
- **Failure scenario:** a research script or per-step noise schedule folding heterogeneous steps into an accountant — `for i in range(N): acct = acct | dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(sigma_i), q)`. No two steps merge, the `Composed` spine grows, and each `|` re-walks it. Measured on this tree: 125 steps 0.064 s, 250 steps 0.241 s, 500 steps 0.929 s, 1000 steps 4.07 s — a clean 4× per doubling. Monkeypatching `_has_same_repeated_pld` back to `self is other or self == other` restores linearity: 200 steps 0.0033 s through 3200 steps 0.0392 s. Extrapolating the fitted quadratic, 10 000 heterogeneous steps costs several minutes and 50 000 costs hours of pure bookkeeping, with no warning and no indication the cost is in `|`.
- **Evidence:** `_base.py:405-415` (the cache-key materialization), `:379` (the default `_leaf_and_count`), `:499-503` (both merge tests calling it); `_composed.py:43-46` and `_iter_cache_key.py:11-40,30-34` (the full tree walk); `_iter_eq.py:38-40` (the O(1) path it replaced); `git show 4bfd0c16` shows the exact `-if left_leaf == right_leaf` / `+if left_leaf._has_same_repeated_pld(...)` swap; the timing series and the monkeypatched control; cProfile at n=700 attributes 8.065 s of 8.069 s to `__or__` → `_has_same_repeated_pld` → `iter_cache_key` → `_pld_cache_key` (493 490 calls).
- **Fix:** short-circuit before building the cache keys — return `False` immediately from `_has_same_repeated_pld` when `type(self) is not type(other)` (the overwhelmingly common case, and what `iter_eq` already did), and/or skip the direct-merge test entirely when `self` is a `Composed`/`CachedProcess`, since `_leaf_and_count` returning `(self, 1)` means the merge can never apply to a differently-typed right leaf. Lock it in with a test that counts `iter_cache_key` calls over a 2000-step heterogeneous fold and asserts the count is O(n).
- **Notes:** results are numerically unchanged, so severity stays low by the rubric even though the practical cost is large. The homogeneous path is unaffected — 20 000 identical compositions take 0.075 s, because `Repeated._leaf_and_count` returns the small inner leaf — and the `DPTrainer` is on that path (`_dp_trainer.py:287` folds one reused `ctx.step_process`, with periodic `acc.cached(...)` wraps keeping the spine bounded). Distinct from open issue #911, which is about Monte Carlo calibration cost.

**OPQ-411 — Monte Carlo shard seeds alias across nearby user seeds: runs at seed S and S+2 share all but one shard of the transcript corpus.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/amplification/b_min_sep/mc.rs:34-37`, `packages/opaque-accounting/src/amplification/balls_in_bins/monte_carlo.rs:50-55` · monte-carlo-reproducibility · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the shard-seeding scheme introduced by the OPQ-301 remediation (6e3a7d03, #902) derives each per-shard RNG seed as `seed.wrapping_add((shard as u64).wrapping_mul(2)).wrapping_add(direction as u64)` — the flat sequence seed, seed+1, seed+2, … over (shard, direction) pairs. It is unique within one run, which is all the crate test `shard_seeds_separate_adjacency_directions` (`mc.rs:710-721`) checks, but it is not domain-separated across runs: `shard_seed(S, k, d) == shard_seed(S+2, k−1, d)` for every k ≥ 1. The shard index feeds nothing else — the RNG is the only per-shard state, built as `StdRng::seed_from_u64(shard_seed(...))` at `mc.rs:221/237/396/423` and `monte_carlo.rs:205/229`, and every other input inside the `par_chunks_mut(...).enumerate()` closure (strategy coefficients, Gram matrix, σ, p, n_steps, Cholesky factor) is shard-independent — so shard k of run S is bit-identical to shard k−1 of run S+2. `samples_to_pmf` sorts the sample vector (`monte_carlo.rs:314`), so the PMF is a pure function of the multiset and shared shards flow straight into the reported ε. The arithmetic was copied verbatim from the balls-in-bins sibling (the closed #331 fix), so both MC accountants carry it.
- **Failure scenario:** at the default discretization config `resolved_num_mc_samples(2)` is 2 940 252 = 2872 shards of 1024, so runs at seed S and S+2 draw 2871 of 2872 identical shards in each adjacency direction — they are the same estimate with one shard swapped, not independent replicates. Independently measured at 17 shards (b-min-sep over `band_mf_strategy(bands=2)`, n_steps=40, p₀=0.05, nm=1.0, discretization=1e-3, `mc_resolution=1e-3`, `mc_failure_probability=1e-3`): stride-2 seeds 1000…1010 give `epsilon_at(1e-2)` = 1.29801422, 1.29974058, 1.30809399, 1.31104217, 1.30283726, 1.30377245 (stdev 0.00493) while stride-1000 seeds give 1.29801422, 1.31705101, 1.26226398, 1.31656696, 1.33825894, 1.31136316 (stdev 0.02557) — 5.2× too tight. A user who reruns the accountant at seeds 1000, 1002, 1004 to gauge Monte Carlo noise sees an artificially tight cluster and overestimates the stability of the bound, and an independently "reproduced" (ε, δ, seed) triple at a nearby seed is not independent evidence.
- **Evidence:** `b_min_sep/mc.rs:34-37` and `balls_in_bins/monte_carlo.rs:50-55` (identical arithmetic); the six `StdRng::seed_from_u64(shard_seed(...))` sites confirming the seed is the only shard-dependent input; `monte_carlo.rs:314` (`samples.par_sort_unstable_by(f64::total_cmp)`, so the PMF depends only on the multiset); `mc.rs:710-721` (the within-run-uniqueness-only test); the stride-2 vs stride-1000 measurement; `opaque_accounting.pyi:166` documents `seed` only as "RNG seed for reproducibility across worker counts within a fixed build".
- **Fix:** hash the (seed, shard, direction) triple instead of adding it — e.g. seed a SplitMix64/ChaCha stream from `splitmix64(seed ^ (0x9E3779B97F4A7C15 * shard) ^ (direction_tag << 63))`, or use explicit stream separation — so distinct user seeds produce disjoint shard streams. Apply it to `b_min_sep/mc.rs::shard_seed` and `balls_in_bins/monte_carlo.rs::shard_seed` together, since the two must stay consistent and the balls-in-bins copy is the origin of the pattern. Add a test asserting the shard-seed *sets* for seeds S and S+1/S+2 are disjoint, not merely unique within one run.
- **Notes:** no individual reported ε is invalidated — each run still carries its own simultaneous KL confidence band, and the public docs promise reproducibility from the seed, not independence across seeds. Changing the scheme changes the sample corpus for a fixed seed, so cached transcript corpora and any pinned golden epsilons need regenerating.

**OPQ-412 — The public exception taxonomy inverted two pre-existing error relationships without a breaking-change marker.** `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/exceptions/__init__.py:27-48`, `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/calibration.py:537`, `packages/opaque-accounting/.../_budgets.py`, `.../_process_codec.py` · back-compat · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 7b26f655 ("feat(exceptions): add public exception taxonomy (#760)" — a plain `feat`, no `!`, no `BREAKING CHANGE` footer) migrated the accounting package's raise sites to the new taxonomy. Most preserve the old base class (ValueError → `ConfigurationError`/`CalibrationError`/`PrivacyBudgetError`, all ValueError subclasses), but two invert it. (a) `calibrate()`'s non-convergence error was `RuntimeError` at `4b13d82:…/calibration.py:489` and is now `CalibrationError` (`calibration.py:537`), which derives from `ConfigurationError` → `ValueError` and is *not* a `RuntimeError`. (b) Every checkpoint/codec raise in `_process_codec.py` (seven sites) and `_budgets.py` was `ValueError`, and `budget_state_dict` on an unregistered type was `TypeError` (`4b13d82:…/_budgets.py:311`); all are now `CheckpointError`, which derives from `OperationError` → `RuntimeError` and is neither a `ValueError` nor a `TypeError` — even though the taxonomy ships an `InputTypeError(OpaqueError, TypeError)` that would have preserved the TypeError case.
- **Failure scenario:** the change shipped in a released line — `git describe 7b26f655` is v0.15.3-9 and `git tag --contains` lists v0.15.4, v0.15.5, v0.15.5.rc1/rc2 and v0.15.6.rc1 — so v0.15.3 code doing `except RuntimeError:` around `calibrate()`, or `except ValueError:` around a checkpoint restore, silently stops catching on v0.15.4 and the job crashes instead of taking its recovery path. Verified by execution: `budget_state_dict(Foo())` raises `CheckpointError` with `isinstance(TypeError)` False, `isinstance(RuntimeError)` True, `isinstance(ValueError)` False.
- **Evidence:** `exceptions/__init__.py:27,38,48` (`ConfigurationError(OpaqueError, ValueError)`, `OperationError(OpaqueError, RuntimeError)`, `CalibrationError(ConfigurationError)`, `CheckpointError(OperationError)`, and the unused `InputTypeError(OpaqueError, TypeError)`); the baseline raise sites at `4b13d82:…/calibration.py:489` and `4b13d82:…/_budgets.py:311` against the current tree; the executed isinstance checks; `git describe`/`git tag --contains 7b26f655` establishing the released range; the commit subject with no `!` and no footer.
- **Fix:** either make the two inverted cases multiply inherit so old handlers keep working — a `CalibrationTimeoutError(CalibrationError, RuntimeError)` for non-convergence, and `CheckpointError(OperationError, ValueError)` plus an `InputTypeError`-based raise for `budget_state_dict`'s unregistered-type case — or accept the break and record it explicitly in the release notes, in `calibrate()`'s Raises section, and in `docs/reference/accounting.md`.
- **Notes:** the original report's framing overstated one point: the taxonomy docstring (`opaque/exceptions/__init__.py:4-7`) reads in full "They preserve familiar Python error relationships: configuration, calibration, and privacy-budget errors are ValueError subclasses, while checkpoint failures are RuntimeError subclasses", which accurately documents the shipped taxonomy rather than claiming the previous relationships were kept. `docs/reference/exceptions.md` agrees. With the project at 0.x alpha, the concrete gap is the missing breaking-change marker and release note, not a wrong description of current behavior. The repo's own tests were updated in lockstep (`test_calibrate_direction.py`, `test_deep_composition.py`), so nothing in-tree catches the change.

**OPQ-413 — The OPQ-307 cross-package dependency is back in `opaque-accounting`'s own shipped source, as an executed doctest.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/accounting/__init__.py:17-22` · package-boundary · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-307 was closed by moving the one test in `packages/opaque-accounting/tests` that imported `opaque.dpftrl` into the owning wheel (4867feb1), and a grep confirms that test tree is now clean. Commit 12146ec4 then converted the public façade's previously inert `Example::` block into a live doctest whose first line is `>>> import opaque.dpsgd.accounting as dpsgd_acc` (`:19`), followed by three statements that build and evaluate a Poisson-Gaussian process. `packages/opaque-accounting/pyproject.toml` declares exactly one Opaque dependency (`opaque-base`) and no `[project.optional-dependencies]`, and `opaque-dpsgd` depends on `opaque-accounting`, so the edge cannot be declared without a cycle. maturin's `python-packages` includes `opaque/accounting`, so the docstring ships inside the wheel.
- **Failure scenario:** latent rather than active. Anyone who enables `--doctest-modules` for this package, or runs the shipped doctests as a smoke test on a `pip install opaque-accounting` environment, gets `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'opaque.dpsgd'`. The doctest also asserts no output, so it verifies importability rather than any numerical property.
- **Evidence:** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/accounting/__init__.py:17-22`; `packages/opaque-accounting/pyproject.toml` (single Opaque dependency, no extras); `.github/workflows/pr.yml:290-293` runs `uv run python -m doctest` on that exact file inside the docs job, which at `:285` syncs `--all-packages`, so it passes in CI; the root `pyproject.toml` `[tool.pytest.ini_options] addopts` is only `--import-mode=importlib`, with no `--doctest-modules`, and no workflow lane installs `opaque-accounting` in isolation.
- **Fix:** rewrite the façade docstring example against core-only surface (`acc.eps_delta(...)`, `acc.identity()`, `acc.calibrate(...)`) so it runs on `opaque-base` + `opaque-accounting` alone, and move the DP-SGD-flavoured example to `docs/` or to `opaque-dpsgd`'s own façade docstring where the dependency exists. If the cross-package example must stay in place, mark it `# doctest: +SKIP` and keep the executed copy in `docs/` or `examples/quickstart.py`, which the same CI step already runs.
- **Notes:** because doctest collection is not enabled anywhere, ARC-006's wheel-local-*test* clause is not violated today — this is a verified boundary/hygiene regression, not an active failure, and the docstring itself flags the requirement in prose ("Example (requires ``opaque-dpsgd`` in the environment)").

**OPQ-414 — A checkpoint budget always overrides a stricter template budget on `Accountant` restore, and `budget_exceeded` then reports the looser cap.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_accountant.py:238-250, 207-224` · fail-open · **low** · ε:no · **PLAUSIBLE**.
- **Claim:** the OPQ-302 remediation (51a18563) made the template's budget survive when the checkpoint has none, but left the opposite direction asymmetric: when the checkpoint *does* carry a budget it always wins, with only a `logger.warning(... "the template budget is discarded.")` at `:240-249`. `budget_exceeded` (`:216-222`) still cannot distinguish "no budget configured" (returns `False`) from "the budget I was constructed with was replaced" — the tri-state or raise that was the second half of OPQ-302's fix sketch did not land.
- **Failure scenario:** a caller resumes with `from_state_dict(Accountant(budget=epsilon_budget(1.0, delta=1e-5)), saved)` where the checkpoint was written by an accountant carrying `epsilon_budget(10.0, delta=1e-5)`. Reproduced: `restored._budget == EpsilonBudget(10.0, 1e-05)`, `epsilon_at(1e-5) == 2.499990499950025`, `budget_exceeded == False`, so a `while not acct.budget_exceeded` loop runs well past the intended ε ≤ 1.0.
- **Evidence:** `_accountant.py:240-249` (`budget = template._budget`, then unconditionally replaced when `state.get("budget") is not None`, with only a warning when they differ); `:216-222` (`if self._budget is None: return False`, no tri-state); the executed reproduction; `packages/opaque-accounting/tests/test_budget_serialization.py:85-99` (`test_checkpoint_budget_override_logs_warning`) pins checkpoint-wins plus the warning as intended behavior, asserting only that the warning text appears.
- **Fix:** when both the template and the checkpoint carry a budget and they differ, prefer the template — the caller's explicit current intent — and warn, or raise a `CheckpointError` naming both budgets and require the caller to pick; a resume that changes the privacy cap should be an explicit decision, not a log line. Independently, give `Accountant` a public `has_budget`/`budget` accessor so `budget_exceeded is False` can be distinguished from "no cap configured" without reading `_budget`, and add a test that pins whichever precedence is chosen.
- **Notes:** **PLAUSIBLE, not CONFIRMED** — the mechanics reproduce exactly, but the verifier could not establish this as a defect rather than a deliberate design choice: an explicit test pins checkpoint-wins as intended, the warning does reach stderr through logging's `lastResort` handler in a bare process (so "silently" overstates the case), and no documented contract covers template-vs-checkpoint precedence (`docs/reference/accounting.md:74-93` covers the codecs only). The `DPTrainer` is unaffected — it constructs a bare `Accountant()` (`_dp_trainer.py:280-281`) and restores with `opaque_from_state_dict(Accountant(), ...)` (`:5537`), so no `budget` key is ever written or restored there. Exposure is limited to harnesses that suppress or never read WARNING logs, and only when the caller deliberately passes a different budget on restore. What remains for certain is the asymmetry plus the unlanded half of the OPQ-302 fix sketch.

### 2.4 Removal coherence (loss scaler, bounded Gaussian)

**OPQ-415 — The loss-scaler removal deleted `ClippingStats.all_finite`, the only signal that clipping silently zeroes non-finite per-example gradients, while leaving the sanitization it diagnosed in place.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_clipped_fun.py:72-80`, `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_pytree.py:464-471, 600-607`, `docs/user-guide/precision.md:47-51` · removal-coherence/diagnostic-loss · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 7f55aae5 (#889) removed the data-dependent loss scaler and, in the same commit, deleted `_all_finite()`, the `aux_dict["all_finite"]` production in `clipped_fun`, the `all_finite` accumulation in `_microbatch_accumulate_stats_only`, and the public `ClippingStats.all_finite` field (whose docstring read "Whether every pre-clipping output in the batch was finite"). The behavior that field reported is unchanged: `clip_pytree` (`_pytree.py:600-607`) and `auto_scale` (`:464-471`) still begin with `tree_map(lambda t: torch.nan_to_num(t, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0), pytree)`, so a per-example gradient that overflows or NaNs is replaced by zeros and that record contributes nothing to the released sum. After #889 there is no signal at any layer: `dataclasses.fields(ClippingStats)` is exactly `['num_clipped', 'clipping_rate', 'batch_size']`; `ClippedGradAux.grad_norms` is computed after the sanitization, so a dropped record reports norm 0.0, indistinguishable from a genuinely zero gradient; and `clipping_rate` goes *down* when records are dropped, so the failure reads as an improvement. A grep finds no `isfinite`/`isnan` check anywhere in opaque-engine, dpsgd or transformers source outside the `nan_to_num` calls themselves. OPQ-330 (#804) was specifically about the feedback loop `stats.all_finite → LossScaler.update`; the fix also removed the read-only diagnostic, which was never part of the privacy defect — a statistic that is never fed back into training is not an adaptive query.
- **Failure scenario:** with low-level primitives under float16, reproduced on torch 2.14: fp16 params, `loss = ((w*x)*s).sum()`, `x=[10,20,30,1000]`, `s=[1,1,1,100]`, `clipped_grad(..., clipping_norm=1.0, normalize_by=4, return_aux=True)` returns `loss_values` all finite (`[4.0e-3, 8.0e-3, 1.2e-2, 40.0]`) but `grad_norms = [10., 20., 30., 0.]` and `clipped_grad_norms = [0.9995, 0.9995, 0.9995, 0.0]`; the same call with `return_stats=True` returns `ClippingStats(num_clipped=3.0, clipping_rate=0.75, batch_size=4)`. Row 4's per-example gradient overflowed fp16 to inf, was zeroed, and nothing in either aux or stats reports it. Under `adaptive_clipped_grad` the quantile tracker sees the sanitized 0.0, so the threshold drifts down.
- **Evidence:** `git show 7f55aae5 -- .../_clipped_fun.py` (the four deletions in the same commit as `_loss_scaler.py`) and `-- .../_auto.py` (the AUTO-S docstring line "Its ``all_finite`` field is measured before AUTO-S sanitizes non-finite gradients" deleted with the field); `_clipped_fun.py:72-80` (the three surviving fields, confirmed at runtime); `_pytree.py:464-471` and `:600-607` (the unchanged `nan_to_num`); the executed fp16 reproduction; `docs/user-guide/precision.md:47-48` ("Opaque therefore does not expose a loss scaler or an aggregate pre-clipping finiteness signal") and `:50-51`; a grep for `non-finite|nonfinite|NaN/Inf|sanitiz` over `docs/` returns no matches, as does `ClippingStats|return_stats`.
- **Fix:** restore a read-only, non-feedback drop counter on `ClippingStats` (e.g. `num_nonfinite: float | dict[str, float]`, computed from the same pre-clipping value `_all_finite` used) and document, in the adaptive-clipping gating style, that it must not drive any training decision. If the signal must stay removed, state explicitly in `docs/user-guide/clipping.md` and `docs/user-guide/precision.md` that per-example gradients containing NaN/Inf are replaced with zeros, that the affected records contribute nothing to the release, and that `grad_norms == 0.0` is the only — ambiguous — trace.
- **Notes:** severity reduced from medium to low. No ε impact: dropping records only lowers the realized sensitivity, and the adaptive-threshold drift is utility loss, not privacy loss. `DPTrainer` rejects float16 and the docs declare the mode unsupported, and the zeroing *is* documented on `clip_pytree`/`auto_scale_pytree` (rendered into `docs/reference/clipping.md` via mkdocstrings). The genuine gap is that neither `clipped_grad`'s docstring nor `docs/user-guide/clipping.md` tells a user records with non-finite per-example gradients are silently dropped.

**OPQ-416 — The bounded-Gaussian removal's replacement docs advertise float16/bfloat16 as accepted `compute_dtype` values on the DPTrainer surface, contradicting `precision.md`'s explicit "do not lower" rule for the same knob.** `docs/reference/transformers.md:196`, `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:138`, `docs/user-guide/precision.md:68-70`, `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/noise/_gaussian.py:59-61, 111-124, 195-205` · removal-coherence/docs-contradiction · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 873de427 (#1021) replaced the `bound` documentation on `privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs` with text that presents `compute_dtype` and enumerates `float16, bfloat16, float32, float64` as accepted string names, with no caveat and no pointer to `precision.md`, in both DPTrainer-facing tables. The same commit added `_SUPPORTED_COMPUTE_DTYPES` and `_validate_compute_dtype` in `_gaussian.py` (`:59-61`, `:111-124`) and `_normalize_gaussian_compute_dtype` in `_training_arguments.py` (`:1637-1662`), all of which accept float16/bfloat16 silently — no warning, no log line. `docs/user-guide/precision.md:68-70`, the page that owns this knob and was rewritten by the sibling removal 7f55aae5, says the opposite: "Do not lower precision without a separate numerical argument. Setting compute_dtype=torch.bfloat16 or torch.float16 can make the realized sensitivity and noise scale diverge from the accountant's model." `_dp_trainer.py:1676-1690` forwards the key verbatim into `gaussian_noise`, and `_gaussian.py:195-205` then does `center_c = center.to(compute_dtype)` before `sample = center_c + std * noise`, i.e. the clipped sum is downcast before noise is added.
- **Failure scenario:** a DPTrainer user reads only the reference table, sets `{"compute_dtype": "bfloat16"}`, and it is accepted silently (normalized to `torch.bfloat16`, no warning). Measured over 200 draws of a fresh random 4096-dimensional gradient on the clip sphere with C=1.0 and `noise_multiplier=1e-8`: worst realized `‖released‖₂` was 1.0001159 under bfloat16 versus 1.0000002 under float32 (and 1.0000169 under float16) — roughly a 1.2e-4 relative excess over the C the accountant is calibrated to, with an adversarial bound around 0.4 % of C for bf16. The noise is also coarsely discretized: 2945 distinct values under bfloat16 versus 199 721 under float32 over 200 000 draws at nm=1.0, so the released mechanism is a discrete approximation of the continuous Gaussian the PLD assumes.
- **Evidence:** `git show 873de427 -- docs/reference/transformers.md` and `-- docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md` (the substituted rows, at `:196` and `:138`); `docs/user-guide/precision.md:68-70` (the contradicting rule); `_gaussian.py:59-61` and `:111-124` (all four dtypes accepted, no warning path), `:195-205` (the downcast before noising); `_training_arguments.py:1637-1662` (string mapping, no warning); `_dp_trainer.py:1676-1690` (verbatim forwarding); executed config checks (`'bfloat16'` → OK `torch.bfloat16`, `'float64'` → OK, `'int8'` → `ConfigurationError`); the 200-trial realized-norm and noise-cardinality measurements.
- **Fix:** either restrict the DPTrainer surface to the dtypes `precision.md` endorses (float32/float64) and reject float16/bfloat16 in `_normalize_gaussian_compute_dtype` with a message pointing at `precision.md`, or keep them and emit a one-time `RuntimeWarning` from `_validate_compute_dtype` whenever `compute_dtype` is narrower than float32, adding the caveat inline to both doc rows. Independently, stop downcasting the query: compute the noisy sum in the wider of `compute_dtype` and `center.dtype`, so lowering `compute_dtype` cannot inflate the realized sensitivity above the accounted `max_norm`. Pin it with a test asserting the realized released norm stays within tolerance of C across the supported dtypes.
- **Notes:** severity reduced from medium to low, and the ε flag does not survive: both effects require an explicit non-default opt-in that `precision.md` already warns against, and the resulting ε understatement is sub-percent. This is a docs cross-reference and missing-warning gap rather than a silent privacy failure.

**OPQ-417 — The bounded-Gaussian removal left `bound` in the gaussian mechanism-kwargs allow-list, so the unknown-key error recommends the removed, privacy-unsound key.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_training_arguments.py:229, 273-289, 1222-1228` · removal-coherence/stale-allowlist · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 873de427 (#1021) added an explicit guard rejecting `privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs["bound"]` at `_training_arguments.py:1222-1228` ("is unsupported: Opaque does not provide a matching privacy accountant") and stripped `bound` from the trainer's `_gn_extra` filter, but left `_MECHANISM_KWARGS["gaussian"] = frozenset({"bound", "compute_dtype"})` at `:229` untouched. That set is the allow-list `_validate_privacy_kwargs` prints in its unknown-key error (`:273-289`, `f"Allowed: {sorted(allowed) or '<none>'}."`), so any typo in the gaussian mechanism kwargs produces an error recommending the key the very next validator refuses — and naming a mechanism the library deliberately deleted because it has no matching accountant.
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced verbatim. `TrainingArguments(..., privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs={"bnd": 3.0})` raises "privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs contains unsupported keys for privacy_noise_mechanism='gaussian': ['bnd']. Allowed: ['bound', 'compute_dtype']."; following that advice with `{"bound": 3.0}` raises "privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs['bound'] is unsupported: Opaque does not provide a matching privacy accountant.", because the explicit guard in `__post_init__` runs before the allow-list check at `:1353-1365`. `bound` is correctly rejected as a dict key, as a JSON string, as an HF-style `bound=3.0` string, and under `privacy_noise_mechanism='mf_identity'`.
- **Evidence:** `_training_arguments.py:229` (untouched by 873de427, whose hunks in this file start at 150, 507, 1161 and 1553), `:273-289` (the allow-list printed in the error), `:1222-1228` (the removal guard); `_dp_trainer.py:1678-1686` (`_gn_extra` keeps only `_k == "compute_dtype"`); both error messages reproduced verbatim in the repo venv.
- **Fix:** set `_MECHANISM_KWARGS["gaussian"] = frozenset({"compute_dtype"})` and keep the explicit `bound` guard purely as the migration message, or fold removed keys into a `_REMOVED_KWARGS` map consulted by `_validate_privacy_kwargs` so a removed key gets a targeted message instead of appearing in the "Allowed" list. Add a test asserting that no key named in an `Allowed:` list is rejected by a later validator.
- **Notes:** the configuration still fails closed in every case, so there is no ε or silent-corruption consequence. The stale entry would become load-bearing if the explicit guard at `:1222-1228` were ever refactored away, at which point `_dp_trainer.py`'s `compute_dtype`-only filter would drop `bound` silently rather than reject it.
## 3. DP-SGD, DP-FTRL, engine and optimizers

Twenty findings, IDs **OPQ-420 … OPQ-439**, grouped by subsystem and severity-ordered within each
group. Two are high (both in `opaque-optimizers`), one is high in the distributed resume path, five
are medium, and the remainder are low. Three are ε-affecting.

### 3.1 DP-SGD (`opaque-dpsgd`)

**OPQ-420 — `dpsgd_acc.poisson()` lost its inner-mechanism guard and now silently amplifies processes that are already amplified or already whole-horizon.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpsgd/amplification/_poisson.py:21, 39-46, 91-98, 162-173` · fail-open-validation · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** commit 3b22a6af (`feat(accounting): generalize Poisson PLD amplification`, #850) widened `_Inner` from `Gaussian | AdaClip | NonPrivate` to `DpProcess` (`:21`) and deleted the factory's `match inner: … case _: raise TypeError("poisson() requires a Gaussian, AdaClip, or NonPrivate inner mechanism, …")`. `Poisson.__post_init__` now checks only `isinstance(self.inner, DpProcess)` (`:39-46`), the surviving type guard lives inside the truncated branch alone (`:91-98`), and the non-truncated path falls straight through to `_native.poisson_pld(self.inner.pld(...), self.sample_rate)` (`:162-173`). The generic PLD-subsampling transform is sound for the model it implements — subsample the dataset once, then run the inner mechanism — but that is not what any Opaque runtime does, and nothing signals that the composed expression has left the DP-SGD model. The DP-FTRL sibling still refuses: `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpftrl/amplification/_poisson.py:279-288` raises unless the inner is an `MfGaussian` with a BandMf/Identity strategy, so the two stacks now disagree on strictness.
- **Failure scenario:** the guard removal was deliberate and documented (3b22a6af also updated `docs/reference/accounting.md` and `docs/user-guide/accounting.md` to say plain Poisson accepts any `DpProcess`, and replaced `test_rejects_non_gaussian` with `test_rejects_non_process`), but nothing replaced it for inners that are themselves participation models or compositions. Reproduced on the current tree: `dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(1.1), 0.01), 0.01) * 1000` reports `epsilon_at(1e-5) = 0.009837` where the correct single-amplification DP-SGD figure is `1.515442` — a 154× understatement with no error and no warning, where the pre-3b22a6af tree raised `TypeError`. Likewise `dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.k_out_of_t(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(1.1), k=2, t=100, allocation="block"), 0.1)` reports `0.108651` against the `0.970344` the horizon process reports on its own, for a mechanism ("Poisson-subsample the dataset, then run k-out-of-t on the subsample") that no Opaque sampler implements. A user who reaches either expression through a helper or config path and calibrates noise against it deploys at far less noise than the claimed epsilon requires.
- **Evidence:** `_poisson.py:21` (`_Inner = DpProcess`), `:39-46` (the only remaining check), `:91-98` (guard surviving in the truncated branch only), `:162-173` (fall-through to the native transform); `git show 3b22a6af -- …/dpsgd/amplification/_poisson.py` shows the `-_Inner = Gaussian | AdaClip | NonPrivate` / `+_Inner = DpProcess` swap and the factory-guard deletion; `git show 4b13d82:…/_poisson.py:170-175` holds the removed `TypeError`; executed numbers 1.5154423566628403 / 0.009836885508304796 and 0.970343695110472 / 0.10865088995647369; `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpftrl/amplification/_poisson.py:279-288` for the asymmetry.
- **Fix:** restore a guard in `Poisson.__post_init__` that rejects inners which are themselves participation models or compositions — `DpHorizonProcess` (`KOutOfT`, `BMinSep`, `BallsInBins`, `CyclicPoisson`), `Poisson` / `ParallelPoisson`, and the `Composed` / `Repeated` wrappers — while keeping the generalisation for genuine single-release mechanisms, and name the construction the user probably meant (`poisson(gaussian(nm), q) * n_steps`) in the message. Lock it in with a test that asserts each rejected inner class raises, alongside the existing `test_rejects_non_process`.
- **Notes:** the documented single-amplification path (`poisson(gaussian(nm), q) * T`) is unchanged and correct, and the Gaussian path was cross-validated against `dp_accounting` at four `(nm, q, T)` points (Opaque conservative by 7e-5 to 6.7e-4).
