Triggering this needs a user-constructed expression, and the composition layer is permissive elsewhere too — `k_out_of_t(...) * 10` is likewise accepted with no guard, returning 3.096846 — which is why the verifier held this at medium rather than raising it.

**OPQ-421 — `adaclip` is priced with `train_batch_size` under `sampling_mode="k_out_of_t"`, whose expected batch size is `dataset_size * k / n_steps`.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:1472, 4470-4479, 4495` · runtime-accounting-mismatch · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_build_mechanism` wraps the base Gaussian in `dpsgd_acc.adaclip(..., expected_batch_size=expected_batch_size, num_groups=num_groups)` (`:4470-4479`) *before* it branches on `a.sampling_mode` (`:4495`), and `expected_batch_size = a.train_batch_size` (`:1472`). That is the right expectation only for Poisson sampling, where `sample_rate = train_batch_size / dataset_size`. The k-out-of-t sampler's own expectation is `num_samples * k / t` (`packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/sampling/_k_out_of_t.py:110-116`) with `t = n_steps` from `_steps_breakdown` (`_dp_trainer.py:4740-4767`), so the two agree only when the user happens to set `k == num_train_epochs`, and never reliably when `max_steps` is set. Nothing derives, constrains or warns about `k`: `_training_arguments.py:1311-1345` checks only that `k` and `allocation` are present and that `allocation` is `"block"` or `"total"`, and `:1281-1292` resolves adaptive to fixed only for DP-FTRL mechanisms. The coupling used to be structural — at the audit baseline the equivalent parameter was `num_bins = expected_steps_per_epoch`, computed from `train_batch_size` (`git show 4b13d82:…/_dp_trainer.py:4264-4277`) — and fc44ec8d (#761) replaced the derived `num_bins` with a free user-supplied `k` without re-establishing the link.
- **Failure scenario:** `dataset_size=10000`, `per_device_train_batch_size=32`, `num_train_epochs=3` (so `steps_per_epoch=313`, `n_steps=939`), `clipping_mode="adaptive"`, `sampling_mode="k_out_of_t"`, `sampling_kwargs={"k": 1, "allocation": "block"}`. `TrainingArguments` accepts this with no warning (verified by execution, `clipping_mode` still `"adaptive"`). The sampler draws batches averaging `10000 * 1 / 939 = 10.65` examples and `_adaptive.py:580-600` noises the realised rate `num_clipped / batch_size`, i.e. an absolute count noise of `realised_batch * 0.05`; the accountant is handed `expected_batch_size = 32`, i.e. `sigma_b = 32 * 0.05`. Reproduced with the real factories: `k_out_of_t(adaclip(gaussian(1.0), expected_batch_size=32), k=1, t=939, allocation="block").epsilon_at(1e-5) = 0.16464` (effective noise multiplier 0.954480), against `0.49026` (0.728991) when the process is built with the sampler's true expectation of 10.65 — a 3.0× understatement in the unsafe direction. The mismatch is unbounded when `max_steps` is set, because `_steps_breakdown` then uses `t = max_steps` with no link to `train_batch_size` at all. The direction reverses and becomes conservative when `k > num_train_epochs`.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:1472` (`expected_batch_size = a.train_batch_size`), `:4478` (passed into the adaclip wrapper), `:4495` (the sampler branch that comes after); `_k_out_of_t.py:110-116` (`num_samples * k / t`); `_dp_trainer.py:4740-4767` (`n_steps` derivation); `_training_arguments.py:1281-1292, 1311-1345` (no `k` constraint, adaptive not resolved away for Gaussian); `_adaptive.py:106-121, 580-600` (the runtime noise scale); the executed epsilon pair 0.16464 / 0.49026; `git show 4b13d82:…/_dp_trainer.py:4264-4277` for the removed structural coupling; `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/validation/test_dp_guarantees.py:237-259` is the only trainer k-out-of-t guarantee test and it uses the default `clipping_mode="fixed"`.
- **Fix:** compute the accounting expected batch size from the sampling model rather than from `train_batch_size` — for `sampling_mode="k_out_of_t"` pass `dataset_size * k / n_steps` into `dpsgd_acc.adaclip`, and use the same value for `normalize_by` so the gradient scale matches the batches actually drawn. Alternatively derive `k` from `num_train_epochs` and reject an explicit contradicting `k`, restoring the pre-#761 coupling. Either way add a `TrainingArguments` check (or a loud warning naming both values) that `dataset_size * k / n_steps` is within tolerance of `train_batch_size`, and a guarantee test that runs the adaptive + k-out-of-t combination.
- **Notes:** held at medium because `clipping_mode` defaults to `"fixed"` and `sampling_mode` to Poisson, so it takes two explicit opt-ins plus a `k` that disagrees with the horizon; within that configuration the understatement is silent and ~3×.

**OPQ-422 — `AdaClip.__post_init__` guards only the non-positive half of its range, so `expected_batch_size=inf` still prices the quantile release as free.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpsgd/mechanisms/_adaclip.py:40-62, 74-80, 164-173` · accounting-validation / fail-open · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the #859 validation added to close OPQ-310 checks `fraction_noise_std <= 0`, `expected_batch_size <= 0` and `num_groups < 1 or != int(...)`. None of those comparisons is true for `+inf`, so an `AdaClip` deserialized with `expected_batch_size=inf` or `fraction_noise_std=inf` is accepted, `sigma_b` becomes infinite, the `K / (4 * sigma_b^2)` term in `adaclip_sensitivity` vanishes, and `effective_noise_multiplier` collapses to the bare inner multiplier. That is the same "prices adaptive clipping as free" outcome, in the same threat model, that the comment at `:40-44` says the validation exists to prevent. Separately, `inner` is validated by the `adaclip()` factory (`:164-173`) but not by `__post_init__`, so a foreign inner makes the `match` in `effective_noise_multiplier` (`:74-80`) fall through and return `None` from a `-> float` property.
- **Failure scenario:** a hand-edited or corrupted accountant checkpoint whose `AdaClip` JSON carries `Infinity` for `expected_batch_size` (or `fraction_noise_std`) is rebuilt by `from_state_dict` without error, and the run's adaptive-clipping quantile releases are then unaccounted. Verified through the real codec (`state_dict(adaclip(gaussian(1.1), expected_batch_size=250.0, num_groups=4))`, field replaced, `from_state_dict`): `effective_noise_multiplier` collapses from 1.0957654 to 1.1 and `poisson(..., 0.01).epsilon_at(1e-5)` drops from 0.14418328 to 0.14229776 — bit-identical to the unamplified-quantile `poisson(gaussian(1.1), 0.01)` figure, and matching to five digits what the original OPQ-310 report measured for `num_groups=0`. `NaN` in either field is also accepted by `__post_init__` but fails closed downstream (`ValueError: Infinite epsilon bounds: [NaN, NaN]`), so `+inf` is the surviving fail-open value. Separately, `AdaClip(inner=poisson(gaussian(1.1), 0.01), ...)` constructs without error and its `effective_noise_multiplier` returns `None`; its `pld()` takes the generic `case _` branch and is conservative (6.038 vs 1.515 over 1000 steps), and no shipped path consumes the `None`.
- **Evidence:** `_adaclip.py:40-44` (the comment stating the validation exists so deserialization "cannot produce an instance that prices the quantile release as free"), `:45-56` (the only bounds are `<= 0` comparisons), `:74-80` (the un-guarded `match`), `:164-173` (factory-only `inner` validation); the codec run producing 1.1 / 0.1422977567766371 against the untampered 1.0957654 / 0.14418327866598055; the `NaN` path failing closed in the native PLD.
- **Fix:** require finiteness — `if not math.isfinite(self.fraction_noise_std) or self.fraction_noise_std <= 0`, and the same for `expected_batch_size`; apply the same treatment to `Gaussian.noise_multiplier` in `mechanisms/_gaussian.py:28`. Add an `isinstance(self.inner, (Gaussian, NonPrivate))` check to `AdaClip.__post_init__` so the dataclass and the factory agree, and give `effective_noise_multiplier` an explicit `case _: raise` instead of an implicit `None`. Lock it in with a round-trip test that tampers each numeric field with `inf`, `-inf` and `NaN` and asserts `from_state_dict` raises.
- **Notes:** ε marked **no** despite the mispricing being real, because the verifier characterised this as a fail-open hardening gap rather than a live epsilon defect: neither `inf` value is reachable through the `adaclip()` factory or an untampered serialization round-trip, so it needs a corrupted or hand-edited checkpoint. It is exactly the threat model the code comment claims to cover.

**OPQ-423 — the #881 random-allocation memoisation is dead along its most valuable dimension, because `epsilon_at` folds a Monte-Carlo-only field into the cache key.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_random_allocation_cache.py:29-46` · efficiency · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `epoch_pld` is an `lru_cache` keyed on `(noise_multiplier, t, k, config)` where `config` is the whole frozen `DiscretizationConfig` — `mc_resolution` and `mc_failure_probability` included, and the dataclass is frozen so both participate in `__eq__`/`__hash__` (`discretization.py:17-40`). `DpProcess.epsilon_at` overwrites `mc_resolution` with `min(configured, delta / 2.0)` before calling `pld()` (`core/_base.py:212-227`) while `delta_at` does not, so every distinct delta and every switch between the two entry points produces a distinct key. The underlying transform is delta- and Monte-Carlo-independent: `packages/opaque-accounting/src/amplification/random_allocation.rs:164-227` plus `Accuracy::derive` / `alloc_remove` / `alloc_add` read only `discretization`, `log_mass_truncation_bound`, `max_conv_grid`, `max_grid_size` and `tail_mass_truncation`. The per-process `pld_cache` keys on the same config object, so it misses in lockstep.
- **Failure scenario:** any epsilon-vs-delta sweep, or simply `epsilon_at(1e-5)` followed by `epsilon_at(1e-6)` or `delta_at(eps)`, on a k-out-of-t or balls-in-bins/identity process recomputes the delta-independent O(G²) geometric-grid transform from scratch. Measured at `k=8, t=512` with default discretization: 16.5 s for `eps(1e-5)`, 0.01 s for the identical repeat, then 16.6 s for `eps(1e-6)` and 16.5 s for `delta_at(3.0)`, with `epoch_pld.cache_info()` reporting `hits=0, misses=3`. Efficiency only — the returned epsilon values are correct.
- **Evidence:** `_random_allocation_cache.py:29-35` (`@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=8) def epoch_pld(noise_multiplier, t, k, config)`); `core/_base.py:212-227` (`effective_resolution = min(configured_resolution, delta / 2.0)` passed down as `mc_resolution`); `random_allocation.rs:164-227` (no `mc_*` field reaches the transform); the timing/cache-info run above; `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/accounting/test_k_out_of_t_cache.py:35-39` asserts `misses == 1` across two direct `process.pld(**_CFG)` calls, which the per-process `pld_cache` satisfies on its own, so the test cannot observe this.
- **Fix:** key `epoch_pld` on only the fields the transform reads — `(noise_multiplier, t, k, config.discretization, config.log_x_mass_truncation_bound, config.max_grid_size, config.max_conv_grid)` — or give `DiscretizationConfig` a `deterministic_key()` projection that drops `mc_resolution` / `mc_failure_probability` and hash that. Then retarget the regression test at `epsilon_at` with two different deltas and assert `epoch_pld.cache_info().hits >= 1`.
- **Notes:** the original title's "never serves a hit" is too strong and has been narrowed: the verifier observed `hits=1` when a structurally different process (`allocation="total"` at the same `nm`/`k`/`t`) queried the same delta, so `epoch_pld` does deduplicate across distinct processes at a fixed delta. Delta is its one useless dimension. The original report's `delta_at(3.0) = 6.67e-09` did not reproduce (measured 0.0), which is immaterial to the defect.

**OPQ-424 — the accounting guide still names the removed `random_allocation` factory.** `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:586` · docs-drift-api · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** fc44ec8d (#761) deleted `opaque.dpsgd.accounting.random_allocation` and its module, folding the scheme into `k_out_of_t(..., allocation="block")`. The Monte-Carlo-versus-deterministic note still reads "… Balls-in-Bins with `identity_strategy()`, and `random_allocation` and `k_out_of_t`, use deterministic transforms" — and the two neighbouring backticked names in that same sentence are real public API, so `random_allocation` reads as one too. It is not: `opaque.dpsgd.accounting.__all__` is `['adaclip', 'gaussian', 'k_out_of_t', 'parallel_poisson', 'poisson']`.
- **Failure scenario:** a reader following the deterministic-versus-Monte-Carlo guidance writes `dpsgd_acc.random_allocation(...)` and gets an `AttributeError`. Documentation-only, with no runtime or accounting consequence, and the failure is loud.
- **Evidence:** `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:586` (the sentence); the executed `__all__`; `git show --stat fc44ec8d` lists the deletion of `…/dpsgd/amplification/_random_allocation.py` (216 lines) and `…/dpsgd/sampling/_random_allocation.py` (225 lines); a repo-wide grep outside `.git`/`.venv` finds only this docs line plus legitimately-surviving internal names (`_random_allocation_cache`, the native `random_allocation_gaussian_pld` and its PyO3 binding, and the third-party `random_allocation` cross-validation package imported in `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/accounting/test_cross_validation_allocation.py`), making this the only user-facing reference.
- **Fix:** replace `random_allocation` with `k_out_of_t` in that sentence — it is already listed there — so it reads "Balls-in-Bins with `identity_strategy()` and `k_out_of_t` use deterministic transforms." No dedicated regression test is available or appropriate for a prose fix; the durable guard would be a docs link-check or API-name check over backticked identifiers.
- **Notes:** same defect class as OPQ-357 (docs naming factories that do not exist), which the #843/#844 doc sweeps were closed for; those sweeps predate or missed this line.

### 3.2 DP-FTRL (`opaque-dpftrl`)

**OPQ-425 — the reference and user-guide accounting pages still claim bare BandMF sensitivity accounts for one participation, contradicting both the code and `band-mf.md` since #998.** `docs/reference/accounting.md:420` and `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:280` · misleading-docs · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** both files carry the verbatim sentence "Bare BandMF and Identity sensitivity currently accounts for one participation. Do not use bare calls with these strategies to budget repeated participation." Commit e44d6ab6 made `BandMfStrategy.sensitivity` schema-aware (`packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_band_mf.py:138-187`: `k` is computed via `minsep_true_max_participations` and the result is `sqrt(minsep_sensitivity_upper_bound(...))` whenever `k > 1`, with the column-norm shortcut only at `k == 1`), so the BandMF half is false. `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md` — updated by the same commit — says the opposite twice (`:74-76` "pass `min_sep=n_steps, max_participations=1` to ask for κ instead"; `:177-186` "Pass the participation schema you actually run under … because the bare PLD is calibrated to the schema sensitivity S"). The Identity half of the sentence is still true, and it is the only place the Identity limitation is documented at all.
- **Failure scenario:** a reader of `docs/reference/accounting.md` or `docs/user-guide/accounting.md` is told not to use bare BandMF multi-participation accounting, which now works correctly — a utility loss in the conservative direction. The sentence is measurably false on this tree: `band_mf_strategy(bands=100).sensitivity(n_steps=1000, min_sep=100, max_participations=5) = 2.23607` against `1.00000` at `k=1`. Documentation-only; no code path or epsilon value changes.
- **Evidence:** the identical sentence at `docs/reference/accounting.md:420` and `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:280` (grep found exactly these two occurrences); `_band_mf.py:138-187` (schema-aware sensitivity with the `k == 1` shortcut); `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md:74-76, 177-186` (the contradicting, correct description); `git show --stat e44d6ab6` confirms the commit touched `band-mf.md` but neither of the two stale pages; the executed 1.00000 / 2.23607 pair.
- **Fix:** split the sentence into two separate statements — bare BandMF/BLT/BSR/BISR/λ-CGD sensitivity honours `(min_sep, max_participations)` and must be given the schema actually run; bare `IdentityStrategy` still prices one participation and must not be used to budget repeated participation until `_identity.py:45` is fixed. Link `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md#privacy-analysis` from the reference entry so the two cannot drift apart again. Per the repo's test-design rule this prose fix gets no verbatim-string test; the behavioural half is already pinned by the `k == 1` versus `k > 1` sensitivity values.
- **Notes:** **severity reduced from medium to low** by the verifier: there is no epsilon impact and no wrong result, and the stale half misdirects readers away from functionality that now works, which is the conservative direction. The original report's secondary argument — that a reader who spots the BandMF error will discount the Identity warning and so lose the only published guard against the real Identity under-report — was judged a plausible but entirely unestablished hypothesis. The Identity half must be preserved rather than deleted when the sentence is rewritten.

**OPQ-426 — a 60 KB internal research diary, `issue-795-research.md`, was committed at the repository root by the BISR fix, never removed, and ships inside the root sdist.** `issue-795-research.md:1-13` · repo-hygiene / docs-diary · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** d09d7b9a (`fix(dpftrl): bound BISR noise state by bandwidth`, #886) added a 1064-line, 60,100-byte working document at the repository root alongside the real fix, and no later commit removes it (`git log --oneline --all -- issue-795-research.md` returns exactly that one commit). Its content is a dated development diary — line 1 "# Issue 795: bounded-memory BISR noise execution", line 3 "Research snapshot: 2026-08-31 UTC", line 5 "## Post-research implementation update (2026-09-01 UTC)", then rebase SHAs, PR #857 narrative and internal GitHub links, with roadmap language at `:31` ("follow-up work but is not a condition"), `:125` ("possible follow-ups") and `:1012` ("should be tracked as follow-up work"). That is precisely what the AGENTS.md Documentation section rules out: "Keep user-facing docs and code comments diary-free … Migration narrative belongs in PR bodies and the changelog."
- **Failure scenario:** the file sits at the root of a repository whose milestone was public-release readiness, next to `README.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `LICENSE` and the other six root `*.md` files, where anyone browsing will take it for project documentation — including statements describing as pending work that has since merged, and roadmap language indistinguishable from a commitment. The packaging half was settled by building: there is no `MANIFEST.in`, and `uv run --with build python -m build --sdist` produced `opaque-0.15.6rc2.dev22+gbd83bbb0.tar.gz` which **contains** `issue-795-research.md`, because setuptools_scm ships all git-tracked files by default. No wheel carries it (`pyproject.toml:67-74` declares `py-modules = []` and `packages.find` include `[]` / exclude `["*"]`). No functional or privacy impact.
- **Evidence:** `ls -la issue-795-research.md` → 60,100 bytes, 1064 lines at the repository root; `git log --oneline --all -- issue-795-research.md` → single entry d09d7b9a; `issue-795-research.md:1-13` for the snapshot framing and `:31, :125, :1012` for the roadmap prose; AGENTS.md "Documentation" section for the diary-free rule; the built sdist listing; `pyproject.toml:67-78` for the packaging claims.
- **Fix:** delete the file — the durable content is already in `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md` and the PR body. If any of it must survive, move the still-true parts under `docs/` as present-tense reference and drop the dated snapshot framing. Add a CI guard that fails when a new top-level `*.md` appears outside a known allowlist (README, AGENTS, CLAUDE, CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY, CODE_OF_CONDUCT, NOTICE), which is the machine-checkable form of the rule.

**OPQ-427 — `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md` carries issue-tracker and roadmap narrative in a user-facing mechanism reference.** `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md:112-118` · docs-diary · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the BISR execution section added by d09d7b9a tells the reader which GitHub issue the design satisfies and what work is not being done: "This bounded ring is the complete `O(p)` execution design required by [issue #795](…/issues/795)." followed by "PRNG replay could trade that persistent tensor storage for extra noise generation, but replay and a reusable generic banded-inverse executor are follow-up directions rather than requirements for this bounded BISR path." AGENTS.md requires user-facing docs to describe current API and behavior rather than development history, and states that forward references to features that do not yet exist "don't belong anywhere".
- **Failure scenario:** a user consulting the page to size BISR's memory cost is handed an internal tracker number — for an issue that is now **closed** (state_reason completed, closed 2026-09-02 by PR #886), so the reference has no current meaning — plus a roadmap sentence that reads as a commitment that a PRNG-replay BISR is planned, instead of a plain statement of the current cost. Documentation-only; no code, numerical or privacy impact.
- **Evidence:** `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md:112-118` (both sentences, verified verbatim); `git log -- docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md` confirms d09d7b9a is the most recent commit to the file; issue #795 state verified as closed/completed; AGENTS.md "Documentation" section for the diary-free rule and the forward-reference prohibition; the surrounding factual content (the `y_t` formula, the `min(p, n_steps) - 1` retained draws, the `O(min(p, n_steps) * model_size)` statement, and the checkpoint-layout warning at `:121-140`) is present and correct.
- **Fix:** keep the formula, the `O(min(p, n_steps) * model_size)` statement and the checkpoint-layout warning; delete the issue link and the follow-up-directions paragraph. If a memory/compute trade-off note is wanted, state it as a present-tense property ("BISR trades bandwidth for retained noise buffers; raise bandwidth only while the retained pytrees fit") with no tracker reference. No verbatim-prose test, per the repo's test-design rule.
- **Notes:** the separate link at `:136-138` to **open** issue #789 (calibrated DP-FTRL resume) is a legitimate user-facing warning about a live bug and should stay. The numerical BISR measurements quoted in the original report's evidence (3 retained buffers at bandwidth 4, 3.7e-15 relative agreement with the dense reference over 418 configurations) were **not** re-run by the verifier and are unverified context; they are not needed to establish the docs claim.

### 3.3 Engine (`opaque-engine`)

**OPQ-428 — the empty-batch short-circuit runs `pre_clipping_transform` on zeros without the NaN/Inf sanitization the non-empty path applies, so a value-dependent transform emits NaN gradients on empty Poisson rounds.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_helpers.py:120-127`; callers `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_clipped_grad.py:222-258` and `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/clipping/_adaptive.py:455-462` · correctness/regression · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the #856 fix for OPQ-334 (commit f59e3c12) made the empty-batch short-circuit call the user's transform — `zeros = zero_grads_like(args, argnums); transformed = pre_clipping_transform(zeros)` — followed only by an optional dtype cast, with no `torch.nan_to_num`. On a non-empty step the transform is applied per example inside `grad_fn` (`_clipped_grad.py:290`) and its output flows through `clip_pytree`, which opens with `tree_map(lambda t: torch.nan_to_num(t, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0), …)` (`_pytree.py:599-606`, with the identical opener in `auto_scale_pytree` at `:463-470`) and whose own docstring lists "NaN/Inf values: Replaced with zeros before clipping" as a documented edge case. The empty path bypasses that documented invariant: `empty_clipped_grads_like` returns the transformed zeros straight into `clipped(zeros, max_norm=...)`. Any transform whose output depends on the gradient values — the obvious case being a normalization `t / global_norm(g)` — evaluates 0/0 on the zero pytree and yields NaN. Before f59e3c12 the empty path was plain `zero_grads_like` and could not produce a non-finite value, so this is a regression introduced by the fix.
- **Failure scenario:** non-default config only — functional DP-SGD with a Poisson sampler *and* a caller-supplied value-dependent `pre_clipping_transform`, e.g. `tr = lambda g: tree_map(lambda t: t / global_norm(g), g)`. On any round where the Poisson draw is empty, `clipped_grad` / `auto_clipped_grad` / `adaptive_clipped_grad` all take the short-circuit and nothing sanitizes the resulting NaN. Reproduced in the repo venv (torch 2.14.0+cu130): `clipped_grad(loss, argnums=0, batch_argnums=(1,2), clipping_norm=1.0, pre_clipping_transform=tr)` returns `ClippedPytree(tensor([1.1542, 1.9901, -0.9160]))` on a 4-example batch and `ClippedPytree(tensor([nan, nan, nan]))` on a `(0,3)` batch; `auto_clipped_grad(R=1.0, …)` behaves identically; and `gaussian_noise(noise_multiplier=1.0, key=key(0))` on the empty-step output returns `NoisedPytree(tensor([nan, nan, nan]))`, so the NaN is not contained — the first empty round corrupts the parameters through the optimizer, with no warning and no exception, surrounded by non-empty steps that look healthy. With the default identity transform, or any transform that does not divide by a gradient-derived quantity, the path is unaffected.
- **Evidence:** `_helpers.py:120-127` (transform on zeros, no finiteness handling); `_pytree.py:599-606` and `:463-470` (the sanitizers the non-empty path gets, and the documented edge case); `_clipped_grad.py:290` (per-example application on the non-empty path, so the transform is never evaluated on a synthetic all-zero tree there) and `:222-258` (`_empty_batch_response`); `_adaptive.py:455-462` (the same helper call); `git show f59e3c12` replacing `zeros = zero_grads_like(args, argnums_tuple)` with `empty_clipped_grads_like(args, argnums_tuple, pre_clipping_transform, dtype)`; the reproduction above; nothing between `empty_clipped_grads_like` and `clipped()` sanitizes, and no test pins finiteness of the empty-batch output.
- **Fix:** sanitize the empty-batch zeros the way the non-empty path does — wrap the transformed tree in `torch.nan_to_num(t, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)` before the dtype cast in `empty_clipped_grads_like`, and do the same for the squared-stream zeros built at `_clipped_grad.py:238-245`. That preserves the OPQ-334 structure/dtype guarantee while restoring the pre-fix invariant that an empty draw contributes exactly zero. A stricter alternative is to derive the empty output's structure and dtype without evaluating the transform on synthetic data (cache a template from the first non-empty step, as `empty_collate` does). Lock it in with a test that drives a normalizing `pre_clipping_transform` through an empty draw for all three factories and asserts every leaf is finite, and that `gaussian_noise` on the result is finite too.
- **Notes:** held at medium rather than raised: with the default identity transform — and with any value-independent transform, including the documented preconditioner-scaling use case — there is no defect at all. `adaptive_clipped_grad` routes through the same helper but was not executed by the verifier (it needs a `key=` argument that was not wired up); the code path is the identical call.

**OPQ-429 — `clipped_fun` and `auto_clipped_fun`, the documented power-user primitives, have no empty-batch handling at all and abort from inside `vmap` on a zero-length batch.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_clipped_fun.py:683-688` and `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_auto.py:111` · sibling-path/robustness · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-334 and its #856 fix are entirely about `clipped_grad`'s empty-batch short-circuit being structure- and dtype-faithful. The sibling primitives one level down have no empty-batch path at all: grepping `_clipped_fun.py` for empty/`batch_size == 0` returns only the stats-shaping branch at `:326-340` inside `_compute_clipping_stats`, and the fast path reaches `vmapped = _vmap(per_example_fn, …); outputs = vmapped(kernel_clipping_norm, *args, **kwargs)` (`:683-688`) unconditionally; `_auto.py` contains no occurrence of "empty" and `auto_clipped_fun` delegates to the same machinery. Yet `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/fun.py:14-16` advertises both as "stable, supported public API … for users who need to clip arbitrary per-example outputs or compose custom pipelines", and `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/functional/_collate.py:4-8` states that empty batches "arise from any Poisson-style sampler" — so the two supported surfaces disagree about whether an empty batch is a supported input.
- **Failure scenario:** a user composing a custom pipeline directly on `clipped_fun` or `auto_clipped_fun` with a Poisson sampler plus `empty_collate` — exactly as the collate docstring describes — gets a mid-run abort on the first empty draw. Reproduced in the repo venv (torch 2.14.0+cu130): both factories return correctly on a `(4,3)` batch and both raise `RuntimeError("cannot reshape tensor of 0 elements into shape [0, -1] because the unspecified dimension size -1 can be any value and is ambiguous")` from inside `vmap` on a `(0,3)` batch — an error naming neither the empty batch nor the API. The exact exception type and message vary with the wrapped function and the torch version. Fail-closed: no privacy or numerical consequence, only the abort and the inconsistency with `clipped_grad`, which short-circuits the same input.
- **Evidence:** the grep result over `_clipped_fun.py` (only the `:326-340` stats branch); `_clipped_fun.py:683-688` (unconditional vmap); `_auto.py:111` (delegation); contrast `_clipped_grad.py:222-258`, where `_empty_batch_response` exists precisely "so downstream buffers stay stable across empty and non-empty draws"; `functional/_collate.py:4-8`; `fun.py:14-16` ("stable, supported public API"); the reproduction above; no test in `packages/opaque-engine/tests/clipping/` pins the behavior and neither module's docstring mentions empty batches.
- **Fix:** give `clipped_fun` the same guard `clipped_grad` has — when `batch_size_from_args(args, batch_argnums) == 0`, skip the vmap and return an empty-batch-shaped output. Because `clipped_fun`'s output structure is `fun`'s rather than the parameter pytree's, the template cannot be derived from `args`, so either require an `out_template=` for the empty case or (cheapest) raise a `ConfigurationError` that names the empty batch and points at `clipped_grad`, making the limitation explicit instead of a `vmap` internal error. Document it in `fun.py`'s module docstring next to the "stable, supported public API" sentence, and add a test asserting the chosen behaviour on a zero-length batch for both factories.
- **Notes:** the originally reported exception (`IndexError: select(): index 0 out of range for tensor of size [0] at dimension 0`) did **not** reproduce — the observed error is the reshape `RuntimeError` above; the message is torch-version and function-shape dependent (the audit ran torch 2.13.0, the tree ships 2.14.0). The substance is unchanged. Held at low: fail-closed, no privacy or numerical consequence, predates the #856 delta, and the headline `clipped_grad` / `auto_clipped_grad` surface that ordinary users reach does handle the case.

**OPQ-430 — `tree_leaves` still returns `[]` for a `ClippedPytree` / `NoisedPytree`, the same silent fail-open shape #869 hardened in `global_norm`, in the same module.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/pytree.py:126-127`, contrast `:377-400` · fail-open-behavior · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 3acdba01 (#869) fixed OPQ-333 by making `global_norm` raise `InputTypeError` on the four DP wrapper types and tell the caller to use `.pytree` (`:377-400`, covering `ClippedPytree`, `NoisedPytree`, `SecondMomentClippingOutput`, `SecondMomentNoiseOutput`). Its immediate neighbour keeps the old behavior: `tree_leaves` is `flat, _ = _ot.tree_flatten(tree); return [x for x in flat if isinstance(x, torch.Tensor)]` (`:126-127`), and because `ClippedPytree` is an unregistered frozen dataclass it flattens to a single opaque non-tensor leaf and is filtered out, so the function returns `[]`. The module header at `:16` still reads "Only tensor leaves are considered for `tree_leaves` and `global_norm`", which is now true of only one of the two functions it names, and `tree_flatten` returns the wrapper as a leaf — so three sibling entry points disagree three ways on the same input.
- **Failure scenario:** a user inspecting DP gradients writes `sum(t.numel() for t in tree_leaves(grads))` or `max(t.abs().max() for t in tree_leaves(grads))` on the `ClippedPytree` that `clipped_grad` returns — the same mental slip OPQ-333 documented for `global_norm` — and the count silently reads 0, or the `max` raises a bare `max() arg is an empty sequence` with nothing pointing at the wrapper. Reproduced side by side on the current tree with `clipped({'g': tensor([3.,4.])}, max_norm=1.0)`: `tree_leaves` → `[]`; `global_norm` → `InputTypeError('ClippedPytree global norm is unsupported … Use .pytree …')`; `tree_flatten` → `([ClippedPytree(...)], PyTreeSpec(*))`; `tree_map(lambda t: t*2, ·)` → `ClippedPytree(pytree={'g': tensor([6., 8.])}, max_norm=2.0)`. No privacy or numerical consequence — a monitoring or sizing figure silently reads zero.
- **Evidence:** `pytree.py:126-127` (the filter), `:377-400` (the guard #869 added), `:16` (the module-header claim); the four-way side-by-side run above.
- **Fix:** mirror the `global_norm` guard in `tree_leaves` (and in `tree_flatten_with_paths`, which has the same shape): raise the same `InputTypeError` naming `.pytree` when the top-level argument is one of the four wrapper types, while continuing to drop nested non-tensor leaves as documented. Extend the #869 regression test to cover `tree_leaves` and `tree_flatten_with_paths` on each wrapper type.
- **Notes:** deliberately not raised above low — `tree_leaves` is documented to ignore non-tensor leaves and a bare wrapper at the root *is* a non-tensor leaf, so this is a hardening/consistency gap against the direction #869 set rather than a violation of a stated guarantee. One nuance the original report omits: `tree_map` "works" only because `ClippedPytree` overloads arithmetic operators and the wrapper reaches `fn` as a single opaque leaf; a `tree_map` with a tensor-method callable would fail on it too.

**OPQ-431 — `per_group`'s own shipped example cannot work on any supported transformer, because substring patterns collide by prefix.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_per_group.py:116, 141-142, 155-177` · docs/api-usability · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the Examples block in `per_group`'s docstring — the file the #867 fix rewrote to document the new unmatched-pattern rule — ends with `>>> per_group(params, **{f'layers.{i}': norms[i] for i in range(32)})`. Patterns are matched as plain unanchored substrings of the leaf's dotted display path (`matches = [pat for pat in all_patterns if pat in display]`, `:141-142`), so for `i >= 10` the pattern `layers.1` is a substring of `model.layers.10…`; the parameter then matches two patterns and `per_group` raises the multi-match `ConfigurationError` at `:155-162`.
- **Failure scenario:** a user copies the shipped example to assign per-layer clipping norms. Reproduced with 12 synthetic params named `model.layers.{i}.mlp.weight`: `ConfigurationError("Parameter path ('model.layers.10.mlp.weight',) (display 'model.layers.10.mlp.weight') matched multiple patterns: ['layers.1', 'layers.10']. Each parameter must match exactly one pattern.")`. The identical call with 10 layers succeeds, so the example looks correct in a small smoke test and fails on the real model — and as literally written with `range(32)` there is in fact **no** model size for which it works, because a model with fewer than 32 layers instead trips the unused-patterns error at `:168-177`. Fail-closed and docs-only. The trailing-dot variant `f'layers.{i}.'` succeeds on 12 layers, confirming the fix.
- **Evidence:** `_per_group.py:116` (the example), `:141-142` (substring matching, unanchored), `:155-162` (multi-match error), `:168-177` (unused-patterns error); the 12-layer failure, the 10-layer success and the trailing-dot success, all executed; no doctest collection exists anywhere in the repo (no `--doctest-modules` and no doctest config in the root or package `pyproject.toml`), so CI never executes the example.
- **Fix:** change the example to a prefix-free form — `>>> per_group(params, **{f"layers.{i}.": norms[i] for i in range(32)})` — and state in Args/Notes that patterns are matched as substrings of the dotted display path, so one pattern must not be a prefix of another. Optionally add a construction-time check that no explicit pattern is a substring of another, raising once with the trailing-dot suggestion instead of a confusing per-parameter multi-match error; that check is the testable artifact (assert the prefix-colliding pattern set raises with the suggestion, and that the trailing-dot set succeeds on 12 layers).
- **Notes:** two corrections to the original framing: the module docstring at `:55-59` *does* already state that patterns are matched as substrings of the dotted display path (what it omits is the non-prefixing requirement), and the raised error *does* name both colliding patterns, which is a usable hint.

**OPQ-432 — DPTrainer documents per-group `clipping_norm` keys as regex when they are plain substrings, and exposes no `allow_unused_patterns` escape hatch.** `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:92-93`; code path `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:1531-1546` · docs/misleading-safety-relevant-behavior · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the trainer documentation says `clipping_norm` "accepts a positive scalar (global clipping), a dict keyed by regex on parameter names with a `\"fallback\"` entry (per-group clipping)". The implementation does no regex matching: `_dp_trainer.py:1531-1546` pops `fallback` and forwards the remaining keys verbatim as `per_group_clipper(trainable_params, fallback=fb, **patterns)` with no `re.compile`, and `per_group` matches with `pat in display` (`_per_group.py:141-142`). Per-group norms feed the accountant through `PerGroup.effective = sqrt(sum v^2)`, so which leaves land in which group is sensitivity-relevant and the doc misdescribes the rule that decides it. The same call site also hard-codes the new strict behavior: it never passes `allow_unused_patterns`, which grep confirms exists only in `opaque-engine` source, its tests and `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:373` — so the "one config across architectures" case #867 added the flag for is unreachable from `TrainingArguments`.
- **Failure scenario:** a user follows the doc and passes `clipping_norm={'model\\.layers\\.\\d+\\.self_attn': 0.1, 'fallback': 1.0}`. Construction succeeds; `trainer.train()` then raises `ConfigurationError('Patterns did not match any parameter: […]. Use allow_unused_patterns=True …')` from `_per_group.py:170-177`, because the key matches nothing as a substring. Fail-closed, so no silent privacy loss — the harm is that the error contradicts the documentation the user followed, that there is no supported way to express a deliberately-partial pattern set from `TrainingArguments`, and that the substring rule quietly widens intent for keys that *do* match: verified that pattern `layers.1` with a fallback puts `layers.1`, `layers.10` **and** `layers.11` into one group while `layers.0` and `layers.2` fall through to fallback, which changes `PerGroup.effective = sqrt(sum v^2)` and therefore the sensitivity the accountant is calibrated with.
- **Evidence:** `training-arguments.md:92-93` (the "keyed by regex" sentence, present unchanged at both 4b13d82 and 12146ec4, so two doc-reconciliation commits in the delta — ba39783d and 12146ec4 — passed over it; grep shows "regex" appears nowhere else in the HuggingFace docs or `clipping.md`); `_dp_trainer.py:1531-1546` (verbatim forwarding, no `allow_unused_patterns`); `_per_group.py:141-142` (substring containment) and `:170-177` (the raise); the two executed `per_group` calls (the regex key matching nothing, and `layers.1` capturing layers 1/10/11); `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:365-373` describes the substring rule correctly, so the two user-facing pages contradict each other; grep for `allow_unused_patterns` finds nothing in `opaque-transformers`.
- **Fix:** replace "keyed by regex on parameter names" with "keyed by substring patterns matched against the parameter's dotted display path (see the clipping guide)" and cross-link `docs/user-guide/clipping.md` so the pages agree. If cross-architecture pattern sharing is meant to be supported from the trainer, plumb an `allow_unused_patterns` knob (e.g. a `TrainingArguments.clipping_kwargs` key) through to the `per_group_clipper` call at `_dp_trainer.py:1541`; otherwise state that every non-fallback key must match at least one trainable parameter and that the trainer fails when one does not. The behavioural test to add is a trainer-level assertion of the group assignment produced by a prefix-colliding key set, since that is what moves `PerGroup.effective`.
- **Notes:** the failure is raised at `train()` time, not at construction — the clipping block lives in `_setup_training` (defined at `:1397`, called from `train()` at `:1188`), so `DPTrainer(...)` itself succeeds. Severity stays low because today's outcome is a `ConfigurationError` rather than silent mis-grouping; before 88f81f2f the same config degraded silently to the fallback norm, which was the OPQ-332 defect.

### 3.4 Engine — distributed execution and resume

**OPQ-433 — DDP resume collapses every rank's Poisson sampler stream to rank 0's, so co-indexed records across shards are perfectly co-included while the accountant still prices independent Poisson.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:1871-1877, 3945-3950, 5195-5197, 5273-5310, 3878-3885` · sampler-accounting-desync / amplification-hypothesis-violation · **high** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges three independent reports of the same defect: "DDP resume collapses every rank's Poisson sampler stream to rank 0's", "Resuming a DDP run restores rank 0's sampler key on every rank", "DDP resume installs rank 0's sampler snapshot on every rank, silently re-correlating the participation".)*
- **Claim:** under DDP the trainer shards the dataset per rank and folds the rank into the sampler key — `if self._ddp.world_size > 1: sampler_key = fold_in(sampler_key, self._ddp.rank)` (`:3945-3950`, inside the `ctx.current_sampler is None` branch) — precisely so each rank draws an *independent* Bernoulli(q) mask over its shard. That independence is what makes the union of the shard draws an exact Poisson sample of the whole population, which is the hypothesis `acc.poisson` prices (`:1489-1494`, "The accountant uses regular `acc.poisson` over the global rate"). But the sampler snapshot is written **only** by the saving rank — `_save_dp_runtime` (`:5273-5310`, which serialises `ctx.current_sampler` into the single `DP_STATE_NAME` file) is called at `:5197` inside `if _distributed.should_save(a, self._ddp)`, world rank 0 by default (`trainer/_distributed.py:211-222`) — and is restored by **every** rank: the resume load at `:1175-1178` is not rank-gated and `:1871-1877` runs `ctx.current_sampler = from_state_dict(ctx.current_sampler, saved_sampler_state)` unconditionally, overwriting the rank-folded template. Every sampler family rebuilds its stream key verbatim out of the snapshot (`RngKey(seed=int(sd["key_seed"]), impl=str(sd["key_impl"]))` — `dpsgd/_poisson.py:255-261`, `dpsgd/_k_out_of_t.py:218`, `dpftrl/_poisson.py:304`, `dpftrl/_b_min_sep.py:259`, `dpftrl/_balls_in_bins.py:260`), using the template only for `data_source` / `n_steps`. The in-file comment at `:3869-3873` asserts the opposite — "the per-record marginal stays Bernoulli(q) either way, so the privacy accounting is unaffected" — which is wrong as a justification: Poisson amplification needs independence across records, not merely the right marginal.
- **Failure scenario:** multi-rank DDP (`torchrun --nproc-per-node>=2`) with checkpointing on, the job preempted and restarted with `resume_from_checkpoint`. Each rank owns a disjoint `local_shard` (`:3909-3913`) and on a fresh run draws an independent mask; after the resume point every rank replays rank 0's stream and therefore selects the **same local offsets** in its own disjoint shard, so records sharing a local index across the W shards are included or excluded together with probability 1 for the rest of training — while the accountant keeps pricing independent Poisson. Verified at the sampler level: `PoissonSampler(ds16, q=0.25)` under `fold_in(key(42), 0)` emits `[[4,5,8],[1,5,9,14,15],[4,12,13]]` and under `fold_in(key(42), 1)` emits `[[3,5,7,13,15],[0,6],[6,7,9,13]]`; after both are rebuilt through `from_state_dict` with rank 0's snapshot both emit `[[4,5,8],[1,5,9,14,15],[4,12,13]]` — identical. Magnitude, re-derived by numerical hockey-stick integration at σ=1, q=0.01, δ=1e-5 for a single step: the intended independent Poisson mechanism has ε = 0.199 (dominating pair `N(0)` vs `(1-q)N(0) + qN(1)`), while the neighbouring pair actually realisable with W=2 correlated records — `(1-q)N(0)+qN(1)` vs `(1-q)N(0)+qN(2)` — gives ε = 2.399, and W=4 gives 3.139. So the reported per-step epsilon understates a worst-case realisable one by roughly 12× at W=2 in this configuration. No warning, no accountant signal, and `docs/user-guide/distributed-trainer.md` has no resume section at all.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:3945-3950` (the fresh-run per-rank fold, in the `current_sampler is None` branch only); `:5197` + `:5273-5310` and `trainer/_distributed.py:211-222` (snapshot written under `should_save`, world rank 0 by default); `:1175-1178` and `:1871-1877` (load and `from_state_dict` on every rank, not rank-gated); `dpsgd/_poisson.py:219, 255-261` and the identical pattern at `_k_out_of_t.py:181/218`, `dpftrl/_poisson.py:259/304`, `_b_min_sep.py:219/259`, `_balls_in_bins.py:221/260` (stream key restored verbatim); `:1489-1494` (accounting is `acc.poisson` at the global rate); the sampler-level index-list reproduction above; the hockey-stick figures 0.19945 / 2.39891 / 3.13866; `:3869-3873` (the incorrect justification) and `:3878-3885` (the in-source acknowledgement that "resuming a DDP run currently restores rank 0's per-rank key on every rank, re-introducing the cross-rank correlation after the resume point"), which maps to no issue in either tracked list.
- **Fix:** write per-rank sampler snapshots (`dp_runtime_rank<N>.pt`, unconditional of `should_save`, mirroring the per-rank RNG snapshot `_save_rng_state` already writes) and restore each rank's own; or, minimally, re-apply the per-rank fold after deserialisation, which requires storing the pre-fold root in the snapshot so the cursor replay stays meaningful. Failing either, refuse to resume a `world_size > 1` run from a single-rank snapshot, or emit a loud runtime warning and stop calling the resulting epsilon a Poisson-amplified bound. Correct the `:3869-3873` comment: independence, not the marginal, is what the amplification theorem needs. The regression test to add is a `distributed`-marked two-rank resume that asserts the ranks' restored sampler stream keys differ and that their emitted index lists differ after the resume point.
- **Notes:** the verifier confirmed every link of the chain in the code plus the sampler-level reproduction and re-derived both epsilon magnitudes exactly, but did **not** re-run the full two-rank gloo trainer resume; the original report did, observing fresh-run stream keys `rank0=1950457451192556855` / `rank1=16528425509808239245` and, after resume, `1950457451192556855` on both ranks at every step.

**OPQ-434 — the distributed guide documents a `ddp_shard` `TrainingArguments` option, and a `global` / `parallel_poisson` accounting mode, that do not exist; its launch example raises `TypeError`.** `docs/user-guide/distributed-trainer.md:36-44, 46-58` (plus `docs/user-guide/index.md:63`) · docs-drift-api · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the page publishes `ddp_shard` as a `TrainingArguments` field, shows it in its only runnable launch snippet (`ddp_shard="per_rank"`, `:41`), and documents two modes with *different privacy accounting* in a `## ddp_shard modes` section (`:46-58`): `per_rank` ("disjoint shard per rank, Poisson accounting") and `global` ("full dataset per rank with rank-folded streams and `parallel_poisson` accounting"). No such argument, attribute or branch exists: `grep -rn ddp_shard --include=*.py packages/` returns nothing, `dataclasses.fields(TrainingArguments)` yields only `ddp_backend` and `ddp_timeout`, the trainer's `world_size > 1` branch (`_dp_trainer.py:3886-3913`) unconditionally trims and `local_shard`s with no alternative, accounting is always `acc.poisson` over the global rate (`:1489-1494`), and no `parallel_poisson` reference exists anywhere in `opaque-transformers/src` even though the factory exists. This is not the residue of a removed feature — `git show 65518ae4:docs/user-guide/distributed-trainer.md` already contains the `ddp_shard` lines, so the option never existed — and the delta made it worse: a624a34b (#1007) added five lines of `per_rank` divisibility prose on top of the nonexistent option, and ba39783d (#1026, "docs(transformers): reconcile trainer references") swept the page without catching it.
- **Failure scenario:** a user copies the launch snippet verbatim and gets `TypeError: TrainingArguments.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'ddp_shard'` (reproduced). A reader who does not copy it instead believes two accounting modes exist — `per_rank` with Poisson and `global` with full-dataset-per-rank and `parallel_poisson` — when the trainer has exactly one implemented behaviour (trim to a multiple of `WORLD_SIZE`, disjoint `local_shard`, `acc.poisson` at the global rate) and no code path anywhere selects `parallel_poisson`. Fail-loud and docs-only, so no run is silently mis-accounted, but the page's only statement about which amplification theorem applies to multi-GPU training is unverifiable against the code.
- **Evidence:** `distributed-trainer.md:41` (the kwarg inside the documented `TrainingArguments(...)` call) and `:46-58` (the modes table); the executed `TypeError` and the `['ddp_backend', 'ddp_timeout']` field list; `grep -rn 'ddp_shard' --include=*.py packages/` → no matches; `_dp_trainer.py:3886-3913` (the single unconditional sharding branch) and `:1489-1494` (always `acc.poisson`); `git log --oneline 4b13d82..12146ec4 -- docs/user-guide/distributed-trainer.md` → ba39783d (#1026) and a624a34b (#1007); `git show 65518ae4:docs/user-guide/distributed-trainer.md` for the never-existed history; `docs/user-guide/index.md:63` also advertises "ddp_shard behavior for DPTrainer".
- **Fix:** delete the `ddp_shard` kwarg from the example and either delete the `## ddp_shard modes` section or rewrite it as a statement of the single implemented behaviour ("at `WORLD_SIZE > 1` the training population is trimmed to a multiple of `WORLD_SIZE`, sharded disjointly with `local_shard`, and accounted with `acc.poisson` at the global rate"); fix `docs/user-guide/index.md:63` too. If a `global` / `parallel_poisson` mode is actually wanted, file it as a feature rather than documenting it as shipped. The durable guard is a check that documented `TrainingArguments` field names resolve against `dataclasses.fields(TrainingArguments)`.
- **Notes:** kept at medium rather than dropped to low because the fabricated `global` / `parallel_poisson` mode is the page's only statement about which amplification theorem applies to multi-GPU training; the `docs/user-guide/index.md:63` hit was missed by the original report and is included here.

**OPQ-435 — #1008 left the sibling `save_model` barrier untouched: a saving-rank failure there still strands peers in `barrier()`.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:5036-5044` · distributed-fail-open / incomplete-fix · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** fee28d2f (#1008) introduced `_distributed.checkpoint_barrier(ddp, local_error)`, whose docstring states it "Replaces plain `barrier()` calls that let a saving-rank failure leave peers blocked forever" (`trainer/_distributed.py:233-243`) by all-reducing a failure flag so non-failing ranks raise `OperationError` instead of blocking, and rewired the three stages of `_save_checkpoint` to use it (`:5203`, `:5212`, `:5239`). `save_model` — the other rank-gated write path, with exactly the same shape: `if _distributed.should_save(a, self._ddp): … self._save_model_artifacts(target); self._save_training_args(target); self.save_accountant(target)` with no `try`/`except`, followed by `if not _internal_call: _distributed.barrier(self._ddp)` — was not converted, and `:5044` is now the only surviving `_distributed.barrier(self._ddp)` call site in the trainer.
- **Failure scenario:** all ranks call `trainer.save_model(dir)` after training (the documented usage, which the barrier comment at `:5043` presumes). The saving rank's save raises — read-only or full output volume, a safetensors shared-tensor error, an NFS hiccup — and because that block has no `try`/`except` and the following barrier is the plain one, the failing rank propagates its exception out of `save_model` without reaching the barrier while every peer blocks inside it. Reproduced on 2-rank gloo with an injected `_save_model_artifacts` failure: rank 0 printed `RuntimeError: injected save_model artifact failure` while rank 1 sat in `barrier()` and only unblocked when rank 0's process exited, surfacing `RuntimeError: [gloo/transport/tcp/pair.cc:545] Read error [127.0.0.1]:20194: Connection reset by peer. This is typically caused by a remote worker hanging` — an unrelated transport error rather than a diagnostic about the failed save. If the caller wraps `save_model` in `try`/`except` (natural for a best-effort final save) the failing rank stays alive and peers block for the full store timeout. No privacy or numerical impact: the cost is a hang or a misleading transport error in place of the clean cross-rank abort #1008 was written to provide.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:5036-5044` (the un-guarded body and the plain barrier); `trainer/_distributed.py:233-243` (`checkpoint_barrier`'s docstring and failure-flag all-reduce); `grep -n '_distributed.barrier(self._ddp)' _dp_trainer.py` → only line 5044, while the three `_save_checkpoint` stages use `checkpoint_barrier` at `:5203`, `:5212`, `:5239`; the 2-rank gloo reproduction above; `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/distributed/_ddp_runner.py` has `scenario_checkpoint_save_failure` (reached via an `on_save` callback) but no `save_model` equivalent.
- **Fix:** wrap the `should_save` body of `save_model` in `try`/`except` and replace `_distributed.barrier(self._ddp)` with `_distributed.checkpoint_barrier(self._ddp, stage_error)`, keeping the `_internal_call` short-circuit since `push_to_hub`'s internal save runs on one rank only. Add `scenario_save_model_failure` alongside `scenario_checkpoint_save_failure` in `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/distributed/_ddp_runner.py`, asserting the non-saving rank raises `OperationError` rather than hanging.

### 3.5 Optimizers (`opaque-optimizers`)

OPQ-436 and OPQ-437 are the same root cause in two optimizers — a missing negative-`v` fallback in the private second-moment branch — and **share a single fix**: the `torch.where(v > 0, v, signal²).clamp(min=floor)` guard that Adam, RMSprop and AdEMAMix already apply. They should be fixed and tested together.

**OPQ-436 — Adadelta's private second-moment branch diverges geometrically because it lacks the negative-`v` fallback every sibling optimizer applies.** `packages/opaque-optimizers/src/opaque/api/optimizers/_adadelta.py:115-117, 251-253, 267-273, 321` · numerical-correctness · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** in the `noisy_squared_grads` (private second-moment) branch, `_scale_by_adadelta` guards a possibly-negative second moment with only `v_g_corrected = torch.clamp(v_g_corrected, min=0.0)` (`:251-253`). Adam (`_adam.py:194-197`: `torch.where(v_hat > 0, v_hat, m_hat * m_hat).clamp(min=bc_floor)`), RMSprop (`_rmsprop.py:126-128`) and AdEMAMix (`_ademamix.py:136-140`) all instead fall back to the squared signal — exactly what `_adam.py`'s own comment says the fallback is for: "Falling back to the squared first moment keeps the update magnitude ≈1 … instead of collapsing the denominator to `bc_floor` → infinity update." In Adadelta the collapse is worse than a one-off spike because it feeds back: clamping to 0 makes `rms_g = sqrt(0 + eps) = 1e-3` at the default `eps=1e-6` (`:115-117`, `:321`), so `coef = rms_dx_prev / rms_g` is inflated ~1e3×, `dx = coef * g` is inflated with it, and `v_dx_new = rho * v_dx + (1 - rho) * dx * dx` (`:273`) carries the inflation into the next step's `rms_dx_prev`. `_bias_correction.py:31-34` already states that "the separate `bc_floor` clamp seen in some optimizers belongs to the *private second-moment* branch (`noisy_squared_grads`), where v-hat … can be genuinely negative" — Adadelta has that branch and has neither the clamp nor the fallback.
- **Failure scenario:** with the opt-in paired release (`mf_gaussian_noise(..., second_moment_strategy=...)`, documented at `docs/reference/optimizers.md:110-140`) routed into `adadelta` — the LR-free optimizer the same docs recommend "when learning-rate tuning is hard" (`optimizers.md:48`) — `_chain.py:138, 169-176` auto-routes the privatised stream in because the scaler's `update()` accepts the kwarg (`_adadelta.py:158`). In any realistic high-dimensional DP setting the per-coordinate second-moment signal (~‖g‖²/d) is far below the per-coordinate release noise, so about half the stream is negative. Reproduced with the repo's own test fixture (`tests/test_adadelta.py:296-307`, `sq = -ones`, both streams at `noise_stddev` 0.1) run for 10 steps instead of 1: `max|update|` = 1.52e0, 7.33e2, 3.53e5, 1.70e8, 8.19e10, 3.94e13, 1.90e16, 9.14e18, 4.40e21, `inf` — it breaches the test's own `< 10.0` bound at step 2, but the test performs exactly one update from a fresh state (`v_dx = 0`), so the feedback loop never starts and the bound can never fail. Reproduced in a realistic regime (d=100000, unit-norm clipped gradient, per-coordinate noise `1/sqrt(d)`, 50.0 % of the squared stream negative): 1.83e-2 at step 0 growing to 4.74e3 by step 20, against ~1.4e-2 for adamw and ademamix and ~8e-2 for rmsprop on identical inputs. Training silently diverges to inf/NaN with no exception and no warning; epsilon accounting is unaffected.
- **Evidence:** `_adadelta.py:251-253` (clamp only, no `where`-fallback, no positive floor), `:115-117` + `:321` (`_rms` and the default `eps=1e-6`, giving `rms_g = 1e-3` on a fully clamped `v_g`), `:267-273` (the `coef` → `dx` → `v_dx_new` feedback path), `:158` (the accepted kwarg); `_adam.py:194-197`, `_rmsprop.py:126-128`, `_ademamix.py:136-140` (the sibling guards) and `_adam.py`'s comment; `_bias_correction.py:31-34`; `_chain.py:138, 169-176` (auto-routing) and `_second_moment.py:174` plus `docs/reference/optimizers.md:110-140` (the documented producing flow); the 10-step fixture divergence and the d=100000 regime numbers; `tests/test_adadelta.py:296-307` (one-step bound) against `tests/test_adam.py:502-518` (the honest version — adamw stays at ~1.0e-3 over 8 steps).
- **Fix:** mirror the sibling branches at `_adadelta.py:253` — `v_g_corrected = torch.where(v_g_corrected > 0, v_g_corrected, updates_node * updates_node).clamp(min=eps * eps)`, which is what OPQ-347's second option ("actually apply it in the substitution branch") asked for, and which was verified to hold `max|update|` at ~1e-2 over 20 steps in the realistic regime. Then extend `tests/test_adadelta.py:296` from one update to at least five to eight, matching what `tests/test_adam.py:502` already implies, so the `< 10.0` bound can actually fail. If Adadelta is instead deemed unsupported with private second moments — as Adafactor explicitly is (`_adafactor.py:34-38`) — raise from the branch rather than diverging, and drop the auto-routing for it.
- **Notes:** high rather than critical because the path requires the opt-in paired second-moment mechanism plus the adadelta scaler; `affects_epsilon` is correctly false — accounting stays valid, only the training run is destroyed. No upstream guard, warning or exception exists anywhere on the path. Shares its fix with OPQ-437.

**OPQ-437 — RAdam's private second-moment branch collapses the denominator to `bc_floor`, the exact failure mode `_adam.py` documents guarding against, and has no boundedness test.** `packages/opaque-optimizers/src/opaque/api/optimizers/_radam.py:230-245, 298, 348` · numerical-correctness · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** RAdam's `noisy_squared_grads` branch computes `r_t * (m / bc1) / (torch.clamp(v / bc2, min=bc_floor).sqrt() + eps)` (`:236-245`) with no `torch.where` fallback. With the default `eps=1e-8` (`:298`), `bc_floor = eps * eps = 1e-16` (`:348`), so every coordinate whose privatised second moment is non-positive gets denominator `sqrt(1e-16) + 1e-8 = 2e-8` and an update inflated by ~5e7. That is verbatim the behaviour `_adam.py:194-197` calls out and guards against with `torch.where(v_hat > 0, v_hat, m_hat * m_hat)` before the same clamp; `_rmsprop.py:128` and `_ademamix.py:138` carry the same guard. RAdam is the only one of the five substitution-capable scalers (adam, rmsprop, ademamix, radam, adadelta) that applies the clamp without the fallback, and the only one with no negative-stream regression test at all — the only "negative" test in `tests/test_radam.py` is `test_negative_weight_decay`.
- **Failure scenario:** `radam` paired with `mf_gaussian_noise(..., second_moment_strategy=...)` through the documented routing. For the first ~5 steps RAdam's rectification term `r_t` is `None` and the branch is bypassed entirely (the warmup path at `:230-232` returns plain `m / bc1`), so the run looks healthy; once `r_t` activates, every coordinate with a non-positive privatised g² divides by 2e-8. Reproduced with the repo's own fixture (the `tests/test_adam.py:502` style: `sq = -ones`, both streams at `noise_stddev` 0.1, `lr=1e-3`, 10 steps): `max|update|` = 1.52e-3 for steps 0-4, then 1.97e3, 2.49e3, 2.95e3, 3.36e3, 3.73e3 — six orders of magnitude, and far past the `< 10.0` bound the equivalent adam/rmsprop/ademamix tests assert, except that no such test exists for radam. In a realistic regime (d=100000, unit-norm clipped gradient, per-coordinate noise `1/sqrt(d)`, ~50 % of the squared stream non-positive) `max|update|` goes from 1.83e-5 at step 0 to 1.50e1 at step 8 and ~2.3e1 at step 19, roughly 1600× adamw's bounded 1.4e-2 on identical inputs. Training does not NaN immediately — it silently takes enormous steps from the moment rectification turns on. Epsilon accounting is unaffected.
- **Evidence:** `_radam.py:236-245` (clamp only, no `where`), `:230-232` (the `r_t is None` warmup bypass), `:298` (`eps` default 1e-8) and `:348` (`bc_floor = eps * eps`); `_adam.py:194-197` (the contrasting guard and its comment "instead of collapsing the denominator to `bc_floor` → infinity update"), `_rmsprop.py:128`, `_ademamix.py:138`; `tests/test_adam.py:502`, `tests/test_rmsprop.py:239`, `tests/test_ademamix.py:128` all have a `test_(second_moment_)negative_squared_stream_bounded` while `tests/test_radam.py` has none; the 10-step fixture run and the d=100000 regime numbers above.
- **Fix:** at `_radam.py:240` apply the same guard as `_adam.py:197` — `v_eff = torch.where(v / bc2 > 0, v / bc2, (m / bc1) * (m / bc1)).clamp(min=bc_floor)`, dividing by `v_eff.sqrt() + eps`. Add `tests/test_radam.py::test_negative_squared_stream_bounded` modelled on `tests/test_adam.py:502` but run for enough steps that `r_t` is not `None` (≥6 with the default betas), asserting the same `< 10.0` magnitude bound.
- **Notes:** the verifier's realistic-regime magnitudes are **smaller** than the original report's claimed 1.75e4 / 3.95e4 (different seed and scale) and have been restated above; the defect and its reachability are unchanged. High rather than critical for the same reason as OPQ-436: the path needs the opt-in paired second-moment mechanism plus the radam scaler, and epsilon is unaffected — but within that config the parameter step is ~1e3× too large and the run is destroyed silently. Shares its fix with OPQ-436.

**OPQ-438 — the optimizer chain silently discards a privately-released second-moment stream for optimizers that cannot consume it, inflating first-moment noise by `sqrt((D1+D2)/D1)` for nothing.** `packages/opaque-optimizers/src/opaque/api/optimizers/_chain.py:134-140, 169-176` · api-misuse · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_route_second_moment_output` computes `accepts_second_moment` from `inspect.signature(moment_scaler.update)` (`:134-140`) and, when the scaler does not accept `noisy_squared_grads`, does `return _route_noisy_pytree(updates.noisy_grads)` (`:169-176`) — never referencing `updates.noisy_squared_grads`, with no error, no warning and no record. `adafactor`, `adagrad`, `sgd` and `lion` all take that path. Meanwhile the paired mechanism that produced the output has already split a joint Mahalanobis budget across the two streams: `_second_moment.py:146-151` calls `paired_noise_stddevs(noise_multiplier/c1, first=max_norm*c1, second=squared_max_norm*c2)` and `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/noise_allocation.py:151-178` sets `sigma1 = nm * sqrt(D1 * S)` with `S = D1 + D2`, so the first-moment gradient the optimizer *does* use carries a factor `sqrt((D1+D2)/D1)` more noise than the single-stream path — `sqrt(2) ≈ 1.41×` for equal sensitivities — purchased for a release that is thrown away.
- **Failure scenario:** a user builds `mf_gaussian_noise(..., second_moment_strategy=...)` as shown in `docs/reference/optimizers.md:113-138` and pairs it with `adafactor` — which the same page (`:50`) calls the "Recommended default for DP LM fine-tuning". Verified: passing a `SecondMomentNoiseOutput` whose squared stream is a constant `-1e9` yields updates bit-identical to passing the bare `NoisedPytree` for all four affected optimizers (`adafactor update[0,0] = 8.170019e-04` either way) and emits zero warnings under `warnings.catch_warnings(record=True)`. Meanwhile `paired_noise_stddevs(1.0, first=1.0, second=1.0)` returns `(1.4142135623730951, 1.4142135623730951)` against the single-stream `base_stddev = nm * max_norm = 1.0` (`_mf_gaussian_noise.py:172-175`), so the run consumes exactly the same epsilon as a correctly-paired run but trains on ~41 % noisier gradients than a single-stream run at the same epsilon would have. Privacy accounting remains valid and conservative; the cost is purely utility and wasted budget, and nothing at runtime signals the misconfiguration.
- **Evidence:** `_chain.py:134-140` (signature inspection) and `:169-176` (the drop at `:172-173`); `noise_allocation.py:151-178` (`sigma1 = nm * sqrt(D1 * S)`, `S = D1 + D2` — the first stream is deliberately over-noised to pay for the second) and the executed `paired_noise_stddevs(1.0, 1.0, 1.0) = (1.4142, 1.4142)`; `_second_moment.py:146-151` (the paired call whenever `second_moment_strategy` is set); `_mf_gaussian_noise.py:172-175` (the single-stream baseline); the bit-identical-update / zero-warning reproduction for adafactor, adagrad, sgd and lion; `_adafactor.py:34-38` documents that the substitution path is not offered for Adafactor, but the chain drops the stream rather than surfacing that at the call site, while `docs/reference/optimizers.md:44-52` lists second-moment support per optimizer in a table nothing enforces at runtime.
- **Fix:** at `_chain.py:172-173` raise a `ConfigurationError` naming the optimizer and pointing at the supported set, or at minimum emit a `warnings.warn` once per optimizer instance — a `SecondMomentNoiseOutput` reaching a scaler that cannot consume it is always a misconfiguration, since the user has already paid for the second release. A softer variant is to let `mf_gaussian_noise` / `clipped_grad` accept the optimizer (or a capability flag) so the paired budget split is only taken when a consumer exists. The test to add asserts that routing a `SecondMomentNoiseOutput` into each of adafactor/adagrad/sgd/lion raises (or warns) rather than returning silently.
- **Notes:** kept at medium — it needs the opt-in `second_moment_strategy` configuration and costs utility only — and not lower, because the loss is a silent ~41 % noise inflation on an optimizer the reference documentation recommends as the default for DP LM fine-tuning.

**OPQ-439 — the exact-equality optimizer-state drift audit (#872) false-positives on bit-identical state when ranks differ in intra-op thread count.** `packages/opaque-optimizers/src/opaque/api/optimizers/distributed.py:73-86, 99-124` · distributed-correctness · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the #872 fix (commit 6ea47e6e) sets `atol=0.0` and `rtol=0.0` on `_assert_float_equal`, and `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/distributed/_state.py:66-72` compares with `torch.isclose(min, max, atol=atol, rtol=rtol)` — with both zero, that is bit-exact equality. The four tensor fingerprint statistics are computed *locally* on each rank before the collective (`t = tensor.detach().double(); float(t.sum().item())`, `:99-124`), and torch's CPU reduction is order-dependent in the number of intra-op threads, so two ranks holding byte-identical state can produce fingerprints differing by 1 ULP and the audit raises. The docstring's mitigation at `:77-78` — "Assumes ranks share an intra-op thread count, which standard launchers guarantee" — is an assumption, not a guarantee: it does not cover heterogeneous CPU core counts or per-node `OMP_NUM_THREADS`, differing CPU vector ISAs across nodes, or mixed accelerator models. Before #872 the `rtol=1e-5` default absorbed this class of artifact.
- **Failure scenario:** a user who defensively calls `opaque.distributed.sync` on a bare optimizer-state dataclass, or `opaque.api.optimizers.distributed.sync_optimizer_state` on a chain state, across CPU ranks that do not share an intra-op thread count gets a hard abort on healthy state. Verified with two gloo ranks holding the same 5,000,000-element float32 `AdamState` leaf (built from the same seed, so bit-equal) and `torch.set_num_threads(4)` on rank 0 / `(1)` on rank 1: both ranks raise `OperationError: "AdamState.mu['w'].sumsq mismatch across ranks: min=4997394.779571936, max=4997394.7795719365"` — a 1-ULP difference produced entirely by the audit's own local reduction. The underlying nondeterminism is directly observable single-process: the same tensor's float64 `sum` is -650.3623083472755 at 1/4/8 threads but -650.3623083472758 at 2 threads, and `sumsq` differs in the last ULP between {1,2} and {4,8} threads, so the float64 upcast does not remove it.
- **Evidence:** `distributed.py:80-86` (`assert_scalar_equal(float(value), name=name, atol=0.0, rtol=0.0, compute_dtype=torch.float64)`), `:99-124` (per-rank local reduction before the collective), `:77-78` (the thread-count assumption stated as a launcher guarantee); `engine/distributed/_state.py:66-72` (`torch.isclose` with both tolerances zero); `git show 6ea47e6e` for the tolerance change; the 2-rank gloo reproduction and the single-process thread sweep; blast radius confirmed by grep — `sync_optimizer_state` and the registered handlers are called only from `packages/opaque-optimizers/tests`, and `_dp_trainer.py:2231` syncs only `ctx.clip_state` and `aux`, never `ctx.opt_state`, so there is no production caller.
- **Fix:** either make the fingerprint reduction order-independent before comparing — reduce with a fixed grain (`t.flatten().view(-1, k).sum(0).sum()`) or thread-invariant Kahan accumulation — or keep float64 and restore a tolerance tight relative to the drift being hunted but above ULP noise, e.g. `rtol=1e-12` at tensor scale, which still catches the 1e-6 relative drift the new DDP test pins while absorbing last-bit reduction artifacts. The latter is a one-line change and keeps `test_optimizer_state_audit_detects_micro_scale_drift` passing; add a companion test that runs the audit across ranks with deliberately different `torch.set_num_threads` values on bit-identical state and asserts it does *not* raise. Also soften `distributed.py:77-78`, which currently states the assumption as a guarantee.
- **Notes:** the failure is a loud abort rather than silent corruption, and no production code path reaches the audit, so the impact is confined to users who invoke it themselves on a thread-heterogeneous CPU cluster.
## 4. Checkpoint, resume and distributed state

Ten findings from the state-compatibility sweep and the trainer fix-verification pass. The first
group covers what survives a `resume_from_checkpoint` — model weights, sampler cursors, noise
streams, optimizer state and their cross-checks; the second covers two trainer regressions in the
`torch_compile` path and one in the custom-loss path that the same fix-verification pass turned up.

### 4.1 Checkpoint and resume state

**OPQ-445 — PEFT/LoRA resume silently restores no adapter weights: the model continues from fresh random init while the accountant, noise counter, sampler cursor and optimizer state are all restored faithfully.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:5479-5481` (resume), `:4910-4912` (`load_best_model_at_end`), `:4974-4976` (adapter file discovery) · silent-state-loss · **critical** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_load_model_weights` ends with `if not mutated: strict = not self._is_peft; self._model.load_state_dict(new_state, strict=strict)` (`:5479-5481`), and `_load_best_model` repeats those same three lines verbatim (`:4910-4912`). For a PEFT-wrapped model `self._is_peft` is `True` (set at `:467` from an `isinstance(PeftModel | PeftMixedModel)` test), so the load runs with `strict=False`. That is fatal here, because the two key spaces do not overlap at all: `_save_model_artifacts` (`:5244-5247`) checkpoints through `self._model.save_pretrained(...)`, and PEFT's `save_pretrained` strips the adapter name from every key (`base_model.model...lora_A.weight`), while the live module's `state_dict()` keys carry it (`base_model.model...lora_A.default.weight`). Every saved key is therefore "unexpected" and every model key "missing"; the returned `_IncompatibleKeys` is discarded and nothing is logged. The resumed run continues with the *freshly initialised* adapter while `dp_state.pt` (clip/noise/sampler state), `dp_optimizer.pt`, `accountant.json` and `rng_state.pth` are all restored correctly, so every signal a user would look at says the resume worked. There is no second restore path: `_apply_runtime_state` (`:5548-5573`) rewrites `ctx.trainable_params` only for `ScheduleFreeState`, so the trainable parameters come from the unmutated module. Hugging Face's own `Trainer` — whose behaviour DPTrainer claims parity with — uses `model.load_adapter(resume_from_checkpoint, active_adapter, is_trainable=True)` for exactly this case (`transformers/trainer.py:3440` on resume, `:3502` for best-model load); `grep` for `set_peft_model_state_dict`, `get_peft_model_state_dict` or `load_adapter` across `packages/` (including `opaque-alignment`) returns nothing.
- **Failure scenario:** any PEFT/LoRA run — the documented DPTrainer fine-tuning shape — resumed with `trainer.train(resume_from_checkpoint='.../checkpoint-N')`. Reproduced end to end on the real trainer in the repo venv (peft 0.20.0, transformers 5.15.1; tiny GPT-2 + LoRA r=4 on `c_attn`, 4 steps, `save_steps=2`, `clipping_norm=1.0`, `noise_multiplier=1.0`, `use_cpu`): instrumenting `_load_model_weights` during the resume prints `_is_peft: True`, `weights changed by load?: False`, `post-load == checkpoint?: False`, `post-load == fresh-init?: True`, and the run then completes to `global_step=4` reporting `privacy_epsilon=0.8132` with no warning. With dropout disabled so the trajectory is otherwise deterministic, the continuous run logs `grad_norm` 0.836132824 and 0.929508567 at steps 3 and 4 while the resumed run logs 1.277820706 and 1.491086841 on the same batches and the same reported epsilon; the final `|Δw|` over the LoRA tensors is 0.1716. The user gets a plausible-looking completed DP run whose model has thrown away all prior learning — and in a DP setting the loss is not recoverable by re-running, because the dataset's privacy budget for those steps has already been spent. The identical three lines at `:4910-4912` make `load_best_model_at_end=True` a no-op for PEFT as well: reproduced with `eval_steps=2`/`save_steps=2` and the best checkpoint recorded as checkpoint-2, the final in-memory adapter equals checkpoint-4 (the last checkpoint), not the best — exactly the outcome the flag's own docstring at `:4877-4880` says it exists to prevent. Non-PEFT full-model checkpoints take the `strict=True` branch and are unaffected by *this* defect (see OPQ-448 for what happens to them instead).
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:5479-5481` (`strict = not self._is_peft`, return value discarded); `:4910-4912` (same code in `_load_best_model`); `:467` (`_is_peft`); `:5244-5247` (`save_pretrained`); `:4974-4976` (`adapter_model.safetensors` probed by `_read_weights_file`); `:5548-5573` (`_apply_runtime_state` does not re-point trainable params); measured key sets — disk `base_model.model.transformer.h.0.attn.c_attn.lora_A.weight` vs module `...lora_A.default.weight`; the isolated check that the literal call at `:5481` returns 4 unexpected / 33 missing keys and restores nothing without raising; the empty `grep` for the PEFT state-dict helpers; `transformers/trainer.py:3440, :3502` for the upstream idiom; `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/validation/test_dp_guarantees.py:347-349, :567-572` (every existing resume test asserts only `global_step` and `privacy_epsilon`, never restored weights); `docs/user-guide/huggingface/dptrainer.md:79-88` documents resume with no caveat.
- **Fix:** branch on `self._is_peft` in both `_load_model_weights` and `_load_best_model` and call `peft.set_peft_model_state_dict(self._model, new_state, adapter_name=self._model.active_adapter)` — or `self._model.load_adapter(ckpt_dir, active_adapter, is_trainable=True)`, matching upstream.
