Independently of the PEFT branch, capture the `_IncompatibleKeys` result at both sites and raise when *no* key matched: that single assertion converts this class of failure from silent to loud for any future key-space skew. Lock it in with a regression test that checkpoints a LoRA run, resumes in a fresh process, and asserts the live adapter tensors equal the checkpoint's (not the fresh init), plus a `load_best_model_at_end=True` variant asserting the final adapter equals the *best* checkpoint rather than the last. The existing resume tests should gain a weight assertion so that "resume works" can no longer mean only "the step counter advanced".
- **Notes:** `affects_epsilon` is false — the reported epsilon correctly prices the mechanism that actually executed. What is destroyed is the *product* of that spend. Reaching it needs two explicit opt-ins (a PEFT-wrapped model plus `resume_from_checkpoint` or `load_best_model_at_end`), which is the only argument for high rather than critical; the verifier kept critical because the failure is silent and total at the library's primary documented fine-tuning shape and the wasted budget is unrecoverable.

**OPQ-446 — DP-SGD resume keeps the checkpoint's Poisson `sample_rate` in the sampler but rebuilds the accountant from the new arguments, so a batch-size change across a resume under-reports epsilon behind a `log.warning`.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/sampling/_poisson.py:229-266` (esp. `:256`, `:260`); `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:4525-4545` (accountant built from current args), `:5679-5760` (warn-only drift branch) · sampler-accounting-desync · **high** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_from_state_dict_poisson` rebuilds the sampler with `sample_rate=float(sd["sample_rate"])` and `truncated_batch_size=sd.get("truncated_batch_size")` taken from the *snapshot*, using the template only for `n_steps` and the data source; its one equality guard is on `num_samples`. Meanwhile `_build_mechanism` closes the accountant's `dpsgd_acc.poisson(gaussian(nm), sample_rate=...)` over the *current* `ctx.sample_rate = train_batch_size / effective_dataset_size`. `RuntimeCheckpoint.sample_rate` is tagged `drift="dp_relevant"` (`_checkpoint.py:232-234`), but `_warn_on_arg_drift` raises only when the saved mechanism is not `"gaussian"` or the resume is a horizon resume — so for plain Poisson DP-SGD it emits a `log.warning` and continues. The sampler then draws at the saved q while the accountant charges the new q. This is the same defect class as the `RandomAllocationSampler.num_bins` desync (closed #792), and the fix for it (#1019, `fix(dpsgd)!: reject allocation-law drift on sampler restore`) added a `k`/`t`/`allocation` equality guard to `KOutOfTSampler` **only** (`_k_out_of_t.py:204-212`). Every sibling codec still resolves the same class of amplification parameter in favour of the snapshot: `PoissonSampler` (`sample_rate`, `truncated_batch_size`), `CyclicPoissonSampler` (`sample_rate`, `bands`, `partition_type`), `BMinSepSampler` (`bands`, `sampling_prob`), `BallsInBinsSampler` (`num_bins`), `SequentialBatchSampler` (`batch_size`). `truncated_batch_size` is not a `RuntimeCheckpoint` field at all, so drifting it produces no diagnostic whatsoever.
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced verbatim on HEAD (GPT-2 + LoRA, N=64, `privacy_noise_multiplier=1.0`, default Poisson sampling, δ=1e-5, `save_steps=4`, `use_cpu`). Phase 1 at `per_device_train_batch_size=32` (q=0.5) realises batch sizes `[32,25,29,31,36,26,41,36]` and reports `privacy_epsilon=4.75151`. Resuming checkpoint-4 with `per_device_train_batch_size=8` (q=0.125) realises `[36,26,41,36]` for steps 5-8 — bit-identical to the continuous q=0.5 run, which proves the restored sampler is still drawing Bernoulli(0.5) — yet reports `privacy_epsilon=3.25344`, i.e. 31.5 % below the epsilon of the mechanism that actually ran (the post-resume steps add ≈0.025 each instead of ≈0.40). The resumed `accountant.json` carries four `"sample_rate": 0.125` Poisson nodes for the post-resume steps while the same checkpoint's `dp_state.pt` records `sampler_state.sample_rate: 0.5`. The only diagnostics are three `log.warning` lines (`Resume arg drift on sample_rate (dp_relevant, DP-SGD): saved=0.5, current=0.125`, plus `expected_steps_per_epoch` and `expected_batch_size`); none of them states which value won, or that the reported epsilon no longer bounds what ran. Reaching it requires changing `per_device_train_batch_size` (or the dataset-size denominator) on resume of an all-defaults Poisson DP-SGD run — an ordinary operational action.
- **Evidence:** `_poisson.py:256` and `:260` (snapshot wins for `sample_rate` / `truncated_batch_size`; only `num_samples` guarded); `_dp_trainer.py:4525-4545` (accountant over `ctx.sample_rate`); `_checkpoint.py:232-234` (`drift="dp_relevant"`) and `_dp_trainer.py:5679-5760` (raise only for non-gaussian or horizon resume); `_checkpoint.py:229-247` (`truncated_batch_size` absent from the compared fields); `_k_out_of_t.py:204-212` (the #1019 guard that was not replicated); the same snapshot-wins pattern in `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/sampling/_poisson.py`, `_b_min_sep.py`, `_balls_in_bins.py`, `_sequential.py`; the reproduced epsilon pair and the two contradicting artifacts in the same checkpoint directory.
- **Fix:** mirror the #1019 guard in every remaining sampler codec — compare the snapshot's amplification-law parameters (`sample_rate`, `truncated_batch_size`, `bands`, `sampling_prob`, `num_bins`, `partition_type`, `batch_size`) against the template and raise `ConfigurationError` on any difference, exactly as the existing `num_samples` check does — and make `sample_rate`/`expected_batch_size` drift fatal rather than warn-only whenever a cursor-carrying sampler was restored. Add `truncated_batch_size` to `RuntimeCheckpoint`'s compared fields. The regression test is a two-phase trainer resume that changes `per_device_train_batch_size` and asserts a raise; note that the sampler-side behaviour is currently *pinned* by `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/sampling/test_poisson_state_dict.py:88-106` (it asserts `restored.sample_rate == 0.2` against a template built at 0.5), so that test has to be updated as part of the fix.
- **Notes:** the codec's snapshot-wins choice and the accountant's current-args choice are each individually intentional; the defect is the trainer-level desync between them. Kept at high rather than critical because a diagnostic *is* printed and the direction is fail-open only when the resume batch size is *smaller* — a larger resume batch over-charges epsilon and is conservative. #792 and #1019 are both closed and neither covers this pairing.

**OPQ-447 — DDP resume installs rank 0's sampler snapshot on every rank, collapsing the per-rank Poisson streams the rank fold exists to keep independent while the accountant keeps pricing i.i.d. Poisson amplification.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:3944-3949` (per-rank fold, fresh builds only), `:5151` + `:5197` (rank-0-only `_save_dp_runtime`), `:1861-1877` (unconditional restore on every rank), `:3872-3885` (the acknowledging-but-wrong comment); `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/sampling/_poisson.py:255-265` · distributed-resume-desync / amplification-hypothesis-violation · **high** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges three independent reports: the engine-distributed fix-verification pass, the privacy-invariants sweep and the state-compatibility sweep.)*
- **Claim:** on a fresh start the trainer folds the rank into the sampler key — `if self._ddp.world_size > 1: sampler_key = fold_in(sampler_key, self._ddp.rank)` (`:3944-3949`), reached only in the `ctx.current_sampler is None` branch at `:3938` — which is what makes each shard's Bernoulli(q) mask independent and therefore makes the union of the shard draws an exact Poisson sample of the whole population, the hypothesis `acc.poisson` prices at the global rate (`:1494`). But the sampler snapshot stores an *absolute* stream key (`_state_dict_poisson` writes `key_seed`/`key_impl`) and is written only by the saving rank: `_save_dp_runtime(staging_dir, ctx)` at `:5197` sits inside `if _distributed.should_save(a, self._ddp):` (`:5151`), and `should_save` returns `ddp.is_world_zero` by default (`_distributed.py:211-222`). The restore, `ctx.current_sampler = from_state_dict(ctx.current_sampler, saved_sampler_state)` (`:1874-1876`), is guarded only by `resume_path is not None and saved_sampler_state is not None and not a.ignore_data_skip` — no rank branch — and `_from_state_dict_poisson` rebuilds `RngKey(seed=int(sd["key_seed"]), impl=str(sd["key_impl"]))` with no re-fold. Every sampler family does the same (`_k_out_of_t.py:218`, `dpftrl/_poisson.py:304`, `_b_min_sep.py:259`, `_balls_in_bins.py:260`). Since `local_shard` hands out contiguous disjoint shards of equal length (`_shard.py:20-35`, dataset trimmed to a multiple of world size), every rank then selects the *same local offsets* in its own shard, so the `world_size` records sharing a local index are included or excluded together with probability 1 for the rest of training. The in-source comment at `:3872-3874` justifies deferring this with "the per-record marginal stays Bernoulli(q) either way, so the privacy accounting is unaffected" — which is false: amplification needs the target record's inclusion indicator to be *independent* of the rest of the sample, not merely Bernoulli(q) marginally.
- **Failure scenario:** any DPTrainer DDP run with `world_size > 1`, default `save_on_each_node=False` and default `ignore_data_skip=False`, using per-record Bernoulli sampling (`poisson` or `cyclic_poisson`), that is resumed from a checkpoint — the ordinary preempt-and-restart path. Verified on a real 2-rank Gloo run (the `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/distributed/_ddp_runner.py` fixtures, `save_steps=2`, resume from checkpoint-2, `use_cpu`): the fresh phase gives each rank a distinct stream key (rank 0 seed 1950457451192556855, rank 1 seed 16528425509808239245), and after `resume_from_checkpoint` **both** ranks report 1950457451192556855. Isolated at the sampler level too: `PoissonSampler(range(1000), 0.05, key=fold_in(key(7), r))` yields different first batches for r=0/1, and after both templates are passed through `from_state_dict` with rank 0's snapshot both hold `RngKey(seed=12201757873983228112)` and emit the byte-identical batch `[20,43,76,86,160,197,215,224]`. The accounting consequence was then quantified by numerically integrating the hockey-stick divergence of the executed group-sampled mechanism against the amplified one the accountant charges, with worst-case aligned co-indexed gradients: at `noise_multiplier=1.0`, q=0.01, world_size=4 the real per-step δ at ε=1 is 1.26e-3 versus the charged 2.73e-9 (ratio 4.6e5); at q=0.1, W=4 it is 1.27e-2 versus 2.07e-4; at nm=2.0, q=0.1, W=8 it is 6.8e-4 versus 1.1e-10. In the well-separated regime the executed δ converges to exactly `q · δ_Gaussian(unamplified)` — subsampling buys only the factor q, not the subsampled-Gaussian bound. A single-step reference at σ=1, q=0.01, δ=1e-5 gives ε=0.199 for the intended independent Poisson versus 2.399 for the W=2 correlated pair (3.139 at W=4). No warning is emitted at resume, and the `distributed` marker lane has no sampler-snapshot case.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:3944-3949` (the fold, fresh builds only, `:3938`); `:5151` + `:5197` with `_distributed.py:211-222` (`should_save` → `is_world_zero`); `:1874-1876` (restore, no rank branch) versus `:5205-5210`, where `_save_rng_state` already writes one file per rank — the per-rank-artifact pattern exists and was simply not applied to the sampler; `_checkpoint.py:69-75` (`rng_state_path` is per rank); `_poisson.py:255-265` and the four sibling codecs (absolute key restored verbatim); `_shard.py:20-35` (contiguous equal shards); the 2-rank Gloo key collapse; the sampler-level identical-batch reproduction; the hockey-stick integrations above; `_dp_trainer.py:3872-3885` for the incorrect justification and the acknowledged caveat.
- **Fix:** write the sampler snapshot per rank, unconditionally of `should_save`, next to the per-rank RNG snapshot (`sampler_state_{rank}.pt`, or move `sampler_state` out of the rank-0 bundle) and restore this rank's own file; alternatively persist only the rank-independent root key plus the cursor and re-apply `fold_in(base, rank)` inside `from_state_dict`. Until one of those lands, fail closed: refuse to restore a sampler snapshot when `world_size > 1` unless the snapshot records the rank it was written for and that rank matches, rather than proceeding silently. Correct the comment at `:3872-3874` — independence, not the marginal, is what the amplification theorem needs. The regression test belongs in the `distributed` lane: run two Gloo ranks, checkpoint, resume, and assert the ranks' sampler stream keys are still distinct and their emitted index sets differ.
- **Notes:** the worst-case magnitude needs an adversarial dataset with aligned co-indexed gradients; the qualitative loss of amplification is unconditional and applies to every sampling mode, since all five codecs restore a saved absolute key (for cyclic-Poisson and balls-in-bins the shared key also fixes a common partition/bin assignment). The original state-compat report rated this medium and reasoned the magnitude rather than measuring it; verification ran the 2-rank reproduction and the divergence integrals and raised it to high, on the grounds that it is reachable at stock settings with no warning and fails open. The in-code note that this is "tracked for the multi-GPU work" is not backed by any open issue found in the milestone/issue exports (the FSDP2 issues #55-58 are a different subject).

**OPQ-448 — full-finetune resume and `load_best_model_at_end` crash for every tied-embedding model, because the checkpoint omits `lm_head.weight` but the loader is strict.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:5242-5247` (save via `save_pretrained`), `:5469-5481` (`strict = not self._is_peft`), `:4892-4912` (same in `_load_best_model`) · resume-break · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_save_model_artifacts` writes checkpoint weights with `self._model.save_pretrained(output_dir, safe_serialization=self.args.save_safetensors)`, and `save_pretrained` drops tied parameters (those in `_tied_weights_keys`) from the file. `_load_model_weights` then reads that file and calls `load_state_dict(new_state, strict=True)` for every non-PEFT model, so the absent `lm_head.weight` is a hard error; neither loader calls `tie_weights()` afterwards nor filters `_tied_weights_keys`. On transformers 5.15.1 a tied Llama reports `_tied_weights_keys == {'lm_head.weight': 'model.embed_tokens.weight'}`, `save_pretrained` writes 11 tensors with `lm_head.weight` absent while `model.state_dict()` contains it, and the strict load raises. Only the single-file path is affected — the sharded branch of `_read_weights_file` already loads with `strict=False`.
- **Failure scenario:** any non-PEFT (full-finetune) DPTrainer run whose model ties input and output embeddings. Reproduced at the trainer level twice: (a) tiny GPT-2, 4 steps, `save_steps=2`, `trainer.train(resume_from_checkpoint=.../checkpoint-2)` raises `RuntimeError: Error(s) in loading state_dict for GPT2LMHeadModel: Missing key(s) in state_dict: "lm_head.weight".` from `_dp_trainer.py:5481`, before the first post-resume step; (b) a `LlamaConfig(tie_word_embeddings=True)` full finetune, whose checkpoint-2 contains `accountant.json`, `config.json`, `dp_optimizer.pt`, `dp_state.pt` and `model.safetensors`, fails identically for `LlamaForCausalLM`. The saved `model.safetensors` for the GPT-2 case holds 28 tensors including `transformer.wte.weight` and no `lm_head.weight`. Because `_load_best_model` shares the code, a completed full-finetune run with `load_best_model_at_end=True` crashes at the *end* of training — after the privacy budget has been spent — leaving the run's result unrecoverable through the trainer.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:5242-5247` (`save_pretrained`); `:5469-5481` and `:4892-4912` (`strict = not self._is_peft`, no `tie_weights()`, no tied-key filtering); the transformers-5.15.1 isolation showing `lm_head.weight` present in `state_dict()` and absent from the written file; the two trainer-level tracebacks; the checkpoint tensor inventory; the sharded branch's `strict=False` contrast; every resume test in `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/validation/test_dp_trainer.py` uses the `gpt2_with_lora` fixture, i.e. PEFT and therefore `strict=False`, which is why nothing catches it.
- **Fix:** load with `strict=False` and then verify coverage explicitly — compute `missing = set(model.state_dict()) - set(new_state)`, subtract `getattr(model, "_tied_weights_keys", [])` (or its mapping keys), raise if anything remains, and call `model.tie_weights()` after loading. Doing the explicit-coverage check is what keeps this from degrading into OPQ-445's silent no-op. Pin it with a resume test over a `tie_word_embeddings=True` config (a 2-layer `LlamaConfig` is enough) plus a `load_best_model_at_end=True` variant, so at least one resume test exercises the non-PEFT branch.
- **Notes:** one correction to the original report: `tie_word_embeddings` is *not* the class default for `LlamaConfig`/`Qwen2Config`/`Qwen3Config` on transformers 5.15.1 (all `False`); it is the default for `GPT2Config` and `Gemma2Config`, and it is set `True` in the published `config.json` of many real Hub checkpoints (Llama-3.2, Qwen3, SmolLM, and every Gemma/Gemma2/Gemma3). The failure is loud and not epsilon-affecting, but it breaks a documented core workflow, and in the `load_best_model_at_end` variant it breaks it only after the budget is gone.

**OPQ-449 — MF noise state carries no derivation-version or execution-identity tag except for BISR, so the #1018 λ-CGD stream change and any strategy-hyperparameter drift restore silently onto a different mechanism on the direct-library path.** `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_state_codec.py:26` (`_LAYOUT_VERSION = 1`), `:369-446` (`_load`); `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_lambda_cgd.py:281, 293`; `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_bisr.py:43-51, 96-119` · missing-version-guard · **low** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `MFNoiseState`'s wire format records only `layout_version`, `_step_counter`, `_rng_key`, the max-norm latch and the inner-state field *names*. It records neither the strategy hyperparameters (bands, `max_buffers`, momentum, alpha/beta, `lambda_`, `normalized`), nor the horizon (`n_steps`, `min_sep`, `max_participations`), nor the compute dtype, nor a version of the noise-derivation law. BISR alone got an execution identity from `d09d7b9a fix(dpftrl): bound BISR noise state by bandwidth (#886)` — `_BisrExecutionIdentity(version, inverse_coefficients, normalized, n_steps, compute_dtype)`, validated in `_validate_saved_execution_identity` — and BISR consequently fails closed on all of these; no sibling strategy got one. Separately, `f2d90d61 fix(random)!: namespace lambda-CGD noise stream (#1018)` changed λ-CGD's per-step key derivation from `rng_fold_in(st._rng_key, step)` to `_mf_gaussian_column_key(st._rng_key, step)` without bumping `_LAYOUT_VERSION` (still 1); `git log -S"_LAYOUT_VERSION" -- _state_codec.py` returns only 1794504b (#901). The repo's designated versioning seam, `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/serialization/_compat.py`, is still an empty placeholder.
- **Failure scenario:** reachable only through direct library use of `opaque.dpftrl.noise` + `opaque.serialization`, not through DPTrainer. Re-ran the accept/reject matrix on HEAD: an `MFNoiseState` saved mid-stream and restored into a template built with a different `n_steps` (band_mf 8→16) is **accepted** and emits different noise (−0.76381 → −0.70943); `momentum` 1.0→0.5 accepted (−0.76381 → −1.01377); bsr `alpha` 0.9→0.7 accepted (−1.10275 → −1.03146); `lambda_cgd` `lambda_` 0.5→0.9 accepted (−1.27672 → −1.77366). Bands/bandwidth drift is caught only incidentally, by inner-state shape checks, and only BISR rejects explicitly ("BISR checkpoint execution identity does not match the configured runtime"). The realised linear operator is then a hybrid of two strategies while an accountant built for the new strategy prices the whole horizon. Independently, a λ-CGD state written by a pre-#1018 build (derivation monkeypatched back to `fold_in(key, step)`) and serialised at step 4 is accepted verbatim by HEAD's `from_state_dict` and the step-4 release differs from the uninterrupted pre-#1018 run (`[0.33594, 1.21912, 0.66765]` vs `[1.37039, 0.54192, -0.19998]`); because `C⁻¹` is 2-banded for λ-CGD, the resume-step correlation term multiplies a `z_{s-1}` drawn from the new stream that was never released at step s−1. `GaussianNoiseState` has the same shape of hole for `compute_dtype`, whose wire format is `{_step_counter, _rng_key.seed, _rng_key.impl}` only.
- **Evidence:** `_state_codec.py:26` and `:369-446` (what the format does and does not carry); `_bisr.py:43-51, 96-119` (the protection that exists for exactly one strategy); the re-run accept/reject matrix with the noise values above; `git log -S"_LAYOUT_VERSION"` returning only #901 while `f2d90d61` changed the λ-CGD derivation; `git show <tag>:.../trainer/_checkpoint.py` giving `DP_STATE_BUNDLE_VERSION = 7` at v0.15.4, v0.15.5.rc1, v0.15.5.rc2 and v0.15.6.rc1 versus 8 at HEAD; `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/serialization/_compat.py` (`__all__: list[str] = []`, "Reserved for future use").
- **Fix:** bump `_LAYOUT_VERSION` whenever a derivation changes, and give `MFNoiseState` a strategy-agnostic execution identity mirroring `_BisrExecutionIdentity` — `{derivation_version, strategy_state_dict, n_steps, min_sep, max_participations, compute_dtype}` — validated in `_load` before any field is restored. Make bumping `DP_STATE_BUNDLE_VERSION` a required part of any PR that changes a noise or sampler stream; a CI check comparing the touched modules against the constant would have caught #1018. The test to add is a per-strategy round-trip matrix asserting that restoring into a template with any differing construction input raises, exactly as the BISR case already does.
- **Notes:** the original report framed the trainer as "protected only by coincidence" (an unrelated bundle bump two hours later); verification refuted that half with two real trainer resumes — mf_band with `momentum` 1.0→0.5 and mf_lambda_cgd with `lambda_` 0.5→0.9 are **both** rejected with `CheckpointError: Whole-horizon resume forbids drift on 'horizon_process_state'`, because that field is `compare_on_resume`/`dp_relevant` (`_checkpoint.py:271-274`) and carries the fully serialised strategy, and `_initialize_accounting` (`_dp_trainer.py:275-281`) returns a non-`None` horizon process for every `mf_*` mechanism, so `_warn_on_arg_drift` raises rather than warns. Severity was therefore reduced from medium to low: what remains is the direct-library path, which `MFNoiseState`'s own docstring (`_engine.py:57-63`) explicitly assigns to the caller, plus the one case the caller *cannot* discharge — preserving every construction input but upgrading opaque across #1018. `#1018` is in no release tag (`git tag --contains f2d90d61` is empty), so no released version pair can hit the cross-version stream break today. The ε relevance is real but confined to that standalone path.

**OPQ-450 — optimizer-state restore silently degrades to template defaults when the optimizer or the parameter set changes across a resume.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/serialization/_structural.py:65-73`; `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/serialization/_structural.py:110-112`; `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:5559-5567` · silent-state-loss · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the generic loader is template-driven and intentionally tolerant: `_tensor_load` does `saved = sd.get(""); if saved is None: return template`, and `walk_load` on a `Mapping` iterates only the template's keys, so keys present in the snapshot but not the template are dropped and keys present in the template but not the snapshot fall back to the freshly initialised value. Shape mismatches *are* caught by `_check_shape`, but name-compatible slots are not checked for provenance — and `'[0].step'` is a shared key name across optimizer families, so a step counter carries over from the wrong optimizer. `_apply_runtime_state` feeds `dp_optimizer.pt` straight into `from_state_dict(ctx.opt_state, opt_sd)` unconditionally, and `optim` is not among `RuntimeCheckpoint`'s `compare_on_resume` fields (`_checkpoint.py:227-303`), so changing the optimizer across a resume neither raises nor warns; the DP trainer has its own resume path, so HF's own optimizer-state checks never run either.
- **Failure scenario:** a user resumes after editing `optim` (or the trainable-parameter set, e.g. the LoRA `target_modules`). Reproduced at library level: an `adamw` chain state stepped three times serialises to `{'[0].mu.b','[0].mu.w','[0].nu.b','[0].nu.w','[0].phi','[0].step'}`, and `from_state_dict(lion.init(params), adam_sd)` is **accepted**, yielding `LionState(step=3)` with an all-zero momentum buffer — Lion resumes believing it has taken three steps with an empty buffer, so bias correction and any step-dependent warmup run against state that does not exist. The subset case reproduces too: a 2-parameter Adam snapshot restored into a 3-parameter template leaves the new parameter's `mu` at max 0.0 while `mu['w']` restores to 0.135 and `step` stays 3. Nothing warns in either case. The consequence is a degraded training trajectory and lost utility, not lost privacy.
- **Evidence:** `opaque-engine/.../serialization/_structural.py:65-73` (`sd.get("")` → template fallback); `opaque-base/.../_structural.py:110-112` (template-key-only iteration); `_dp_trainer.py:5559-5567` (unconditional `from_state_dict(ctx.opt_state, opt_sd)`); `_dp_trainer.py:5764-5845` (`_current_values_for_drift` has no `optim` entry) and `_checkpoint.py:227-303` (no `optim` among the compared fields); the reproduced "Adam sd → Lion template ACCEPTED: LionState step= 3 m all-zero: True" and the 2-into-3-parameter run.
- **Fix:** record the optimizer identity (`type(state).__name__` per chain slot) in `dp_optimizer.pt` and reject a mismatch on load; separately, let `from_state_dict` optionally report keys present in the template but absent from the snapshot, so the trainer can warn about optimizer tensors it failed to restore. Lock it in with a test that serialises an `adamw` chain state and asserts `from_state_dict(lion.init(params), sd)` raises, plus one asserting a parameter-set change is reported.
- **Notes:** the tolerance is deliberate and documented (`from_state_dict`'s docstring: "missing leaves keep the template's value (forward compatibility when new fields appear)"), which is why the fix belongs at the optimizer-identity layer rather than in the generic walker. Verification reproduced the library-level behaviour directly; the trainer reachability is established by code reading — a full trainer resume with a changed `optim` was not executed.

**OPQ-451 — checkpoints written by an earlier release fail with unactionable errors: a bare `KeyError` for pre-#761 k-out-of-t sampler state and a bare version number for the trainer bundle, with no migration path in the tree.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/sampling/_k_out_of_t.py:191-214` (the `k`/`t`/`allocation` lookups at `:204-212` are unguarded `state[name]`); `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_checkpoint.py:399-405`; `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/serialization/_compat.py` · migration-ergonomics · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `fc44ec8d refactor(dpsgd)!: unify k-out-of-t allocation (#761)` renamed the sampler's persisted fields from `total_participations`/`n_steps` to `k`/`t` and added `allocation` (confirmed by `git show fc44ec8d^`, where the persisted key set was `key_seed`/`key_impl`/`consumed`/`num_samples`/`total_participations`/`n_steps`). `_from_state_dict_k_out_of_t` guards `state["num_samples"]` with a fully explanatory `ConfigurationError` (`:196-203`) and then indexes `state[name]` for `k`, `t` and `allocation` with no key guard at all, so a pre-#761 snapshot raises a bare `KeyError: 'k'` annotated only with "while restoring …". `load_dp_runtime_state` similarly raises `CheckpointError: unsupported dp_state bundle version N (expected 8)` and names neither the release that wrote N nor what the operator should do. The designated versioning seam, `_compat.py`, is still an empty placeholder ("Reserved for future use", `__all__ = []`), there is no in-tree CHANGELOG or migration page, and `docs/limitations.md` says nothing about checkpoint portability.
- **Failure scenario:** a user on v0.15.6.rc1 upgrades mid-run and calls `trainer.train(resume_from_checkpoint=...)`, and gets `CheckpointError: unsupported dp_state bundle version 7 (expected 8)` — with no way to learn from the message that the correct action is to finish the run on the old version, which it is: three noise/sampler-stream changes in the delta (#884, #1018, #1019) make silent migration unsafe. A user resuming a hand-managed k-out-of-t sampler through `opaque.serialization` gets `KeyError: 'k'` (reproduced: "old k_out_of_t sd REJECTED: KeyError : k"). Both failures are fail-closed — the run stops rather than resuming wrongly — so the cost is operator time, not correctness.
- **Evidence:** `git show fc44ec8d^:.../_k_out_of_t.py` for the old key names versus `_state_dict_k_out_of_t` (`:179-188`) for the new ones; `_k_out_of_t.py:196-203` (the good guard) against `:204-212` (the unguarded lookups); the reproduced `KeyError`; `_checkpoint.py:399-405` with `DP_STATE_BUNDLE_VERSION = 8`; the empty `_compat.py`; the absence of a root CHANGELOG or `docs/` migration page.
- **Fix:** validate the snapshot's key set up front and raise `CheckpointError` naming the renamed fields and the release that renamed them; add an `opaque_version` field to `RuntimeCheckpoint` so `load_dp_runtime_state` can say "written by opaque 0.15.6.rc1; bundles before version 8 cannot be migrated because the λ-CGD and k-out-of-t streams changed — finish the run on that version". A small test that feeds each pre-rename snapshot shape to its codec and asserts the error type and that the message names the missing field would keep future renames honest.
- **Notes:** this is an error-message and ergonomics defect, not a correctness one — no silent wrong resume and no epsilon impact. The original report's "no way to learn" is slightly too strong: generated GitHub Release bodies exist outside the tree (`cliff.toml` drives them). What is genuinely missing is the up-front key-set validation and the writing-release identity in the message.

### 4.2 Trainer compile and custom-loss regressions (fix-verification pass)

**OPQ-452 — `torch_compile=True` aborts on the first training step for every Hugging Face model: the fullgraph fallback was deleted rather than narrowed.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:209-219`, `:4337-4369` (`_grad_compiler`), `:4412, :4425, :4437` (threaded into every clipping factory); `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_clipped_fun.py:588-602` · regression/usability · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `fae93a55` (#979) replaced `_compile_with_fullgraph_fallback` with `_compile_strict_chunk`, a bare `torch.compile(fn, backend=backend, mode=mode, fullgraph=True, dynamic=True)` with no exception handling. The compiled chunk kernel wraps `vmap(grad_and_value(per_example_loss))` — `_clipped_fun.py:588-602` shows the body is `vmapped = _vmap(_per_example_fn, ...); outputs = vmapped(kernel_clipping_norm, *chunk_args)` — so the user's entire model forward sits inside the fullgraph region. Hugging Face Transformers 5.15.1 calls `logger.warning_once` inside that forward (the `use_return_dict` deprecation in `configuration_utils`, `loss_type=None` in `modeling_utils`), and Dynamo rejects `logging.Logger` methods under `fullgraph=True`. Before `fae93a55` the same exception was caught — `except torch._dynamo.exc.TorchDynamoException` after `f55c2a89` (#891), `except Exception` before that — and the step was retried under `fullgraph=False`, so the run completed. There is no escape hatch: nothing in the tree registers anything in `torch._dynamo.config.ignore_logging_functions`.
- **Failure scenario:** `DPTrainer(model=LlamaForCausalLM(...), args=TrainingArguments(..., torch_compile=True))`; `trainer.train()` raises on step 1 with `torch._dynamo.exc.Unsupported: logging.Logger method not supported for non-export cases`, the developer-debug context naming `<Logger transformers.configuration_utils>.warning_once('`use_return_dict` is deprecated!...')`. Reproduced with the repo venv (torch 2.14.0+cu130, transformers 5.15.1) on a 2-layer `LlamaForCausalLM` (hidden 32, vocab 32) with `apply_runtime_patches` + `apply_model_patches`, and on the repo's own tiny GPT-2 + LoRA fixture, across `clipping_mode` in {fixed, adaptive, auto} and backends {aot_eager, inductor} — 8/8 configurations fail. Monkeypatching only `_compile_strict_chunk` back to `fullgraph=False` makes the identical run complete (3 steps, train_loss 2.3148, ε 0.2308; 3.64476 on the other fixture for all three clipping modes), which pins the deleted fallback as the only thing that was making the feature work on HF models.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:209-219` (the bare `torch.compile`, no try/except); `:4337-4369` and `:4412, :4425, :4437` (threaded in as `_chunk_compiler` for every clipping factory); `_clipped_fun.py:588-602` (the model forward is inside the compiled region); `git show fae93a55` (removes `_compile_with_fullgraph_fallback`, adds `_compile_strict_chunk`); the 8/8 failure sweep and the fullgraph=False control run; `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/opaque_transformers/test_compile_and_kernel_args.py:112-140` — `test_torch_compile_runs_poisson_training_strictly` is the only end-to-end compile test and it uses the synthetic `_TinyLM`, which emits no `warning_once`, so the full transformers suite is green with the feature broken; `docs/reference/transformers.md:218` and `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:216` document the flag as compiling "with fullgraph=True" and never mention that HF models abort.
- **Fix:** either restore a narrow fallback — catch `torch._dynamo.exc.TorchDynamoException` on the first call, log, and recompile the same chunk with `fullgraph=False`, while explicitly re-raising `torch.OutOfMemoryError` so `_synchronize_grad_failure` still sees it first-hand (the `f55c2a89` shape) — or keep `fullgraph=True` and register the HF logging functions in `torch._dynamo.config.ignore_logging_functions` inside `_grad_compiler` before compiling. Either way, add the end-to-end regression test that would have caught it: run `DPTrainer(torch_compile=True)` against a real transformers causal LM (a 2-layer `LlamaForCausalLM` suffices) rather than a synthetic `nn.Module`.
- **Notes:** `torch_compile` defaults to `False` and the failure is a loud exception on step 1 — no silent wrong results, no epsilon impact — which is why this stays medium despite making a documented flag unusable on the model family `opaque-transformers` exists to serve.

**OPQ-453 — `dynamic=True`, added for Poisson graph reuse, makes `clipping_mode="auto"` + `torch_compile` crash even with no graph break.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:209-219` (the flag at `:218`); `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/torch/checkpoint/reparametrize_recompute.py:37, 66` · regression · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `afa0247f` (`perf(transformers): reuse compiled graphs across Poisson batch shapes`, #989) added `dynamic=True` to `_compile_strict_chunk`, and that is its only source change. On the trainer's stack — `torch.func.functional_call` plus the opaque-patches `_reparametrize_module` recompute patch — the flag makes Dynamo's speculation log diverge for the AUTO-S clipping path specifically: `clipping_mode="auto"` + `torch_compile=True` aborts with `torch._dynamo.exc.SpeculationLogDivergence` at the thread-local stack mutation in `reparametrize_recompute.py` (expected `:37` `STORE_ATTR`, actual `:66` `CALL`), while `fixed` and `adaptive` compile and run on the identical model, data and seed. The trigger is the flag, not the mode.
- **Failure scenario:** `TrainingArguments(clipping_mode="auto", clipping_kwargs={"gamma": 0.01}, torch_compile=True)` makes `train()` abort on the first compiled chunk. Reproduced in a fresh process on a plain 2-layer `nn.Module` `TinyLM` (embedding + linear, no HF logging involved, so OPQ-452 cannot be masking it) with backend `aot_eager`, batch size 6, `max_steps=3`, `privacy_noise_multiplier=1.0`: `fixed` → OK (loss 2.92617), `adaptive` → OK (loss 2.92621), `auto` → `SpeculationLogDivergence ... diverged at index 622`. The original sweep on an HF-shaped model failed 6/6 auto runs (batch sizes 2 and 6, with and without an explicit microbatch size, backends aot_eager and inductor) against 4/4 successes for fixed and adaptive. Flag sweep on the identical run isolates the cause: fullgraph=True + dynamic=True FAIL; fullgraph=True + dynamic=False OK (loss 2.92617, bit-identical to the fixed-mode eager number); fullgraph=True + dynamic unset OK (identical); and, importantly, fullgraph=False + dynamic=True also FAILS with the same divergence at index 622 — so this is independent of OPQ-452 and would survive restoring the fullgraph fallback.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:209-219` with `dynamic=True` at `:218`; `git show afa0247f` (the flag is the whole change); the three-mode comparison and the four-way flag sweep above; traceback frames `_clipped_fun.py:601` → `_clipped_grad.py:289` → `saved_tensor_hooks_guard.py:45` → `_dp_trainer.py:2444` (`fmodel(params, **inputs)`) → `reparametrize_recompute.py:66`; not reproducible at engine level — `auto_clipped_grad(..., _chunk_compiler=torch.compile(..., fullgraph=True, dynamic=True))` over a plain tensor loss runs fine, so the divergence needs the trainer's `functional_call` + patch stack, which no test exercises (`packages/opaque-engine/tests/clipping/test_compile_chunks.py` and `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/clipping/test_compile.py` both use pure tensor losses).
- **Fix:** drop the explicit `dynamic=True` and let PyTorch's automatic dynamic shapes do the graph reuse — verified to keep AUTO-S working and to give identical numerics — or keep `dynamic=True` only when `clipping_mode != "auto"` until the AUTO-S `_scale_fn` path is made speculation-stable. Add an end-to-end parametrised test over `clipping_mode` in {fixed, adaptive, auto} that actually runs `DPTrainer(torch_compile=True)`; the current compile tests either only build the arguments or use a pure-tensor loss.
- **Notes:** two non-default options are required (`torch_compile=True` plus `clipping_mode="auto"`) and the failure is a loud exception, not a silent numeric change. The rest of `afa0247f` checked out clean: with fixed/adaptive clipping, compiled and eager parameters agreed to 0.000e+00 across batch shapes `[1,3,1,2,1,3,3,4,5,1,4,2]` and `[3,0,0,2,1,1,0,1,2,1,2,1]` (empty Poisson rounds included) and with microbatch remainders (bs=6/mbs=4), and empty batches never reach the compiled kernel because `clipped_grad` short-circuits first (`_clipped_grad.py:322, 337, 348`) and `_microbatch_accumulate_reduced`'s `range(0, 0, mb)` is empty — so the #808 empty-batch/step-shape concern is clean.

**OPQ-454 — `compute_loss_func` is now invoked at two different tensor ranks, so a per-example loss silently reports a different eval objective than the one being trained.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:2719-2726` (batched eval call) vs `:2452-2456` (per-example call under vmap, docstring at `:2409-2412`); `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trl/_sft_trainer.py:646-656` · api-misuse/silent-divergence · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `25bb4ed7` (`fix(transformers): honor custom loss during evaluation`, #1028) made the default eval path call `self._compute_loss_func(output, custom_labels)` on a *batched* `ModelOutput` (`:2719-2726`), removing the previous `output.get("loss")` read and the comment that told users to set `include_for_metrics=["loss"]`. Training and the `include_for_metrics=["loss"]` eval path call the same callable under `vmap` with the leading batch dimension stripped — `compute_per_example_loss` at `:2452-2456`, whose own docstring at `:2409-2412` says "One example's input dict (under vmap; the caller stripped the leading batch dim)". Nothing validates the rank of the inputs or of the return value, and nothing warns. A loss that indexes the leading axis — the natural way to write a causal-LM objective under the documented per-example contract — therefore computes a different quantity at eval than at training, and when sequence length happens to equal vocabulary size it does so without raising. `docs/alignment/trainers.md:112-114` still states the SFT custom loss "runs inside vmap, so it must be a pure per-example op", which is no longer true of the path SFTTrainer takes: `_sft_trainer.py:646-656` delegates every non-DFT `loss_type` to `super().prediction_step`, landing on the batched call.
- **Failure scenario:** `compute_loss_func=lambda out, labels: F.cross_entropy(out['logits'][:-1], labels[1:])` — the per-example causal-LM shift the documented contract tells users to write — with `eval_strategy="steps"`, `metric_for_best_model="eval_loss"` and `load_best_model_at_end=True`. Reproduced on a dict-returning 2-layer module with T=V=4 over a 16-example eval set: the true training objective, and the `include_for_metrics=["loss"]` eval path, give 1.70644, while the default `evaluate()` reports `eval_loss` 1.34594 — a silent ≈0.36-nat divergence, because the slice shifted the *batch* axis and `cross_entropy` read the sequence axis as the class axis. Best-model selection, early stopping and every logged eval curve are driven by the wrong series. Changing only V to 5 (T ≠ V) turns the same default eval into `RuntimeError: Expected target size [3, 5], got [3, 4]`, so a user whose custom loss previously worked (the batched branch used to ignore `compute_loss_func` entirely) now gets a crash instead.
- **Evidence:** `_dp_trainer.py:2719-2726` (the batched call, no rank check) against `:2452-2456` and the docstring at `:2409-2412`; `git show 25bb4ed7` (removed guard and comment); the measured 1.34594 vs 1.70644 vs hand-computed 1.70644 triple and the T≠V `RuntimeError`; `docs/alignment/trainers.md:112-114` (unchanged per-example claim); `_sft_trainer.py:646-656` (non-DFT loss types delegate to `super().prediction_step`); `_dp_trainer.py:2946-2966` (the returned scalar is weighted by `int((labels[..., 1:] != _IGNORE_INDEX).sum())`, which only makes sense for a per-token-mean CE); the tests added by `25bb4ed7` in `test_trainer_contract.py` use only rank-agnostic losses (`F.cross_entropy(output['logits'], labels)` on 2-D logits), so none of them catch the divergence.
- **Fix:** make the contract single-ranked. On the batched branch, either `vmap` the user callable over the batch (a Python loop over the batch dimension reusing one forward's outputs preserves the per-example contract and the existing token weighting), or, if the batched call is kept, validate it: assert the returned value is 0-dimensional and emit a one-shot `RuntimeWarning` naming the rank change the first time `_compute_loss_func` is reached from `prediction_step`'s batched branch. Update `docs/alignment/trainers.md:114` and the `compute_per_example_loss` docstring to state whichever convention wins, and add a regression test with a leading-axis-sensitive loss asserting that the default eval and the `include_for_metrics=["loss"]` eval agree.
- **Notes:** eval metrics only — no effect on the training objective, gradients, clipping, noise or the accounting. One correction to the original report: the token-weighted aggregation at `:2946-2966` is now additionally gated on `self._is_causal_lm` (added by the same commit), so the compounding it describes applies to real HF causal LMs but not to the plain module used in the reproduction; the gate was verified to exist, but the token-weighting amplification was not separately measured.
## 5. Patched-model fidelity and fused kernels

Nineteen findings (from 21 verified reports, after two merges). This is the heaviest
high-severity cluster in the delta, and it has a single theme: **the performance work in this
milestone caused correctness regressions.** Every high finding below traces to a commit whose
stated purpose was throughput — `c0f95ced` (#988, "activate fused loss-only cross-entropy") flipped
the fused-CE forward wrapper from opt-in to default-on and thereby made a pre-existing wrong
fallback branch reachable at trainer defaults (OPQ-460) while also making `torch_compile` unusable
(OPQ-462); `a839782e` (#987, "reuse fused cross-entropy statistics") broke bare `torch.func.grad`
(OPQ-463); `d80d3f99` (#954) and `4e61b09d` (#925) extended the compact-SDPA mask elision to
gemma2 and reopened OPQ-337 in a sibling path (OPQ-472); the four QKV-fusion commits
`41722a0b` / `414504dc` / `8ced13cf` / `bf2190b9` (#947/#949/#951/#950) built dedicated wrappers for
two families and left six more on a generic wrapper that is not faithful to any of them
(OPQ-465, OPQ-466); `b9fb8674` (#985) added a functorch guard that never fires (OPQ-464). None of
these move ε — clipping and noise still apply to whatever gradient is produced — so the damage is
silent infidelity to the loaded architecture, a different trained objective, or a hard outage.

### 5.1 Fused cross-entropy and fused DFT loss

**OPQ-460 — the fused-CE forward wrapper's non-fused fallback branch recomputes logits without the family's post-`lm_head` transform, so Cohere/Cohere2/Granite/Gemma2 train on a different objective.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/transformers/components/cross_entropy.py:282-292, 356-364` · silent-training-corruption · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges two independently reported instances: "Default-on fused-CE forward wrapper silently drops Cohere logit_scale / Granite logits_scaling / Gemma2 final_logit_softcapping on its non-fused branch", "The fused-CE wrapper's non-fused fallback branch recomputes logits without family logit scaling".)*
- **Claim:** `c0f95ced` (#988) changed the gate at `_factory.py:374-376` from `kwargs.get("fused_linear_cross_entropy", False)` to "inherit from `performance`", and `_dp_trainer.py:844-850` always calls `apply_model_patches(..., performance=True)`, so the fused causal-LM forward wrapper is now installed on all seventeen families in `_FUSED_CE_CAUSAL_LM` (`cross_entropy.py:122-140`) by default. The same commit made the DP training loss set the marker (`_dp_trainer.py:2437-2442` adds `loss_only=True`). The wrapper's fused branch resolves and threads the family transform into the kernels (`cross_entropy.py:308-323, 336`), but when `use_fused_ce` (`:282-292`) is False the wrapper does **not** delegate to `original(...)` — it re-implements stock Llama's tail, `logits = self.lm_head(hidden_states[:, slice_indices, :])` followed by `self.loss_function(logits, labels, self.vocab_size, **kwargs)` (`:356-364`), with no `logit_scale`, no `logits_scaling`, and no `final_logit_softcapping`. In transformers 5.15.1 those transforms live in `ForCausalLM.forward`, not in `loss_function` (`cohere/modeling_cohere.py:503`, `cohere2:481`, `granite/modeling_granite.py:497`, `gemma2:527-530`), so the fallback silently scores a different objective.
- **Failure scenario:** DP-SFT of a Cohere / Cohere2 / Granite / Gemma2 checkpoint loaded in **float32 on CUDA** with the DPTrainer default `bf16=False` (`_training_arguments.py:415`; `_setup_precision` leaves `_amp_dtype=None` and never casts the model, `_dp_trainer.py:904-905`). `use_fused_ce` requires half-precision hidden states on CUDA, so fp32 hidden states take the else branch on every step and every per-example loss *and its gradient* are computed from untransformed logits. No exception, no warning; clipping and noise still apply, so the run looks healthy. Measured on CPU by forcing the identical branch with tiny models: the fused branch matches unpatched HF to 0.0000 % for every family, while the fallback gives Cohere +0.27 % at random init, +53.0 % with `lm_head` scaled 10×, and +602 % at 50×; Granite −0.36 % / +10.0 % / +215 %. The error grows with logit magnitude, so a trained checkpoint is badly wrong rather than marginally. A second, device-independent route exists: a caller (for example a padding-free collator) passing `shift_labels` of shape `[B,T]` reproduces Cohere +53.0 % on CPU. A separate forced-branch run measured max per-logit differences of 0.665 (Cohere, `logit_scale=0.0625`) and 0.344 (Granite, `logits_scaling=8.0`), with Llama and un-softcapped families bit-identical.
- **Evidence:** `cross_entropy.py:356-364` (the un-transformed re-implementation); `:282-292` (the CUDA + fp32 decline); `:151-172` (`_fused_linear_ce_loss_is_supported` also declines for `shift_labels`, class `weight` and tensor `ignore_index`, on any device); `:308-323, 336` (the fused branch does apply the transform); `:204-220` (the wrapper *does* delegate to `original` when `labels is None or not loss_only`, which is why ordinary inference is unaffected); `_factory.py:374-376` plus `git show c0f95ced` for the old `False` default; `_dp_trainer.py:844-850, 866-875, 2437-2442`; `_training_arguments.py:415`; transformers 5.15.1 `cohere:503`, `cohere2:481`, `granite:497`, `gemma2:527-530`; config defaults `CohereConfig.logit_scale=0.0625` and `Gemma2Config.final_logit_softcapping=30.0`.
- **Fix:** make the fallback delegate to `original(self, ...)` — it already has every argument it needs, and the wrapper already does exactly that at `:204-220` and for MoE router logits at `:225-244`. Failing that, hoist the `logit_scale` / `logits_scaling` / `final_logit_softcapping` resolution above `if use_fused_ce:` and apply it to `logits` before calling `self.loss_function`. Lock it in by extending `test_fused_ce_text_family_parity.py` to Cohere, Cohere2, Granite and Gemma2 over **both** branches: its fixtures are currently olmo2/olmo3/smollm3/ministral/glm4 (none of which has logit scaling) and its scaling tests install a `loss_function` that raises `AssertionError("fallback should not run")`, pinning only the fused path.
- **Notes:** not reachable on CPU or MPS, where the chunked fused branch always wins, and not reachable with the transformers 5 `from_pretrained` default `dtype="auto"`, since Cohere/Granite/Gemma2 checkpoints are bf16 and route to the correct fused branch. The trigger is fp32-on-CUDA, a documented first-class mode (`docs/user-guide/precision.md` "float32 training", `examples/train_dpftrl.py --dtype float32`) rather than the most literal default — which is why the first report's "critical" was reduced to high. One sub-claim was cut: the returned `output.logits` are *not* corrupted for any consumer, because the fallback only runs on calls that set `loss_only=True` and every in-repo caller sets that marker exactly when discarding logits. Gemma3 is largely spared in practice (`Gemma3TextConfig` defaults softcapping to `None`), and stock `GraniteConfig` defaults `logits_scaling=1.0`, so Granite bites only on real checkpoints that set it above 1.

**OPQ-461 — SFT loss-only DFT evaluation scores an unscaled-logit objective, so the same checkpoint reports two different `eval_loss` values depending only on whether `compute_metrics` is set.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trl/_sft_trainer.py:230-236, 596-605, 666-670`; root cause `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/sft/loss/_dft.py:141-147` and `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/_fused_lce.py:107-122` · correctness/objective-divergence · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `7edd41be` (#929) added `_fused_dft_loss_only = args.loss_type == "dft" and self._lm_head_param_name is not None` (`_sft_trainer.py:233-235`), deliberately dropping the `_fused_loss_eligible` gate, and widened the dispatch to `elif self._fused_dft or (not return_logits and self._fused_dft_loss_only):` (`:596`). Because `SFTConfig.log_completion_metrics` defaults to `True` (`_sft_config.py:90`), `_fused_loss_eligible` — and therefore `_fused_dft` — is False at defaults, so **training** keeps the eager `dft_loss(out.logits, labels)` path at `:606-615`, scoring HF's already-scaled logits. But `prediction_step` requests the no-logits closure whenever `prediction_loss_only` is set (`:666-670`), and `DPTrainer.evaluate()` sets `prediction_loss_only=True` whenever `compute_metrics is None` (`_dp_trainer.py:3187`). `fused_dft_loss` has no scale argument at all: its CUDA/chunked branch calls `linear_nll_sum(...)`, which pins `logit_scale=1.0` (`_fused_lce.py:113-114, 120-122`), and its eager fallback is `dft_loss(hidden_states @ lm_head_weight.transpose(-2, -1), labels)` (`_dft.py:147`). Neither applies `config.logit_scale` nor `config.logits_scaling` — the exact sibling that the `c0f95ced` logit-scale work fixed for the model-level fused CE and did not fix for the DFT primitive.
- **Failure scenario:** `SFTTrainer(model=<Cohere | Cohere2 | Granite with logits_scaling != 1>, args=SFTConfig(loss_type="dft", ...))`. Whenever evaluation runs without `compute_metrics`, `eval_loss` measures DFT against the raw `hidden @ lm_head.T` projection. Measured on this tree with the repo's own DFT trainer fixture and a tiny Cohere: `eval_loss = 0.060834` without `compute_metrics` versus `0.064736` with it — and `0.064736` is also the training objective, so the divergence is 6.0 %. `metric_for_best_model="eval_loss"` and early stopping therefore select on the wrong objective. Primitive-level per-example measurements: Llama 0.000 %, Cohere (`logit_scale=0.0625`) 0.610 %, Granite (`logits_scaling=8.0`) 2.085 %, Granite (1.0) 0.000 %. Llama is unaffected, which is exactly why the existing consistency test passes. Same root cause, worse consequence: with `log_completion_metrics=False`, `_fused_dft` becomes True (verified by direct construction) and the unscaled DFT loss becomes the **training** objective for these families, silently.
- **Evidence:** `_sft_trainer.py:216-220` (`_fused_loss_eligible` requires `not self._log_completion_metrics`) against `_sft_config.py:90` (`log_completion_metrics: bool = True`); `_sft_trainer.py:233-235, 596, 604, 606-615, 666-670`; `_dp_trainer.py:3187`; `_dft.py:141-147`; `_fused_lce.py:113-114`; contrast `cross_entropy.py:308-320, 336`, where the model-level path does thread the scale; transformers 5.15.1 Cohere `logits = logits * self.logit_scale` and Granite `logits = logits / self.config.logits_scaling`; `test_trl_trainers.py:2030-2037` is `test_sft_dft_eval_loss_consistent_across_metric_modes`, the test that exists to catch precisely this and passes only because `_dft_trainer` uses `_tiny_model()` = `LlamaForCausalLM` (`:41-51`); grep confirms no logit-scale plumbing exists anywhere in `opaque-alignment`.
- **Fix:** thread the family scale into the DFT primitive the way `c0f95ced` did for the model-level fused CE — give `linear_nll_sum` a `logit_scale` parameter (it already forwards positionally into kernels that accept one) and give `fused_dft_loss` `logit_scale` / `logit_softcapping` arguments applied in both the fused and the eager `hidden @ W.T` fallback, with `SFTTrainer.compute_per_example_loss` reading them off `self._model.config`. Until that lands, gate `_fused_dft` and `_fused_dft_loss_only` on the model having no logit scaling so a scaled family keeps the eager logits path. Re-parametrize `test_sft_dft_eval_loss_consistent_across_metric_modes` over a logit-scaled family, not just Llama.
- **Notes:** needs the opt-in `loss_type="dft"` (`SFTConfig` defaults to `"nll"`, `_sft_config.py:80`) plus a logit-scaled family; no privacy guarantee is involved. Distinct from open issues #905/#906 (wandb metric naming) and #862 (fp32 logit upcasting).

**OPQ-462 — `torch_compile=True` aborts DP training on the first step for every registered dense HF causal-LM family, because the now-default fused loss-only CE forward is not Dynamo-traceable under `fullgraph=True`.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/transformers/components/cross_entropy.py:257-259`; `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/kernels/_linear_ce_chunked.py:88-101, 120`; `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dp_trainer.py:209-218` · regression/feature-outage · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** two independent Dynamo blockers sit behind the same default-on wrapper. First, the fused branch executes `return_dict = return_dict if return_dict is not None else self.config.use_return_dict` (`cross_entropy.py:257-259`), and in the pinned transformers 5.15.1 `PretrainedConfig.use_return_dict` is a deprecation property whose body is a `logger.warning_once(...)` call (`configuration_utils.py:210-212`). Dynamo refuses to trace a logger call, and `fae93a55` (#979) / `afa0247f` (#989) made the chunk compiler strict with no fallback: `_compile_strict_chunk` is `torch.compile(fn, backend=..., mode=..., fullgraph=True, dynamic=True)` (`_dp_trainer.py:209-218`), applied at `_clipped_fun.py:723-730` with no `try/except`, and `_synchronize_grad_failure` special-cases only `torch.OutOfMemoryError` (`_dp_trainer.py:1310-1318`). That line sits *before* the CUDA/CPU kernel branch, so the first blocker is device-independent. Second, once the property is neutralized, the portable chunked kernel's tile planner calls the pybind builtin `torch._C._functorch.is_gradtrackingtensor` (`_linear_ce_chunked.py:96`, reached from `_tile_plan` at `:120`, added by `0a002a50` #990), which Dynamo also cannot trace.
- **Failure scenario:** verified by live repro on this tree (CPU, torch 2.14.0, transformers 5.15.1): `DPTrainer(model=LlamaForCausalLM(tiny cfg), args=TrainingArguments(torch_compile=True, torch_compile_backend="aot_eager", use_cpu=True, per_device_train_batch_size=3, max_steps=3)).train()` reaches step 1 and raises `torch._dynamo.exc.Unsupported: logging.Logger method not supported for non-export cases`, developer context naming the `transformers.configuration_utils` logger and the `` `use_return_dict` is deprecated! `` message; the run never trains. `Qwen2ForCausalLM` fails identically. A/B isolation, all executed: the same Llama run with `performance_kernels_config={"fused_linear_cross_entropy": False}` completes 3/3 steps and returns a `TrainOutput`, and `Phi3ForCausalLM` (absent from `_FUSED_CE_CAUSAL_LM`) completes as well. Monkeypatching `use_return_dict` into a plain attribute read exposes the second blocker, `torch._dynamo.exc.Unsupported` on `torch._C._functorch...is_gradtrackingtensor`.
- **Evidence:** `cross_entropy.py:257-259` against `transformers/configuration_utils.py:210-212`; `_dp_trainer.py:209-219` (`fullgraph=True`, and grep for a fallback wrapper returns nothing), `:844-849` (`performance=True` is a literal, not `use_performance_kernels`), `:868-874` and `:2437-2442` (the marker), `:1310-1318`; `_factory.py:374-376`; `_clipped_fun.py:723-730`; `_linear_ce_chunked.py:96, 120`; `_recommended_compile_backend` returns `inductor` for cpu/cuda/mps, so the flag is live on CPU too; `docs/reference/transformers.md:218` and `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:216` both document `torch_compile` and flag only `gradient_checkpointing` as incompatible; `test_compile_and_kernel_args.py:46, 56-67` compiles only an `nn.Linear` and a hand-written non-HF `_TinyLM`.
- **Fix:** stop reading the deprecated property — transformers 5.x dropped `return_dict` from every causal-LM signature, so resolve it as `getattr(self.config, "return_dict", True)` or drop the tuple-return branch and always build the `ModelOutput`. Then make `_vmap_batch_factor` Dynamo-safe (wrap the functorch probe in `torch._dynamo.disable`, or compute the tile plan outside the traced region and pass token/vocab tiles in as ints). The test that would have caught both blockers is one that actually runs a single compiled DP step against a registered HF family at default patch settings; add it. Separately, consider having `_compile_strict_chunk` fail with a message naming the offending patch bucket instead of surfacing a raw Dynamo error mid-step.
- **Notes:** the failure is loud on step 1 and needs the opt-in `torch_compile=True`, which is why the verifier kept this at high rather than critical. No ε or numerical consequence — but it is a total outage of a documented feature across every supported dense family, with no fallback and no error message pointing at the cause. Not covered by the perf-audit issues #956–#970, which are about kernel throughput.

**OPQ-463 — the multi-output `apply` override added by #987 breaks bare `torch.func.grad` for both linear-CE kernels, disabling `opaque.auditing.gradient_scores`.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/kernels/_linear_ce_chunked.py:278-281, 330`; same pattern at `kernels/linear_cross_entropy.py:1005-1008, 1061` · regression · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `a839782e` (#987, "reuse fused cross-entropy statistics") made both linear-CE `autograd.Function`s return three/four outputs and added `@classmethod def apply(cls, *args): return super().apply(*args)[0]` to hide the extras. Under a bare functorch `Grad` transform, `torch/_functorch/autograd_function.py:124-150` builds a `Generated` function whose forward re-enters `custom_function_call` one level down; with no vmap level left it lands on the overridden `apply` and receives a single tensor, so `Generated.setup_context` hands that tensor to `_ChunkedLinearCE.setup_context`, where `_, lse, token_weight = output` (`:330`) cannot unpack it. `vmap(grad(...))` survives only because the intermediate vmap level intercepts the re-entry, and plain `.backward()` survives because it never re-enters `apply`. The Triton twin carries the identical override and a four-way unpack.
- **Failure scenario:** on the current tree any bare `torch.func.grad` over the fused linear cross-entropy raises instead of returning a gradient. Reproduced on CPU with the repo venv: `grad(lambda h: linear_cross_entropy_chunked(h, w, labels))(h)` → `ValueError: too many values to unpack (expected 3)`, and `grad(lambda h: fused_nll_loss(h, w, labels))(h)` → the same, while `vmap(grad(...))` and `loss.backward()` both work. The same probe against `git show a839782e^:.../_linear_ce_chunked.py` gives `OLD grad(0) OK`, so this is a regression introduced by the perf commit. The concrete blast radius is `opaque.auditing.gradient_scores`, whose only differentiation path is `grad_fn = torch.func.grad(loss_fn)` with no enclosing vmap (`attacks/_gradient.py:144`, called per example at `:159`): a gradient-norm privacy audit against any model or loss using the fused LM-head CE cannot run at all. Instrumenting `setup_context` shows it called twice, once with the real 3-tuple and once with a bare tensor.
- **Evidence:** `_linear_ce_chunked.py:277-281` (the override), `:330` (the unpack); `torch/_functorch/autograd_function.py:124-150`; `_fused_lce.py:40-62` (`fused_nll_loss` routes to the chunked kernel on CPU whenever `opaque-patches` is importable); `auditing/attacks/_gradient.py:144, 159`; `linear_cross_entropy.py:1005-1008, 1061` (the Triton twin); `tests/kernels/test_linear_ce_chunked.py` and `test_linear_cross_entropy.py` use only `vmap(grad(...))` and `torch.autograd.grad`, which is why the regression landed green.
- **Fix:** stop overriding `apply`. Keep the `Function` single-output and pass `lse` / `valids` / `token_weight` to the backward through `ctx` fields computed in `forward` (they are all `mark_non_differentiable` anyway), or wrap the tuple-returning `Function` in a plain Python function that does the `[0]`. A tolerant `setup_context` is not enough, because the aux tensors are genuinely missing at that level. Add a bare-`grad` test to both kernel test modules and to `opaque-auditing`.
- **Notes:** the failure is always loud — never a silently wrong gradient. One correction from verification: it is not always `ValueError`; when the per-token loss vector happens to hold exactly three elements (sequence length 4), iterating the 1-D tensor satisfies the three-way unpack with garbage and the failure moves to `TypeError: _ChunkedLinearCE.backward() missing 2 required positional arguments`. The DP-SGD training path (`clipped_grad` → `vmap(grad)`) and ordinary `.backward()` are unaffected. The Triton CUDA twin is expected to break the same way; that one link is read from code, not executed (no CUDA host).

**OPQ-464 — `_can_use_native_gqa`'s functorch guard never fires on the DP-SGD transform, because `is_batchedtensor` is False under `vmap(grad(...))`.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/transformers/components/attention.py:116-131 (guard at 124)` · dead-guard · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `b9fb8674` (#985) added `if query.device.type != "cuda" or torch._C._functorch.is_batchedtensor(query): return False` to keep the native `enable_gqa=True` SDPA path (and its `torch.backends.cuda.SDPAParams` probe) away from functorch-wrapped tensors. Under `vmap(grad(f))` — the transform `clipped_grad` / `clipped_fun` actually uses, since `_clipped_grad.py` calls `grad_and_value` wrapped by `_vmap` in `_clipped_fun.py` — the tensor reaching the attention forward is a `GradTrackingTensor` wrapping a `BatchedTensor`, and `is_batchedtensor` inspects only the outermost wrapper. Verified on this torch build: plain `vmap` gives `is_batchedtensor=True` / `is_gradtrackingtensor=False`, while `vmap(grad(...))` and bare `grad` both give False / True. The correct two-level unwrap idiom already exists in the same package at `_linear_ce_chunked.py:88-103`.
- **Failure scenario:** on CUDA, a DP-SGD step under `vmap(grad(...))` reaches `_can_use_native_gqa` with a `GradTrackingTensor`, the guard does not fire, and the native `enable_gqa=True` branch runs inside the transform instead of `_grouped_sdpa`. The established consequence is branch selection and nothing more. The guard is masked on CPU by the `device.type != "cuda"` clause, which is why no test catches it.
- **Evidence:** `attention.py:124` (the single-level check); the three-transform probe above; `_clipped_grad.py:283` (`grad_and_value`) wrapped by `_clipped_fun.py:595, 682`; `_linear_ce_chunked.py:88-103` (the correct idiom); `tests/transformers/components/test_attention.py:192-201` is the only eligibility test and uses plain CUDA tensors, so nothing asserts the negative case.
- **Fix:** test both predicates — `f = torch._C._functorch; if f.is_batchedtensor(query) or f.is_gradtrackingtensor(query): return False` — or reuse the `_vmap_batch_factor` unwrap loop. Lock it in with a CPU-runnable unit test that monkeypatches the device check and asserts `_can_use_native_gqa` returns False inside `vmap(grad(...))`.
- **Notes:** the verifier cut the originally claimed consequences substantially. "`SDPAParams` reads wrapper geometry / wrong shapes" is **refuted**: constructing `SDPAParams` inside `vmap(grad(...))` succeeds and reports the correct logical per-example shape. "Numerics could be wrong" is **refuted**: `F.scaled_dot_product_attention(..., enable_gqa=True)` under `vmap(grad(...))` agrees bit-for-bit with the `repeat_interleave` reference for dQ/dK/dV (maxdiff 0.0 in float64); it merely emits functorch's "no batching rule" performance-fallback warning. "Defeats #985's purpose" is unmeasured and plausibly backwards, since `_grouped_sdpa` issues its own SDPA calls on the same wrapped tensors and takes the same fallback once per KV head. What remains is a guard that silently does nothing on the DP transform — code hygiene and perf intent, not correctness. The perf direction was not measured (no CUDA host).

### 5.2 Fused LoRA QKV and MLP wrappers

**OPQ-465 — the generic fused LoRA QKV wrapper applies RoPE on Cohere2's NoPE full-attention layers, so 25 % of layers compute a different architecture than the one loaded.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/components/qkv.py:25, 43-44, 127-133` · numerical-correctness · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** Cohere2 alternates sliding-window layers (RoPE) with full-attention layers that are deliberately NoPE: transformers 5.15.1 `modeling_cohere2.py:196` sets `self.sliding_window = config.sliding_window if config.layer_types[layer_idx] == "sliding_attention" else None`, and `:226-228` applies rotary only `if self.sliding_window is not None`. `"Cohere2Attention"` sits in `_FUSEABLE_QKV_ATTENTION_CLASSES` (`qkv.py:25`), `_resolve_fused_qkv_forward_factory` routes it to the generic `_make_fused_qkv_attention_forward` (`:43-44`), and that wrapper calls `apply_rotary_pos_emb(query_states, key_states, cos, sin)` unconditionally at `:127-133`. The four delta commits that extended fusion built dedicated wrappers for the two families whose pipeline differs (Gemma3, Qwen3) and left Cohere2 on the generic one.
- **Failure scenario:** load a Cohere2 model (default config: `layer_types` repeat three `sliding_attention` plus one `full_attention`, so 10 of 40 layers are NoPE), wrap it with a `LoraConfig` targeting `q_proj`/`k_proj`/`v_proj`, call `opaque.patches.apply_model_patches(model)` — PEFT fusion is on by default — and train on CUDA. Every full-attention layer then computes attention on RoPE-rotated Q/K although the architecture defines it as NoPE: forward, loss and per-sample gradients are wrong for 25 % of layers, with no warning and no raise. Verified device-independently by forcing the CUDA branch on a 4-layer Cohere2 with `layer_types = ['sliding_attention','full_attention','sliding_attention','full_attention']`: both sliding layers match upstream exactly (maxdiff 0.0) while both full-attention layers diverge — 1.9e-04 (1.24 % relative) and 1.3e-04 (0.84 %) on randomly initialised weights.
- **Evidence:** `qkv.py:25, 43-44, 127-133` (no `sliding_window` guard on the rotary call); transformers 5.15.1 `modeling_cohere2.py:196, 226-228`; `apply_model_patches` has `peft: bool = True` (`patches/__init__.py:25`); `_auto_fuse_lora` installs the wrapper whenever q/k/v all carry LoRA with frozen bias and no `lora_dropout` (`peft/_router.py:74-97`, and PEFT's default `lora_dropout` is 0.0); the wrapper delegates to the original forward only when `not hidden_states.is_cuda` (`qkv.py:106`), so the bug fires exactly on the GPU training path; `patches/transformers/models/cohere2.py` installs only MLP/causal-LM patches and never replaces `Cohere2Attention.forward`, so upstream's guard is live right up until the PEFT wrapper displaces it; `Cohere2Config(num_hidden_layers=1).layer_types == ['sliding_attention']`, and the only Cohere2 fused-LoRA test (`tests/peft/test_fused_lora_parallel_residual.py:47-53, 127-131`) uses `num_hidden_layers=1` and compares vmap against eager of the *same fused* model, so no test ever instantiates the broken case.
- **Fix:** give Cohere2 a dedicated wrapper, as Gemma3 and Qwen3 got, that reproduces `if self.sliding_window is not None: apply_rotary_pos_emb(...)`; or make the generic wrapper conditional on the same predicate. The safest immediate action is to remove `"Cohere2Attention"` from `_FUSEABLE_QKV_ATTENTION_CLASSES` until a faithful wrapper exists. Lock it in with a device-independent pipeline test in the style of `_install_reference_fused_qkv` (`tests/peft/test_gemma3_fused_lora_qkv.py:126-139`) comparing the wrapper against upstream `attn(...)` for a Cohere2 config with at least four layers.
- **Notes:** reduced from critical to high — silent numerical divergence at default patch settings for a documented supported family, but it needs that family plus LoRA on all of q/k/v plus CUDA, and has no ε effect. CPU runs are unaffected because the wrapper delegates to the original forward for non-CUDA inputs.

**OPQ-466 — the generic fused LoRA QKV wrapper drops `sliding_window` and `softcap`, silently disabling Mistral/Ministral/Gemma2 windowed attention and Gemma2 logit softcapping.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/components/qkv.py:155-164` · numerical-correctness · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the generic wrapper calls `attention_interface(self, q, k, v, attention_mask, dropout=..., scaling=self.scaling, **kwargs)` and never forwards the family-specific arguments upstream passes: `sliding_window` (Mistral `modeling_mistral.py:172`, Qwen2 `:228`, Gemma2 `:281`, Cohere2) and `softcap=self.attn_logit_softcapping` (Gemma2 `:282`). This compounds with Opaque's own masking design: the Mistral/Ministral/Gemma2 family patches install `apply_compact_sdpa_sliding_window_masking_patch`, whose builder deliberately returns `None` on a no-padding SDPA prefill (`runtime/masking.py:132-143`) and delegates the look-back limit to the attention implementation via the `sliding_window` kwarg, which `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_sliding_window` only applies when that kwarg is not None (`components/attention.py:279-315`). With the kwarg dropped, the window is applied by neither the mask nor the kernel. The dedicated Gemma3 and Qwen3 wrappers added in this delta *do* pass `sliding_window` (`qkv_gemma3.py:83`, `qkv_qwen3.py:81`); the generic wrapper they were factored out of was never fixed.
- **Failure scenario:** a CUDA host with Triton, a PEFT model with LoRA on `q_proj`/`k_proj`/`v_proj`, and `apply_model_patches(model)` (`lora` defaults to True) so `_auto_fuse_lora` installs the generic wrapper. **(a)** Gemma2 + LoRA loses attention-logit softcapping in every fused layer, at every sequence length, in both eager and sdpa: verified bit-identical to running the same weights with `attn_logit_softcapping=None` (`base(softcap)` vs `nocap` differ by 6.5e-05 while `nocap` vs fused differ by exactly 0.000000). **(b)** Mistral / Ministral / Gemma2 lose the sliding window whenever the compact SDPA prefill path is taken — `attn_implementation="sdpa"`, `attention_mask=None`, no KV cache, `attention_dropout == 0`, query length greater than `config.sliding_window` (4096 for stock Mistral-7B and Gemma2): on a 1-layer Mistral with `sliding_window=4`, `seq_len=16`, "plain SW vs opaque unfused = 0.0" but "plain SW vs opaque FUSED = 0.3116" on a logit scale of 0.573, and "plain causal vs opaque FUSED = 0.0" exactly — the fused output is bit-identical to the same weights with `sliding_window=None`, so the window is entirely gone and every query attends to the whole prefix. Opaque's own unfused path is bit-exact with upstream in both cases, so the defect is confined to the fused wrapper.
- **Evidence:** `qkv.py:155-164`; upstream `modeling_mistral.py:172` (`sliding_window=getattr(self.config, "sliding_window", None),  # main diff with Llama`), `modeling_qwen2.py:228`, `modeling_gemma2.py:281-282`; `qkv_gemma3.py:83` and `qkv_qwen3.py:81` (the dedicated wrappers do pass it); `models/mistral.py:32-33` and `ministral.py`/`gemma2.py` install the compact masking patcher; `runtime/masking.py:132-143`; `components/attention.py:277-315`; `_lora_patching_allowed()` returns True whenever CUDA and Triton are present and `apply_peft_model_patches` defaults `lora=performance=True`, with sdpa the HF default; grep shows no test under `packages/opaque-patches/tests/peft/` mentions `sliding_window` or `softcap` and none exercises `_make_fused_qkv_attention_forward`; `docs/user-guide/huggingface/model-patches.md:25-28` calls the fused kernels "Drop-in, numerically equivalent replacements" and `:349-353` lists Mistral, Gemma, Gemma2, Granite, Cohere2 and Qwen2 as eligible through the generic wrapper.
- **Fix:** make the generic wrapper forward the same arguments the family's own forward does — build an `extra` dict carrying `sliding_window` from `getattr(self, "sliding_window", getattr(self.config, "sliding_window", None))` and `softcap` from `self.attn_logit_softcapping` when set, and pass it into the `attention_interface` call. Better: replace name-set routing with a per-family wrapper table, so adding a family forces an explicit decision about its extra kwargs. Lock it in with a device-independent parity test per eligible family using the `_install_reference_fused_qkv` pattern already proven for Gemma3.
- **Notes:** Qwen2 and Cohere2 under eager/sdpa are currently **unaffected**, because their family patches keep the default dense masking patcher, which materializes the band — so the Qwen2 addition in `41722a0b` (#947) is latent rather than live. The same kwarg drop should also void the window for `flash_attention_*` on those two families, since `_backend_enforces_sliding_window` (`runtime/masking.py:42-48`) deliberately leaves the band out of the mask for flash backends; that half could not be executed here (no CUDA/flash-attn) and remains unverified. Utility only, no ε effect.

**OPQ-467 — the fused QKV wrapper validates its preconditions only at patch time and reads only `active_adapters[0]`, so adapter switching silently drops dropout and extra adapters.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/_router.py:70-97`; `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/components/_utils.py:46-58` · correctness · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_auto_fuse_lora` evaluates `_has_lora` / `_bias_is_frozen` / `_no_lora_dropout` once, when the wrapper is installed, then sets `attn._opaque_lora_qkv_patched = True` and never re-checks. At runtime `_extract_lora_params` reads `lora_linear.active_adapters[0]` only. The sibling `Opaque_LoRA_W` path does both things the fused path omits: `linear.py:37-47` recomputes `dropout_is_noop` on every forward and returns `original(self, x, ...)` when dropout is live, and `linear.py:75-83` explicitly folds in `self.active_adapters[1:]`.
- **Failure scenario:** a CUDA host with Triton and a PEFT model patched by `apply_model_patches(..., lora=True)` whose adapter at patch time had `lora_dropout=0` (the gate requirement). The user then mutates the adapter set, which the fused wrapper never re-validates. **(a)** `add_adapter(name, LoraConfig(lora_dropout=0.9, ...))` + `set_adapter(name)` + `train()`: the module reports active `['noisy']` with `Dropout(p=0.9)`, yet the spread across four fused forwards is exactly 0.0 while the same four forwards through the unfused PEFT path spread by 0.911 — the fused Q/K/V use the new adapter's weights with none of its regularization, while `o_proj` and the MLP still apply dropout. **(b)** `set_adapter(['default','b'])`: the module reports both active, yet "peft(a) vs fused(a+b) = 0.0" exactly while "peft(a) vs peft(a+b) = 0.595" — adapter b's q/k/v contribution is entirely absent, so its q/k/v parameters receive no gradient, while `linear.py` correctly folds extra adapters into `o_proj`.
