- **Evidence:** `_router.py:70-97` (one-shot gates, then `attn.forward = types.MethodType(fused_qkv_fwd, attn)`); `components/_utils.py:46-58` (`active = lora_linear.active_adapters[0]`); contrast `components/linear.py:37-47` and `:75-83`; both repros above on a 1-layer Llama with `Tensor.is_cuda` forced; patch-time gating itself is correct and separately verified (`bias="all"` → not fused, `bias="lora_only"` → not fused, `lora_dropout=0.1` → not fused, partial `target_modules` → not fused, pinned by `test_gemma3_fused_lora_qkv.py:85-111`).
- **Fix:** re-check the preconditions per call inside `_opaque_fused_lora_qkv` and delegate to the unfused projections when they no longer hold — `if len(active_adapters) > 1 or dropout is not a no-op or any base bias requires grad: return self.q_proj(h), self.k_proj(h), self.v_proj(h)` — or mirror `linear.py` and add the extra-adapter deltas after the fused call. Lock it in with a test that switches adapters *after* patching and asserts the fused path either applies dropout and extra adapters or falls back.
- **Notes:** strictly about post-patch adapter mutation; it needs non-default PEFT multi-adapter usage and only bites on CUDA, since the non-CUDA wrapper falls back to the unmodified forward.

**OPQ-468 — the fused QKV path never got the fp32-adapter fallback that #977 added to the LoRA Linear and MLP paths, so q/k/v lose fp32 adapter precision while `o_proj` and the MLP keep it.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/components/qkv.py:55-65` · incomplete-fix · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `5b41925c` ("fix(patches): preserve fused LoRA mixed-precision behavior", #977) introduced `_active_adapters_use_dtype` / `_lora_projections_use_dtype` (`components/_utils.py:20-37`) and wired them into `linear.py:52-55` and `mlp.py:58-62`: when CUDA autocast is off and the active adapters are not already in the hidden-state dtype, the fused kernel is bypassed and PEFT's own forward runs so the adapter branch keeps fp32 precision. `git show --stat 5b41925c` confirms it touched `_utils.py`, `linear.py` and `mlp.py` — and not `qkv.py`. `_opaque_fused_lora_qkv` has no such guard: it unconditionally does `hidden_states.to(_active_lora_dtype(hidden_states))`, and `Opaque_LoRA_QKV.forward` then casts the fp32 adapters down via `cast_to_dtype(active_cuda_dtype(X), ...)` (`kernels/lora.py:512-514`), which with autocast off is `X.dtype`. `grep` confirms the two helpers are referenced only from `_utils.py`, `mlp.py` and `linear.py`.
- **Failure scenario:** a CUDA host with Triton, `model.to(torch.bfloat16)` followed by `get_peft_model(...)` so `lora_A`/`lora_B` stay fp32, LoRA on q/k/v (and optionally o/MLP), forward run **without** `torch.autocast`. Kernel-call tracing on a 1-layer Llama shows only `['Opaque_LoRA_QKV']` is invoked — `Opaque_LoRA_W` correctly declines for `o_proj`, which is precisely the intra-block inconsistency. Logits differ from the PEFT reference by about one bf16 ulp (2.1e-3 on a 0.51 scale) and the q/k/v adapter gradients by 0.5–0.9 % relative (`q_proj.lora_A` 4.6e-3, `q_proj.lora_B` 8.8e-3; the 3–4e-3 seen on `o_proj` is downstream contamination from the bf16 q/k/v activations).
- **Evidence:** `qkv.py:55-65` against `linear.py:52-55` and `mlp.py:58-62`; `kernels/lora.py:512-514`; the grep result above; `tests/peft/test_fused_lora_qkv.py:58` and `:123` both wrap their bodies in `with torch.autocast("cuda", ...)` — exactly the branch the guard skips — so the no-autocast QKV case is untested.
- **Fix:** add the same early-out `linear.py`/`mlp.py` use, keyed on `_lora_projections_use_dtype(self, ("q_proj","k_proj","v_proj"), dtype)`; because the fallback has to run inside the wrapper, it is cleanest either to move the check into `_auto_fuse_lora`'s runtime path or to have the wrapper delegate to `original_forward` when the dtypes disagree. Lock it in with a no-autocast bf16-model / fp32-adapter parity test for QKV.
- **Notes:** reduced from medium to low. The structural inconsistency with the deliberate #977 fix is real and confirmed, but the established magnitude is bf16-rounding level — loss of precision that #977 chose to preserve elsewhere, not a wrong result.

**OPQ-469 — the fused QKV wrapper closes over a *bound* `original_forward`, so a `deepcopy` of a patched model routes the copy's CPU/MPS fallback to the original model's attention.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/_router.py:89-96` · correctness · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_auto_fuse_lora` does `attn._opaque_fused_qkv = types.MethodType(_opaque_fused_lora_qkv, attn)`, then `fused_qkv_fwd = fused_forward_factory(attn.forward)` — capturing a *bound* method of that specific instance in the wrapper's closure — and installs the result as an instance attribute. `copy.deepcopy`'s `_deepcopy_method` rebinds `__self__` to the copy but treats `__func__` as atomic, so the closure cell holding the original bound `attn.forward` is shared. Every non-CUDA call on the copy (`qkv.py:109-117`, `qkv_gemma3.py:110-117`, `qkv_qwen3.py:108-115`) therefore executes the *original* model's attention with the original weights. The same bound capture exists for the MLP wrapper (`_router.py:135`); `linear.py` is safe because `_router.py` passes the unbound `type(module).forward`.
- **Failure scenario:** user code (not the Opaque library) does `copy.deepcopy` on a PEFT model already patched by `apply_model_patches(..., lora=True)` — a hand-rolled reference, EMA or eval snapshot — and then runs the copy on CPU or MPS, where the fallback is the only path. Verified on plain CPU: `clone.forward.__self__ is clone attn: True` but "closure cells bound to ORIGINAL attn: [True]"; after `attn.q_proj.lora_B["default"].weight.add_(5.0)` on the original only, the clone's logits move by 0.184 and become bit-identical to the original's (diff 0.0) — the clone's attention is fully aliased.
- **Evidence:** `_router.py:89-96`; `qkv.py:87-117` (the factory's forward closes over `original_forward` and calls it verbatim when `not hidden_states.is_cuda`); the CPU repro above; `_opaque_fused_qkv` itself is safe because `_opaque_fused_lora_qkv` is a module-level function, so deepcopy rebinds `__self__` correctly — only the captured `original_forward` is stale; `_router.py:135` for the MLP twin.
- **Fix:** capture the unbound function and re-bind at call time — `original_forward = type(attn).forward` in `_auto_fuse_lora`, with the wrappers calling `original_forward(self, hidden_states, ...)`. Lock it in with a deepcopy smoke test: copy a patched model, perturb the original's adapters, assert the copy's CPU output is unchanged.
- **Notes:** reduced from medium to low because the originally cited reachable call site was **refuted**. Opaque's only model `deepcopy` in `packages/*/src` is `_dpo_trainer.py:729` inside `_setup_tr_dpo`, and `_dpo_trainer.py:396-403` raises `ConfigurationError("sync_ref_model (TR-DPO) requires full fine-tuning, not PEFT")` whenever `_sync_ref_model and _is_peft`, while `_auto_fuse_lora` only installs the wrapper on PEFT LoRA modules — so that scenario cannot occur. On CUDA, where the fused kernels are meant to run, the fallback is never taken and the aliasing is invisible; it bites only on CPU/MPS, where `_lora_patching_allowed()` still installs the wrapper.

**OPQ-470 — no fused-vs-unfused parity test names the generic QKV wrapper on any device, and the Gemma3 device-independent pipeline pattern was not reused for Qwen3 or the generic path.** `packages/opaque-patches/tests/peft/test_fused_lora_qkv.py:26-51, 54` · test-coverage · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the delta's parity coverage is family-shaped rather than wrapper-shaped. `grep` over `packages/` shows `_make_fused_qkv_attention_forward` and `_fused_qkv_qwen3_attention_forward` are referenced only in `src` and by no test file; `_resolve_fused_qkv_forward_factory` appears in exactly one test. `test_fused_lora_qkv.py:26-51` is the only non-CUDA test and asserts only `hasattr(attn, "_opaque_fused_qkv")` plus `torch.isfinite(logits)`; line 54's `@pytest.mark.cuda class TestFusedLoRAQKV` gates every numeric test. Gemma3 got a genuine device-independent pipeline suite that stubs `_opaque_fused_qkv` with the unfused projections and compares the wrapper against upstream `attn(...)` on CPU (`test_gemma3_fused_lora_qkv.py:126-139` plus `TestGemma3FusedWrapperPipeline` at `:143`, four `allclose`-vs-upstream tests) and that pattern is reused nowhere else.
- **Failure scenario:** the generic wrapper serves eight families (Llama, Mistral, Gemma, Gemma2, Granite, Cohere2, Qwen2, Glm4) and its only end-to-end parity coverage is CUDA-only, on Glm4 (`test_glm4_lora.py:211`) and Qwen2 — the two families for which the wrapper's omission of `sliding_window`/`softcap` is a no-op (Qwen2 `use_sliding_window=False` → None; Glm4 never passes it). No test, on any device, compares the wrapper against upstream attention for Gemma2 (`sliding_window=4096`, `attn_logit_softcapping=50.0`), Mistral (4096) or Cohere2 (4096), so the divergences in OPQ-465 and OPQ-466 are invisible to CI, and any new entry added to `_FUSEABLE_QKV_ATTENTION_CLASSES` ships with no parity check.
- **Evidence:** the grep results above; `test_fused_lora_qkv.py:26, 54`; `test_glm4_lora.py:21, 43, 211`; `test_fused_lora_parallel_residual.py:19, 25, 29, 114` (Cohere2 tests use `num_hidden_layers=1` and compare vmap against eager of the *same fused* model, so they cannot detect wrapper-vs-upstream divergence); `test_gemma3_fused_lora_qkv.py:126-139, 143, 146, 165, 185, 216`; family-routing coverage itself was checked and is exact — a scan of all 478 importable `transformers.models.*.modeling_*` modules in transformers 5.15.1 found exactly the ten expected attention classes resolving through `_resolve_fused_qkv_forward_factory`, with no look-alike family mis-routed.
- **Fix:** parametrize the Gemma3 `_install_reference_fused_qkv` pipeline test over every entry in `_FUSEABLE_QKV_ATTENTION_CLASSES` plus the two dedicated sets, building each family's tiny config so the relevant feature actually binds (at least four layers for Cohere2, `seq_len > sliding_window` for Mistral/Gemma2) and asserting `allclose(wrapper_out, attn(...))` on CPU. That is device-independent, cheap, and would have failed on all three wrapper defects.
- **Notes:** reduced from medium to low — this is a test-gap meta-finding, and the concrete numerical harm belongs to OPQ-465/OPQ-466 and should not be counted twice. One correction: the title's "on any device" is overstated; `test_glm4_lora.py:211` is a genuine fused-vs-unfused logits parity test routed through the generic wrapper, just CUDA-only. Note also that a naive CPU parity test would pass *vacuously*, because the wrapper bails out to `original_forward` when `not hidden_states.is_cuda` (`qkv.py:110`) — which is exactly why the Gemma3 pattern calls the inner forward directly. Distinct from open #880 (restoring CUDA markers on existing LoRA kernel tests) and #968 (packing more projections per GEMM).

**OPQ-471 — `_auto_fuse_lora` skips MLP fusion for any layer whose attention is already QKV-patched.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/peft/_router.py:67-72` · control-flow · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the idempotency guard `if getattr(attn, "_opaque_lora_qkv_patched", False): continue` continues the *outer per-layer loop*, not just the QKV block, and the MLP-fusion half of the same `for layer in layers:` body begins at `:99`. Within one call this is harmless, because the flag is set at the end of the QKV block; it bites only on a second call.
- **Failure scenario:** performance only, and it requires two patch calls. Reproduced on a tiny 2-layer Llama: LoRA on q/k/v only → `apply_peft_model_patches` (QKV fused, MLP not eligible) → `model.add_adapter("mlpad", ...)` targeting gate/up/down → `apply_peft_model_patches` again leaves every MLP unfused (`mlp._opaque_lora_mlp_patched` absent, `forward` not in `vars(mlp)`) even though `gate_proj` now has `lora_A`. Counterfactual isolating the cause: deleting only the `_opaque_lora_qkv_patched` attribute before the second call makes the MLPs fuse. Numerical results stay correct, because the unfused PEFT path is the reference implementation; the fused-MLP speedup is silently absent with no log line, since the `logger.debug` at `:145` only fires when a count is nonzero.
- **Evidence:** `_router.py:67-72` (the `continue`) against the MLP block at `:99-137`; `apply_peft_model_patches` (`:151`) returns early only when `lora` is falsy or `_lora_patching_allowed()` is False, and otherwise always calls `_auto_fuse_lora(model)` (`:191`), so nothing makes this unreachable; `git log -L 60,75:.../peft/_router.py` shows the `continue` predates and survives both `41722a0b` (#947) and `8ced13cf` (#951), which rewrote the surrounding gate.
- **Fix:** replace `continue` with a guarded block — `if attn is not None and not getattr(attn, "_opaque_lora_qkv_patched", False): <qkv fusion>` — so control falls through to the MLP section. Lock it in with a two-call test that adds MLP adapters between the calls and asserts the MLPs end up fused.
- **Notes:** latent today, since no shipped Opaque flow calls the patcher twice (`DPTrainer` calls `apply_model_patches` once at `_dp_trainer.py:844`). Distinct from open #968.

### 5.3 Attention masking and patched-model parity

**OPQ-472 — the compact SDPA sliding-window elision reopens OPQ-337: the mask builder and the attention forward encode the same precondition twice and disagree, so the window is silently lost.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/transformers/components/attention.py:670-738 (fall-through at 729-738), 292-299`; `runtime/masking.py:78-107`; `models/gemma2.py:35` · patched-model-correctness · **high** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges two reports of the same fall-through: "Compact SDPA sliding-window elision reopens OPQ-337: gemma2 with softcapping disabled attends to the full context", "Gemma2 silently loses its sliding window under SDPA when `attn_logit_softcapping` is None".)*
- **Claim:** the perf work in `4e61b09d` (#925) and `d80d3f99` (#954) made mistral, ministral and gemma2 use `vmap_create_compact_sdpa_sliding_window_causal_mask`, which returns `None` — no mask at all — whenever `_can_use_compact_sdpa_sliding_window` holds (`runtime/masking.py:78-107`: sdpa, no padding mask, no cache, dropout-free-or-no-grad, `seq_len > sliding_window`), on the assumption that the family's patched SDPA forward applies the look-back limit itself. That assumption is encoded twice, in two independently written predicates, and they diverge. **(a)** `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2` (`attention.py:670-738`) consumes `sliding_window` only inside its `if softcap is not None:` branch (`:683`); when `Gemma2Attention` passes `softcap=self.attn_logit_softcapping=None` it falls through at `:729` to `return vmap_sdpa_attention_forward(module, query, key, value, attention_mask, dropout=dropout, scaling=scaling, **kwargs)` — and because `sliding_window` is a named parameter of the enclosing function it is not in `**kwargs` and is dropped entirely, while `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward` (`:169-241`) has no window handling at all. **(b)** the builder admits the elision when `not torch.is_grad_enabled() or config.attention_dropout == 0.0` (`masking.py:103-105`) while `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_sliding_window` requires the *runtime* `dropout == 0.0` (`attention.py:297`); in train mode under `no_grad` with a non-zero `attention_dropout` the builder elides and the forward declines, falling back to the same window-blind function. Nothing asserts that the forward actually applied what the builder skipped, so both paths fail open. Before `d80d3f99` gemma2 had no masking patcher and the dense mask always carried the window, so hole (a) is new in this delta.
- **Failure scenario:** both breaks need `attn_implementation="sdpa"` (the transformers default), `attention_mask=None`, no KV cache, and `seq_len > sliding_window`. **(a)** Gemma2 with `attn_logit_softcapping` explicitly set to `None`: every sliding-attention layer attends to the whole causal prefix. Measured on a tiny 2-layer Gemma2 (`sliding_window=4`, `seq=12`, `layer_types=['sliding_attention','full_attention']`), logits-sum on identical seeded weights: patched 11.014459269936197, bit-identical to the upstream no-window control (`sliding_window=64` → 11.014459269936197), against upstream −2.3921028781624045. With the default softcapping of 50.0 patched matches upstream to 1e-6. A second run on a 4-layer float64 Gemma2 gave max |logit diff| = 0.1065 against a mean |logit| of 0.081, versus 6.6e-08 at softcapping 50.0. Unit-level, `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2(..., softcap=None, sliding_window=4)` matches a plain full-causal reference to 8.9e-16 and deviates 2.83 from the explicit windowed reference, while `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_sliding_window` matches the windowed reference to 4.4e-16. **(b)** Mistral/Ministral with `attention_dropout != 0` evaluated in `.train()` under `torch.no_grad()`: patched 11.734835026116343, again equal to the no-window control, against upstream 11.663893043994904; with `attention_dropout=0.0` the pair agrees bit-for-bit. Both paths are silent — no warning, no error. An all-ones `attention_mask` or `attn_implementation="eager"` avoids both (verified bit-identical in each case).
- **Evidence:** `attention.py:683` (`if softcap is not None:`) and `:729-738` (the fall-through); `attention.py:169-241` (`vmap_sdpa_attention_forward` never reads a window); `runtime/masking.py:102-107` against `attention.py:297` (the two divergent dropout predicates); `runtime/masking.py:89-107` (`_can_use_compact_sdpa_sliding_window` never consults `attn_logit_softcapping`); `models/gemma2.py:30-36` pairs `sdpa_attention_replacement=vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2` with the compact patcher, while `mistral.py:32-33` and `ministral.py:32-33` pair the same patcher with `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_sliding_window`, which does honour the window (`attention.py:277-305`); `git show 4b13d82:.../components/attention.py:160-196` and `git log -S` on `models/gemma2.py` establish that before `d80d3f99` the gemma2 softcap-None branch delegated to stock `sdpa_attention_forward` with a dense mask; `tests/transformers/models/test_parity.py:57` (`_SLIDING_WINDOW_FAMILIES = {"ministral", "mistral"}`) and `:348` (`attn_logit_softcapping = 50.0` pinned), plus `tests/transformers/components/test_attention.py:316-346` which exercises the gemma2 forward only with `softcap=1.0`.
- **Fix:** make the elision and the application one predicate instead of two. Either have `vmap_create_compact_sdpa_sliding_window_causal_mask` receive the family's attention-side precondition (pass the concrete forward or a capability probe and require exactly the same conditions: runtime dropout, softcap disposition, position bias), or make the family SDPA forwards total for sliding windows — give `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2` a non-softcap branch routed through `_compact_sliding_window_sdpa` (equivalently, route the fall-through to `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_sliding_window(..., sliding_window=sliding_window, ...)`), and have `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_sliding_window` build the band mask itself via `_sdpa_sliding_window_mask` rather than fall back to the window-blind function. Add a fail-closed raise (or at minimum a debug assertion) when a compact-elided mask reaches a forward that will not apply the window. Lock it in by extending `test_parity.py::test_sliding_window_parity` to gemma2 with `attn_logit_softcapping` parametrized over `{50.0, None}`, and add a component test for `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2` with `softcap=None`.
- **Notes:** the two reports were verified independently and reached different severities for the gemma2 half — the second reviewer reduced it to medium on reachability grounds, noting that every released Gemma2 checkpoint ships `attn_logit_softcapping=50.0` (real checkpoints also have `sliding_window=4096`) and that Opaque's own trainers always pass an `attention_mask` (`_dp_trainer.py`, `trl/_sft_trainer.py:586, 610`, `trl/_dpo_trainer.py:1113, 1118`), which forces the dense correct path. The merged entry keeps **high**, because the first report additionally establishes path (b) on stock Mistral/Ministral and because the underlying two-predicate design fails open generally; the practical trigger for each individual path does need one non-default knob plus a direct `model(input_ids=...)` call. No ε effect. Not a duplicate of open #963, which is purely performance (chunk-size tuning, padded-batch support) with no correctness claim about either path.

**OPQ-473 — `or_mask_function` / `and_mask_function` are accepted and silently ignored by Opaque's mask builders, so a bidirectional Gemma3 trains as a causal one.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/transformers/runtime/masking.py:165-166, 186-315, 359-372, 388-389` · patched-model-correctness · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the same defect pattern as OPQ-337 — Opaque's mask builder silently discarding a constraint upstream applies — survives in a sibling path the #811 fix did not touch. `vmap_create_causal_mask` declares `or_mask_function=None, and_mask_function=None` at `masking.py:165-166` and no line in its body (`:186-315`) references either name. `vmap_create_sliding_window_causal_mask` forwards both down to it at `:388-389`, and its own compact/generic SDPA short-circuit at `:359-372` returns `_sdpa_sliding_window_mask(...)` or `None` without consulting `or_mask_function`, `and_mask_function` or `block_sequence_ids` — unlike `_can_use_compact_sdpa_sliding_window` (`:96-101`), which checks all three. transformers 5.15.1's `Gemma3TextModel` uses `or_mask_function` to implement bidirectional attention (`modeling_gemma3.py:547-549` sets a constant-True `or_mask_function` plus `_bidirectional_window_overlay` when `config.use_bidirectional_attention`) and blockwise/sliding overlays for packed inputs (`:748-752` passes `or_mask_function=blockwise_overlay(...)` and `and_mask_function=sliding_window_overlay(...)`). Gemma3 is a registered Opaque family. `block_sequence_ids` is dropped the same way, absorbed by `**kwargs`.
- **Failure scenario:** a Gemma3 text model configured with `use_bidirectional_attention=True` (the EmbeddingGemma-style setting) and patched via `apply_model_patches` silently runs plain causal — and causal-windowed — attention. Reproduced on a tiny 2-layer `Gemma3ForCausalLM` (`Gemma3TextConfig`, hidden 64, `layer_types=['sliding_attention','full_attention']`, `sliding_window=4`, `seq=12`, all-ones mask), logits-sum on identical seeded weights: upstream 4.5553 vs patched −3.7556 under eager and −1.4175 under sdpa; with the flag off both agree bit-for-bit (−2.5061). The same silent drop applies to the `block_sequence_ids`-driven blockwise/sliding overlays. No warning is emitted and no parity case sets the flag.
- **Evidence:** `masking.py:165-166` (declared) with a scoped grep over `:186-315` confirming neither name is read; `:388-389` (forwarded to the function that ignores them); `:359-372` (short-circuit does not check); `:96-101` (the predicate that does check, as the contrast); transformers 5.15.1 `modeling_gemma3.py:547-549, 748-752`; the eager and sdpa repros above; grep confirms no parity test sets `use_bidirectional_attention`.
- **Fix:** implement the two hooks in `vmap_create_causal_mask` the way transformers' `masking_utils` composes them — vectorized `mask_function`, OR with `or_mask_function`, AND with `and_mask_function` — using vmap-safe tensor ops, and make every short-circuit in `vmap_create_sliding_window_causal_mask` and `vmap_create_compact_sdpa_sliding_window_causal_mask` refuse to elide when either hook or `block_sequence_ids` is present. Until that lands, fail closed: raise `ConfigurationError` from the builders when a non-None `or_mask_function` / `and_mask_function` / `block_sequence_ids` arrives, so a bidirectional Gemma3 cannot silently train as a causal one. Lock it in with a Gemma3 parity case at `use_bidirectional_attention=True`.
- **Notes:** kept at medium on reachability — the flag is non-default, and the `block_sequence_ids` overlay belongs to the multimodal/packed path that Opaque's text-first support does not advertise. Privacy and accounting are unaffected; the harm is training or evaluating a different architecture than the config specifies.

**OPQ-474 — the fail-closed guard for unregistered HF families does not see PEFT-wrapped models, so DP-LoRA over an unregistered family skips the vmap-safety patches with only a warning.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/transformers/_router.py:50-56, 133-149` · fix-incomplete · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the #814 fix decides whether to fail closed with `isinstance(model, transformers.PreTrainedModel)` (`_router.py:50-56`), and the raise at `:133-142` fires only when that is True (with `compat`) or when `dropout`/`batchify` were passed explicitly True. `peft.PeftModel` inherits `torch.nn.Module` + `PushToHubMixin`, not `PreTrainedModel`, so a LoRA-wrapped model of an unregistered HF family takes the warn-and-return branch at `:144-149`. `detect_family` still resolves the family correctly through PeftModel's attribute forwarding, so the router knows it is looking at an HF architecture it cannot patch and proceeds anyway. Two vmap-safety steps are lost, not one: the `disable_dropout` block (`:164-168`) and the `PeftModel` batchify block (`:170-182`) both sit below the early `return` at `:149`. DP-LoRA on a Hugging Face causal LM is the primary documented Opaque workflow, so the escape hatch is wider than the one the fix intended for custom non-HF `nn.Module`s.
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced on transformers 5.15.1 / peft 0.20.0 with falcon, which `supported_families()` does not contain. A bare `FalconForCausalLM` raises `ConfigurationError: opaque: compatibility/dropout/batchify patches require a registered transformers family; got 'falcon' (FalconForCausalLM)`. But `peft.get_peft_model(FalconForCausalLM(cfg), LoraConfig(target_modules=["query_key_value"]))` gives `isinstance(pm, peft.PeftModel)=True`, `isinstance(pm, transformers.PreTrainedModel)=False`, `detect_family(pm)="falcon"`, and `apply_model_patches(pm)` raises nothing — only `WARNING opaque: no registered apply function for model family falcon; no model-level patches applied.` After the call the model still carries `('base_model.model.transformer.h.0.self_attention.attention_dropout', 0.3)` and `config.attention_dropout = config.hidden_dropout = 0.3`. The failure is not loud downstream either: `torch.vmap(torch.func.grad(...), randomness="same")` over `functional_call` on the unpatched falcon+LoRA model in `.train()` runs without error, so every example in the microbatch shares one dropout mask and the per-example gradients differ from both ordinary training and the dropout-free configuration the trainer documents.
- **Evidence:** `_router.py:50-56, 133-142, 149, 164-168, 170-182`; the falcon repro above; `_dp_trainer.py:844-850` passes `compat`/`performance`/`kernels` but not `dropout` or `batchify` explicitly, so the explicit-kwarg arm of the guard does not rescue it; `tests/transformers/test_public_factory.py:671-690` and `opaque-transformers`' `test_compile_and_kernel_args.py:74` use only bare `PreTrainedModel` subclasses — no test wraps an unregistered family in PEFT.
- **Fix:** make `_is_hf_pretrained_model` unwrap PEFT — treat the model as HF when it is a `PreTrainedModel`, or when `peft` is importable and `isinstance(model, peft.PeftModel)` and the unwrapped base (`get_base_model()` / `model.base_model.model`) is a `PreTrainedModel`. Equivalently, key the decision off `detect_family(model)` returning a non-None `model_type` absent from the registry, which already works through the wrapper. Lock it in with a test asserting that `get_peft_model` over an unregistered `PreTrainedModel` raises `ConfigurationError`.
- **Notes:** kept at medium — a WARNING is logged, the family must be unregistered, and clipping still bounds per-record sensitivity, so ε is untouched.

**OPQ-475 — the parity suite still compares patched against patched for 21 of 26 families, because sibling per-family test modules re-contaminate what #812 restores.** `packages/opaque-patches/tests/transformers/models/test_llama.py:33` and the fifteen sibling modules listed in the evidence; harness at `_test_utils.py:266-300` · test-quality · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-338's fix hardened `parity_model_patches` so the context it owns restores every module-level rebinding. It did not address the other producer of the same contamination: every per-family test module applies Opaque's model patches at test scope with no restore (`test_llama.py:33` calls `apply_model_patches(model, eager_attention=True)` inside the `tiny_model` fixture), and pytest collects those files before `test_parity.py` in the same process under default alphabetical order. `parity_model_patches` snapshots `_FAMILY_MODULE_PATCH_NAMES` + `ALL_ATTENTION_FUNCTIONS` + class forwards at context *entry* (`_test_utils.py:266-300`), so an already-patched modeling module is recorded as the "original" and restored to that state. `build_patched_model_pair`'s "unpatched" reference model therefore runs Opaque's own `eager_attention_forward`, `repeat_kv` and `create_causal_mask` / `create_sliding_window_causal_mask`.
- **Failure scenario:** running the patches model test directory in one process — the CI lane — leaves 21 of the 26 per-family modules collected before `test_parity.py`. Demonstrated by injecting a 5 % scaling error into `opaque.api.patches.transformers.components.attention._grouped_eager_attention` via a pytest plugin: `pytest test_parity.py -k eager-llama` alone reports 1 failed ("llama [eager] forward parity failed"), but `pytest test_llama.py test_parity.py -k "eager-llama or (llama and vmap)"` reports 4 passed; and the whole directory under the same injection reports 5 failed / 204 passed / 15 skipped, with failures only at `test_forward_logits_parity[eager-{phi3,qwen2,qwen3,qwen3_moe,smollm3}]` — exactly the families whose module sorts after `test_parity.py`. The consequence is lost regression detection in the parity suite, which means the parity evidence cited for the other patches fixes in this milestone is weaker than it looks.
- **Evidence:** `pytest --collect-only -q` over `packages/opaque-patches/tests/transformers/models` listing `test_attention_impls`, `test_cohere`, …, `test_olmo3`, then `test_parity.py` and `test_parity_harness.py`, then `test_phi3`, `test_qwen2`, `test_qwen3`, `test_qwen3_5_moe`, `test_qwen3_moe`, `test_qwen3_next`, `test_smollm3`; the per-family fixtures at `test_llama.py:33`, `test_cohere.py:33`, `test_cohere2.py:36`, `test_exaone4.py:36`, `test_gemma.py:33`, `test_gemma2.py:33`, `test_gemma3.py:43`, `test_glm4.py:33`, `test_gpt2.py:40`, `test_granite.py:36`, `test_mellum.py:74`, `test_ministral.py:36`, `test_mistral.py:36`, `test_olmo2.py:33`, `test_olmo3.py:33`, `test_attention_impls.py:38, 95`; `_test_utils.py:266-300` (entry-time snapshot); a setup-time probe confirming that for every `test_parity` llama case `modeling_llama.eager_attention_forward` is `vmap_eager_attention_forward`, `create_causal_mask` is `vmap_create_causal_mask` and `repeat_kv` is `vmap_repeat_kv`; `test_parity_harness.py::test_reference_output_survives_a_patch_cycle` cannot see this, since it compares a reference built before the cycle with one built after and both are equally contaminated, and it also runs after `test_parity.py`.
- **Fix:** reuse the isolation the fix already built — add a `conftest.py` under `tests/transformers/models` with an autouse fixture that snapshots and restores `_FAMILY_MODULE_PATCH_NAMES` + `ALL_ATTENTION_FUNCTIONS` + class forwards around every test (factor `parity_model_patches`' `finally` block into a reusable restore helper), or convert the per-family modules to use `parity_model_patches` / `build_runtime_patched_model_pair` inside a context. Then make the contamination detectable: assert in a harness test that `modeling_<family>.eager_attention_forward` and `create_causal_mask` are the stock objects at the start of each parity test, and keep the injected-error check as a meta-test.
- **Notes:** test-evidence quality only — no runtime or ε impact. Not a duplicate of an open issue: the contamination issue (OPQ-338 / #812) is closed, and open child #813 covers the `pytest.skip`/`xfail` exemptions and the gpt2 gradient mismatch, not reference contamination.

**OPQ-476 — binding sliding-window parity covers only mistral and ministral; five other windowed families are untested and the gap masks one live mismatch.** `packages/opaque-patches/tests/transformers/models/test_parity.py:57` · test-coverage · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_SLIDING_WINDOW_FAMILIES = {"ministral", "mistral"}` and `test_sliding_window_parity` (`:305-340`) is the only binding-window model-level parity case, parametrized over exactly those two. Enumerating the default config of all 26 `supported_families()` on transformers 5.15.1 gives a non-None `sliding_window` for cohere2 (4096), deepseek_v4 (128), exaone4 (4096), gemma2 (4096), gemma3 text (4096), gpt_oss (128), mellum (1024), ministral (4096), mistral (4096) and olmo3 (4096) — and OPQ-337's own failure scenario named Gemma2, Gemma3 text, Cohere2 and Phi-3 explicitly. `test_softcapping_parity` (`:342-…`) pins `attn_logit_softcapping=50.0` for gemma2/gemma3, so the `softcap=None` branch of `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2` has no end-to-end coverage either.
- **Failure scenario:** a sliding-window regression in the five other families whose default config carries a real window (gemma2, gemma3 text, cohere2, exaone4, olmo3) ships green. Establishing this concretely: running the existing `assert_parity_forward` harness with `sliding_window=4` / `seq_len=16` plus the `sliding_window=64` vacuity control over those families shows the window binds for all of them, and parity holds today in 9 of the 10 (family × `attention_mask`) cases — the single failure is gemma2 with the non-default `attn_logit_softcapping=None` and `attention_mask=None`, `abs_err = 5.30e-01`, caused by OPQ-472. So the gap is a real, cheaply closable coverage hole that today masks one non-default-config forward mismatch; it does not by itself cause a wrong result at defaults.
- **Evidence:** `test_parity.py:57`, `:305-340`, `:342`; the enumerated per-family default configs above; running the models parity suite on this tree gives 214 passed / 20 skipped *with* the gemma2 window drop present.
- **Fix:** derive `_SLIDING_WINDOW_FAMILIES` from the family's config rather than a hand-maintained literal — include any family whose default config exposes a non-None `sliding_window`, or whose `layer_types` can contain `"sliding_attention"` — keep the binding geometry and the vacuity guard, and parametrize gemma2/gemma3 over `attn_logit_softcapping` in `{50.0, None}`.
- **Notes:** two details from the original report are overstated. The honest count of missing *testable* families is five, not six: mellum's window is vacuous (all `layer_types` are `full_attention`), and gpt_oss and deepseek_v4 are already excluded by `_STRICT_FORWARD_PARITY_SKIP_FAMILIES`. And gemma2's binding window is not entirely untested — `tests/transformers/components/test_attention.py:316` exercises `vmap_sdpa_attention_forward_gemma2` with `sliding_window=3` against a dense reference, but only with `softcap=1.0`. The fix sketch's request to assert parity with `attention_mask=None` is already satisfied by the existing `use_attention_mask` parametrization. No tracked issue covers this (#813 is about parity skips and the gpt2 gradient mismatch; #963 is a perf item).

### 5.4 Also surfaced by the slate-regression re-check

These two came out of the same fix-verification sweep but belong to other subsystems; they are recorded here so they are not lost.

**OPQ-477 — the new random-allocation `lru_cache` retains Rust-backed PLD handles past `calibrate`'s cache teardown, with a docstring that denies it.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_random_allocation_cache.py:5-9, 24-27, 28-46` · resource-management · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** Slate #437 replaced every per-method `functools` PLD cache with a bounded registry whose native handles `calibrate` drops in its `finally` (`calibration.py:356-357`, `_clear_all_native_caches()`). Delta commit `20dd47aa` (#881) introduced a new `@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=8)` over `_native.random_allocation_gaussian_pld`. Its return type is `Pld`, and `_base.py:60` is `Pld: TypeAlias = _native.Pld` — a PyO3 Rust-backed object — so the module docstring's claim that the cache holds "immutable Python `Pld` objects (no native handles), so it is self-limiting and needs no byte budget or destructor" is false, as is the "~0.5 MB per entry at the default config" sizing comment. The cache is a bare `functools` LRU that never joins `_native_cache._registry` (the only non-test `NativeCache(` construction is the factory at `_native_cache.py:186`, used by the DP-FTRL b-min-sep transcript cache), so `_clear_all_native_caches()` cannot touch it; only `clear_random_allocation_caches()` / `epoch_pld.cache_clear()` empties it, and those are called from tests only.
- **Failure scenario:** a process that calls `calibrate` over a k-out-of-t or balls-in-bins process leaves up to eight Rust-backed PLD objects alive after the search finishes. Measured with the prebuilt extension: `cal.calibrate(cal.epsilon_budget(3.0, delta=1e-5), lambda nm: k_out_of_t(gaussian(nm), k=4, t=64, allocation="block"), 0.5, 4.0)` ends with `CacheInfo(hits=0, misses=22, maxsize=8, currsize=8)`, and `_clear_all_native_caches()` leaves `currsize` at 8 (an explicit `cache_clear()` drops it to 0). Retained memory is roughly 10–20 MB per entry: eight distinct handles took RSS from 18.1 to 189.1 MB with 78.8 MB returned on clear, and a warm-allocator run gave 97.0 MB for four entries with 76.2 MB returned — against a documented "~0.5 MB per entry". Note the cache yields **zero** hits during calibration (each probe has a distinct noise multiplier), so the retention buys nothing there.
- **Evidence:** `_random_allocation_cache.py:5-9` (the false docstring), `:24-27` (the 0.5 MB figure), `:28-46`; `_base.py:60`; `calibration.py:356-357` and `_native_cache.py:26, 40-42` (the registry iteration this cache never joins); consumers `_k_out_of_t.py:19, 114-119` and `dpftrl/amplification/_balls_in_bins.py:60`; the measurements above. The 0.5 MB figure corresponds to 2 × `max_conv_grid`(32768) × 8 B, but the retained PLD grid is bounded by `max_grid_size`, which defaults to 10,000,000.
- **Fix:** either register this cache as a `NativeCache` (like the b-min-sep one) so its bytes are budgeted and `calibrate`'s `finally` clears it, or drop the second cache layer entirely — `KOutOfT.pld` and `BallsInBins.pld` are already wrapped in `@pld_cache`, and the only value `epoch_pld` adds is sharing between two processes that differ solely in a field the PLD ignores. At minimum correct the docstring: the entries are native handles and measure ~10–20 MB each.
- **Notes:** bounded at eight entries, so this is a fixed ~100–200 MB resident footprint in a long-lived accounting service plus an incorrect docstring, not an unbounded leak. No effect on ε or on any computed number, and `calibrate`'s own docstring is narrowly worded ("Native LRU caches registered via … `_native_cache`"), so only the module docstring is untrue. Not a duplicate of open #911 (Monte Carlo calibration performance).

**OPQ-478 — Python 3.12 is advertised by every wheel and executed by no CI lane.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:56, 69, 117, 131` (and `pr.yml:96, 109, 165, 182`; `validate-distributions.yml:19`; `python-tests.yml:37`) · ci-coverage · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `grep -rn '3\.12' .github/workflows/` returns nothing, while every explicit interpreter pin is 3.11 or 3.13 (`ci.yml:56, 69, 117, 131`; `pr.yml:96, 109, 165, 182`; `build-distributions.yml:37, 129`; `prepare-release-implementation.yml:335, 353, 421, 440`; `validate-distributions.yml:19` matrixes exactly `["3.11", "3.13"]`). The reusable `python-tests.yml` defaults `python-version` to `"3.11"` (`:37`) and every caller either passes 3.11/3.13 or takes that default; the composite `.github/actions/setup-python` also defaults to `"3.11"`. Meanwhile `pyproject.toml:12` declares `requires-python = ">=3.11,<3.14"` and `:29` the `Programming Language :: Python :: 3.12` classifier, repeated per package (e.g. `packages/opaque-accounting/pyproject.toml:24`), so pip resolves and installs the wheels on 3.12 with no CI signal behind them.
- **Failure scenario:** a 3.12-only regression — a dependency resolving differently, or interpreter-specific behaviour in the `torch.func` / optree layer — ships unnoticed and is first seen by users. Bounded: 3.11 and 3.13 bracket most interpreter-sensitive behaviour, the native extension is a single `cp311-abi3` wheel shared across all three, and no privacy guarantee or computed result is affected.
- **Evidence:** the grep result and the workflow line numbers above; `pyproject.toml:12, 29` and the per-package classifiers.
- **Fix:** add one cheap 3.12 leg — the `validate-distributions` install scenario is the smallest — or drop the 3.12 classifier and narrow `requires-python` so endpoints-only testing becomes an explicit documented policy rather than the residue of the #665 version bump.
- **Notes:** a coverage / metadata-consistency gap, not a defect in the library; testing the supported endpoints and relying on abi3 for the native wheel is a defensible policy. This re-opens ground covered by a closed issue (Slate #428, Bazalt #841 / OPQ-386) rather than being a fresh defect, and neither tracker appears in the open-issue lists.
## 6. Public-release posture, packaging and CI

The repository is public (Apache-2.0, `visibility: public` per the GitHub API) and the
documentation site at `https://jetbrains-research.github.io/opaque/` is live, so every item
below is live exposure rather than backlog. Two are immediately actionable by anyone who
reads the README today: the documented extras install commands resolve to an unrelated PyPI
project, and six of the ten published distribution names are unregistered on PyPI.

### 6.1 Packaging, distribution and the PyPI namespace

**OPQ-480 — Every documented "Extras" install command omits the registry index and therefore installs an unrelated PyPI project named `opaque`.** `docs/getting-started/installation.md:33-36`, `README.md:78-81` · packaging/supply-chain · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges two reports of the same defect: "Published install instructions install an unrelated PyPI project instead of Opaque", "Public install documentation's 'Extras' commands omit the registry index and resolve to an unrelated PyPI project named `opaque`".)*
- **Claim:** Opaque is published only to `https://packages.jetbrains.team/pypi/p/fed/python/simple/`, and the first install block on each page carries that index — `installation.md:19-28` and `README.md:67-73` both show `pip install opaque --index-url https://packages.jetbrains.team/pypi/p/fed/python/simple/` and the `uv --index` equivalent. The "Extras" block immediately below drops the flag entirely: four bare commands, `pip install "opaque[auditing]"`, `"opaque[dpftrl]"`, `"opaque[transformers]"` and `"opaque[all]"` (`installation.md:33-36`, `README.md:78-81`). `--index-url` does not persist across shell invocations, so pip resolves the name `opaque` from PyPI — where it belongs to a different project: `opaque` 1.0.0, summary "python libopaque wrapper", author Stefan Marsiske, home page `https://github.com/stef/libopaque`. pip only *warns* about extras a distribution does not provide, so nothing stops the install. A grep for `packages.jetbrains.team` across `README.md` and `docs/` returns only those four correct lines; two further bare commands exist outside the audited blocks, `docs/user-guide/index.md:9` ("Install with `pip install opaque` (plus `opaque[...]` extras)") and `docs/alignment/trainers.md:319` (`pip install opaque[trl]`).
- **Failure scenario:** a reader of the live installation page or the README copies any of the four Extras commands. Reproduced verbatim twice in clean virtualenvs: `pip install --dry-run --no-deps "opaque[auditing]"` prints `Using cached opaque-1.0.0.tar.gz (10 kB)` then `Would install opaque-1.0.0`; `pip install --dry-run "opaque[all]"` emits only `WARNING: opaque 1.0.0 does not provide the extra 'all'` and resolves to `opaque-1.0.0 pysodium-0.7.18`. Worse, the extras form *replaces* a working install: with a real `opaque` 0.15.0 already present in the venv, `pip install "opaque[all]"` still reported `Would install opaque-1.0.0`, whereas the bare `pip install opaque` in the same state was a no-op (`Requirement already satisfied ... 0.15.0`). The user is left with a libopaque C-binding wrapper and no Opaque sub-wheel at all, so `import opaque.dpftrl` raises `ModuleNotFoundError`; a CI image built from these docs silently ends up with the wrong `opaque` distribution. The same hazard applies to `uv add`, since the second block never re-specifies `--index`. No privacy guarantee is affected — the failure is loud at import time.
- **Evidence:** `docs/getting-started/installation.md:19-28` (correct block) vs `:33-36` (bare extras); `README.md:67-73` vs `:78-81`; `https://pypi.org/pypi/opaque/json` → name `opaque`, version 1.0.0, summary "python libopaque wrapper", author Stefan Marsiske; the JetBrains simple index serves the real `opaque-0.15.0-py3-none-any.whl` anonymously; clean-venv dry-run reproductions with pip 24.0 as quoted above; live page `https://jetbrains-research.github.io/opaque/latest/getting-started/installation/` returns 200 and renders all four commands; `docs/user-guide/index.md:9`, `docs/alignment/trainers.md:319`.
- **Fix:** carry the index into every documented command — either append `--index-url https://packages.jetbrains.team/pypi/p/fed/python/simple/` to each extras line, or restructure the section to configure the index once persistently (`pip config set global.index-url ...` / `[[tool.uv.index]]`) and say explicitly that the setting applies to the whole page. Add one sentence warning that the `opaque` name on PyPI is an unrelated project. Fix the two stray bare commands in `docs/user-guide/index.md` and `docs/alignment/trainers.md` and the same block in `packages/opaque-accounting/README.md`. Lock it in with the ARC-011 post-build scenario the contract already describes: extract each tagged install command from the documentation, run it verbatim in a clean container against the generated artifacts, and assert `import opaque.dpsgd` succeeds.
- **Notes:** the wrong package is a benign third-party wrapper today rather than attacker-controlled, which is why the verifier held this at medium; the same missing-index pattern is, however, exactly the shape a dependency-confusion upload against OPQ-481's unregistered names would exploit. ARC-011 is marked **Planned** in `.junie/architecture-contracts.md`, so no existing check covers documented install commands.

**OPQ-481 — Defensive PyPI registration covers only four of the ten published distribution names; the other six are unregistered and claimable.** `README.md:34-45`, `pyproject.toml:83-94` · supply-chain/publishing · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** Opaque publishes every wheel to the JetBrains private-namespace index and never to PyPI (`ci.yml:236-241`, `release.yml:124-133`), which is the classic dependency-confusion setup. The team recognised this and on 2026-08-12 published JetBrains security-tooling stubs on PyPI at version 999.9.9 whose description reads verbatim "This public copy of the package is a stub published on pypi.org designed to mitigate risks from Dependency Confusion" — but only for `opaque-accounting` (uploaded 15:05:40Z), `opaque-auditing` (15:05:27Z), `opaque-optimizers` (15:05:34Z) and `opaque-dpftrl` (15:05:46Z). Re-querying PyPI for all eleven names returns HTTP 404 for `opaque-base`, `opaque-engine`, `opaque-dpsgd`, `opaque-patches`, `opaque-transformers` and `opaque-alignment`, all six of which *are* really published (each returns 200 on the JetBrains simple index) and all six of which the public `README.md` package table names verbatim. The umbrella name cannot be held at all: `opaque` on PyPI is already the unrelated libopaque wrapper.
- **Failure scenario:** any third party registers `opaque-base` or `opaque-engine` on PyPI with a malicious sdist. Attacker code then executes when (a) someone installs a sub-package by bare name — the form `AGENTS.md`'s installation matrix documents (`pip install opaque-dpsgd`, `pip install opaque-engine`, …), or (b) an environment adds PyPI as an extra index alongside the JetBrains one, since pip merges candidates across indexes and prefers the highest version so a 999.9.9-style release wins, or (c) uv is configured with `index-strategy = "unsafe-best-match"`. Because `opaque-base` and `opaque-engine` are transitive dependencies of every other wheel, one captured name reaches every Opaque install in such an environment — inside a DP training pipeline handling sensitive data. The primary documented path, `pip install opaque --index-url ...`, is *not* affected: `--index-url` never consults PyPI.
- **Evidence:** PyPI JSON API for all eleven names, re-queried during verification — four 999.9.9 stubs with the quoted description and the upload timestamps above, 404 for the six named, and `opaque` 1.0.0 as a third-party project; each of the six unregistered names returns 200 on `https://packages.jetbrains.team/pypi/p/fed/python/simple/<name>/`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:241` and `release.yml:132` publish only to that index; `pyproject.toml:83-94` `workspace.members`; `README.md:34-45` publishes the names; `AGENTS.md` installation matrix uses the bare per-package form; GitHub API `visibility: public`.
- **Fix:** ask JetBrains security-tooling (`security-tooling@jetbrains.com`, the address in the existing stubs) to publish the same 999.9.9 stub for `opaque-base`, `opaque-engine`, `opaque-dpsgd`, `opaque-patches`, `opaque-transformers` and `opaque-alignment`, and make that registration a checklist item attached to `pyproject.toml`'s `workspace.members` list so a new distribution cannot ship unregistered. For the umbrella name, which is taken, state in `README.md` and `docs/getting-started/installation.md` that `opaque` on PyPI is a different project and that the index flag is mandatory — never document a bare `pip install opaque*`.
- **Notes:** the verifier reduced this from high to medium because exploitation is not reachable from the primary documented install path and the JetBrains index is anonymously readable (verified), so users do not need `--extra-index-url`; the realistic vectors are a bare-name install or a user misconfiguration. It interacts directly with OPQ-480: the documented commands that drop the index are precisely the misconfiguration this gap needs.

**OPQ-482 — The per-package wheel build inventory in `build-distributions.yml` has no coverage guard, so a new package can ship untested and unbuilt.** `.github/workflows/build-distributions.yml:46-74` · ci-fail-open/packaging · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-380 named two hand-maintained inventories — the test-package matrix in `python-tests.yml` and the per-package build steps in `build-distributions.yml`. PR #870 guarded the first (`python-tests.yml:102-125` awks its own file and `comm`s it against `find packages -mindepth 2 -maxdepth 2 -type d -name tests`) and left the second untouched. `build-distributions.yml:44-74` still contains one `uv build . --wheel` for the umbrella plus nine literal `uv build packages/opaque-<name> --wheel --out-dir dist` steps (base, engine, optimizers, dpsgd, dpftrl, auditing, patches, transformers, alignment), with `opaque-accounting` built separately by maturin at `:130-163`, and nothing compares that list against `packages/*`. This is the higher-stakes inventory: a package missing from the test matrix runs no tests, while a package missing here is never built and never published. Neither downstream check closes it — `check_wheel_internal_pins.py:177-201` iterates `args.wheel_dir.glob("*.whl")`, i.e. only the wheels that exist, and `validate-distributions.yml` asserts only `len(opaque_versions) > 1` plus version equality.
- **Failure scenario:** a contributor adds `packages/opaque-<new>/` with a `tests/` tree and a pyproject. The #870 guard correctly fails the lane until `python-tests.yml` is updated, so tests get wired up; nothing forces the corresponding edit to `build-distributions.yml`. If the new package is not reachable from an `opaque[...]` extra — the common case for an optional stack — no wheel is ever built, `validate-distributions` still passes, and both the dev-wheel publish on `main` and the release publish ship a set that silently omits it. Users installing from the JetBrains index then get an `ImportError` for a package the repository documents. All ten current packages are covered, so there is no gap today.
- **Evidence:** `build-distributions.yml:44-74` (nine hardcoded steps plus the root) and `:130-163` (maturin job); `python-tests.yml:102-125` (the only inventory guard in the repository, scoped to the test matrix); `check_wheel_internal_pins.py:188-195`; `validate-distributions.yml:61-69`; `set_build_versions.sh` globs `packages/*/pyproject.toml` but only to rewrite version pins; grep over `.github` and `tests` finds no build-inventory guard; `ls packages/` returns exactly the ten distributions currently enumerated.
- **Fix:** replace the nine steps with a loop over `packages/*/pyproject.toml` (excluding `opaque-accounting`, which maturin builds), or add the same `comm`-based guard used in `python-tests.yml` as the first step of `build-python-wheels`. Stronger still: have `check_wheel_internal_pins.py` additionally assert that the set of distribution names found in `--wheel-dir` equals the set of `project.name` values across the root and `packages/*/pyproject.toml`, which makes the completeness check part of an existing behavioural gate.
- **Notes:** a partial mitigation exists and narrows the hole — `validate-distributions` installs `opaque[all]` with `--find-links dist --only-binary :all:`, and the root pyproject's extras (`pyproject.toml:33-59`) currently reach all ten distributions, so a new package wired into an `opaque` extra *would* fail resolution there. The exposure is confined to a new standalone package outside the umbrella bundle. Issue #835 (OPQ-380) is closed as completed; its acceptance criteria named only `python-tests.yml`, so this half was never in scope rather than regressed.

**OPQ-483 — Python 3.12 is advertised by eleven distribution classifiers with zero CI coverage and no recorded endpoints-only policy.** `.github/workflows/validate-distributions.yml:19` · ci-coverage/packaging · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** issue #841 (OPQ-386) was closed `not_planned` with no comment, and neither of its two acceptance branches was taken. `grep -rn '3\.12' .github/` returns nothing across all workflows, composite actions and scripts; the only Python versions anywhere under `.github` are `3.11` and `3.13` (`validate-distributions.yml:19` matrix `["3.11","3.13"]`; `python-tests.yml:34-38` input defaulting to `"3.11"`; `build-distributions.yml:37,129`; `ci.yml:56,69,117,131`; `pr.yml:96,109,165,182`; `prepare-release-implementation.yml:335,353,421,440`). Meanwhile `requires-python = ">=3.11,<3.14"` resolves 3.12 and all eleven pyprojects carry `Programming Language :: Python :: 3.12` (root `:29`, accounting `:23`, alignment `:26`, auditing/base/dpftrl/dpsgd/engine/optimizers/patches `:21`, transformers `:28`). The endpoints-only choice is nowhere written down: `CONTRIBUTING.md:162-166` names only "minimum (Python 3.11) and latest (Python 3.13)" dependency validation and `AGENTS.md`'s fourteen-lane list mentions no 3.12.
- **Failure scenario:** a 3.12-only regression — a dependency resolving to a different wheel, or interpreter-specific behaviour in the `torch.func`/optree layer — reaches a published release whose metadata explicitly advertises 3.12, with no CI signal having ever existed. The wheels go to a publicly readable index, so the claim is now made to external users. Bounded in practice because 3.11 and 3.13 bracket most interpreter-sensitive behaviour, and no privacy guarantee, ε value or numeric result is involved. The second-order cost is that a future reader of the lane list cannot distinguish a deliberate endpoints-only decision from the drift introduced when the #665 version bump retargeted the old 3.12 lanes to 3.13.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn '3\.12' .github/` → no output; `validate-distributions.yml:19`; `python-tests.yml:34-38`; the eleven classifier lines cited above; `AGENTS.md` lane list and `CONTRIBUTING.md:162-166`; GitHub issue #841 `state_reason: not_planned`, zero comments, with its parent #774 still open.
- **Fix:** cheapest close is adding `"3.12"` to the `validate-distributions.yml` matrix at `:19` — that job installs the built wheels and runs one cross-stack scenario, so it costs a few minutes of runner time and exercises the resolver on the advertised interpreter. If endpoints-only is the real policy, drop the 3.12 classifier from all eleven pyprojects and add one sentence to the `AGENTS.md` lane list recording that 3.11 and 3.13 are tested as the supported endpoints.
- **Notes:** this re-raises a maintainer-recorded `not_planned` decision; its value is that the decision was never written down, so the metadata and the lane list now disagree with no explanation either way.

**OPQ-484 — The documented torch-free `opaque-accounting` guarantee has no automated enforcement anywhere, in the repository or post-build.** `.github/workflows/validate-distributions.yml:50-88` · test-coverage · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** commit 864a6442 (PR #587, closing Marble #586, 2026-08-14) deleted `tests/contracts/test_accounting_torch_free.py` (55 lines) and `tests/contracts/test_accounting_factory_leakage.py` (89 lines) along with eight other enumeration/import tests; `tests/` now holds only `_support/` and `integration/`. The torch-free test was not a structural proxy — it enforced a guarantee the project advertises as a supported installation option (`README.md:42`; `AGENTS.md:32,41,130` "pip install opaque-accounting # PLD accounting (torch-free standalone)"; `packages/opaque-base/pyproject.toml:26-30` warns that any third-party dependency there "would propagate into opaque-accounting … and break the 'torch-free accounting' guarantee"). Nothing replaced it: a repo-wide grep for `torch.free` returns only prose plus one orphaned section header at `packages/opaque-auditing/tests/auditing/test_eps_delta.py:161` (`# ---- Contract: torch-free ---`) whose body the same commit deleted. `validate-distributions.yml` is the only artifact-validation workflow and has exactly one job, which installs `"${opaque_wheels[0]}[all]"` and runs a cross-stack DP-SGD/DP-FTRL composition — never an accounting-only install in a torch-free environment.
- **Failure scenario:** a future change adds an `import torch`, directly or through an `opaque-engine` helper, under `packages/opaque-accounting/src`. Every CI lane passes, because the test runner's environment always has torch installed via the other workspace packages. The published `opaque-accounting` wheel — which declares only `opaque-base>=0.0.0.dev0` — then fails to import in the environment the README, `AGENTS.md` and the installation docs promise, and the break is discovered by an end user. The property is verified to hold at the audited commit (grep over `packages/opaque-accounting/` matches `torch` only in a docstring at `_process_codec.py:6`), so this is an unguarded invariant rather than a live break.
- **Evidence:** `git show 864a6442 --stat` (the two deletions plus eight others); `git show 864a6442^:tests/contracts/test_accounting_torch_free.py` (it AST-scanned `packages/opaque-accounting/{src,tests}` for torch imports); `validate-distributions.yml:50-88`, the single `install-all` scenario; the dangling comment at `test_eps_delta.py:161`; `packages/opaque-accounting/pyproject.toml:13-15`.
- **Fix:** add one backend-neutral scenario to `validate-distributions.yml`: create a venv, install only the built `opaque_accounting` and `opaque_base` wheels with their real dependencies, assert `torch` is not importable in that venv, then run a minimal accounting operation (`gaussian(1.0)` composed, `epsilon_at(1e-5)`). That is behavioural post-build validation — exactly what ARC-004 and ARC-011 describe — so it restores the guard without reintroducing the source-scanning check #586 deliberately removed.
- **Notes:** ARC-004, ARC-005 and ARC-011 are all marked **Planned** in `.junie/architecture-contracts.md`, which instructs reviewers not to report absent planned infrastructure as a violation; this gap is therefore consciously deferred. It is listed because the deferral leaves an advertised installation option with no guard at all, in either the old or the new enforcement model.

**OPQ-485 — The committed `uv.lock` pins `accelerate` 1.14.0, the last version affected by a live GitHub-reviewed advisory.** `uv.lock:41-55` · dependency-security · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `uv.lock:40-56` pins `accelerate` 1.14.0. An OSV querybatch over every registry-sourced package in the lock (217 entries) returns exactly one hit: `accelerate` 1.14.0 → GHSA-4j2p-28q2-5m79 / PYSEC-2026-3804 / CVE-2026-69112, MODERATE, `github_reviewed_at` 2026-09-08, CWE-22 — path traversal and denial of service via unsanitised `weight_map` entries in sharded-checkpoint indexes consumed by `load_checkpoint_in_model` and `load_checkpoint_and_dispatch` — with ranges `{introduced: 0, last_affected: 1.14.0}`. OSV returns `{}` for 1.15.0 and PyPI confirms 1.15.0 exists, so a fixed version is available. `accelerate` reaches the lock transitively through `peft` 0.20.0 (`uv.lock:2862`) and `trl` 1.12.0 (`:4146`). The same OSV sweep over all crates in `Cargo.lock` returns zero hits.
- **Failure scenario:** every `uv sync --group dev --all-packages --extra all` and every CI lane installs a package carrying a live MODERATE advisory, and any repo-wide OSV or `pip-audit` scan flags it. No Opaque code path is exploitable: `accelerate` is not a declared dependency of any published `opaque-*` wheel (only transitive through `peft`/`trl`, which are dev-group and extras), `transformers` 5.16.1 in the lock does not depend on it, a grep over the whole tree finds zero uses of `load_checkpoint_in_model`, `load_checkpoint_and_dispatch` or `dispatch_model`, and the only `device_map=` in the repository is `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/validation/test_training.py:539`. The advisory itself is `AV:L/UI:R` — it requires an operator to point an accelerate checkpoint loader at an attacker-controlled shard index. Net effect today is dependency-scanner noise in dev and CI environments plus a one-line lock bump.
- **Evidence:** `uv.lock:40-56`, `:2862`, `:4146`; OSV querybatch over the lock (one hit) and over `Cargo.lock` (zero hits); OSV `vulns/GHSA-4j2p-28q2-5m79` metadata as quoted; PyPI `accelerate` 1.15.0 published 2026-09-09; `git log -1 -- uv.lock` → last modified 2026-09-07; `.github/dependabot.yml:47-66` (uv ecosystem, `versioning-strategy: lockfile-only`, all patterns grouped into one weekly Monday PR).
- **Fix:** `uv lock --upgrade-package accelerate`. The durable part is the process: give security updates their own ungrouped Dependabot lane, since the `workspace-dependencies` group batches every uv update into a single PR and makes a security bump wait on an unrelated failing update; and add a recurring OSV or `pip-audit` check over `uv.lock` and `Cargo.lock` to CI, which the repository has today for neither.
- **Notes:** the verifier reduced this from medium to low and corrected the framing — the fixed 1.15.0 was published two days *after* `uv.lock` was last regenerated, and the weekly Dependabot uv lane had not yet fired, so this is a pending bump rather than an ignored fix. It also refuted the original claim that `examples/train_*.py` triggers the vulnerable path: none of the eight example scripts passes `device_map` or uses a sharded-index loader.

### 6.2 CI gates and fail-open behaviour

**OPQ-486 — A GPU runner with under 24 GB silently skips the entire Triton kernel suite and the CUDA lane still reports green.** `packages/opaque-patches/tests/kernels/conftest.py:20-48` · test-integrity/ci-fail-open · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `pytest_collection_modifyitems` in the kernels conftest reads `torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_memory` and returns early only when CUDA is absent or the card has at least `MIN_KERNEL_CUDA_MEM_GB = 24` GiB (`:20`). Otherwise it attaches `pytest.mark.skip` to **every** collected item whose resolved path is relative to the kernels directory (`:36-48`) — there is no stress or perf predicate. That directory is the numerical-correctness suite for every fused kernel (kernel precision, RMSNorm, SwiGLU, GeGLU, RoPE, cross-entropy, linear cross-entropy, LoRA, MoE, autocast, compile, vmap-grad parity), not just the MELLUM perf cases the skip reason names. pytest then exits 0, `run_python_test_package.sh:38-48` records the path as a success because only exit code 5 is special-cased, and the lane is green. The CUDA preflight added by 039fc012 (PR #876) to close OPQ-377 probes `is_available()`, `device_count()` and one tensor add (`assert_cuda_available.sh:24-52`) and has no memory check, so it cannot distinguish an emptied lane from a real run.
- **Failure scenario:** the self-hosted runner is re-provisioned onto a smaller card (a 16 GB T4/L4/A4000 is the common case) or the `GPU-runner-001` label is repointed. All three CUDA lanes on `pr.yml` and all three on `ci.yml` then run `-m 'cuda and not slow'` / `-m cuda`, collect the kernel tests, skip 100 % of them at collection, and pass. Proven functionally rather than assumed: loading that conftest, stubbing `torch.cuda.is_available()` → True and `get_device_properties()` → 16 GiB, and feeding it one item per kernels test file plus one dpsgd item marks 19/19 kernel files and 0 items outside the directory, with reason `Kernel stress tests require >= 24GB CUDA memory (found 16.00GB).` The lost selection is 225 of the 397 tests in that directory under `-m 'cuda and not slow'` — the only validation of the Triton kernels the DP-SGD `vmap(grad)` pipeline runs on GPU. `publish-dev-wheels` (`ci.yml:199-213`) needs exactly those jobs, so `main` keeps publishing dev wheels on unvalidated kernels. The lane passes `-rs` so the skips appear in the log, but nothing fails on them.
- **Evidence:** `packages/opaque-patches/tests/kernels/conftest.py:20` (`MIN_KERNEL_CUDA_MEM_GB = 24`) and `:36-48` (unconditional directory-wide marking); the functional proof above; `pytest --collect-only -m 'cuda and not slow' packages/opaque-patches/tests/kernels` → 225/397 collected; `.github/scripts/run_python_test_package.sh:38-48`; `.github/scripts/assert_cuda_available.sh:24-52`; `.github/workflows/python-tests.yml:76-81` places `packages/opaque-patches` in the `runtime-and-patches` suite the CUDA lanes run; `.github/workflows/ci.yml:199-213`.
- **Fix:** scope the gate to the tests it was written for — mark the stress/perf cases explicitly (e.g. `@pytest.mark.kernel_stress`) and skip only those, leaving the numerical-parity tests to run at whatever size the card allows, since most already use tiny shapes and only the MELLUM config cases need 24 GB. Independently, make the lane fail rather than pass on a fully-skipped selection: have `run_python_test_package.sh` parse the summary (or use a strict-nonempty plugin) and fail when `selected > 0 and passed == 0`, and extend `assert_cuda_available.sh` with a minimum-memory assertion keyed to `MIN_KERNEL_CUDA_MEM_GB` so a too-small card fails the lane instead of emptying it.
- **Notes:** latent — triggering requires the self-hosted runner to be re-provisioned, and the live runner's card could not be inspected, so no library behaviour is wrong today. Not a duplicate of open #880 (two kernel files missing `pytest.mark.cuda` — different trigger, different files), though #880's acceptance criterion about asserting a minimum collected-test count would fix half of this; #774 is the natural umbrella.

**OPQ-487 — The executable-docs gate added for Sweep A executes no Markdown snippet and only three of the seven tutorial notebooks.** `.github/workflows/pr.yml:290-301` · ci-coverage/docs · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** issue #843's acceptance criteria were "Every documented accounting and training snippet executes as written, in CI" and "The executable-docs check runs on every PR, so this class cannot recur silently". The step that landed runs exactly three things: `uv run python examples/quickstart.py`, `uv run python -m doctest packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/accounting/__init__.py`, and a loop over `accounting_and_calibration.ipynb`, `dp_sgd_training.ipynb` and `dp_ftrl_training.ipynb`. It executes none of the 369 ```python fences across the 46 Markdown files under `docs/` — including the eleven Markdown files Sweep A itself hand-edited (`docs/user-guide/accounting.md`, `sampling.md`, `dp-sgd.md`, `docs/reference/*.md`, …) — and none of the four remaining notebooks in the mkdocs nav (`sampling_and_microbatching`, `llm_finetuning`, `privacy_auditing`, `distributed_training`). The one genuinely protected page, `quickstart.md`, is protected because it was rewritten to transclude the executed script (`:11`, `--8<-- "examples/quickstart.py:4:"`) — the right pattern, applied to exactly one page. Separately, `.github/workflows/docs.yml` deploys the public site on every push to `main` with `uv sync --group docs` then `mike deploy`: no `--strict`, no examples group, no execution step, and no dependency on `ci.yml`.
- **Failure scenario:** a future PR changes an accounting factory signature and updates the reference table but not the snippet in `docs/user-guide/accounting.md`. `mkdocs build --strict` still passes because it does not execute code, the three notebooks and `quickstart.py` still pass because they do not use that snippet, and the drifted snippet ships to the public docs site — the same mechanism that produced the Sweep A findings in the first place. This is a gate-coverage gap rather than a defect in shipped code; its one currently live consequence is the Poisson-versus-shuffled mismatch in `docs/tutorials/privacy_auditing.ipynb`, reported as OPQ-495.
- **Evidence:** `.github/workflows/pr.yml:290-301`; an independent scan counting 369 ```python fences across 46 files under `docs/`, with a grep confirming the only execution verbs anywhere in `.github/workflows` are those three lines; `mkdocs.yml:137-145` (seven notebooks, four unexecuted); `docs/getting-started/quickstart.md:11`; `.github/workflows/docs.yml` (push-to-main deploy with no strict build or execution step); `git show --stat 12146ec4` (eleven hand-edited Markdown files the gate does not cover); no test under `packages/*/tests` or `tests/` reads a docs file.
- **Fix:** extend the pattern `quickstart.md` already uses — move each ε-publishing snippet into a runnable file under `examples/` or `docs/_snippets/`, transclude it with `pymdownx.snippets`, and execute those files in CI. For the remainder, add a pytest collector (e.g. under `tests/docs/`) that extracts every ```python fence tagged runnable and execs it in a fresh namespace, so the fence list grows automatically. Add the four unexecuted notebooks to the `pr.yml` loop, and mirror the strict build plus the execution step into `docs.yml` so a direct push to `main` cannot deploy an unchecked site.
- **Notes:** the verifier dropped this from medium to low for two reasons. The originally cited "live instance that survived Sweep A" — `optimizer.step(noisy_grads)` at `docs/reference/rng.md:325`, filed here as OPQ-497 — sits in an illustrative block with undefined `model`, `batch`, `compute_dp_grads` and `optimizer`, so it is pseudocode no executable-docs gate would have run and is not evidence the gate missed something runnable. And the `docs.yml` half is largely theoretical, since pushes to `main` come from PRs that already ran the strict build, so an unchecked deploy needs a direct push. Natural parent is the still-open CI umbrella #774 or docs umbrella #775, neither of which tracks this gap.

**OPQ-488 — `TRY003` was enabled in the same commit that introduced a 179-site idiom Ruff cannot match, so the merge-gate rule is structurally incapable of firing.** `pyproject.toml:220, 248, 299-300` · ci-fail-open · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 7b26f655 (PR #760, "feat(exceptions): add public exception taxonomy") moved `TRY003` from `ignore` into `select` (`pyproject.toml:248`) and, in the same commit, rewrote 158 raise sites into the form `raise SomeError(*("message",))`. Ruff's `TRY003` matches a long message passed as a positional argument to the exception constructor; a starred tuple is not matched. `git grep -E 'raise [A-Za-z_]+\(\*\(' -- packages` now returns exactly 179 hits, all under `packages/*/src`, against 0 at the delta base. The same commit added `TRY003` to the per-file-ignores for both `**/tests/**` and `tests/**` (`:299-300`), so the rule is off in every test tree as well. The comment block at `:215-223`, headed "Omitted only when they fight intentional design", still lists `TRY003` with a rationale three lines above the `select` entry that enables it — the file documents the opposite of its own configuration.
- **Failure scenario:** established by direct reproduction, not hypothesis. `uv run ruff check --no-cache --select TRY003 packages/ tests/` on the current tree reports `All checks passed!` and exits 0. In isolation, `ruff check --isolated --select TRY003` on `raise E(f"n_steps must be >= 1, got {n}")` reports TRY003 and exits 1, while the byte-identical message written as `raise E(*(f"..."))` reports `All checks passed!` and exits 0. So a contributor who writes the obvious form fails the autoformat gate, greps the codebase, finds 179 examples of the splat workaround, copies it, and the gate goes green — having changed nothing about the property the rule exists to enforce, while making the call site harder to read and defeating any type checker's arity check on the constructor. PR #897, whose subject is "restore the lint gate by fixing three TRY003 violations", converted its three findings into exactly this splat form rather than into message-carrying exception classes.
- **Evidence:** `pyproject.toml:248` (select), `:261-267` (ignore no longer contains it), `:215-223` (contradictory comment), `:299-300` (test-tree per-file-ignores); `git grep -E 'raise [A-Za-z_]+\(\*\(' -- packages` → 179 hits now, 0 at 4b13d82; `git show 7b26f655 -- pyproject.toml` plus the 158 splat sites the same commit added; `git show ff174f11` (PR #897) converting `ValueError` sites into `ConfigurationError(*( ... ,))`; the isolated Ruff exit-code reproduction above; the whole-tree `All checks passed!` result.
- **Fix:** decide which it is and make the configuration say so. Either keep `TRY003` in `ignore` with the existing rationale and revert the 179 splats to plain string arguments, which read better and type-check, or keep the rule and give the exception taxonomy message-carrying constructors so the splat is unnecessary. Either way fix the comment at `:215-223` so it no longer documents `TRY003` as omitted while selecting it. A regression test is awkward for a lint config; the closest equivalent is a CI assertion that `git grep -cE 'raise [A-Za-z_]+\(\*\('` over `packages/` stays at zero once the splats are removed.
- **Notes:** no runtime, numerical or privacy consequence — the splat is semantically identical to the plain call. The cost is a merge-gate rule pinned at zero violations, a self-contradicting config comment, and 179 harder-to-read call sites. No test or script anywhere asserts anything about exception message placement.

**OPQ-489 — The required status check "Qodana / Qodana" can never report on a fork pull request, so the documented external-contribution path cannot merge.** `.github/workflows/pr.yml:252-257` · ci/community-health · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the active `main` ruleset (id 12831618, enforcement active) requires seven status checks, one of which is the context `Qodana / Qodana`; the `pull_request` rule requires one approving review, `allowed_merge_methods` is `["squash"]`, and `bypass_actors` is a single `RepositoryRole 5` with `bypass_mode: always`. That context is produced only when `pr.yml`'s `qodana` caller job (`name: Qodana`, `pr.yml:248-272`) instantiates the reusable `qodana.yml` `scan` job (also `name: Qodana`, `qodana.yml:28-31`). The caller is guarded by `if: (success() || failure()) && !github.event.pull_request.draft && github.event.pull_request.head.repo.full_name == github.repository`, so for a fork PR the job is skipped, the reusable workflow is never instantiated, and the composed context is never created. The authors applied the correct pattern elsewhere: `pr.yml:186-217` and `:231-247` add matrix-independent gate jobs named "Python tests" and "Rust tests" with `if: always()` whose jq explicitly treats skipped `python-tests-cuda*` legs as success, under the comment "Keep a stable, matrix-independent check name for branch protection rules". No equivalent gate exists for Qodana.
- **Failure scenario:** proven empirically on the other branch of the same guard rather than assumed. In run 34686188621 (draft PR #1030, sha c4be0b93) the jobs API reports a single job named exactly `Qodana` with conclusion `skipped`, and the check-runs API for that sha lists `Qodana` skipped with **no** `Qodana / Qodana` entry at all, while "Python tests" and "Rust tests" both report success. A non-draft fork PR takes the same path: an external contributor forks the repository as `docs/development/index.md:3,9-11` and `README.md:186` instruct, opens a PR, every check goes green except `Qodana / Qodana`, which sits permanently at "Expected — waiting for status to be reported", and the PR cannot be merged except by a user holding the admin role the ruleset grants unconditional bypass. The fork PR also receives no static-analysis coverage, which is the case where untrusted code most needs it.
- **Evidence:** GitHub API `GET /repos/JetBrains-Research/opaque/rulesets/12831618` (seven required contexts including `{"context": "Qodana / Qodana", "integration_id": 15368}`, one required review, squash-only, `RepositoryRole 5` bypass always); `.github/workflows/pr.yml:248-272` (the guard and `name: Qodana`); `.github/workflows/qodana.yml:28-31`; the run-34686188621 jobs and check-runs API responses showing a skipped `Qodana` and no composed context; `pr.yml:186-217` and `:231-247` (the gate pattern that exists for Python and Rust); `docs/development/index.md:3,9-11`, `README.md:185-186`, `allow_forking: true`.
- **Fix:** add a stable gate job in the same shape as `python-tests` / `rust-test-gate` — a job named e.g. "Qodana gate" with `if: always()` and `needs: [qodana]` that passes when the upstream result is `success` or `skipped` — and make the ruleset require that context instead of the nested `Qodana / Qodana`.
