Then confirm on a real fork PR that all seven required contexts report.
- **Notes:** the verifier reduced this to low on impact: `forks_count` is 0, so no fork PR has ever hit it, and the repository's regular merger holds the admin role that the ruleset grants `bypass_mode: always`. The guard itself is defensible — `ci.yml:167` shows Qodana needs `secrets.QODANA_TOKEN`, which GitHub withholds from fork `pull_request` runs, so running it unguarded on forks would fail rather than help. The residual defect is that a first external contributor sees a permanently pending required check.

### 6.3 Test integrity and mutation coverage

**OPQ-490 — Applied-versus-reported noise magnitude is untested on four sibling noise draw sites; a 5 % shortfall passes the entire suite.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/noise/_gaussian.py:226, 274-276`, `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_engine.py:156`, `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/clipping/_adaptive.py:121` · test-integrity/mutation-coverage · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges two reports of the same gap: "Applied-vs-reported noise magnitude is untested on four sibling noise paths", "Per-group Gaussian noise has no realized-vs-reported conformance test".)*
- **Claim:** OPQ-378 (issue #833, closed) was fixed by adding one high-n conformance test per mechanism that ties the realized second moment of the drawn noise to the reported `NoisedPytree.noise_stddev` — `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/noise/test_noise.py:133-142` (scalar `gaussian_noise`, n = 1e6, tolerance `6/sqrt(2n)` = 4.2e-3, with a `*0.97` negative control) and `packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests/noise/test_realized_stddev.py:41-48, 136-155` (all six MF strategies, same shape, scalar `max_norm` only). OPQ-378's own fix sketch named per-group `gaussian_noise` as a third shape; PR #871 delivered the scalar and MF cases and not that one. Four sibling paths that draw noise through *different* code are uncovered: per-group DP-SGD Gaussian (`_gaussian.py:226`), the paired second-moment stream (`_gaussian.py:274-276`), per-group MF/DP-FTRL (`_engine.py:156`) and AdaClip quantile noise (`_adaptive.py:121`). The only per-group magnitude assertion in the tree is scale-invariant by construction: `assert mlp_var > attn_var * 2.5` over 100-element tensors (`packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/noise/test_per_group_noise.py:48-50`). The dpftrl per-group file contains no realized statistic at all, `test_gaussian_second_moment.py` uses `torch.zeros(10)` and asserts only reported metadata, and the nine tests in `test_adaptive_quantile_noise.py` measure no realized noise scale.
- **Failure scenario:** a 5 % divergence between the noise actually drawn and the σ the accountant is charged for passes the whole non-CUDA suite on these four shipped draw sites, reproduced independently with runtime-injected mutations (pytest plugins, no repository edit, `git status` clean before and after). Patching `PerGroup.for_path` to return 0.95× only when the immediate caller frame is the draw helper — exactness confirmed, reported 2.4495 vs realized 2.3282, ratio 0.9505 — leaves `pytest -m "not cuda and not mps and not slow and not distributed"` over `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests packages/opaque-engine/tests tests/` at **2130 passed, 26 skipped, 0 failed**, with a serial run over the noise and clipping directories printing "MUTATION draw-side sigma lookups scaled: 144" alongside 484 passed. Wrapping the AdaClip quantile draw at 0.95× `fraction_noise_std` scales 592 draws and leaves **1523 passed, 26 skipped, 0 failed**. The cost if such a regression lands, measured with Opaque's own accountant: `poisson(gaussian(nm), sample_rate=0.01).repeated_pld(1000).epsilon_at(1e-5)` is 1.828322 at nm = 1.0 and 2.042989 at nm = 0.95, so a uniform 5 % per-group shortfall turns a run reported at ε = 1.83 into ε = 2.04 (+11.7 %); scaling only the squared stream by 0.95 breaks the joint Mahalanobis budget (it becomes `1.054/nm²`), implying an effective nm of 0.974039 and ε = 1.933574 (+5.8 %). Per-group clipping is a first-class `TrainingArguments` option (`_dp_trainer.py:1533-1546`, `clipping_norm` as a dict), so these are shipped configurations.
- **Evidence:** the four draw sites as cited; `test_per_group_noise.py:48-50` (the scale-invariant ratio); `test_noise.py:16-23, 133-142` and `test_realized_stddev.py:41-48, 136-155` (the conformance tests and their scalar-only inputs); the mutation runs above, plus the control showing a 1 % shortfall on the *scalar* path is caught (1 failed — `test_realized_noise_matches_reported_stddev` — 738 passed); the absolute-std assertions in `test_gaussian_compute_dtype.py` all use a scalar `max_norm` and take the `tree_map` branch, never the PerGroup branch; `docs/user-guide/noise.md:121-127` states "Privacy accounting is identical under either allocation — just `gaussian(nm)`", a claim that holds only if the applied per-group σᵢ equals `nm·sqrt(Cᵢ·ΣⱼCⱼ)` exactly.
- **Fix:** extend the OPQ-378 pattern to the sibling draw sites rather than the mechanism entry points. Parametrize `_assert_realized_stddev_matches_reported` over (i) `gaussian_noise` with a `PerGroup` `max_norm` over two ~1e6-element leaves, asserting each leaf's realized RMS against `output.noise_stddev.for_path(path)`; (ii) the paired `SecondMomentClippingOutput`, asserting both streams separately; (iii) `mf_gaussian_noise` with a `PerGroup` bound for at least identity and one correlated strategy; and (iv) `adaptive_clipped_grad`, collecting the per-step noisy clipping rate over ≥1e5 steps and asserting its sample stddev against `fraction_noise_std` at `5/sqrt(2n)`. Keep the existing `*0.97` negative control on each new case so the tolerance cannot silently widen.
- **Notes:** latent coverage gap, not a live miscalibration — realized-versus-reported per-group stddev was measured directly at HEAD and matches to within 0.15 % for float32, bfloat16, float16 and float64 leaves. Path (b), the paired second-moment stream, was established by inspection rather than mutation, because the second stddev reaches the draw as a plain float and no surgical injection was available. The verifier's AdaClip ε spot-check used a different step count and gave +7.7 % rather than the originally reported +13.7 %, so treat that one magnitude as setup-dependent. The merged second report argues the per-group exposure is narrower still: a uniform scale error would have to originate in per-group-only code between the σ computation and the draw, and the realistic `for_path` failure — misresolving a leaf to the wrong group — collapses the variance ratio and *is* caught by the existing 2.5× assertion; the residual risk is a future per-group-only cast, rescale or world-size adjustment added to that branch. Issue #348, whose acceptance criteria were exactly "every supported DP-SGD noise path has a conformance case" and "a deliberate scale mismatch makes the suite fail" with AdaClip named in scope, is closed as completed.

**OPQ-491 — The value-level `CachedProcess.repeated_pld` regression test was deleted one day after it landed, leaving only a mock with no real implementor.** `packages/opaque-accounting/tests/test_cached.py:9-29` · test-integrity/regression-coverage · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-379 asked for a value-level ε assertion on a correlated strategy, because the surviving guards used the one strategy where the buggy and correct answers coincide. PR #885 (76fd4e3e, 2026-09-02) added exactly that — `test_cached_correlated_full_horizon_preserves_epsilon`, `band_mf_strategy(bands=16)`, `sample_rate=0.01`, `n_steps=128`, asserting `(acc.cached(acc.per_step(proc)) * n).epsilon_at(delta) == (acc.per_step(proc) * n).epsilon_at(delta)` — which is issue #834's first acceptance criterion. 87404a1b (PR #894, "refactor(accounting)!: remove horizon prefix accounting", 2026-09-03) deleted `packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests/accounting/test_per_step.py` in full, taking that test with the `acc.per_step` API it depended on. What remains is the mock at `test_cached.py:8-29`, which patches `repeated_pld` onto an `eps_delta` process to manufacture a divergence. `grep -rn 'def repeated_pld' packages/*/src` returns only `_base.py:418` (the default) and `composition/_cached.py:84` (the relay), so no shipped process overrides it and the relay is inert today — while `DpProcess.repeated_pld`'s docstring at `_base.py:428-433` still invites subclasses to override it, naming "DP-FTRL per-step adapters, where K steps' joint PLD depends on the deployed N-step strategy's correlation matrix".
- **Failure scenario:** no current ε is wrong and relay *deletion* is still caught — a plugin doing `del CachedProcess.repeated_pld`, run over `packages/opaque-accounting/tests` plus the dpftrl and dpsgd accounting trees, gives 1 failed / 601 passed, and the single failure is the mock test with `assert 3.0 == 2.0`. So issue #834's second acceptance criterion survives; only the first was lost, and it is now unconstructible because the API it used was removed. The exposure is forward-looking: when a future mechanism reintroduces a count-sensitive `repeated_pld` override — which the docstring explicitly anticipates, and the default is not a bare `self_compose` since it rescales `mc_resolution` per release — the only thing standing between that and a silent reversion to `pld().self_compose(K)` is a mock that will still pass because it patches its own stub. OPQ-005 measured that divergence at 2.17× ε for `band_mf(bands=16)`, n = 128, K = 128.
- **Evidence:** `git show 76fd4e3e` (adds both tests); `git log --diff-filter=D 4b13d82..12146ec4 -- packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests/accounting/test_per_step.py` → 87404a1b; a whole-tree grep for `test_cached_correlated_full_horizon_preserves_epsilon` returns no hits at HEAD; `grep -rn 'def repeated_pld' packages/*/src` → only the default and the relay; the surviving mock passes in 0.05 s; the `del CachedProcess.repeated_pld` mutation result quoted above; `_base.py:428-433`.
- **Fix:** keep the mock and add a real one beside it — define a two-line `DpProcess` subclass in the test file whose `repeated_pld(count)` returns a demonstrably different PLD from `pld().self_compose(count)`, wrap it in `acc.cached(...)`, and assert `(cached * n).epsilon_at(delta) == (inner * n).epsilon_at(delta)` while `inner.pld().self_compose(n).epsilon_at(delta)` differs. That guards the relay against both deletion and a wrong-count relay without depending on any mechanism that may later be removed.
- **Notes:** the deletion was a consequence of a deliberate breaking API removal, not test-suite erosion — the test could not have survived the `acc.per_step` removal unchanged — which softens the original framing. Issue #834 is closed as completed by #885; this is a post-closure regression of one of its two criteria.

**OPQ-492 — The only integration coverage of AUTO-S clipping composed with per-group MF noise sits behind a capability probe that converts the feature's removal into a silent skip.** `tests/integration/noise/test_auto_clipping_mf_noise.py:132-154, 333-340` · test-integrity/fail-open-guard · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `TestPerGroupAutoSxMf` (two test methods × six strategies = 12 tests) is guarded by an autouse `_gate` fixture that calls `_per_group_supported_by_mf_noise()`, which invokes `mf_gaussian_noise` with a `PerGroup` bound and returns `False` on `except TypeError as exc: if "PerGroup" in str(exc)`; on `False` the fixture calls `pytest.skip("... test pending PR #192 (per-group MF noise).")`. PR #192 has long since landed — the probe returns `True` and all 12 tests pass today — but the gate remains, and Opaque's public exception taxonomy makes the fail-open shape live: `opaque.exceptions.InputTypeError` is declared `class InputTypeError(OpaqueError, TypeError)` (`packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/exceptions/__init__.py:31`), so an Opaque-native rejection naming `PerGroup` would be swallowed as a skip rather than surfacing as a failure. The module and class docstrings at `:36` and `:330-333` still tell the reader that "on plain main `mf_gaussian_noise` rejects PerGroup bounds" pending a PR that has landed.
- **Failure scenario:** a future change that re-rejects `PerGroup` bounds in `mf_gaussian_noise` with an `InputTypeError` whose message contains "PerGroup" converts those 12 AUTO-S × per-group-MF composition tests from FAIL to SKIP, and that specific composition goes unguarded with the lane green. The loss of per-group MF support itself would still be loud, however: `packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests/noise/test_per_group_noise.py` holds 22 ungated tests driving `mf_gaussian_noise` with `PerGroup`-clipped inputs across all six strategies plus paired second-moment cases, and per-group cases in `test_second_moment_noise.py` and `test_mf_state_serialization.py` would hard-fail too. Same shape as the OPQ-382 pattern the team fixed in `test_trl_trainers.py` by replacing a self-skip with a fail-closed fixture.
- **Evidence:** `tests/integration/noise/test_auto_clipping_mf_noise.py:132-154` (the probe; the verifier located it at `:311-333` in the current tree) and `:333-340` (the autouse `_gate` fixture calling `pytest.skip`); the stale docstrings at `:36` and `:330-333`; `InputTypeError`'s declaration as a `TypeError` subclass, pinned by `packages/opaque-base/tests/test_exceptions.py:23`; running the class at HEAD gives 12 passed, so the probe returns `True`; the 22 ungated per-group tests in `packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests/noise/test_per_group_noise.py`.
- **Fix:** delete `_per_group_supported_by_mf_noise` and the `_gate` fixture — per-group MF noise is shipped and documented (`docs/user-guide/noise.md:95-127`), so its regression tests should fail, not skip, when it stops working. If a capability probe is genuinely wanted, invert it into an assertion in a single test (`test_mf_gaussian_noise_accepts_per_group_bounds`) and let the other twelve run unconditionally. Remove the "pending PR #192" prose in the same pass.
- **Notes:** the verifier narrowed the impact on two points. The originally cited raise sites do not actually produce the trigger — `_expect_clipped` (`_engine.py:456-474`) never mentions `PerGroup` in either message, and the only `PerGroup` `InputTypeError` in `_engine.py:143-149` fires on non-tensor leaves, which the probe's all-tensor template cannot reach — so the fail-open needs a hypothetical future rejection whose message happens to contain the word. And removal of per-group MF support would not be invisible, given the 22 ungated sibling tests. What remains is a fail-open guard over 12 composition tests plus stale pending-PR prose.

**OPQ-493 — Two load-best-model checkpoint-correctness tests skip themselves on exactly the symptom they exist to catch.** `packages/opaque-transformers/tests/validation/test_checkpoint_correctness.py:166-174, 283-284` · test-integrity/fail-open-guard · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `TestLoadBestModelMutates::test_save_model_after_load_best_sees_best_weights` runs a trainer with `load_best_model_at_end=True` and then does `if trainer.state.best_model_checkpoint is None: pytest.skip("Eval never improved; nothing to compare.")` (`:167`), plus a second skip when the expected `adapter_model.safetensors` is missing (`:174`). `TestBestFolderLookup::test_best_folder_lookup` does the same on `trainer.state.best_global_step is None` (`:284`). Those are the only three `pytest.skip` calls in the file, and each guard is the precise observable symptom of the regression the enclosing test exists to catch: the first asserts that `save_model()` after `load_best_model_at_end` writes the best weights (which needs `best_model_checkpoint` and its adapter file), the second that `best_model_checkpoint` resolves to `checkpoint-<best_global_step>` (which needs `best_global_step`).
- **Failure scenario:** a change to the DP trainer's metric bookkeeping — `_dp_trainer.py` was reworked heavily across the delta, +227 lines in 87404a1b alone — that leaves `state.best_model_checkpoint` or `state.best_global_step` unset turns both tests from FAIL into green SKIP, silently removing the only coverage that `save_model()` after `load_best_model_at_end=True` writes the best weights and that the best-checkpoint folder lookup resolves correctly. Users then export last-step weights while believing they exported the best ones, with no CI signal. The guards are unreachable today: running both classes at HEAD gives 3 passed (in 7.9 s), because the first eval necessarily improves over a `None` `best_metric` in these deterministic 4-step fixtures — so they are dead code that only wakes up to hide a failure.
- **Evidence:** `test_checkpoint_correctness.py:167`, `:174`, `:284` (the three skips and their guards); the HEAD run showing 3 passed, 11 deselected; the sibling `test_best_metric_forces_checkpoint_at_intermediate_eval_step` in the same class, which already asserts unconditionally (`assert trainer.state.best_global_step == 2`), proving the assertion form is viable and limiting the blast radius to the two skipping tests.
- **Fix:** make all three preconditions assertions. The fixtures use a deterministic tiny dataset with `eval_strategy="steps"`, `eval_steps=2`, `metric_for_best_model="eval_loss"`, so a best checkpoint is expected on every run: replace the skips with `assert trainer.state.best_model_checkpoint is not None`, `assert trainer.state.best_global_step is not None`, and an assert on the adapter file. If a run can legitimately produce no improvement, force one by seeding the eval metric rather than by skipping.
- **Notes:** latent; no runtime or privacy effect today. Bazalt #774 ("Close CI fail-open gates and restore test integrity") is the right umbrella but all of its children are closed, so this instance is untracked.

**OPQ-494 — No generator or CI check ties the published HF training-argument defaults and accepted-value lists to their dataclasses, so Sweep B's structural criterion is unmet.** `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:148-165` · ci-coverage/docs · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** issue #844 opened by observing that "#419 reconciled the tables once by hand; they drifted again, so the fix needs to be a generator or a check rather than another pass", and its first acceptance criterion was "Every published default and accepted-value list is generated from, or checked against, its dataclass in CI". PR #1026 performed another hand pass. `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:148-155` remains a hand-written mechanism/sampler/default-kwargs table with `:157-165` a prose accepted-value list, duplicating `_SAMPLER_BY_MECHANISM` (`_training_arguments.py:186-195`), `_ALLOWED_SAMPLERS` (`:204-212`), `_CLIPPING_KWARGS`/`_SAMPLING_KWARGS`/`_MECHANISM_KWARGS` (`:214-238`), `_NOISE_CALIBRATION_KWARGS` (`:240`) and `_MECH_DEFAULTS` (`:254-262`). No generator and no check exists: only two files under `packages/*/tests` and `tests/` mention `docs/` and both are prose comments, nothing imports those dicts and parses the Markdown, no script or CI step reads a docs file, and `mkdocs.yml` wires `mkdocstrings` and `pymdownx.snippets` to neither table. `docs/reference/transformers.md:185-199` is a second hand-maintained copy of the same defaults.
- **Failure scenario:** the next change to a mechanism's default kwargs or sampler allow-list — for instance the follow-up to #1010 that already changed `mf_band`'s default sampler to cyclic Poisson — updates the dataclass and forgets the table. Nothing in CI notices: `mkdocs build --strict` does not read Python. A user then configures a sampling mode from a stale accepted-value list and gets a construction error, or copies a stale default and silently trains a different mechanism than the docs describe. The tables and lists are accurate at this commit — checked line by line against the code — so no user is misled today; the exposure is that the same drift mechanism that produced three earlier findings in this family is untouched.
- **Evidence:** `training-arguments.md:148-155` and `:157-165`; the source-of-truth symbols at `_training_arguments.py:186-195, 204-212, 214-238, 240, 254-262`; `grep -rln 'docs/'` over `packages/*/tests` and `tests/` → `packages/opaque-patches/tests/torch/test_cpu_offload.py:106` and `tests/integration/alignment/test_mechanism_agnostic.py:3`, both prose comments; no docs-reading script in `scripts/` or step in `.github/`; `docs/reference/transformers.md:185-199` (the duplicate copy).
- **Fix:** add one package-local test in `packages/opaque-transformers/tests` that imports the five dicts, parses the Markdown table out of `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md`, and asserts equality both ways — no documented mechanism missing, no undocumented mechanism present, kwargs dicts identical. That is a structural check on machine-readable content, not prose pinning, so it is consistent with the repository's test-design rule. Alternatively generate the table into a partial and transclude it with `pymdownx.snippets`, the way `quickstart.md` now transcludes its script. Either way, cover the duplicate in `docs/reference/transformers.md` too.
- **Notes:** two evidence corrections from verification. The symbol names in the original report (`_MECHANISM_SAMPLERS`, `_ALLOWED_SAMPLING_MODES`, `_MECHANISM_DEFAULT_KWARGS`) do not exist — the real names are the ones cited above. And PR #1026 was not documentation-only: it also changed `_dp_trainer.py` and `_training_arguments.py` and added `TestPrivacyDefaults::test_unset_privacy_delta_uses_dataset_dependent_default`, which pins the `1/n**1.1` delta default behaviourally, so the ε-relevant default in this family *is* guarded. What remains unguarded is documentation accuracy only. A naive generated check would need care: `bound` appears in `_MECHANISM_KWARGS["gaussian"]` solely to raise a targeted `ConfigurationError` at `:1222`, so its absence from the docs is correct.

### 6.4 Public documentation and messaging

**OPQ-495 — The `privacy_auditing` tutorial prices a shuffled fixed-batch loop with Poisson amplification and publishes the result as the audit ceiling.** `docs/tutorials/privacy_auditing.ipynb` (code cell 13; training loop in code cell 9) · docs-drift-epsilon · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** this is the OPQ-355 defect alive in a sibling notebook that the Sweep A fix did not touch and that the new CI step does not execute. Code cell 9 trains with `DataLoader(train_data, batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)` — fixed-size shuffled batches, no sampler. Code cell 13 then computes `process = dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(noise_multiplier), sample_rate) * total_steps` and prints it as "Theoretical epsilon", then compares the audited ε against it and prints "OK: audited epsilon is within the theoretical bound". Poisson subsampling amplification requires independent per-record inclusion and does not hold for a shuffled fixed-batch partition; the conservative analysis for that loop is unamplified composition. This is precisely the sanity check `.junie/differential-privacy-review.md` requires to be sound ("treat an empirical lower bound above the theoretical upper bound as a likely defect"). The other four tutorials all construct a `PoissonSampler` and pass it as `batch_sampler`, and `dp_sgd_training.ipynb` cell 13 was rewritten by 12146ec4 with the comment "The sampler and accountant use the same independent-inclusion process", so this notebook is the only remaining mismatch.
- **Failure scenario:** run with the notebook's own parameters (`noise_multiplier=0.5`, `clipping_norm=1.0`, `batch_size=64`, `n_epochs=200`; `coin_flip(key=key(42), num_canaries=500)` leaves `len(train_data)=252`, so `sample_rate=0.25397` and `total_steps = 200 * (252 // 64) = 600`), the notebook publishes **ε = 250.886** at δ = 1e-5 with the shipped accountant. The defensible bound for the loop as written is unamplified composition over the releases actually performed — and there are more of them than the notebook counts: with 252 examples, `batch_size=64` and `DataLoader`'s default `drop_last=False` the loop emits `ceil(252/64) = 4` releases per epoch, i.e. 800, while `total_steps` counts `252 // 64 = 3` per epoch, i.e. 600. So the conservative bound is `gaussian(0.5) * 800` = **1841.258**, a **7.3×** understatement of the published figure (5.6× from the amplification error alone at the accounted 600 steps). A reader who copies this pattern — shuffled `DataLoader` plus Poisson-amplified accountant — into their own run reports an ε they cannot justify, and the notebook's "OK" line actively confirms the wrong number. The auditing guide points readers at this notebook.
- **Evidence:** code cell 9 (`DataLoader(..., shuffle=True)`, then `params = tuple(p - (lr / bs) * g ...)`); code cell 13 (`sample_rate = batch_size / len(train_data)`; `total_steps = n_epochs * (len(train_data) // batch_size)`; the `poisson(gaussian(...))` process; `theoretical_eps = process.epsilon_at(1e-5)`; the `if audited_eps <= theoretical_eps` print); cell 7 parameters and cell 3/5 canary setup; the ε values recomputed with the shipped accountant on torch 2.14 (`poisson` 250.886, `gaussian(0.5)*600` 1408.936, `gaussian(0.5)*800` 1841.258); `.github/workflows/pr.yml:294-301` executes only `accounting_and_calibration`, `dp_sgd_training` and `dp_ftrl_training`, while `mkdocs.yml:135-145` lists all seven; `docs/tutorials/dp_sgd_training.ipynb` cell 13 as fixed by 12146ec4.
- **Fix:** replace the shuffled `DataLoader` in cell 9 with `PoissonSampler(dataset, sample_rate=..., n_steps=total_steps, key=...)` behind `DataLoader(..., batch_sampler=sampler)` so the runtime matches the accountant, exactly as `dp_sgd_training.ipynb` now does; pass `normalize_by=batch_size` to `clipped_grad` and drop the `/ bs` from the update; and reconcile `total_steps` with the number of releases the loop actually performs. Then add `privacy_auditing.ipynb` — and the other three unexecuted notebooks — to the `pr.yml` loop so the pattern cannot come back.
- **Notes:** no library code is wrong; this is a synthetic tutorial whose ε is meaninglessly large either way, and the printed "OK" comparison still holds numerically because the audited ε is far below both bounds. It is above low because the number is published on the live docs site as the theoretical guarantee and the pattern is the one a reader copies. One sub-claim from the original report was dropped on verification: the argument that `p - (lr / bs) * g` leaks because `bs` is "a data-dependent quantity the mechanism never noised" does not apply here — under this fixed shuffled partition `bs` is deterministic per step (64, 64, 64, 60), so it is a per-step learning-rate scale, not a data-dependent release. That objection applies to the Poisson-sampled notebooks instead. The CI half is OPQ-487.

**OPQ-496 — A 60 KB internal research diary sits at the public repository root, unreferenced by any documentation and asserting an urgent privacy defect that was fixed nine days earlier.** `issue-795-research.md:1-60, 45-47, 979, 1014` · docs/public-messaging · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** d09d7b9a ("fix(dpftrl): bound BISR noise state by bandwidth (#886)", 2026-09-02) committed `issue-795-research.md` — a 1064-line, 60,100-byte "Research snapshot" working document — to the root of the public repository, where it now sits directly beside `README.md`, `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `SECURITY.md`. It is referenced by nothing: `grep -rn 'issue-795'` over `*.md`/`*.yml`/`*.toml`/`*.py` excluding the file itself returns nothing, and `mkdocs.yml` does not mention it. Its content is exactly what `AGENTS.md`'s documentation rule excludes from the tree ("Keep user-facing docs and code comments diary-free: describe the current API and behavior, not the development history … Migration narrative belongs in PR bodies and the changelog"): a dated research snapshot header, a "Post-research implementation update (2026-09-01 UTC)" section, rebase provenance naming specific commits and PRs, superseded recommendations, and running commentary on other issues.
- **Failure scenario:** a prospective user or reviewer browsing the public repository root finds, next to the README, a document that reads as an internal engineering memo and states that the library has an urgent unfixed privacy-calibration defect. Lines 45-47 call issue #789 "the separate, urgent calibrated-resume defect", line 979 says "The calibrated-resume failure in #789 remains urgent", and line 1014 repeats "the urgent enforcement gap is #789" — while #789 ("Stop calibrated DP-FTRL resume from silently changing sigma mid-horizon", labelled `impact: epsilon`) was closed as completed on 2026-09-04, nine days before the audited commit. So a public root-level file asserts an outstanding ε-affecting defect that does not exist. Nothing builds, links or tests the file, so it will keep drifting.
- **Evidence:** `git log --oneline -- issue-795-research.md` → the single commit d09d7b9a; `git ls-tree 12146ec4 --name-only` shows it as a tracked root entry, 60,100 bytes; the orphan grep; `issue-795-research.md:1-3, 5` (the snapshot and update headers); `:45-47`, `:979`, `:1014` (the stale urgency claims); GitHub API issue 789 → `state: closed`, `state_reason: completed`, `closed_at: 2026-09-04T07:49:39Z`; `AGENTS.md` "## Documentation" diary-free rule. It also landed three weeks after Marble #558 ("perform a public-facing editorial review") closed on 2026-08-11.
- **Fix:** delete `issue-795-research.md` from the tree; its durable content belongs in the #886 PR body, in the #795 issue thread, or — if the BISR bandwidth reasoning is worth keeping for users — condensed into `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md`, where it is built and reviewed. If an in-repo research archive is genuinely wanted, move such documents under a clearly labelled directory that mkdocs ignores and add a root-level file rule to the editorial checklist so the next one is caught. No behavioural test applies; the enforceable guard is a CI assertion that the repository root contains only an allowlisted set of Markdown files.
- **Notes:** public-messaging and repository hygiene only, with no code, test or privacy effect. Not tracked by any open issue.

**OPQ-497 — `docs/reference/rng.md` documents `optimizer.step()`, which the functional optimizer does not have.** `docs/reference/rng.md:325` · docs-drift-api · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `reference/rng.md` was one of the pages Sweep A (#843) covered — 12146ec4 changed 22 lines in it, including line 322 of this very block, converting a `gaussian_noise(1.1, ...)` call to keyword form — yet the determinism example a few lines below still ends with `optimizer.step(noisy_grads)` at `:325`. `opaque.optimizers.sgd(...)` returns a `torchopt.base.GradientTransformation` whose public members are exactly `chain`, `count`, `index`, `init`, `update`; `hasattr(o, "step")` is `False`, verified by execution. Every other end-to-end snippet in the documentation uses the correct pair — `docs/user-guide/dp-sgd.md:150-151` and `examples/quickstart.py:88-89` both do `updates, opt_state = optimizer.update(...)` then `torchopt.apply_updates(params, updates)`. `grep -rn 'optimizer\.step' docs/ examples/ README.md` returns exactly this one hit.
- **Failure scenario:** a reader following the RNG reference to wire per-step keys into a training loop copies the block and hits `AttributeError: 'GradientTransformation' object has no attribute 'step'`. The surrounding lines are correct, so the natural conclusion is that the optimizer import is wrong rather than the documentation. The error is loud, not silent, and the block is a sketch with several undefined names (`model`, `batch`, `optimizer`, and a `compute_dp_grads` helper that exists nowhere in `docs/`, `examples/` or `packages/`), so it cannot be pasted verbatim in any case — the practical cost is a misleading API idiom in the RNG reference.
- **Evidence:** `docs/reference/rng.md:306-326` (the example, ending at `:325`); the executed check `from opaque.optimizers import sgd; o = sgd(lr=0.01)` → `torchopt.base.GradientTransformation`, members `['chain','count','index','init','update']`, `hasattr(o,'step') is False`; the single-hit grep; the correct idiom at `docs/user-guide/dp-sgd.md:150-151` and `examples/quickstart.py:88-89`; `git show 12146ec4 -- docs/reference/rng.md` showing the Sweep A edit touched `:322` and not `:325`.
- **Fix:** replace `:325` with the standard pair (`updates, opt_state = optimizer.update(noisy_grads, opt_state); params = torchopt.apply_updates(params, updates)`) and either define or drop the `compute_dp_grads` placeholder so the block is runnable, then bring the page under whatever snippet-execution mechanism closes OPQ-487.
- **Notes:** documentation only, no privacy or numerical consequence. Because the block is pseudocode, it would not have been caught by an executable-docs gate either — which is why the verifier declined to treat it as proof that OPQ-487's gate missed a runnable snippet. The open documentation umbrella #775 is generic and its only open child (#845) concerns citations, so this is untracked.

**OPQ-498 — `AGENTS.md`'s "Accounting native module" section is wrong on all three of its claims and contradicts the same file's package table.** `AGENTS.md:191-200` · docs-drift · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the block states that the compiled artifact is `opaque/accounting/opaque_accounting.abi3.so`, that maturin is configured with `module-name = "opaque.accounting.opaque_accounting"` and `python-packages = ["opaque.accounting"]`, and that `opaque.accounting/__init__.py` does `from . import opaque_accounting as _native`. None of that holds. `packages/opaque-accounting/pyproject.toml:38-40` sets `module-name = "opaque.api.accounting.core.opaque_accounting"`, `python-source = "src"` and `python-packages = ["opaque/accounting", "opaque/api/accounting/core"]`; the only built `.so` in the tree is `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/opaque_accounting.abi3.so`, which is also where the importable `_native.__file__` resolves; and `grep -rn 'as _native' packages/opaque-accounting/src/` matches exactly one line, `opaque/api/accounting/core/__init__.py:16`, while the `opaque.accounting` façade `__init__.py` contains no such import. `AGENTS.md:32`, the package table earlier in the same file, already states the extension is at `opaque.api.accounting.core.opaque_accounting` aliased as `_native`, so the file contradicts itself; and `.github/scripts/check_accounting_artifact_policy.py:23` pins `NATIVE_MODULE_PATH = "opaque/api/accounting/core/opaque_accounting.abi3.so"`, so CI agrees with the code.
- **Failure scenario:** `AGENTS.md` is the loaded entry point for Copilot and Claude Code agents working in this repository, per `.junie/architecture-contracts.md`'s agent entry-point table, and these lines are the only place the native layout is documented for agents. An agent asked to touch the native module looks for `opaque/accounting/opaque_accounting`, finds nothing, and either guesses or "fixes" the maturin configuration back to a layout that does not build and that the wheel-policy check would reject. No runtime, build or privacy impact — the build is driven by `pyproject.toml`, not by `AGENTS.md` — so the cost is wasted agent and reviewer time, mitigated by the correct path appearing twice elsewhere. `AGENTS.md` was edited twice in the delta (873de427, ba39783d) without this section being reconciled.
- **Evidence:** `AGENTS.md:193-199` (the three claims) versus `AGENTS.md:32` (the package table); `packages/opaque-accounting/pyproject.toml:38-40`; the single `.so` in the tree and `_native.__file__` resolving there; the one-hit `as _native` grep at `opaque/api/accounting/core/__init__.py:16`; `.github/scripts/check_accounting_artifact_policy.py:23`.
- **Fix:** rewrite `AGENTS.md:193-199` to match the tree — artifact at `opaque/api/accounting/core/opaque_accounting.abi3.so`, `module-name = "opaque.api.accounting.core.opaque_accounting"` with `python-packages = ["opaque/accounting", "opaque/api/accounting/core"]`, `_native` bound in `opaque/api/accounting/core/__init__.py`. Better still, stop restating the layout: a single source of truth already exists as `NATIVE_MODULE_PATH` in `check_accounting_artifact_policy.py`, so cite that script from `AGENTS.md` instead, which is what prevents the two from drifting again.
- **Notes:** the repository's test-design rule forbids tests whose only purpose is pinning documentation prose, so the durable guard here is the citation-instead-of-duplication fix rather than a new test. Not a duplicate of open Bazalt #775/#845, whose open child scope is citation verification rather than the native-module layout note.

**OPQ-499 — `third_party_provenance.toml` attributes riskcal to a nonexistent google-research repository, and one upstream's `homepage` field holds prose instead of a URL.** `third_party_provenance.toml:126` (riskcal) and `:112` (random-allocation) · legal/attribution · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** ARC-009 requires that "repository provenance references resolve to real attributed sources". Two entries fail. The riskcal upstream declares `homepage = "https://github.com/google-research/riskcal"`, which returns HTTP 404; the real project is `https://github.com/interpretable-dp/riskcal` (MIT, "Measure and calibrate operational privacy risks for differentially private algorithms"), and PyPI riskcal 1.5.1 metadata gives `project_urls = {"Repository": "https://github.com/interpretable-dp/riskcal.git"}` with authorship by Bogdan Kulynych and Juan Felipe Gomez — so the manifest credits the wrong organization and gives a dead link. Separately, the "Random-allocation reference implementation" entry puts a sentence in the `homepage` field — `homepage = 'ICML 2026 reference implementation for "Efficient privacy loss accounting for subsampling and random allocation"'` — so that upstream has no resolvable reference at all, even though it is marked `disposition = "adapted"` with `requires_notice = true` and its derived files ship in `opaque-dpsgd`.
- **Failure scenario:** a downstream consumer performing an attribution review of the published wheels follows `third_party_provenance.toml` — the document ARC-009 designates as the machine-readable provenance source of truth that will generate or validate NOTICE — and cannot reach either upstream: the riskcal link 404s, and the random-allocation entry names only a paper. Impact today is confined to this file: riskcal carries `requires_notice = false` and appears nowhere in NOTICE, and NOTICE's random-allocation section (`NOTICE:107-118`) already identifies that upstream by paper title and Apache-2.0 terms at the same granularity as the toml, so no published wheel or NOTICE text is currently wrong. The exposure is that ARC-009's planned NOTICE generator would propagate both values the moment it is built.
- **Evidence:** the two `[[upstreams]]` entries as cited; a WebFetch of `https://github.com/google-research/riskcal` → HTTP 404; the real repository and PyPI metadata as quoted; all 24 file paths referenced across `third_party_provenance.toml` and NOTICE do exist, so only the two `homepage` values are wrong; `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/accounting/test_cross_validation.py:28-30` imports riskcal, confirming the dependency is real; `NOTICE:107-118`.
- **Fix:** change the riskcal homepage to `https://github.com/interpretable-dp/riskcal`. Give the random-allocation entry a resolvable `homepage` (the arXiv or proceedings URL, or the reference implementation's repository) and, if only a paper exists, add a separate `citation` field rather than overloading `homepage`. Lock it in with a small CI validator that parses the manifest and asserts every `files` path exists and every `homepage` is an absolute `http(s)` URL — the same script used during the audit found both defects in one pass, and it is behavioural rather than an inventory check, so it does not conflict with #586's intent.
- **Notes:** line numbers have drifted since the report — in the current tree the riskcal homepage is at `third_party_provenance.toml:105` and the random-allocation homepage at `:82`. ARC-009 is marked **Planned**, the manifest-to-NOTICE generator does not exist yet, and no schema or validator anywhere in the repository defines `homepage` as a URL (a grep for `third_party_provenance` finds no consumers), so "homepage must be an absolute URL" is a reasonable convention rather than a stated contract. The riskcal wrong-URL half is a genuine one-line factual fix worth making on a public repository; the prose-homepage half is a data-quality nit.

### 6.5 Community health, security policy and release process

**OPQ-500 — Contributor documentation routes public users to disabled GitHub Discussions and to label URLs that match no label in the repository.** `CONTRIBUTING.md:85-87, 445` · docs/community-health · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**. *(merges two reports of the same block: "Contributor documentation routes public users to GitHub Discussions (disabled) and to label URLs that do not match the repository's actual label names", "CONTRIBUTING.md routes contributors to GitHub Discussions, which is disabled on the public repository".)*
- **Claim:** Marble #566 ("configure GitHub Discussions for public participation") was closed as `not_planned` — the maintainer's closing comment is explicit: "Withdrawing GitHub Discussions from the public-source preparation scope for now. The repository can remain focused on Issues and the private security-reporting channel." The documentation #566 would have made valid was never reverted. `CONTRIBUTING.md:87` offers "[Open Discussions](https://github.com/JetBrains-Research/opaque/discussions)" in the "where to start" list and `:445` makes it the single answer under "## Getting Help" → "**Questions**", while the repository API reports `has_discussions: false`. In the same block, `:85-86` link `/labels/good-first-issue` and `/labels/help-wanted`, but the repository's 45 labels include "good first issue" and "help wanted" *with spaces* — `GET /labels/good-first-issue` and `/labels/help-wanted` both return "label not found", while the percent-encoded forms resolve. Compounding it, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` does not exist (only `bug_report.yml`, `documentation.yml`, `feature_request.yml`), so the new-issue chooser presents no contact links and offers no alternative route.
- **Failure scenario:** a first-time contributor arriving from the public README opens `CONTRIBUTING.md` to find something to work on. Both "good starting points" links point at slugs that correspond to no label in the repository, so neither surfaces a startable task; and the "Questions" route is a dead end — an anonymous `GET` of `/discussions` returns 403 while `/issues` returns 200 from the same client. With no `config.yml` contact links the new-issue chooser routes nobody either, leaving the public issue tracker as the only working channel — discoverable, but not the one the documentation names, and also the place `SECURITY.md` tells people *not* to put a suspected vulnerability. Three dead links in the contributor entry point; nothing executable, no privacy or numeric consequence.
- **Evidence:** `CONTRIBUTING.md:85-86` (the two hyphenated label URLs), `:87` and `:445` (the two Discussions links); GitHub API `has_discussions: false`, `visibility: public`, `has_issues: true`; `GET /labels/good-first-issue` and `/labels/help-wanted` → "label not found", `GET /labels/good%20first%20issue` → `{name: "good first issue"}` and `/labels/help%20wanted` → `{name: "help wanted"}`; anonymous `/discussions` → 403 versus `/issues` → 200; `ls .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/` → three templates, no `config.yml`; issue #566 `state_reason: not_planned` with the closing comment quoted above; no link checker anywhere in `.github/` or `mkdocs.yml` (no lychee, linkcheck or markdown-link-check).
- **Fix:** either enable Discussions, reopening the #566 decision, or remove both Discussions links and point "Questions" at the issue tracker with a `question` label. URL-encode the real label names in the two starting-point links (`/labels/good%20first%20issue`, `/labels/help%20wanted`). Add `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml` with `blank_issues_enabled` set deliberately and `contact_links` covering security reporting and questions, so the chooser routes people correctly. The durable guard is a link checker in the docs lane, which the repository currently has in no form.
- **Notes:** one mitigating and one aggravating detail. Both label queries currently return zero issues, so even correctly encoded links would list nothing today — the slug bug turns an empty filter into a nonexistent one. And the 403-versus-404 distinction on the Discussions URL could not be confirmed from a non-proxied client, but `has_discussions: false` is authoritative and either code is a dead end.

**OPQ-501 — GitHub private vulnerability reporting is disabled on the public repository and `SECURITY.md` states no concrete disclosure window.** `SECURITY.md:10-14, 38-46` · security-policy · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `SECURITY.md:11-15` directs reporters to `security@jetbrains.com` with a PGP key and explicitly forbids public issues or discussions for suspected vulnerabilities, but the GitHub-native private path is off: `GET /repos/JetBrains-Research/opaque/private-vulnerability-reporting` returns HTTP 200 `{"enabled": false}` on a repository whose API reports `private: false, visibility: "public"`. The Security tab therefore shows the rendered policy but no "Report a vulnerability" draft-advisory form, and no draft GitHub Security Advisory / CVE workflow exists for the published `opaque-*` distributions. The policy also states no disclosure window: `:39-43` asks only that reporters "allow reasonable time for a fix or mitigation before public disclosure" and delegates the timeline to the JetBrains Coordinated Disclosure Policy; a grep confirms no number of days appears anywhere in the file.
- **Failure scenario:** an outside researcher who finds a privacy-accounting defect clicks the Security tab and finds no in-product reporting form — the standard 2026 workflow — and must instead use the `security@jetbrains.com` mailto. That is a working private channel, so the practical consequence is friction plus the absence of the advisory/CVE machinery, not the loss of a private route. Separately, because no project-specific acknowledgement or disclosure expectation is stated, reporter and maintainer share no explicit numeric expectation about when publication is fair. Neither item causes a wrong result or a privacy loss.
- **Evidence:** `GET /repos/JetBrains-Research/opaque/private-vulnerability-reporting` → `{"enabled": false}`; GitHub API `private: false`, `visibility: "public"`; `SECURITY.md:11-15` (private channel and prohibition), `:39-43` (delegated timeline, no days); `https://www.jetbrains.com/legal/docs/terms/coordinated-disclosure/` → HTTP 200, so the delegated policy is real and reachable; `SECURITY.md:28-35` already routes non-sensitive DP/accounting defects to the public tracker and reserves the private channel for material-privacy-impact cases.
- **Fix:** enable private vulnerability reporting in repository Settings → Security — one toggle, which also unlocks draft GitHub Security Advisories for the published distributions — and mention that route first in `SECURITY.md` alongside the email. Add a concrete acknowledgement and disclosure expectation, or state explicitly that the JetBrains Coordinated Disclosure Policy's timeline governs and link the specific clause.
- **Notes:** the verifier softened the original framing on two points. `SECURITY.md` at the repository root *is* what GitHub renders as the Security policy on the Security tab and in the sidebar, so a researcher does see a designated private route; the missing item is the native form. And `SECURITY.md:28-35` already tells reporters where non-sensitive defects go, so the "no visible correct destination" framing does not hold. Delegating a timeline to a real org-level coordinated-disclosure policy is legitimate practice; the absence of a project-specific number of days is a completeness preference.

**OPQ-502 — The repository's squash setting discards every PR body, contradicting `AGENTS.md` and the PR template and disabling git-cliff's `BREAKING CHANGE` detection.** `AGENTS.md:97-118` (config verified via the repository API) · process/release-notes · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `AGENTS.md:64-66` states "The PR title becomes the commit subject; the PR body becomes the commit body (repo-level squash setting = `PR_TITLE` + `PR_BODY`). Both feed `git-cliff`", and `.github/pull_request_template.md:7-9` repeats "On squash merge the body becomes the commit body that git-cliff reads." The repository is actually configured `squash_merge_commit_title: "PR_TITLE"`, `squash_merge_commit_message: "BLANK"`, with `allow_merge_commit: false`, `allow_rebase_merge: false` and the `main` ruleset restricting `allowed_merge_methods` to `["squash"]`. This is not only stale documentation: `cliff.toml:40` contains `{ body = ".*BREAKING CHANGE", group = "Breaking" }` and `cliff.toml:44` sets `protect_breaking_commits = true`, both of which read the commit **body**, and `AGENTS.md:78` explicitly tells contributors a breaking change may be declared with "a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer in the body".
- **Failure scenario:** proven behaviourally rather than inferred. Dependabot PR #974 has a multi-kilobyte body, was squash-merged on 2026-09-07, and the resulting commit 9eabd4c6 has a completely empty body — Dependabot always writes a body, so this is conclusive; every one of the 40 most recent commits has an empty body or only `Co-authored-by` trailers. Two consequences follow. The "why" prose the PR template asks for never reaches `git log` or the release notes, so every release's rationale and the permanent history lose the 2-4 sentences `AGENTS.md` says to preserve "for a future spelunker". And a contributor who follows `AGENTS.md:78` and declares a breaking change with only a footer in the PR body gets the footer dropped, the body parser never fires, and the commit is grouped by its type prefix (e.g. `refactor` → "Changed") instead of under Breaking in the drafted release notes — so downstream users upgrading on those notes hit an undeclared breaking change.
- **Evidence:** authenticated `GET /repos/JetBrains-Research/opaque` → `squash_merge_commit_title: "PR_TITLE"`, `squash_merge_commit_message: "BLANK"`, `allow_merge_commit: false`, `allow_rebase_merge: false`, plus the ruleset's squash-only `allowed_merge_methods`; `AGENTS.md:64-66` and `:78`; `.github/pull_request_template.md:7-9`; PR #974's body versus commit 9eabd4c6's empty body; `git log 4b13d82..12146ec4 --pretty='%b'` (empty bodies throughout, e.g. 873de427, 026200fd, e44d6ab6, 79acf31d, 0a002a50); `cliff.toml:40, 44`.
- **Fix:** pick one and make configuration and documentation agree. Either set the repository's squash commit message to `PR_BODY` — matching what `AGENTS.md` and the PR template already promise and what `cliff.toml`'s body parser needs — or, if empty bodies are deliberate, remove the `body = ".*BREAKING CHANGE"` parser from `cliff.toml`, drop the footer option from `AGENTS.md:78` so `!` in the title is the only supported declaration, and rewrite `.github/pull_request_template.md:7-9` accordingly. Setting `PR_BODY` is the smaller change and preserves the documented behaviour.
- **Notes:** the verifier reduced this to low on two corrections. The documentation contradiction is narrower than reported: `CONTRIBUTING.md:255-260` is actually *consistent* with the real setting ("the PR title is the commit message on `main`") and documents only `!`, so only `AGENTS.md` and the PR template are wrong. And the hazard is latent, not realized: `cliff.toml`'s sibling `{ message = ".*!:" }` parser still works and the team uses `!` in titles in practice (b9b0a23e, 873de427, e8f87cd1, f2d90d61, 79acf31d), so no release has actually lost a Breaking section. No code, artifact or privacy impact; the realized cost is the permanent loss of PR rationale from `git log`.

**OPQ-503 — Internal JetBrains infrastructure identifiers are committed in the public repository, alongside a dead CI prototype whose README forward-references a migration that has not happened.** `.teamcity/pom.xml:18`, `.github/workflows/build-devcontainer.yaml:77` · repository-hygiene · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** Marble #578 ("Remove JetBrains-internal specifics from documentation") is closed, but two internal infrastructure identifiers remain in tracked configuration on a public `main`. `.teamcity/pom.xml:18` hardcodes `<teamcity.server.url>https://jbr-fed.teamcity.com</teamcity.server.url>`; that host is access-controlled (anonymous `GET` returns HTTP 401), and via `.teamcity/README.md` and `.teamcity/settings.kts` the tree also discloses the intended project layout, hosted agent pool (`Linux-Large`, `teamcity.agent.jbHosted`) and pipeline id (`Opaque_LinuxAmd64Tests`). `.github/workflows/build-devcontainer.yaml:77` hardcodes `GAR_IMAGE: europe-west4-docker.pkg.dev/gke-dev-dws-jbr/ml/opaque-devcontainer` (also used at `:104` and `:120`), naming an internal GCP project and repository, right beside the `vars.GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` / `vars.GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL` the same job reads from repository variables. The `.teamcity` tree is additionally dead weight: `git grep -i teamcity -- .github/` returns nothing, `AGENTS.md` documents GitHub Actions as the CI, and `.teamcity/README.md` states in the present tense that the pipeline "replaces the GitHub Actions Linux amd64 test matrix", which has not happened — itself a violation of `AGENTS.md`'s rule against forward-references to features that do not exist.
- **Failure scenario:** neither identifier is a credential and neither enables access on its own — GCP project ids and Artifact Registry paths are routinely non-secret and visible to anyone who can pull the image, and the TeamCity host sits behind authentication with a name trivially derivable from the organization, so the incremental reconnaissance value is negligible and there is no path from either string to compromise without an independent credential leak. The concrete consequence is that the "remove internal specifics" work is incomplete and that a dead, misleading CI prototype ships to public readers, who are told a migration is in effect that is not.
- **Evidence:** `.teamcity/pom.xml:18` (the literal server URL); `curl -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://jbr-fed.teamcity.com` → 401; `.teamcity/README.md:1-10` and `.teamcity/settings.kts:10, 92` (layout, pipeline id, hosted-agent condition); `.github/workflows/build-devcontainer.yaml:77, 104, 120`; `git grep -i teamcity -- .github/` → no output; GitHub API `private: false`, `visibility: public`.
- **Fix:** replace the literal TeamCity server URL with a Maven property supplied at generate time (`-Dteamcity.server.url=...`), or delete the `.teamcity` prototype entirely until the migration is real and drop its forward-referencing README. Move the GAR image path to a repository variable (`${{ vars.GAR_IMAGE }}`) alongside the two GCP variables the job already reads that way. Then re-run a grep for internal host and project identifiers over *all* tracked files, not only documentation prose, before declaring #578 complete.
- **Notes:** the verifier cut this from high to low and reclassified it: it is repository-hygiene and release-readiness completeness, not an information-disclosure vulnerability. The forward-reference half of the finding is what makes it worth fixing independently of the identifiers.
## 7. Public API surface and documentation

This group covers the public surface the three breaking removals and the new exception taxonomy
(#760) left behind, the façade export lists, and the published documentation — the reference pages,
the user guides, the mechanism pages and the tutorials whose stored outputs are served verbatim on
the docs site. It closes with the residue of two earlier fix verifications (alignment chat-template
and auditing dataset-guard work) and with the coverage claims that are supposed to keep this class
from recurring.

The single most serious entry here is **OPQ-517**: the flagship LLM fine-tuning tutorial publishes
ε = 1.09 for a run whose accounted cost is 8.99 because it prices an adaptively-clipped training
loop with the plain Gaussian chain. That is a wrong number on a public page, not a latent code
defect, and it is the only high-severity finding in the section.

### 7.1 Error contracts and the #760 exception taxonomy

**OPQ-505 — `calibrate()`'s runtime-unsafe fallback re-runs the whole binary search at the undivided Monte Carlo failure probability but still reports it as the overall union bound.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/calibration.py:331-341, 353` · accounting-confidence · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `calibrate()` deliberately splits its Monte Carlo failure budget across probes so that the adaptive binary search is covered by a union bound: `probe_count = max_iterations + 2` (`calibration.py:303`) and `probe_config = replace(overall_config, mc_failure_probability=overall/probe_count)` (`:304-309`). The primary path is sound because the calibrated parameter is re-evaluated exactly once, non-adaptively, under `overall_config` (`:320-330`) — that single evaluation is what licenses reporting the undivided value. The `if not runtime_safe:` fallback added by aa15e6ac (#912) instead re-runs the *entire* search via `_calibrate_impl` while still inside `with _use_discretization(overall_config)` (`:331-341`), so every probe of that second search consumes the full `mc_failure_probability`, and `achieved = result.achieved` is taken straight from an adaptively selected probe with no non-adaptive re-check. Line 353 then sets `result.mc_failure_probability = overall_config.mc_failure_probability`, contradicting the field's own docstring at `:79-81` ("Overall failure probability covering every adaptive Monte Carlo probe").
- **Failure scenario:** at the library's true defaults (`mc_resolution=1e-5`, `mc_failure_probability=1e-6`, 2 940 252 MC samples) a user calls `cal.calibrate(cal.epsilon_budget(8.0, delta=1e-5), lambda nm: balls_in_bins(mf_gaussian(nm, blt_strategy()), num_bins=5, n_steps=20), 0.5, 8.0)`. Instrumenting `_calibrate_impl` shows two invocations: the probe-config search converges, the single runtime re-evaluation lands above budget, and the fallback then performs 19 iterations / ≈21 probes each spending the undivided 1e-6 instead of 1e-6/102. `CalibrateResult.mc_failure_probability` is nevertheless reported as 1e-6 (mc_confidence 0.999999) where the honest simultaneous coverage is ≈2.1e-5 — a failure mass larger than the δ = 1e-5 the user calibrated at, i.e. the reported confidence is optimistic by roughly the probe count.
- **Evidence:** `calibration.py:303` and `:304-309` (probe split); `:320-330` (the single non-adaptive re-evaluation that protects the primary path); `:331-341` (the fallback search inside `_use_discretization(overall_config)`); `:353` (the reported value); `:79-81` (the documented contract); reproduced twice by wrapping `_calibrate_impl`, once at `mc_resolution=1e-2` and once at full defaults, both showing `invocations=2` with the second search at 1e-6 and 19 iterations; `packages/opaque-dpftrl/tests/accounting/test_b_min_sep.py:229` pins exactly the reported-equals-configured behaviour and `packages/opaque-dpsgd/tests/accounting/test_calibration.py:608` only pins that the fallback yields a safe `achieved`.
- **Fix:** either run the fallback search under `probe_config` too and take `achieved` from one non-adaptive re-evaluation at the chosen parameter, or report the honest union (`overall_config.mc_failure_probability * probes_performed`) when the fallback fires. Add a regression test that forces the fallback (monkeypatch `budget.evaluate` to return an unsafe value once) and asserts the reported `mc_failure_probability` covers every probe performed.
- **Notes:** the verifier reduced the ε dimension. Re-evaluating the same converged process at 1e-6/22, 1e-6 and 2.2e-5 gave ε = 7.98944 / 7.99999 / 8.01978 — under 0.3 % spread — and at default `mc_resolution` the value was insensitive, so the defect is a mislabelled confidence field rather than a materially understated epsilon. The fallback is reachable at default discretization and default tolerance, not only in a corner case, though it is configuration-dependent (a smaller `num_bins=2` / `n_steps=4` case did not trigger it).

**OPQ-506 — the serialization reference promises `InputTypeError` for an unregistered leaf while the code raises a bare `TypeError`, and the one guarding test cannot tell the two apart.** `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/serialization/_structural.py:37-47, 79, 114` · docs-vs-code · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** commit 7b26f655 (the #760 taxonomy) rewrote `docs/reference/serialization.md:19` from "raises `TypeError`" to "raises `InputTypeError` on both save and restore instead of being silently skipped", but never converted the raise. `_unrecognized_leaf` is still annotated `-> TypeError` and returns `TypeError(...)` (`_structural.py:37-47`), raised on the save path at `:79` and the restore path at `:114`; the module imports nothing from `opaque.exceptions`, and `_dispatch.py` does not translate it (its `_walk_load` only annotates exceptions from *registered* handlers, and the structural path is reached only after `resolve_serializer` misses). Because `InputTypeError` is a *subclass* of `TypeError` (`packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/exceptions/__init__.py:31-32`), neither `except InputTypeError:` nor `except OpaqueError:` catches the bare form. The sibling contract on the same doc page *was* converted — `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/serialization/_structural.py:38` imports `CheckpointError` and raises it at `:52` and `:67` — so this is an incomplete conversion, not a deliberate carve-out.
- **Failure scenario:** a caller writes the handler the exceptions page invites — `try: state = from_state_dict(template, torch.load(path)) except CheckpointError: abort_resume() except InputTypeError: report_unregistered_type()`, or a coarser `except OpaqueError` resume/start-fresh switch — around a state tree containing a leaf with no registered serializer and no generic-container shape (a custom optimizer-state object, a third-party schedule handle). Reproduced with `PYTHONPATH=packages/opaque-base/src`: both `opaque.serialization.state_dict(obj)` and `from_state_dict(obj, {})` raise a plain `TypeError('Cannot serialize/restore ...')` for which `isinstance(exc, InputTypeError)` and `isinstance(exc, OpaqueError)` are both `False`, so neither documented handler fires and the resume path dies with an unhandled `TypeError` instead of taking its fallback branch.
- **Evidence:** `_structural.py:37-47` (the returned `TypeError`), `:79` and `:114` (the two raise sites); `docs/reference/serialization.md:17-20` (the promise); `git show 7b26f655 -- docs/reference/serialization.md` (word changed with no source change under `packages/opaque-base/src`); `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/exceptions/__init__.py:31-32` (`class InputTypeError(OpaqueError, TypeError)`); the converted sibling at `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/serialization/_structural.py:38, 52, 67`; `packages/opaque-base/tests/serialization/test_fail_closed_dispatch.py:64-80` asserts only `pytest.raises(TypeError, match="Cannot serialize"/"Cannot restore")`, which passes identically before and after the intended conversion; stale prose persists in `serialization/__init__.py:7-9` and `_dispatch.py:7`, which still say `TypeError`.
- **Fix:** have `_unrecognized_leaf` build `InputTypeError` (imported from `opaque.exceptions`, which `opaque-base` already owns), update the two module docstrings, and tighten `test_fail_closed_dispatch.py` to `pytest.raises(InputTypeError, ...)` so CI holds the contract instead of prose.
- **Notes:** behaviour is fail-closed in every direction — nothing is silently dropped and no privacy parameter moves — so the cost is confined to error-handling ergonomics plus a broken taxonomy promise (`docs/reference/exceptions.md` advertises `OpaqueError` for "any semantic Opaque failure").

**OPQ-507 — `mpo_combine` moved its documented exception out of the `LookupError` hierarchy in a release shipped without a breaking-change marker.** `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/dpo/loss/_mpo.py:58, 70` · api-compat · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 7b26f655 (`feat(exceptions): add public exception taxonomy (#760)`) replaced `raise KeyError(...)` with `raise ConfigurationError(...)` at `_mpo.py:56-63` and `:70`, and rewrote the docstring's `Raises: KeyError` to `Raises: ConfigurationError` in the same hunk. `ConfigurationError` is `class ConfigurationError(OpaqueError, ValueError)` (`packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/exceptions/__init__.py:27`) with no `LookupError`/`KeyError` in its MRO, so an existing `except KeyError:` around the public `mpo_combine` stops catching. The commit subject carries no `!` and no `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer, so `git-cliff` does not categorize it as breaking for the release lines that contain it.
- **Failure scenario:** application code calling the public `opaque.alignment.dpo.loss.mpo_combine` and guarding the weights/losses key-mismatch with `except KeyError:` — the contract the pre-7b26f655 docstring promised — stops catching as of v0.15.4: the call now raises `opaque.exceptions.ConfigurationError`. `git tag --contains 7b26f655` shows the change shipped in v0.15.4, v0.15.5 and v0.15.6.rc1. The same swap was applied to two `KeyError` sites in `_dp_trainer.py` and one in `_sft_trainer.py`, so the exposure is three public-facing files.
- **Evidence:** `_mpo.py:56-63` and `:70` (the current raises); `git show 7b26f655 -- .../_mpo.py` (both sites were `raise KeyError(...)`, docstring rewritten in the same hunk); `packages/opaque-base/src/opaque/api/base/exceptions/__init__.py:27` (the MRO); `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/alignment/dpo/loss/__init__.py:41, 78` (genuine public export); `git tag --contains 7b26f655`; `packages/opaque-alignment/tests/dpo/loss/test_mpo.py:59-63` was updated in the same commit.
- **Fix:** either introduce a dedicated `class MissingLossKeyError(ConfigurationError, KeyError)` in the taxonomy — mirroring how `ConfigurationError` deliberately keeps `ValueError` — or record the change as breaking in the changelog for the release that contains 7b26f655.
- **Notes:** the verifier corrected two framing points without defeating the finding. The taxonomy docstring never markets `KeyError` preservation (it claims only that configuration/calibration/budget errors are `ValueError` subclasses and checkpoint failures `RuntimeError` subclasses), so this is not an inconsistency the taxonomy promised to avoid; and `_mpo.py` was not singled out — the commit converted all five `KeyError` raises in the repo, so it is a uniform intentional policy. What remains is process-level: a released public function's documented exception type left the `LookupError` hierarchy without the marker AGENTS.md requires. Failure is loud either way.

**OPQ-508 — `risk_at` lets a Rust `clamp` panic escape as `pyo3_runtime.PanicException`, which `except Exception` cannot catch.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/pld/mod.rs:420-426`; Python entry points `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_base.py:335-369` and `_accountant.py:195-205` · robustness · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `Pld::risk_at` computes `let max_risk = prior.min(1.0 - prior); metrics::bayes_risk(self, prior).clamp(0.0, max_risk)`. For any `prior` outside [0, 1] or NaN, `max_risk` is negative or NaN, and Rust's `f64::clamp` panics when `min > max` or either is NaN. Neither the Rust function, the PyO3 shim (`src/python/pld.rs:135-136`, `fn risk_at(&self, prior: f64) -> f64` with no `PyResult`) nor the Python wrapper (`_base.py:369` forwards `prior` untouched) guards the domain. The panic unwinds through pyo3 as `pyo3_runtime.PanicException`, whose MRO is `(PanicException, BaseException, object)` — it does *not* derive from `Exception`.
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced on `da.poisson(da.gaussian(1.0), 0.01) * 100` with the in-tree extension: `prior` 0.0 → 0.0, 0.5 → 0.4745867 and 1.0 → 0.0 return normally, while 1.5, −0.5, ±inf, NaN and 1e300 each panic in `core::num::f64` ("min > max, or either was NaN. min = 0.0, max = -0.5") and surface as `pyo3_runtime.PanicException` with a Rust backtrace on stderr. A defensive `try: log(acct.risk_at(prior)) except Exception: pass` does not absorb it (only `except BaseException` does), so a caller who passes 50 for "50 %" kills the process instead of getting a `ConfigurationError`.
- **Evidence:** `pld/mod.rs:420-426` (the variable-bound clamp); `src/python/pld.rs:135-136` (unguarded shim); `_base.py:335-369` and `_accountant.py:205` (unvalidated forwarding); the reproduced MRO and the non-firing `except Exception`; `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_budgets.py:235-236, 252` — `RiskBudget` is safe only because it validates `0 < prior < 1` at construction.
- **Fix:** guard before the clamp — return an error for a non-finite or out-of-[0,1] `prior`, or compute `max_risk = prior.min(1.0 - prior).max(0.0)` — and, better, validate in `_base.py:335` and raise `ConfigurationError('prior must be in [0, 1], got ...')` so the failure lands in the public taxonomy.
- **Notes:** severity was cut from medium: the finding's "aborts the whole training process" framing is speculative, since no trainer or accounting path calls `risk_at` during training and every in-library consumer is guarded, so the crash happens where the bad prior is supplied. The verifier did not audit the other variable-bound clamps named in the fix sketch (`pld/metrics.rs:273, 373`, `amplification/poisson.rs:301`); those clamp against `1.0 - infinity_mass`, which stays in [0, 1].

**OPQ-509 — `min_sep=0` crashes past its own guard: `ZeroDivisionError` from the Toeplitz strategies and raw `ValueError`/`OverflowError` from the Rust-backed ones.** `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_toeplitz.py:495-496, 536-537`, bypassed via `_band_mf.py:170-172` → `_sensitivity.py:64` · validation-ordering · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `minsep_sensitivity_squared` and `minsep_sensitivity_upper_bound` both hold the correct `if min_sep <= 0: raise ConfigurationError('min_sep must be positive')` guard (`_toeplitz.py:496`, `:537`), but the public strategy path never reaches them: `BandMfStrategy.sensitivity` calls `minsep_true_max_participations` first (`_band_mf.py:170-172`), and that helper computes `max_part_ub = _ceil_div(n, min_sep)` (`_sensitivity.py:64`) with `_ceil_div = -(x // -y)` (`:45`) and no guard of its own. The four strategies then disagree on the error type for the same bad input.
- **Failure scenario:** calling the documented public method `strategy.sensitivity(n_steps=16, min_sep=0)` — documented at `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md:74` and `docs/user-guide/noise.md:494-496` — raises `ZeroDivisionError: integer division or modulo by zero` for `band_mf_strategy(bands=4)` and `blt_strategy()`, and a raw `ValueError('Invalid parameter: min_sep must be >= 1')` from the Rust extension for `bisr_strategy(bandwidth=4)` and `lambda_cgd_strategy(lambda_=0.5)`; `min_sep=-3` gives `ConfigurationError` for the first pair and a raw `OverflowError("can't convert negative int to unsigned")` for the second. None of the four is in the `opaque.exceptions` taxonomy and the `ZeroDivisionError` names nothing useful.
- **Evidence:** `_toeplitz.py:496` and `:537` (the unreached guards); `_band_mf.py:170-172` (the earlier call); `_sensitivity.py:64` and `:45` (the unguarded `_ceil_div`); the four measured error types at `n_steps=16`; `_sensitivity.py:204-207` shows the intended message shape ("min_sep must be in the range [1, n], found ...") already exists for the banded path.
- **Fix:** validate `min_sep` (and `max_participations`) once at the public entry in `_mf_gaussian_noise.py` beside the existing `n_steps` / `noise_multiplier` / `key` checks, add the same check at the top of `minsep_true_max_participations` so the internal helpers cannot be reached with `min_sep <= 0`, and convert the Rust-side "Invalid parameter" and unsigned-conversion failures into `ConfigurationError` at the PyO3 boundary.
- **Notes:** the verifier removed the privacy dimension entirely. The accounting entry point *does* validate — `opaque.dpftrl.accounting.mf_gaussian(1.0, band_mf_strategy(bands=4), n_steps=16, min_sep=0)` raises `ConfigurationError('min_sep must be >= 1, got 0')` — and the original claim that `mf_gaussian_noise` defers a latent failure is wrong: `min_sep` is never used for noise generation, so with `min_sep ∈ {0, −5, 1, 8}` the constructed `noise_fn` produces bit-identical noise and the same published `noise_stddev` (1.2580589969236924). The value is silently ignored rather than latently fatal, so no wrong σ or ε can result; what is left is an error-taxonomy defect on a public method.

**OPQ-510 — accounting privacy-metric queries perform no argument-domain validation, so a NaN is laundered into a publishable-looking privacy number.** `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_base.py:183-228 (epsilon_at), 229-264 (delta_at), 297-333 (beta_at)`; Rust `packages/opaque-accounting/src/pld/mod.rs:270-272` · fail-open-behavior · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the #760 taxonomy commit converted every *constructor* validation in `opaque-accounting` but left the five privacy-metric *query* methods on `DpProcess` and `Accountant` with no argument validation at all, in Python or Rust. `epsilon_at`, `delta_at`, `advantage`, `beta_at` and `risk_at` all forward their argument straight into `Pld.*`; the only use of `delta` on the way is `min(configured_resolution, delta/2.0) if delta > 0.0`. On the Rust side `Pld::delta_at` is `metrics::delta(self, epsilon).clamp(0.0, 1.0)` with no domain guard. The same library validates these exact domains on the auditing side — `packages/opaque-auditing/src/opaque/api/auditing/one_run/_stats.py:23-25` raises `ConfigurationError('delta must be in [0, 1], got ...')` and `_gdp.py:172-173` the same for `alpha` — and `Accountant.beta_at` documents the precondition it does not enforce (`_accountant.py:183`, "alpha: Type-I error rate (false positive). Must be in [0, 1].", forwarded unchecked at `:193`).
- **Failure scenario:** requires a caller mistake — nothing in the library passes an out-of-domain argument. The substantive case is NaN laundering: on `da.poisson(da.gaussian(1.0), 0.01) * 100`, `proc.delta_at(float('nan'))` returns 5.00000096413629e-16 (identical to the truncation/infinity-mass floor returned for `delta_at(1e300)`) and `proc.beta_at(float('nan'))` returns 0.99999999999999944 — values indistinguishable from a genuinely strong guarantee — so a NaN produced by upstream arithmetic becomes a publishable-looking privacy number instead of an exception. Secondary: `beta_at(-0.5)` returns 0.99999999999999944 against its own documented `[0, 1]` precondition, while `est.epsilon_at(delta=2.0)` on a `OneRunEstimate` raises for the same class of argument.
- **Evidence:** `_base.py:183-227`, `:229-263`, `:297-333` (unvalidated forwarding); `pld/mod.rs:270-272` (unguarded clamp); `_accountant.py:183` and `:193` (documented-but-unenforced precondition); the auditing precedents at `_stats.py:23-25` and `_gdp.py:172-173`; measured values ε@1e-5 = 0.7180658390624926, ε@2.0 = 0.0, ε@inf = 0.0, ε@NaN = NaN, ε@0.0 = inf, δ@NaN = 5.00000096413629e-16, δ@−inf = 1.0, β@2.0 = 0.0, β@−0.5 = 0.99999999999999944, β@NaN = 0.99999999999999944; `docs/reference/accounting.md:104-110` documents the five methods with no stated domain and no Raises clause.
- **Fix:** validate at the Python boundary in `_base.py`, mirroring the auditing validators (`epsilon_at`: 0 ≤ delta ≤ 1 and finite; `delta_at`: epsilon ≥ 0 and finite; `beta_at`/`risk_at`: argument in [0, 1]; reject NaN everywhere) and raise `ConfigurationError` naming the offending value; mirror the checks into `Pld::{epsilon_at,delta_at,beta_at,risk_at}` for the `.pyi`-typed direct callers, and add a Raises line to the metric table in `docs/reference/accounting.md`.
- **Notes:** the verifier cut this from medium and narrowed it, refuting the headline cases: every mechanism is trivially (0, δ)-DP for δ ≥ 1, so `epsilon_at(2.0) = 0.0` is the correct extension of the definition; `delta_at` of a negative epsilon is well-defined; `beta_at(2.0) = 0.0` is degenerate but consistent. Only the NaN laundering, the unenforced `beta_at` precondition and the inconsistency with the auditing validators survive, and no library path reaches them, so no default run is affected — hence ε:no despite the original report.

**OPQ-511 — the public patches façade swallows `ImportError` raised *inside* a patch application, and `is_runtime_patched()` returns `True` when nothing was installed.** `packages/opaque-patches/src/opaque/api/patches/__init__.py:63-86, 101-102, 110-135, 152-159` · fail-open-behavior · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** every patch-install site in the façade wraps the *call* as well as the import in `except ImportError`. `apply_model_patches` puts `apply_transformers_model_patches(model, ...)` inside the same `try` as its import and swallows `ImportError` at `logger.debug("opaque: Hugging Face kernel patches not available.")` (`:63-76`), and does the same for `apply_peft_model_patches` with a bare `pass` (`:78-86`). Both symbols are *already* imported unconditionally at module scope (`:12-13`), so the inner imports are `sys.modules` hits that cannot fail — the `except ImportError` can only catch an `ImportError` raised from inside the patch call. `apply_runtime_patches` sets `_runtime_patches_applied = True` at `:101-102`, before any of the four per-concern blocks, and each of those (`:109-137`) wraps its call in `except ImportError: pass` with no log (only the `vmap_checkpointing` block at `:139-149` warns). The correct pattern exists one module away: `patches/torch/checkpoint/__init__.py:42-61` returns early leaving its flag `False` and sets `_is_checkpoint_patched = True` only after the applies succeed. Two further silent skips sit underneath: `transformers/_family.py:200-203` and `_factory.py:283-286` both do `try: mod = importlib.import_module(module_path) except ImportError: return`, and `_router.py:59-91` `_patch_forward` returns `False` without a log when the named class is absent.
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced on the current tree with the repo venv (torch 2.14.0+cu130, transformers 5.15.1, peft 0.20.0). A downstream user follows the documented extension path (`register_family(name, apply_fn)` in `transformers/_registry.py`) and their `apply_fn` raises `ImportError` because an internal transformers symbol it reaches for has moved. `opaque.patches.apply_model_patches(model, compat=True)` returns normally, emits only the DEBUG line, and the model keeps its un-vmap-safe attention path — #899's `ConfigurationError` at `_router.py:141-148` cannot fire because the family *is* registered.
