Also reproduced: `apply_runtime_patches(performance=False, compat=False)` installs nothing yet `is_runtime_patched()` returns `True` while `is_checkpoint_patched()` correctly returns `False`, so no public API distinguishes "entry point called" from "shims installed".
- **Evidence:** `patches/__init__.py:63-76` and `:78-86` (call inside the import `try`), `:12-13` (the module-scope imports that make the guard unreachable for a genuine import failure), `:101-102` (flag set first), `:109-137` (the silent per-concern swallows), `:152-159` (the overclaiming docstring); the correct pattern at `patches/torch/checkpoint/__init__.py:42-61`; `transformers/_family.py:200-203`, `_factory.py:283-286`, `_router.py:59-91` (the three lower silent skips); commit 15b89a92 (#899) added the fail-closed branch at `_router.py:141-148` for unregistered families only; both reproductions above.
- **Fix:** narrow each `try` to the import statement alone so an `ImportError` from inside a patch propagates; track which concerns actually installed and set `_runtime_patches_applied` from that; have `is_runtime_patched()` report installed concerns (or add `is_model_patched(model)`) so the documented check is real; replace the silent `except ImportError: return` in `_family.py` and `_factory.py` with a warning naming the failed module, and log when `_patch_forward` cannot find its class — a registered family that cannot find its own symbols is the same fail-open #899 closed for unregistered ones.
- **Notes:** latent rather than active for the shipped families. All 26 registered modeling modules import cleanly under the installed transformers 5.15.1 (0 failures), every component import in `_factory.py` is top-level and the Triton kernel imports live inside the patched forward, so a future in-range 5.x symbol move would be dropped by the `hasattr` guards in `_family.py:207-226` or by the local `except ImportError: return` rather than by the façade swallow — in every one of those paths the only signal is silence or a DEBUG line. The `is_runtime_patched` leg alone is weak (the docstring's summary line does say "True once `apply_runtime_patches` has run in this interpreter"); only the trailing sentence overclaims, and that leg was rejected on its own in an earlier audit pass.

**OPQ-512 — `tree_leaves` silently returns `[]` for the DP wrapper types whose sibling `global_norm` now rejects them loudly.** `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/pytree.py:111-129` · fail-open-behavior · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the #806 fix made `global_norm` raise `InputTypeError` when handed a `ClippedPytree` / `NoisedPytree` / `SecondMomentClippingOutput` / `SecondMomentNoiseOutput`, naming `.pytree` as the explicit numerical view (`pytree.py:377-400`). Its neighbour in the same module and the same `__all__` was not given the same treatment: `tree_leaves` (`:111-128`) does `flat, _ = _ot.tree_flatten(tree); return [x for x in flat if isinstance(x, torch.Tensor)]`, and because the wrappers are unregistered frozen dataclasses they flatten to a single non-tensor leaf and are filtered out.
- **Failure scenario:** a user holding the `ClippedPytree` returned by `clipped_grad` (or the `NoisedPytree` returned by a `noise_fn`) calls the public `opaque.pytree.tree_leaves` on it and gets `[]` with no error or warning, so a hand-rolled diagnostic such as `sum(p.square().sum() for p in tree_leaves(grads))` silently reads 0.0, or `torch.stack(tree_leaves(grads))` raises an empty-list error far from the real cause. Executed: `tree_leaves(ClippedPytree(pytree={'g': tensor([3.,4.])}, max_norm=1.0))` → `[]`, while `tree_flatten` on the same object returns `([ClippedPytree(...)], PyTreeSpec(*))` and `global_norm` on it raises `InputTypeError: ClippedPytree global norm is unsupported ... Use '.pytree' for an explicit numerical-only view`.
- **Evidence:** `pytree.py:111-128` (the filtering flatten); `pytree.py:377-400` (the hardened sibling); `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/pytree.py:1-31` (both names exported from the same public façade); the executed comparison above; the internal callers (`clipping/_pytree.py:475, 611`, `clipping/_distributed.py:141, 148, 217`, `distributed/_state.py:331`, `pytree.py:414`) all operate on raw gradient pytrees, so there is no internal miscomputation.
- **Fix:** give `tree_leaves` the same guard as `global_norm` — raise `InputTypeError` naming `.pytree`, or unwrap the DP wrappers before flattening — via a shared `_reject_dp_wrapper(tree)` helper, and extend it to `tree_flatten` / `tree_flatten_with_paths` / `partition`, which share the behaviour.
- **Notes:** observability and API consistency only; no library path is affected. Closed issue #807 (OPQ-333) fixed this class for `unscale_grads` and `global_norm` and was explicitly scoped not to include `tree_leaves`; its PR #869 did not touch it. A fix should cover the whole family: `tree_flatten` returns the wrapper as a single leaf, and `tree_map(lambda x: x*2, clipped)` returns a `ClippedPytree` with both the tensors *and* `max_norm` doubled.

### 7.2 Façade exports and the seams left by the breaking removals

**OPQ-513 — a stale `bound` entry in the trainer's mechanism-kwargs allowlist makes the error message advertise the accountant-less noise knob that #1021 deleted.** `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_training_arguments.py:229` vs `:1222-1228` · stale-after-removal · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 873de427 (`delete(dpsgd)!: remove unsupported bounded Gaussian noise (#1021)`) removed the bounded-Gaussian mechanism and added an explicit rejection at `_training_arguments.py:1222-1228`. b9b0a23e (`fix(transformers)!: validate privacy kwargs (#1024)`), merged about an hour later the same day, introduced `_MECHANISM_KWARGS` at `:228-229` with `"gaussian": frozenset({"bound", "compute_dtype"})` — carrying the removed knob forward into the allowlist that `_validate_privacy_kwargs` renders as `Allowed: {sorted(allowed)}` at `:282-289`. The two checks contradict each other in user-visible text.
- **Failure scenario:** a user passes an unrecognized mechanism kwarg with the default `privacy_noise_mechanism='gaussian'`. Reproduced: `privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs={'nope': 1.0}` → `"privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs contains unsupported keys for privacy_noise_mechanism='gaussian': ['nope']. Allowed: ['bound', 'compute_dtype']."` — advertising `bound`, deleted in #1021 precisely for having no matching privacy accountant. If the user then sets it, `{'bound': 1.0}` → `"privacy_noise_mechanism_kwargs['bound'] is unsupported: Opaque does not provide a matching privacy accountant."`
- **Evidence:** `_training_arguments.py:228-229` (the allowlist), `:1360-1365` (its consumer) and `:282-289` (the message template); `:1222-1228` (the unconditional rejection); `git show 873de427` and `git show b9b0a23e` (the one-hour ordering); both reproduced messages; no other bounded-Gaussian residue exists (`opaque.dpsgd.noise.__all__ == ['gaussian_noise']`).
- **Fix:** drop `"bound"` so the entry reads `frozenset({"compute_dtype"})`; the dedicated check at `:1222-1228` can stay as the friendly diagnostic or fold into the allowlist path with a mechanism-specific hint.
- **Notes:** purely diagnostic. The `bound` rejection at `:1222` runs *before* the allowlist at `:1360`, so the kwarg can never actually slip through — the allowlist entry is dead text that only ever surfaces inside an error message. Both paths fail closed; nothing is mis-trained.

**OPQ-514 — `docs/user-guide/distributed.md` advertises six `opaque.distributed` helpers that have no public import path, including the DDP equivalence self-check the DP-review protocol asks users to run.** `docs/user-guide/distributed.md:271-286` · docs-vs-code · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the table headed "`opaque.distributed` API summary" (`:261` heading, `:269-288` body) lists `reduce_scalar` (`:278`), `gather_tensors` (`:281`), `gather_pytree` (`:282`), `assert_pytree_equal` (`:283`), `sync_object` (`:285`) and `assert_scalar_equal` (`:286`). None is exported: `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/distributed/__init__.py:31-48` declares `__all__` as `all_reduce, barrier, gather_for_metrics, get_rank, get_world_size, is_distributed, is_main_process, local_shard, num_processes, process_index, reduce_pytree, reduce_pytree_, sum_gradients, sum_gradients_, sync, wait_for_everyone`. The two documented power-user submodules do not carry them either (`collectives.py` exports six collective helpers, `gradients.py` four). All six exist only at `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/distributed/_state.py:40, 90, 123, 311, 321, 391`, and `opaque/api/engine/distributed/__init__.py` re-exports only `sync` and `register_sync_type` from that module — so no `opaque.api.*` path exposes them either.
- **Failure scenario:** a user following the table writes `from opaque.distributed import assert_pytree_equal` (or any of the other five) to check that DDP ranks hold identical noised gradients, and gets `ImportError`. Verified: `hasattr` is `False` for all six on `opaque.distributed`, `opaque.distributed.collectives` and `opaque.distributed.gradients`.
- **Evidence:** `docs/user-guide/distributed.md:278, 281, 282, 283, 285, 286` (the six rows); `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/distributed/__init__.py:31-48` (the real `__all__`); the runtime `hasattr` probe across the façade and both submodules; `_state.py:40, 90, 123, 311, 321, 391` (where they actually live); `docs/reference/distributed.md` carries 11 `:::` identifiers and none of the six, so the two doc pages also disagree with each other.
- **Fix:** prefer exporting — add the six to `opaque.distributed.__all__` with matching mkdocstrings entries in `docs/reference/distributed.md`, since `assert_pytree_equal` / `assert_scalar_equal` are exactly the DDP equivalence self-checks the DP-review protocol expects users to be able to run. Otherwise drop the rows from the user-guide table and move them to a contributor note.
- **Notes:** fail-closed — an author-time `ImportError` with no runtime, numerical or privacy effect. The substantive part is the ARC-003 docs-vs-code split plus the fact that the guide's suggested cross-rank equivalence check has no supported path. Related to OPQ-526, which reports a second broken distributed-docs path.

**OPQ-515 — the fixed-clipping aux and stats types are split asymmetrically across the two stack type façades, so neither stack can name its own return types.** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/dpsgd/clipping/types.py:21-31`, mirror at `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/dpftrl/clipping/types.py:16-26` · api-surface · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `clipped_grad`, `auto_clipped_grad` and `per_group` are the *same objects* in both façades (`opaque.dpsgd.clipping.clipped_grad is opaque.dpftrl.clipping.clipped_grad` → `True`), but their return types are exported asymmetrically. `opaque.dpsgd.clipping.types.__all__` omits `ClippedGradAux`, `ClippedFunAux` and `ClipPytreeAux` while exporting `ClippingStats`; `opaque.dpftrl.clipping.types.__all__` does the mirror. Both types are produced on both stacks: `return_aux=True` yields `opaque.api.engine.clipping._clipped_grad.ClippedGradAux` and `return_stats=True` yields `opaque.api.engine.clipping._clipped_fun.ClippingStats`, on either façade. The shared ancestor `opaque.api.engine.clipping.types` exports the full set, so this is drift in two hand-written lists, not a design boundary.
- **Failure scenario:** a DP-SGD user annotating a training step writes `from opaque.dpsgd.clipping.types import ClippedGradAux` and gets `ImportError`, even though `opaque.dpsgd.clipped_grad(..., return_aux=True)` returns exactly that class; symmetrically `from opaque.dpftrl.clipping.types import ClippingStats` raises `ImportError` while `opaque.dpftrl.clipped_grad(..., return_stats=True)` returns `ClippingStats`. Both reproduced. The only workarounds are importing from the other stack's façade or from the private `opaque.api.engine` tree.
- **Evidence:** `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/dpsgd/clipping/types.py:21-31` and `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/dpftrl/clipping/types.py:16-26` (the two `__all__` lists, symmetric difference `{AdaptiveClipState, AdaptiveClippedGradAux, ClipPytreeAux, ClippedFunAux, ClippedGradAux, ClippingStats}`); the identity check on the three shared factories; the reproduced `ImportError` in both directions; `docs/reference/clipping.md:32-33` documents `ClippedGradAux` and `ClippedFunAux` as public aux types on a page that renders `opaque.dpsgd.clipping.types` at `:89`, which cannot show them.
- **Fix:** have both type façades export the full set for the shared engine-level clipping factories — add `ClippedGradAux`, `ClippedFunAux` and `ClipPytreeAux` to `opaque.dpsgd.clipping.types` and `ClippingStats` to `opaque.dpftrl.clipping.types` — keeping only `AdaptiveClipState` / `AdaptiveClippedGradAux` DP-SGD-exclusive, since adaptive clipping genuinely is.
- **Notes:** the verifier found the gap broader than reported: `opaque.dpsgd.clipping.fun` exports `clipped_fun` / `clip_pytree`, whose aux types are likewise unnameable from the DP-SGD types façade. Annotation and import ergonomics only — no privacy, numerical or runtime impact.

**OPQ-516 — `docs/reference/accounting.md` calls `opaque.accounting.calibration` a submodule, but it is an attribute re-export of the internal module and `import opaque.accounting.calibration` fails.** `docs/reference/accounting.md:677`; façade at `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/accounting/__init__.py` · api-surface · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `opaque.accounting.__all__` lists `amplification`, `calibration`, `composition`, `discretization` and `mechanisms`, and each resolves as an attribute — but each *is* the internal module object: `opaque.accounting.calibration.__name__ == 'opaque.api.accounting.core.calibration'`, likewise for the other four. The package directory holds only `__init__.py`, `types.py` and `py.typed`, so they are not submodules of the public façade: `from opaque.accounting import calibration` works while `import opaque.accounting.calibration` raises `ModuleNotFoundError`. This also leaks the contributor namespace into tracebacks, `help()`, `repr` and pickled references, against ARC-003's split between public façade paths and `opaque.api.*` internals.
- **Failure scenario:** a user following `docs/reference/accounting.md:677` ("Submodule: `opaque.accounting.calibration`") writes `import opaque.accounting.calibration as cal` and gets `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'opaque.accounting.calibration'` — verified for all five names, while the sibling façades `opaque.dpsgd.accounting.*`, `opaque.dpftrl.accounting.*`, `opaque.auditing.one_run`, `opaque.patches.kernels` and `opaque.accounting.types` all ship real importable submodules. The attribute form works, so the cost is confusion plus roughly 15 unresolvable `:mod:` / `:func:` cross-references in shipped docstrings.
- **Evidence:** the runtime `__name__` probe and the `ModuleNotFoundError` for all five names; `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/accounting/__init__.py` (the `from opaque.api.accounting.core import (...)` re-export and the `__all__` entries); `docs/reference/accounting.md:677` (the only "Submodule:" label in `docs/`); the contrast probe over the sibling façades; the affected docstring cross-references, e.g. `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/api/accounting/core/_budgets.py:14` (`:func:~opaque.accounting.calibration.calibrate`) and `core/types.py:11-13` (`:mod:opaque.accounting.{mechanisms,amplification,composition}.types`).
- **Fix:** either add thin re-export modules at `packages/opaque-accounting/src/opaque/accounting/{amplification,calibration,composition,discretization,mechanisms}.py`, matching how `opaque.dpsgd.accounting.*` is shaped, or reword `:677` to say the name is an attribute of `opaque.accounting` rather than an importable submodule.
- **Notes:** mitigated by the code block immediately under the offending line, which shows the form that does work (`from opaque.accounting import calibration as cal`). No effect on accounting results.

### 7.3 Documentation truth

**OPQ-517 — the published LLM fine-tuning tutorial reports ε = 1.09 for an adaptively-clipped run whose accounted cost is 8.99, because the noisy clip-rate release is never priced.** `docs/tutorials/llm_finetuning.ipynb` cell 11 (nb JSON lines 420-429) and cell 13; published output at nb JSON line 377 · docs-drift-epsilon · **high** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the flagship "Fine-tuning an LLM" tutorial builds its gradient function with `adaptive_clipped_grad(..., initial_clipping_norm=CLIP_NORM, target_quantile=0.80, clipping_norm_min=0.01, clipping_norm_max=10.0, key=key(0), normalize_by=BATCH_SIZE)` and never passes `fraction_noise_std`, so it takes the 0.05 default (`_DEFAULT_FRACTION_NOISE_STD`, `packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/clipping/_adaptive.py:27`); `_sample_noisy_clipping_rate` in the same module adds Gaussian noise to the clipping rate and `_adaptive_clipping_norm_update` consumes it every step, so a noisy quantile statistic is genuinely released on each of the 20 steps — the stored outputs show the threshold moving from 0.013 to 0.027. Both the pre-computation in cell 11 and the per-step tracker in cell 13 nevertheless price the run as `dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(NOISE_MULTIPLIER), sample_rate, truncated_batch_size=MAX_BATCH_SIZE, dataset_size=len(train_dataset)) * steps` — the plain Gaussian chain, with no `adaclip` wrapper. `grep -ic adaclip docs/tutorials/llm_finetuning.ipynb` returns 0 and no markdown cell mentions the extra cost, even though the repo's own guidance is the opposite: `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:230-249` states "Adaptive clipping introduces an additional privacy cost (the noisy clipping rate query). Account for it using `dpsgd_acc.adaclip()`", and `examples/train_dpsgd.py:1553` builds exactly that chain.
- **Failure scenario:** measured with the repo venv at the notebook's own constants (noise multiplier 1.5, sample rate 8/500, `truncated_batch_size=12`, `dataset_size=500`, 20 steps, δ = 1e-5): the notebook chain gives **1.0944**, matching the shipped outputs "Expected final epsilon: 1.09" and "Final: loss=6.752, epsilon=1.09"; the same chain with `dpsgd_acc.adaclip(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(1.5), fraction_noise_std=0.05, expected_batch_size=8)` as the inner mechanism — the form `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:230-249` mandates for exactly this configuration — gives **8.9928**. A reader who copies the tutorial's accounting line into their own adaptive-clipping run therefore reports an epsilon roughly 8× below what their mechanism provides, and the number is published verbatim on the docs site because `mkdocs.yml` renders committed outputs.
- **Evidence:** cell 11's `adaptive_clipped_grad(...)` call with no `fraction_noise_std`, and `_adaptive.py:27` for the 0.05 default; `_sample_noisy_clipping_rate` / `_adaptive_clipping_norm_update` in the same module, plus the stored clip-threshold drift 0.013 → 0.027 as runtime proof the query is released; cell 11's `expected_eps = ...` and cell 13's per-step tracker, both on the plain Gaussian chain; stored output at nb JSON line 377; `grep -ic adaclip` → 0; `docs/user-guide/clipping.md:230-249` and `examples/train_dpsgd.py:1553` (the prescribed form, used by the example script); the measured 1.0944 vs 8.9928; `.github/workflows/pr.yml:290-302` executes only `accounting_and_calibration.ipynb`, `dp_sgd_training.ipynb` and `dp_ftrl_training.ipynb`.
- **Fix:** wrap the inner mechanism in `dpsgd_acc.adaclip(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(NOISE_MULTIPLIER), fraction_noise_std=0.05, expected_batch_size=BATCH_SIZE)` in both cell 11 and cell 13, re-execute the notebook in place so the published outputs carry the honest number, and add `llm_finetuning.ipynb` to the `pr.yml` execution list. Longer term, add an in-notebook assertion (as `examples/quickstart.py:99` does) tying the reported epsilon to the mechanism actually constructed.
- **Notes:** this is the only tutorial that uses adaptive clipping, so the defect is confined to one page — but it is a published privacy number in the optimistic direction, which is why the verifier kept it at high. Adding the notebook to CI would *not* catch it: the wrong chain runs cleanly. See OPQ-538 for the coverage gap that lets this sit unnoticed.

**OPQ-518 — the accounting reference documents `dpftrl_acc.poisson`'s `sample_rate` only as a per-round probability, while the implementation requires the per-active-band conditional rate.** `docs/reference/accounting.md:468`; same omission in `docs/user-guide/accounting.md:327-340, 495-512`, `docs/user-guide/dp-ftrl.md:73-80, 103-112` and `docs/index.md:84-89` · docs-drift-epsilon · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `dpftrl_acc.poisson(inner, sample_rate, *, n_steps)` treats `sample_rate` as the inclusion probability *conditional on the active band*, roughly `bands ×` the whole-dataset rate. `CyclicPoissonSampler`'s module docstring says so ("Step *i* activates only group *i % bands*; each example in that group is included independently with probability `sample_rate`", `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/sampling/_poisson.py:1-20`, with the class note "Expected batch size per step is `|group| * sample_rate` where `|group| = |D| / bands`"), the accountant's own class docstring repeats it (`packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpftrl/amplification/_poisson.py:50-54`, "BandMF requires cyclic sampling over fixed dataset partitions; `sample_rate` is conditional on the active partition"), and the trainer converts explicitly (`packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dpftrl.py:130-163`, `group_rate = float(sample_rate) * local_size / group_size`). The reference bullet says only "Poisson sampling probability per round", and the worked examples in the user guide, the DP-FTRL guide and the index all show `bands=10` / `bands=64` beside a bare `sample_rate=0.01` with no conversion. The only correct statement in the docs tree is a Tips bullet on a different page, `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md:321-325`.
- **Failure scenario:** documentation gap on a privacy-critical parameter, no code defect. A reader who applies the DP-SGD convention `q = expected_batch_size / dataset_size` *consistently* to both sampler and accountant is still soundly accounted — the realized batch is simply `bands ×` smaller than intended, a utility bug. The privacy exposure appears when the reader then fixes the sampler to the conditional rate to recover the intended batch size while leaving the global rate in the accountant: nothing validates the pairing, because `q_global < q_conditional` always passes the (0, 1] check. Measured at the docs' own `bands=10`, `n_steps=1000`, noise multiplier 1.0, δ = 1e-5: `q=0.01` reports **0.71807** where `q=0.1` is the truth at **7.04689** — a 9.8× understatement.
- **Evidence:** `docs/reference/accounting.md:468` (the bullet); `sampling/_poisson.py:1-20` (the sampler's conditional semantics); `accounting/dpftrl/amplification/_poisson.py:50-54` (the accountant's matching requirement); `_dpftrl.py:130-163` (`_cyclic_poisson_group_rate`, the explicit global→conditional conversion); `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md:321-325` (the one correct statement); the measured 0.718 vs 7.047 pair; measured at the larger configuration too, `bands=64`, `n_steps=15624`: `sample_rate=0.01` → 0.9857 vs `sample_rate=0.64` → 102.1295.
- **Fix:** change the reference bullet to "per-active-band conditional inclusion probability; for BandMF with `bands` groups this is `expected_batch_size / floor(dataset_size / bands)`, i.e. roughly `bands ×` the whole-dataset rate", repeat it in the `dpftrl_acc.poisson` sections of `docs/user-guide/accounting.md` and `docs/user-guide/dp-ftrl.md`, and derive the rate from batch size and bands in the worked examples the way `docs/tutorials/dp_ftrl_training.ipynb` cell 5 already does (`sample_rate = batch_size * bands / n_samples`).
- **Notes:** the verifier downgraded this from high and rewrote the scenario. The reference never literally says "whole-dataset" — it is silent — and every place in the repo that pairs a sampler with this accountant uses one variable for both (`docs/tutorials/dp_ftrl_training.ipynb:152`, `examples/train_dpftrl.py:1224`), which is mathematically sound. The original 104× figure did not reproduce; 9.8× at the documented configuration did. The epsilon understatement therefore requires the reader to feed the accountant a different number than the sampler, which nothing detects.

**OPQ-519 — the sampling reference denies that `CyclicPoissonSampler` has a batch-size cap and denies that `dpftrl_acc.poisson` has a truncated form; both are false, and the cap is silently constructible for `bands > 1` where no accountant supports it.** `docs/reference/sampling.md:193-196` · docs-vs-behavior · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the page asserts "There is no batch-size cap on this sampler; `dpftrl_acc.poisson` matches uncapped Poisson draws only." Both halves are false at HEAD: `inspect.signature(CyclicPoissonSampler.__init__)` is `(self, data_source, sample_rate, bands=1, n_steps=1, partition_type=PartitionType.EQUAL_SPLIT, truncated_batch_size: int | None = None, *, key)` and `inspect.signature(opaque.dpftrl.accounting.poisson)` is `(inner, sample_rate, *, n_steps, truncated_batch_size=None, dataset_size=None)`. The parameter tables at `docs/reference/sampling.md:184-190` and `docs/user-guide/sampling.md:164-172` both omit the parameter, while the mkdocstrings directive on the same reference page (`::: opaque.dpftrl.sampling.CyclicPoissonSampler`, `:258`) renders the class docstring, which documents the cap *and* its identity-only accounting restriction — so the rendered page contradicts itself.
- **Failure scenario:** two consequences, both on the direct library surface. (a) A reader never learns that the truncated form of `dpftrl_acc.poisson` exists for identity MF. (b) A hand-rolled `bands > 1` run can cap its batches while no matching accounting exists: executed, `CyclicPoissonSampler(range(1000), sample_rate=0.5, bands=4, n_steps=20, truncated_batch_size=60, key=key(0))` constructs without complaint and yields exactly 60 examples on all 20 steps where the uncapped sampler yields 112-136, but `dpftrl_acc.poisson` refuses that configuration (`ConfigurationError: truncated Poisson is only supported for IdentityStrategy inner (BandMfStrategy per-group truncation is not implemented)`), leaving only the plain-Poisson PLD — which is optimistic for a capped sampler. Measured in the DP-SGD analogue at noise multiplier 1.1, q = 0.032, `dataset_size=1000`, 500 steps, δ = 1e-5: plain **3.8333** versus truncated **15.7361** (4.1×).
- **Evidence:** `docs/reference/sampling.md:193-196` (the two false sentences); the two `inspect.signature` results; the executed capped-vs-uncapped batch sizes; the `ConfigurationError` from `dpftrl_acc.poisson`; `docs/reference/sampling.md:184-190` and `docs/user-guide/sampling.md:164-172` (both tables omit the parameter) versus the rendered docstring at `:258`; `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/sampling/_poisson.py:15-18, 68-73` (the cap is documented in code) and `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/accounting/dpftrl/amplification/_poisson.py:120-130` (the rejection); `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_dpftrl.py:229-236` rejects the combination in the trainer.
- **Fix:** replace the two sentences with the truth — the sampler accepts `truncated_batch_size` and it may only be used with `bands == 1` / `IdentityStrategy`, where `dpftrl_acc.poisson(..., truncated_batch_size=, dataset_size=)` supplies the matching truncated-Poisson PLD — and add the parameter to both tables. Separately, make `CyclicPoissonSampler.__init__` raise when `truncated_batch_size is not None and bands > 1`, so the unaccountable configuration cannot be constructed at all.
- **Notes:** `DPTrainer` is fail-closed here (`_dpftrl.py:229-236` rejects `truncated_batch_size` under `sampling_mode='cyclic_poisson'`), so no default or trainer-driven configuration is affected — the exposure is hand-rolled library code. The finding's original 9.2359 magnitude did not reproduce; the direction and rough scale did (3.83 vs 15.74 at the configuration the verifier used).

**OPQ-520 — the privacy-auditing tutorial prices a shuffled fixed-batch `DataLoader` with Poisson amplification and under-counts its own steps — the exact OPQ-355 defect, surviving in the sibling notebook the closing sweep did not touch.** `docs/tutorials/privacy_auditing.ipynb` cell 9 (nb JSON line 271) and cell 13 (nb JSON lines 372-373) · docs-drift-epsilon · **medium** · ε:yes · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-355 was closed as part of sweep #843 and is genuinely fixed in `docs/getting-started/quickstart.md` (now an include of the runnable `examples/quickstart.py`, which uses `PoissonSampler`) and in `docs/tutorials/dp_sgd_training.ipynb` (cell 13 now iterates `PoissonSampler(dataset, sample_rate=..., n_steps=total_steps, key=key(43))` with the comment "The sampler and accountant use the same independent-inclusion process"). The identical pattern survives untouched in the auditing tutorial, which trains with `DataLoader(train_data, batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=True)` (fixed-size shuffled batches, no Opaque sampler, `drop_last=False`) and then computes its "Theoretical epsilon" with `sample_rate = batch_size / len(train_data)` and `total_steps = n_epochs * (len(train_data) // batch_size)` fed into `dpsgd_acc.poisson(dpsgd_acc.gaussian(noise_multiplier), sample_rate) * total_steps`. Shuffled fixed-size batching is not Poisson subsampling, and the floor division drops the partial batch the loader actually yields.
- **Failure scenario:** run at HEAD with the notebook's own constants (`n_samples=500`, `batch_size=64`, `n_epochs=200`, noise multiplier 0.5, δ = 1e-5): `coin_flip(num_canaries=500, key=key(42))` gives a 252-row training subset, the loader yields 4 batches per epoch, so the loop runs **800** steps while the notebook computes `200 * (252 // 64) = 600`. Epsilons: **250.89** at the documented 600 steps, **314.46** at the 800 actually run, **519.70** with no amplification at all for a deterministic shuffled partition — the published number is the smallest of the three. The tutorial's own bound-check passes either way (audited 2.04 against a theoretical 298.83 in its committed output), so the harm is that it teaches an unsound way to compute the very bound the audit is checked against.
- **Evidence:** cell 9's `DataLoader(..., shuffle=True)`; cell 13's `sample_rate` / `total_steps` / `dpsgd_acc.poisson(...) * total_steps`; the measured 252-row subset, 4 batches per epoch, 600 documented vs 800 actual steps; the three measured epsilons 250.89 / 314.46 / 519.70; the fixed sibling `docs/tutorials/dp_sgd_training.ipynb` cell 13; `.github/workflows/pr.yml:290-302` does not include `privacy_auditing.ipynb`; the notebook's committed outputs (263-row subset, theoretical ε 298.83) were produced by an older tree.
- **Fix:** switch the training loop to `PoissonSampler(train_data, sample_rate=..., n_steps=total_steps, key=...)` as `dp_sgd_training.ipynb` now does, or keep the shuffled loader and price it without Poisson amplification; either way derive `total_steps` from `len(train_loader) * n_epochs` rather than floor division, and add the notebook to the `pr.yml` execution list.
- **Notes:** documentation only, no library defect. The issue that subsumed OPQ-355 (#843, "Sweep A — regenerate and execute every documented accounting snippet") is closed as completed by PR #1034 and named only `quickstart.md` plus `dp_sgd_training.ipynb` cells 9/13 in its evidence; the only open docs child of #775 is #845, scoped to citation hypotheses. Related to OPQ-538.

**OPQ-521 — the README and installation-guide "Extras" block drops the JetBrains index, so the documented command installs an unrelated PyPI project named `opaque`; six of the ten distribution names are unclaimed.** `README.md:77-81`; identical block at `docs/getting-started/installation.md:31-36`; `AGENTS.md:126-138` · docs-vs-behavior · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the primary install commands correctly pin `--index-url https://packages.jetbrains.team/pypi/p/fed/python/simple/` (verified reachable anonymously, serving `opaque` 0.15.x and all ten sub-distributions), but the "Extras" block directly below issues four bare commands with no index: `pip install "opaque[auditing]"`, `"opaque[dpftrl]"`, `"opaque[transformers]"`, `"opaque[all]"`. `AGENTS.md`'s installation matrix is index-less for all eleven of its commands. On PyPI the name `opaque` belongs to a different project — version 1.0.0, author Stefan Marsiske, home page `https://github.com/stef/libopaque`, summary "python libopaque wrapper", whose only artifact ships a regular `opaque/__init__.py` — and pip only *warns* about an unknown extra.
- **Failure scenario:** executed in a clean venv with pip 26.2.1: `pip install "opaque[all]"` resolves against PyPI, prints `WARNING: opaque 1.0.0 does not provide the extra 'all'`, and installs `opaque 1.0.0` plus `pysodium` — not Opaque. The user's subsequent `import opaque.dpsgd...` fails with no hint that the wrong distribution was fetched; no modern-pip hard error rescues them. Separately, six of the ten sub-distribution names are unregistered on PyPI (`opaque-base`, `opaque-engine`, `opaque-dpsgd`, `opaque-patches`, `opaque-transformers`, `opaque-alignment` all 404), where the other four carry defensive 999.9.9 dependency-confusion stubs uploaded 2026-08-12 (`opaque-dpftrl`, `opaque-accounting`, `opaque-auditing`, `opaque-optimizers`); anyone can claim the six and serve arbitrary code to a reader following the index-less matrix or to any resolver that falls back to PyPI.
- **Evidence:** `README.md:64-81` and `docs/getting-started/installation.md:18-37` (pinned primary block, unpinned extras block directly below); `AGENTS.md:126-138` (index-less matrix); the PyPI API response for `opaque` (1.0.0, Marsiske, libopaque wrapper, `top_level.txt` = `opaque`); the 404/200 survey of all ten names with the four stub upload dates; the JetBrains index spot-check (all names 200); the executed `pip install --dry-run "opaque[all]"` transcript.
- **Fix:** repeat `--index-url https://packages.jetbrains.team/pypi/p/fed/python/simple/` on every command in the extras blocks of `README.md` and `docs/getting-started/installation.md` (or show a `pip.conf` / `[tool.uv.index]` snippet once and reference it), do the same in `AGENTS.md`'s matrix, and register the remaining six defensive stubs alongside the four already published.
- **Notes:** the verifier discounted the finding's "worse" variant — in a single venv every `opaque-*` wheel unpacks into the same `site-packages/opaque/` directory, so libopaque's `__init__.py` converting it to a regular package does not by itself hide sibling subpackages. Milestone issues #559 (JetBrains registry migration) and #574 (dependency security alerts) are both closed and neither covered this. No privacy or correctness impact on a working install; the defect is that a documented command reproducibly installs a foreign package and that six names are squattable.

**OPQ-522 — the BSR mechanism page's only accounting snippet raises `TypeError`, and both its parameter tables document arguments the API does not accept.** `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bsr.md:21-27` (snippet), `:40-46` and `:50-55` (tables) · docs-drift-api · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `bsr_strategy` is `(*, bandwidth: int, alpha: float, beta: float) -> BsrStrategy`. The accounting snippet calls `bsr_strategy(bandwidth=8, n_steps=total_steps, min_sep=steps_per_epoch, max_participations=num_epochs, alpha=1.0, beta=0.95)` and the "Strategy parameters" table at `:43-45` lists `n_steps`, `min_sep` and `max_participations` as strategy parameters; none is accepted. The "Accounting parameters" table at `:52-54` additionally lists `sensitivity` and `gram_matrix`, but `balls_in_bins(inner, *, num_bins, n_steps)` takes neither and `BsrStrategy.gram_matrix` is a method, not an attribute. The page contradicts itself: the noise snippet at `:63-76` uses the correct `bsr_strategy(bandwidth=8, alpha=1.0, beta=0.95)` and passes the horizon and participation pattern to `mf_gaussian_noise`, which is the split the sibling pages state explicitly (`docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/bisr.md:17-20`, `lambda-cgd.md:16-22`: the strategy is a recipe, the amplifier supplies the participation pattern and horizon).
- **Failure scenario:** a reader copies the only accounting recipe on the published BSR page and gets `TypeError: bsr_strategy() got an unexpected keyword argument 'n_steps'` on its first statement — reproduced verbatim at HEAD. Removing the three arguments gives the correct result: `balls_in_bins(mf_gaussian(1.0, bsr_strategy(bandwidth=8, alpha=1.0, beta=0.95)), num_bins=100, n_steps=500).epsilon_at(1e-5)` = 37.93, so only the strategy call is wrong.
- **Evidence:** the `inspect.signature` of `bsr_strategy`; the reproduced `TypeError`; `bsr.md:21-27` (the offending call) versus `bsr.md:63-76` (the correct one on the same page); `bsr.md:43-45` and `:52-54` (the two tables); `type(getattr(s, 'gram_matrix'))` is `method`; the verified corrected end-to-end value 37.93; `bisr.md:17-20` and `lambda-cgd.md:16-22` (the sibling pages that get the split right).
- **Fix:** drop `n_steps` / `min_sep` / `max_participations` from the `bsr_strategy(...)` call and from the strategy table, move them to the amplifier as `bisr.md` and `lambda-cgd.md` already do, and delete the `sensitivity` / `gram_matrix` rows from the accounting table — both are derived internally, not passed.
- **Notes:** documentation only and loud on the first line, with no epsilon consequence, because the `balls_in_bins` amplifier already supplies the participation geometry. Sweep #843 ("executable snippets") and #844 ("reference tables") are both closed and neither touched this page.

**OPQ-523 — the BLT parameter table publishes a non-existent `error` knob and a `max_buffers` range whose upper half is hard-rejected.** `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/blt.md:195-196` and the Tips bullet at `:206-208` · docs-drift-defaults · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the table still lists `error | "max" or "mean" | Error metric to optimize`, with a Tips bullet advising how to choose between the two values, but `inspect.signature(blt_strategy)` is `(*, max_buffers: int = 10, momentum: float = 1.0, lr_schedule: Schedule | None = None)` — there is no `error` parameter anywhere on the public surface. The value is hard-coded `error="max"` at `packages/opaque-dpftrl/src/opaque/api/dpftrl/noise/_blt.py:83` inside `_blt_optimize_cached`, and the trainer's allowlist for the same strategy is `frozenset({"max_buffers", "momentum", "lr_schedule"})` (`packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trainer/_training_arguments.py:231`). The table also publishes `max_buffers | 1 - 20 (default 10)` while `_blt_math.py:45` sets `_MAX_OPTIMIZATION_BUFFERS = 15` and `:1290-1293` raises above it — and it omits the two parameters `blt_strategy` really takes besides `max_buffers`.
- **Failure scenario:** a reader picks a value from the published 1-20 range: `blt_strategy(max_buffers=16)` constructs fine, and the first accounting query — `mf_gaussian(1.0, s, n_steps=500, min_sep=100, max_participations=5).epsilon_at(1e-5)` — raises `ConfigurationError: max_buffers > 15 will likely cause numerical issues.` (the same call at `max_buffers=15` returns 21.457). A reader following the `error` row and its Tips bullet cannot reach the documented `"mean"` behaviour at all: the parameter does not exist and the trainer's `mf_blt` key allowlist rejects it.
- **Evidence:** `blt.md:195` (the `error` row), `:206-208` (the Tips bullet), `:196` (the 1-20 range); `inspect.signature(blt_strategy)`; `_blt.py:83` (the hard-coded `error="max"`); `_training_arguments.py:231` (the trainer allowlist); `_blt_math.py:45` and `:1290-1293` (the real ceiling and its raise); the executed `max_buffers=16` failure and the `max_buffers=15` success returning 21.457; `momentum` is not named on the page at all and `lr_schedule` only appears in prose at `blt.md:87`.
- **Fix:** delete the `error` row and its Tips bullet (or expose the parameter if it is meant to be user-selectable), change the range to `1 - 15 (default 10)` to match the validator, and add the missing `momentum` and `lr_schedule` rows.
- **Notes:** this is the surviving half of OPQ-358. The training-arguments half *was* fixed — `docs/user-guide/huggingface/training-arguments.md:150` now shows the `mf_blt` default `{"max_buffers": 10}`, matching `_training_arguments.py:259`, and the `noise_calibration_kwargs` defaults match `_training_arguments.py:1048`. The verifier found no deprecated alias, kwargs passthrough or config gate that would make the documented `error` knob reachable. Loud failure, no privacy impact; the cost is a misled reader and a wasted debugging cycle.

**OPQ-524 — `docs/reference/accounting.md` still warns that bare BandMF accounting prices only one participation, two hours after #998 made it participation-aware; the two reference pages now give contradicting instructions.** `docs/reference/accounting.md:420-421` · docs-drift · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 79acf31d (#1022, 2026-09-11 08:10:34) added "Bare BandMF and Identity sensitivity currently accounts for one participation. Do not use bare calls with these strategies to budget repeated participation." Two hours later e44d6ab6 (#998, 2026-09-11 10:17:06) made BandMF participation-aware; its `--stat` shows it touched `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md` only, not this page. The sentence is now half-false, and `band-mf.md:176-183` — which #998 *did* update — now affirmatively instructs the reader to use the bare path with the schema ("Pass the participation schema you actually run under — `mf_gaussian(sigma, strategy, n_steps=n, min_sep=b, max_participations=k)` — because the bare PLD is calibrated to the schema sensitivity S").
- **Failure scenario:** a reader consulting the accounting reference is told not to budget repeated participation through bare BandMF calls, while the BandMF mechanism page tells them to do exactly that. Measured: `band_mf_strategy(bands=8).sensitivity(n_steps=64, min_sep=8, max_participations=k)` returns 1.0 / 1.4142 / 2.0 / 2.8284 for k = 1/2/4/8, and bare `mf_gaussian` epsilon rises monotonically in k for BandMF while staying flat for Identity — so the BandMF half of the warning is false and the Identity half is true.
- **Evidence:** `docs/reference/accounting.md:420-421` (still present at 12146ec4); `git log -S 'Bare BandMF and Identity sensitivity currently accounts'` → 79acf31d; the two commit timestamps; `git show --stat e44d6ab6` (touches `band-mf.md` only); the measured sensitivity ladder; `docs/mechanisms/dp-ftrl/band-mf.md:176-183` (the contradicting instruction).
- **Fix:** rewrite as: bare BandMF accounting honours `min_sep` / `max_participations` since #998, while bare Identity accounting still prices one participation regardless and must not be used to budget repeated participation (link the Identity issue). Add a docs example exercising the BandMF bare path so the #1034 doc-execution gate covers it.
- **Notes:** the verifier lowered this from medium and dropped the ε dimension: the stale sentence errs in the *conservative* direction — it steers users away from a path that now works — and the chain "reader discounts the Identity half and walks into the 145× under-report" is speculative. What is established is a direct contradiction between two reference pages about how to budget repeated participation, on a page whose single stale sentence is also the only documented warning about the Identity under-accounting defect.

**OPQ-525 — the RNG-key guide's one self-contained runnable snippet raises `InputTypeError` and so never demonstrates its own reproducibility claim.** `docs/user-guide/rng-key.md:274-288` · docs-drift-api · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the "Reproducibility" block sets `grads = {"w": torch.randn(100)}` at `:279` — a bare dict — and then calls `noisy1, _ = noise_fn(grads, s1)` at `:282`. HEAD rejects that: `gaussian_noise` requires `ClippedPytree` inputs.
- **Failure scenario:** copied verbatim into a file and run under `uv run`, the block fails at its 8th line with `opaque.api.base.exceptions.InputTypeError: gaussian_noise expects ClippedPytree inputs. Wrap manual values with opaque.types.clipped(...).` (`packages/opaque-dpsgd/src/opaque/api/dpsgd/noise/_gaussian.py:302` → `:322`), so the page never shows that identical keys reproduce identical noise. The fix is one line: `grads = clipped({"w": torch.randn(100)}, max_norm=1.0)` from `opaque.types` — exactly what the raised error instructs.
- **Evidence:** `docs/user-guide/rng-key.md:279` and `:282` (the bare dict and the call); the executed traceback into `_gaussian.py:322`; `grep -rn 'noise_fn(' docs/` returns roughly 30 sites and every other one binds `grads` from a `grad_fn` / `compute_dp_grads` call inside non-runnable pseudo-code, including `docs/reference/rng.md:324`, so this is the only executable-verbatim instance.
- **Fix:** wrap the manual value with `opaque.types.clipped(...)` as the error message says.
- **Notes:** this is the sibling page of the OPQ-362 fix — `docs/reference/rng.md` was rewritten by sweep #843 and is fine; the user-guide companion was not. No CI check executes docs snippets, so it will not self-correct (see OPQ-538).

**OPQ-526 — the distributed reference names `opaque.distributed._state.register_sync_type`, a module path that does not exist.** `docs/reference/distributed.md:80-86` · docs-drift-api · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the page states that "clipping and noise states register themselves with `opaque.distributed._state.register_sync_type`" and that "Lower-level scalar reductions, tensor gathers, and object syncs live in `_state.py`". `import opaque.distributed._state` raises `ModuleNotFoundError`, and the façade exports no `register_sync_type` at all: `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/distributed/__init__.py` re-exports 16 names and the package directory holds only `collectives.py`, `gradients.py`, `__init__.py` and `py.typed`. The real symbol is at `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/distributed/_state.py:537`, re-exported from `opaque.api.engine.distributed` (`__init__.py:22`), which is how every registrant imports it.
- **Failure scenario:** a contributor adding a new sync-able DP state follows `:82` and writes `from opaque.distributed._state import register_sync_type`, which raises `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'opaque.distributed._state'`. The working import is `from opaque.api.engine.distributed import register_sync_type`.
- **Evidence:** `docs/reference/distributed.md:80-86` (the prose); the executed `hasattr` → `False` and `ModuleNotFoundError`; the façade's 16-name `__all__` and the package contents; `_state.py:537` and `opaque/api/engine/distributed/__init__.py:22`; the registrants `packages/opaque-dpftrl/.../noise/_distributed.py:20`, `packages/opaque-optimizers/.../distributed.py:42`, `packages/opaque-engine/.../clipping/_distributed.py:21`, `.../profiling/_distributed.py:15`; one more instance of the same wrong path in a source docstring at `packages/opaque-engine/src/opaque/api/engine/clipping/_distributed.py:6` (`:func:opaque.distributed.register_sync_type`).
- **Fix:** correct the path to `opaque.api.engine.distributed._state.register_sync_type` and note that it is contributor-internal, which ARC-003 permits in implementation discussion; fix the stale cross-reference in `_distributed.py:6` too.
- **Notes:** `mkdocs --strict` cannot catch this because the bad path is inline prose in backticks rather than a `:::` mkdocstrings directive — the neighbouring `::: opaque.distributed.gradients.*` directives do resolve. Contributor-facing only. Companion to OPQ-514.

**OPQ-527 — two user-guide "Runnable references" sections point readers at integration tests that do not exist at the paths given.** `docs/user-guide/dp-sgd.md:172` and `docs/user-guide/dp-ftrl.md:249` · docs-drift · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** both sections name `tests/integration/test_dpsgd_pipeline.py` / `tests/integration/test_dpftrl_pipeline.py` as the canonical executable version of the guide's pipeline. Neither path exists: `ls tests/integration/` shows only `README.md`, `accounting`, `alignment`, `conftest.py`, `ddp`, `noise`, `rng`, `sampling` and `transformers`, with no `test_*.py` at that level. The files live one directory deeper, at `tests/integration/transformers/test_dpsgd_pipeline.py` and `tests/integration/transformers/test_dpftrl_pipeline.py`, and they are the files the prose describes (`test_dpsgd_pipeline.py`'s module docstring names the tiny synthetic `LlamaConfig` fast case and the slow-marked real Qwen2-0.5B case).
- **Failure scenario:** a reader of the DP-SGD or DP-FTRL guide runs `uv run pytest tests/integration/test_dpsgd_pipeline.py` and pytest exits with "file or directory not found". Only the `transformers/` path segment is missing.
- **Evidence:** `docs/user-guide/dp-sgd.md:172` and `docs/user-guide/dp-ftrl.md:249` (the two claims); `ls tests/integration/`; `find . -name test_dpsgd_pipeline.py -o -name test_dpftrl_pipeline.py`; the target modules' docstrings matching the prose; the fixed sibling `docs/user-guide/distributed-trainer.md:92-96`, whose four paths all resolve.
- **Fix:** insert the missing `transformers/` segment in both paths, and add a CI check that resolves every `tests/...py` and `examples/...py` path mentioned in `docs/*.md` against the working tree.
- **Notes:** OPQ-370 was closed in sweep #844 and did fix `distributed-trainer.md`; these two paths were not in its evidence list. Trivial drift, recoverable in seconds, with no runtime, API or privacy consequence.

**OPQ-528 — a published tutorial serves a maintainer's absolute local path, OS username and internal branch name on the public documentation site.** `docs/tutorials/llm_finetuning.ipynb`, nb JSON lines 153, 455, 464 · public-release-hygiene · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** three stored warning-stream outputs contain `/Users/evgeny.grigorenko/Workspaces/copilot-worktrees/opaque/evgri243-issue-416-execute-documentation-examples-and-valid-38f84f/.venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/...` (from `peft/tuners/lora/layer.py:2285`, `transformers/integrations/sdpa_attention.py:92` and `torch/autograd/graph.py:865`). Because `mkdocs.yml:59-61` sets `mkdocs-jupyter: include_source: true, execute: false` and `mkdocs.yml:140` puts the notebook in the published nav, the committed outputs are what ships, and no nbstripout or `ClearOutputPreprocessor` step exists in `mkdocs.yml`, the workflows or pre-commit.
- **Failure scenario:** the chain was closed against the live site rather than inferred: `curl -L https://jetbrains-research.github.io/opaque/latest/tutorials/llm_finetuning/` returns HTTP 200 and contains the string `evgeny.grigorenko` three times, with the full worktree path and the issue-416 branch name intact. Any visitor to the "Fine-tuning an LLM" tutorial reads a maintainer's OS username, local workspace layout, the `copilot-worktrees` convention and an internal branch name tied to an issue number.
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn 'Users/' docs/tutorials/*.ipynb` hits only `llm_finetuning.ipynb` lines 153, 455, 464 (and no `/home/<user>` paths anywhere under `docs/tutorials`); `mkdocs.yml:59-61` and `:140`; `.github/workflows/docs.yml` deploys via mike/GitHub Pages on every push to main with no output-stripping step; the fetched live page containing the path three times.
- **Fix:** re-execute the notebook in a clean checkout so the warning paths are neutral, or strip warning-stream output with an nbstripout / `nbconvert --ClearOutputPreprocessor` filter, and add a CI check that fails on absolute `/Users/` or `/home/` paths in `docs/tutorials/*.ipynb`.
- **Notes:** release hygiene with no functional, numerical or privacy consequence, but durable — nothing in CI strips or normalizes notebook outputs. Milestone issue #578 ("Remove JetBrains-internal specifics from documentation") is closed, and this is the only notebook affected.

### 7.4 Residue of the alignment and auditing fix verifications

**OPQ-529 — `get_training_chat_template` silently changes the rendered conversation text, because Jinja's `trim_blocks` eats the newline after the inserted block tag.** `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/data/_chat_template.py:326-332` (insertion), `:469-520` (validator) · correctness/train-inference-mismatch · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** `_wrap_assistant_content` splices the literal Jinja *block* tags `_GEN_START` / `_GEN_END` (`:36-37`) around a `{{ ... }}` expression with no whitespace control (`:326-332`). transformers compiles chat templates with `trim_blocks=True` (`_cached_compile_jinja_template` builds `ImmutableSandboxedEnvironment(trim_blocks=True, lstrip_blocks=True, ...)` in 5.15.1), so a newline immediately following a block tag is deleted from the output. The only acceptance gate, `_generation_block_marks_only_assistant` (`:469-520`), inspects the *content* of the generation spans (assistant probe present, user and system probes absent) and never compares the transformed render against the untransformed one, so a text-changing transform passes validation.
- **Failure scenario:** with `SFTConfig(assistant_only_loss=True)` on a tokenizer whose template is multi-line and not whitespace-controlled, `packages/opaque-transformers/src/opaque/api/transformers/trl/_sft_trainer.py:426-428` overwrites `processing_class.chat_template` with the regex-spliced template and every row is tokenized from it. Verified on the real cached Hub templates: 9 are accepted by the transform and 3 of them — `HuggingFaceH4/zephyr-7b-beta`, `TinyLlama/TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat-v1.0`, `stabilityai/stablelm-2-zephyr-1_6b` — render differently, producing `...A1</s><|user|>\nU2...` where the canonical template emits `...A1</s>\n<|user|>\nU2...`, plus a lost trailing newline: one whitespace token per assistant turn. `llama3`, `gemma-2`, `Qwen2.5`, `Phi-3`, `Mistral-v0.3` and `SmolLM3` are unaffected because they are single-line or fully whitespace-controlled. The assistant mask stays correct (`spans_ok=True`) and nothing warns.
- **Evidence:** `_chat_template.py:326-332` (the splice) and `:36-37` (the block-tag constants); `:469-520` (the probe-membership-only validator); transformers 5.15.1 `_cached_compile_jinja_template` with `trim_blocks=True`; the render diff reproduced on the three real templates; `_sft_trainer.py:426-428` (the permanent overwrite); TRL 1.10's own `get_training_chat_template` uses hand-curated exact-match template pairs rather than a regex splice, so no upstream defence catches it.
- **Fix:** make `_generation_block_marks_only_assistant` reject any candidate whose full rendered string differs from the untransformed template's rendering for the same probe messages — it already renders both shapes, so this is one added equality. Independently, emit whitespace-neutral markers (`{%+ generation +%}` / `{%+ endgeneration +%}`, or re-append the swallowed newline) so the insertion is text-preserving by construction.
- **Notes:** downgraded from medium for a reason the original report missed. `DPTrainer` mutates the *same* `processing_class` object it later hands to `super().__init__`, and `_dp_trainer.py:5264-5265` calls `self._processing_class.save_pretrained(output_dir)`, so the checkpoint's `tokenizer_config` carries the transformed template and a consumer loading the fine-tuned checkpoint renders identically to training. The divergence bites only when serving uses the base model's canonical template — and the saved artifact's `chat_template` silently differs from the upstream one it was derived from.

**OPQ-530 — `apply_chat_template_with_mask` reports a per-row truncation as a chat-template misconfiguration and aborts the whole dataset map with the wrong remedy.** `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/data/_completion_mask.py:114-124` · error-handling/misleading-diagnostic · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** commit 62a96b72 replaced `if not assistant_masks:` with `if not _has_assistant_tokens(assistant_masks):` (`:114`, helper at `:136-142`), so an all-zero mask now raises. But the template-level guard at `:93-103` has already run and passed by the time `:114` executes, so a `False` result past that point cannot mean a missing marker — it means this particular conversation rendered no assistant tokens (truncation, or a row ending on a non-assistant turn). The error text nonetheless asserts "This means the chat template does not carry the `{% generation %}` / `{% endgeneration %}` markers" and tells the user to install markers that `get_training_chat_template` already installed and that `:93` already verified.
- **Failure scenario:** `DPTrainer` with `SFTConfig(assistant_only_loss=True)` and the default `max_length=1024` (`_sft_config.py:56`) maps `apply_chat_template_with_mask(..., max_length=1024, truncation=True)` over the dataset from `_sft_trainer.py:455-461`, inside the `dataset.map` at `:436-441` — so truncation is on by default. Any single conversation whose system+user prefix alone exceeds `max_length` yields an all-zero assistant mask and aborts preparation of the *entire* dataset. Reproduced with the real cached `Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct` tokenizer: `get_training_chat_template` succeeds, the untruncated conversation gives `len(input_ids)=274` with `completion_mask.sum()=12`, and the same conversation at `max_length=64, truncation=True` raises `ConfigurationError('apply_chat_template_with_mask: the tokenizer returned no assistant-token mask. This means the chat template does not carry the {% generation %} / {% endgeneration %} markers...')`.
- **Evidence:** `_completion_mask.py:93-103` (the template guard that already passed), `:114-124` (the misdirected raise), `:136-142` (the helper); `_sft_trainer.py:455-461` and `:436-441` (the caller inside `dataset.map`); `_sft_config.py:56` (`max_length` default 1024); the reproduced Qwen2.5 transcript; `git show 62a96b72 -- .../_completion_mask.py` (the guard changed from `if not assistant_masks:`, true only for `None`/empty); `_nll.py:23` and `_dft.py:107, 145` clamp their divisor to `min=1`, so the previously-passing all-zero row contributed 0.0 rather than NaN.
- **Fix:** split the two conditions. Keep the template check at `:93` as the `ConfigurationError`; for an empty mask on a rendered row raise a distinct error naming truncation / `max_length` and the offending conversation, or return the row with an all-zero mask and let the caller drop or report it, so one over-long conversation does not abort dataset preparation with a misdirected remedy.
- **Notes:** downgraded from medium because this is fail-closed — a hard error, not silent mistraining — with no privacy or numerical consequence. The fault is the wrong remedy plus the whole-dataset abort: before 62a96b72 the same row passed with an all-zero mask and contributed 0.0 loss, so the change traded a silently useless row for a blocking abort with the wrong diagnosis.

**OPQ-531 — `language_modeling_collator(completion_only_loss=True)` now supervises the full sequence for maskless rows and fabricates an all-ones `completion_mask` for them.** `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/sft/collator/_language_modeling.py:147-161` · fail-open · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** 08d8b1dc inverted the maskless-row behaviour of the public collator. Previously a row without `completion_mask` had all real tokens set to `-100` under `completion_only_loss=True` (fail-closed: no prompt token is ever supervised). Now the `if has_completion_mask and cm is not None` branch (`:147`) simply does not fire, labels stay a full copy of `input_ids`, and the `elif has_completion_mask` branch (`:158-161`) additionally sets `completion_mask_t[i, :seq_len] = 1` — asserting that those prompt tokens *are* completion tokens.
- **Failure scenario:** a caller using the exported `opaque.alignment.sft.collator.language_modeling_collator` directly passes `completion_only_loss=True` with a batch where some rows lack `completion_mask`. Reproduced verbatim through the public façade: `collator(pad_token_id=0, max_length=16, completion_only_loss=True)` on `[{'input_ids':[1,2,3]},{'input_ids':[4,5,6]}]` returns labels `[[1,2,3],[4,5,6]]` with no `completion_mask` key, and on the mixed batch `[{'input_ids':[1,2,3],'completion_mask':[0,1,1]},{'input_ids':[4,5,6]}]` returns labels `[[-100,2,3],[4,5,6]]` and `completion_mask [[0,1,1],[1,1,1]]` — the maskless row is trained on in full and its output mask is fabricated as all ones.
- **Evidence:** `_language_modeling.py:147-161` (the two branches with no `_completion_only_loss` honouring for a maskless row); `git show 08d8b1dc` (the removed `if self._completion_only_loss: labels_t[i,:seq_len] = _IGNORE_INDEX` branches); the reproduced outputs above; `_language_modeling.py:21-24` (module design notes) versus the public factory docstring at `:210-214`; `_sft_trainer.py:406-417` / `_resolve_completion_only` (the trainer-level guard).
- **Fix:** keep the TRL-matching default but make it explicit rather than silent — when `_completion_only_loss` is set and a row supplies no `completion_mask`, either raise (the collator knows both facts) or emit a one-shot warning naming the row index. At minimum, stop fabricating `completion_mask=1` for tokens the caller never marked.
- **Notes:** capped at low by two corrections. The labels half is deliberate and documented: the module design notes at `:21-24` state "Examples without a mask retain full-sequence labels, matching TRL's language-modeling collator", and TRL 1.10's `DataCollatorForLanguageModeling` does take `labels = example.get('labels', example['input_ids'])` — so "silently" is true only relative to the public factory docstring at `:210-214`, which still describes the fail-closed contract. And `DPTrainer` is guarded: `_resolve_completion_only` raises when `completion_only_loss=True` with neither a prompt-completion dataset nor a `completion_mask`, so the default trainer path does not reach this branch (that guard inspects only `dataset[0]`, so a pre-tokenized dataset with a nullable `completion_mask` column could still slip through). The genuinely undocumented part is the fabricated all-ones mask at `:158-161`.

**OPQ-532 — a parametrized test labels its cases `gemma-3-1b-it` and `shared-chatml` while exercising neither; both real template shapes are still rejected.** `packages/opaque-alignment/tests/data/test_chat_template.py:379-383`; root cause at `packages/opaque-alignment/src/opaque/api/alignment/data/_chat_template.py:450-452` · test-integrity/incomplete-fix · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-349(b) named a specific root cause — `content_pat` matches two expressions in the real gemma-3 template, so the `len(content_matches) != 1` bail rejects it — and that regex and bail are unchanged at `_chat_template.py:450-452`. The fix took the documentation route, which the OPQ fix sketch allowed, but the same commit added a parametrized test whose ids are `['meta-llama-3-8b-instruct', 'gemma-3-1b-it', 'shared-chatml']` while the actual parameters are `_LLAMA_3_TEMPLATE` (genuinely the real Llama-3 template), `_GEMMA_INLINE_TEMPLATE` (a synthetic one-line `{% set role = "model" if ... %}` template at `:73-80`, nothing like the real gemma-3, which uses branch mapping and carries a multimodal content branch and a `raise_exception` branch) and `_CHATML_TEMPLATE` (which has an explicit `{% elif message["role"] == "assistant" %}` branch at `:40-48`, i.e. not shared-expression ChatML). Only the Llama-3 id is honest.
- **Failure scenario:** a maintainer reading a green `test_representative_templates_render_assistant_only[gemma-3-1b-it]` / `[shared-chatml]` concludes those two template shapes are covered. Neither is. Running the real `google/gemma-3-1b-it` chat template or canonical shared-expression ChatML through the current tree raises `ConfigurationError('get_training_chat_template: could not identify and validate an assistant-only render path')`, because `content_pat.finditer` yields `["{{ message['content'] | trim }}", '{{ raise_exception("Invalid content type") }}']` and the `len(content_matches) != 1` bail at `:452` rejects it; all four wrap strategies (`_wrap_assistant_content`, `_wrap_shared_content_expression`, `_wrap_gemma_shared_expression`, `_wrap_shared_or_clause`) return `None`. The runtime failure is loud and correctly worded; the defect is that the test ids claim coverage the suite does not have.
- **Evidence:** `test_chat_template.py:379-382` (the ids), `:73-80` and `:40-48` (what the parameters actually are); `_chat_template.py:450` (`content_pat`) and `:452` (the bail), unchanged by 62a96b72; the instrumented run on the real gemma-3 template showing the two content matches and all four strategies returning `None`; by contrast the real Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct, gemma-2-2b-it, Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct, Phi-3-mini-4k-instruct, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3 and SmolLM3-3B templates all succeed with correct assistant-only spans.
- **Fix:** either rename the ids to describe the shapes actually exercised (`shared-set-content`, `inline-role-mapping`, `explicit-assistant-branch`), or make the named templates real — and if the real templates stay unsupported, say so explicitly in `docs/alignment/sft.md`.
- **Notes:** one correction to the original report: `docs/alignment/sft.md` no longer names Gemma (62a96b72 replaced "supported shared-render templates such as Gemma and Qwen" with the generic "selected shared-render forms"), so the docs are not over-claiming — the misleading artifact is the test ids alone. Also note that the fix sketch's "exclude `raise_exception` from `content_pat`" would not suffice for real gemma-3, which has a second renderable content path (the iterable `item['text']` loop) and emits `<end_of_turn>` outside the wrapped expression.

**OPQ-533 — the #895 dataset guard covers the scoring half only: `train_subset()` / `train_indices()` still accept a dataset the partition was not built from.** `packages/opaque-auditing/src/opaque/api/auditing/_coin_flip.py:189, 209` · incomplete-fix · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** e6e3d478 (#895, closing #830 / OPQ-352) records `dataset_size` on `CoinFlip` and asserts it in `_scoring_loader`, but the two methods that turn the partition into the training set are unguarded. `train_indices` resolves `size = self.dataset_size if dataset_size is None else dataset_size` (`:189`) — an explicit argument silently overrides the recorded value with no consistency check — and `train_subset` always supplies one: `return Subset(dataset, self.train_indices(len(dataset)))` (`:209`), so the recorded size is never consulted on that path at all. `train_subset()` is the tutorial's training path (`docs/tutorials/privacy_auditing.ipynb:153, 505`) and `train_indices(len(dataset))` the user guide's (`docs/user-guide/auditing.md:38, 151, 268`).
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced character for character. `coin_flip(ConcatDataset([base32, canaries8]), num_canaries=8, candidate_indices=range(32,40), key=key(0))` records `dataset_size=40` with `out_indices=[34 35 33 32 36]`; `cf.train_subset(base32)` then returns all 32 base rows, excludes nothing and raises nothing, while `cf.train_subset(audited)` correctly returns 35. A milder variant also holds: a `CoinFlip` built on 20 rows accepts a different 30-row dataset (`train_subset` → 27 rows) and even `train_indices(999)` → 996 indices. A user who makes that dataset mix-up trains a target with no held-out canary; the later scoring call against the correct concatenated dataset passes the length check, and the audit reports an uninformative estimate instead of raising.
- **Evidence:** `_coin_flip.py:189` (the silent override) and `:209` (the bypass); `packages/opaque-auditing/src/opaque/api/auditing/attacks/_helpers.py:152-178` (the symmetric guard the fix *did* add, on the scoring side only); the two reproductions above; `docs/tutorials/privacy_auditing.ipynb:153`; no existing test pins the override as intentional — `test_audit.py:325` and `:563` call `train_indices` with an explicit size on `CoinFlip`s built directly by the `_flip` helper (no recorded `dataset_size`), and `:342` only asserts `train_indices() == train_indices(len(dataset))` for the consistent case, so raising on a contradiction would not break the suite.
- **Fix:** in `train_subset`, drop the explicit argument (call `self.train_indices()`) or compare `len(dataset)` with `self.dataset_size` and raise the same `ConfigurationError` message `_scoring_loader` uses; in `train_indices`, raise when an explicit `dataset_size` contradicts a recorded one instead of preferring the argument.
- **Notes:** fail-open on caller misuse in the auditing tooling, not a defect in any privacy mechanism; it needs the user to hand a different dataset object to `train_subset` than the one `coin_flip` was built on, and the consequence is an audit that under-reports rather than a live epsilon understatement. Complementary to OPQ-534, which reports the weakness of the guard on the scoring side.

**OPQ-534 — the #895 scoring guard compares dataset length only, so an equal-length reordering is still scored silently.** `packages/opaque-auditing/src/opaque/api/auditing/attacks/_helpers.py:152-178` · incomplete-fix · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-352's fix sketch asked for "`dataset_size` (and optionally a cheap content fingerprint of a few canary rows)". Only the length comparison and an index-bounds check landed; no fingerprint field exists on `CoinFlip` (`_coin_flip.py:163-168` lists only `num_canaries`, `canary_indices`, `_in_mask`, `in_indices`, `out_indices`, `dataset_size`). `_IndexedCanaries.__getitem__` (`_helpers.py:88-89`, `return position, self._dataset[int(self._canary_indices[position])]`) still indexes whatever object it is handed with the partition's canary indices, so any same-length substitution passes both new checks.
- **Failure scenario:** reproduced on a 20-row `TensorDataset` with 5 canaries. Correct scoring gives `[-4, -49, -25, -81, -169]` for canary ids `[1 6 4 8 12]` and `one_run` → AUC 1.0; scoring the same rows through a randomly permuted `Subset(ds, perm)` returns `[-400, -16, -196, -121, -64]` with **no exception at all**, and `one_run` accepts it (AUC 0.3333). The scores then belong to different records than the coin flips label, and the audit silently measures nothing.
- **Evidence:** `_helpers.py:152-162` (length comparison is the only identity evidence) and `:88-89` (the blind indexing); `_coin_flip.py:163-168` (no fingerprint field); commit e6e3d478 adds none; the two score vectors and AUCs above.
- **Fix:** record a cheap fingerprint at `coin_flip()` time — e.g. blake2b over the bytes/repr of a handful of canary rows drawn from the dataset it was constructed on — alongside `dataset_size`, and re-check it in `_scoring_loader` with the same "full concatenated dataset" message, skipping the check only when the rows are not cheaply hashable.
- **Notes:** two caveats cap this. OPQ-352's own fix sketch called the content fingerprint *optional*, so the missing half was explicitly discretionary. And the original realism argument does not hold: the shuffling the repo's documented pipelines perform is `DataLoader(shuffle=True)` over the training subset (`docs/tutorials/privacy_auditing.ipynb:271`), which never touches the object passed to the scorer; no doc or example reorders the scoring dataset, so hitting this needs the caller to pass a reordered same-length view. Auditing tooling only, and the direction is under-detection.

**OPQ-535 — the grid-floor justification comment records a measurement that does not reproduce, and the test it cites validates the floor only at n=1.** `packages/opaque-auditing/src/opaque/api/auditing/one_run/_estimate.py:34-42` · comment-accuracy · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** the comment that 3ccf15ba (#890) put above `_MIN_GRID_SIZE = 1_000` (`:44`) says "at `grid_size=16` a fully separable 500/500 canary split audits as eps=0". A fully separable 500/500 split is u = 0, and on the shipped code that is not what happens. The same comment says the floor "is validated against the v_k order-statistic identity (Xiang et al. 2025, Eq. 12)", but the test that landed with it (`packages/opaque-auditing/tests/auditing/test_gdp.py:144-158`, `TestVKGridConvergence`) parametrizes only `_compute_v_k(1, 1, ...)` against `Phi(-mu/2)` — the n = 1 Bayes error, which constrains nothing about the high ranks that dominate the Chernoff sum for a 10³-10⁴-canary audit.
- **Failure scenario:** a future maintainer reads the rationale and mis-tunes or removes the floor. Measured on a 500/500 fully separable estimate (best r = 1000, u = 0), `GdpMethod(grid_size=g).epsilon_at(delta=1e-5)`: grid 16 → **32.801667**, 64 → 37.275869, 1 000 → 38.246718, 10 000 → 38.246718 — contradicting the comment. Sweeping separable splits at m = 50/100/500/1000/2000 and δ ∈ {1e-5, 1e-3} gives 11.5 to 36.8 at grid 16, never 0, so there is no reading of "fully separable" under which the comment holds. The configuration that *does* collapse is u = 250 (75 % attack accuracy): grid 16 → **0.0**, 64 → 2.700027, 1 000 → 5.075480, 10 000 → 5.108089 — precisely the case the #829 regression test builds and documents ("500/500 split whose best threshold makes exactly 250 errors", `test_gdp.py:288-320`).
- **Evidence:** `_estimate.py:34-42` (the comment) and `:44` (the constant); the measured separable ladder 32.80 / 37.28 / 38.25 / 38.25 and the separable sweep; the measured u = 250 ladder 0.0 / 2.70 / 5.08 / 5.11; `test_gdp.py:144-158` (`_compute_v_k(1, 1, ...)` only) and `:288-320` (the regression test's own u = 250 docstring).
- **Fix:** restate the comment with the configuration the regression test actually uses (500/500 with 250 errors at the best threshold: ε 0.0 at grid 16, 2.70 at 64, 5.08 at 1 000, 5.11 at 10 000) and cite the grid sweep across audit sizes rather than the n=1 identity; if the n=1 test is kept, add a high-rank case (compare `_compute_v_k` at grid 1 000 vs 10 000 for n = 10 000, top ranks) so the floor has a test that could actually fail.
- **Notes:** the constant itself is adequate — grid 1 000 tracks grid 10 000 within 0.033 absolute at u = 250 — so this is the recorded justification being wrong, not the value. No runtime behaviour is affected and the public factory still enforces the floor (`test_grid_size_below_floor_raises`).

**OPQ-536 — the #895 fix's new public field and new failure mode never reached the auditing reference.** `docs/reference/auditing.md:92-94, 148-150, 238-249` · docs-drift · **low** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** e6e3d478 added a public `CoinFlip.dataset_size` attribute, made `train_indices`' parameter optional with a fallback to the recorded size, and gave `loss_scores` / `gradient_scores` a new hard failure mode (dataset-length mismatch, canary index out of bounds). None of the three reached the reference page — `git show --stat e6e3d478` touches two source files and three test files, no docs. The `CoinFlip` attribute table (`:238-243`) still lists exactly `num_canaries` / `canary_indices` / `in_indices` / `out_indices`; `:248` still shows `cf.train_indices(dataset_size) -> list[int]` with the argument presented as required and the body text "All indices except held-out canaries" with no mention of the default; and both scorers' Raises blocks (`:92-94`, `:148-150`) still enumerate only `batch_argnums` / `batch_size` / `collate_fn` (plus the `reference_scores` `InputTypeError`). `docs/user-guide/auditing.md` mentions none of it either — `grep` over both pages finds no occurrence of `dataset_size` or the mismatch wording.
- **Failure scenario:** a user calls `loss_scores` / `gradient_scores` with a filtered or subset dataset and hits `ConfigurationError: dataset length (N) does not match the dataset size (M) the CoinFlip was constructed from; scoring requires the full concatenated dataset, not a training subset or split` (or the out-of-bounds variant). The message is clear, but neither scorer's Raises block documents the failure mode, the attribute table omits the public `dataset_size` field so there is no documented way to read back the recorded size, and `train_indices` is still documented as requiring an explicit argument.
- **Evidence:** `docs/reference/auditing.md:238-243`, `:248`, `:92-94`, `:148-150` (the four stale sections); `packages/opaque-auditing/src/opaque/api/auditing/_coin_flip.py` (`dataset_size: int | None = None` as a public dataclass field documented in the class docstring Attributes; `def train_indices(self, dataset_size: int | None = None)`); `attacks/_helpers.py:152-175` (the two new `ConfigurationError` branches); `git show --stat e6e3d478`; the reference page is hand-written prose rather than mkdocstrings-generated, so nothing regenerates it.
- **Fix:** add `dataset_size` to the `CoinFlip` attribute table, show `train_indices(dataset_size=None)` with the recorded-size default, and extend both scorers' Raises blocks with the length-mismatch / out-of-bounds `ConfigurationError` and the "full concatenated dataset, not a training subset" requirement.
- **Notes:** pure documentation incompleteness for a fix that landed 2026-09-03 with no docs change; the code fails loudly with a self-explanatory message, so there is no runtime or privacy impact. Not covered by an open issue: #775 is the natural umbrella but its open child #845 is the citation sweep, and this fix landed after that scope.

### 7.5 Coverage and process claims

**OPQ-537 — three registered model families are skipped by all three parity tests, and the guard named `test_parity_families_covered` structurally reports them as covered.** `packages/opaque-patches/tests/transformers/models/test_parity.py:86-92, 251-252` · test-gap · **medium** · ε:no · **CONFIRMED**.
- **Claim:** OPQ-339 and its child #813 are scoped to the *gradient* skips. The same module carries a second, independent exemption set for the forward test: `_STRICT_FORWARD_PARITY_SKIP_FAMILIES = {"deepseek_v4", "gpt_oss", "qwen3_next"}` (`:86-92`), applied by `test_forward_logits_parity` at `:251-252` with `pytest.skip(f"{family} requires runtime compatibility shims")`. The same three (plus `gpt2`) also sit in `_GRAD_PARITY_SKIP_FAMILIES` (`:97-103`), applied at `:278-279` (`test_backward_grads_parity`) and `:483-484` (`test_vmap_grad_parity`) — so they are skipped in all three core parity tests. The coverage guard at `:509-520` recomputes exactly the expression that defines `FAMILIES` (`supported_families() - _PARITY_SKIP_FAMILIES`, filtered by `find_spec`) and asserts `families - set(FAMILIES)` is empty, so it can never see either skip set and reports the three as covered.
- **Failure scenario:** `deepseek_v4`, `gpt_oss` and `qwen3_next` are registered supported families with no patched-vs-upstream comparison anywhere in the package: `assert_parity_forward` / `assert_parity_grad` / `assert_parity_vmap_grad` are used in `test_parity.py` only, and the dedicated modules `test_gpt_oss.py`, `test_deepseek_v4.py` and `test_qwen3_next.py` contain only smoke tests (forward runs, backward runs, "some gradient exists", experts-not-patched).
